# Ashmark prototype

**March formation preview:** the drawing pane fills its available column and stays independent of canvas bitmap size, preventing resize feedback. Desktop and phone landscape keep a stable army-radius preview.

**Campaign recovery and pace:** wounded companions show recovery progress and return to duty when healed; camp, lodging and Medicine accelerate recovery. Stamina restores at 4/hour stopped, 16/hour camped and 20/hour lodging before supply penalties. Campaign movement is twice the base pace; world battle keeps its previous movement scale.

**Battle order previews:** formation ghosts scale with world battle sprites. Movement paths, attack markers and previews draw beneath characters.

Open `Ashmark.html` in a browser. There is no build step and nothing to install.

**Which world.** The generated world is the only one (doc/WORLD_DESIGN.md §15.2, D42, D49): `Ashmark.html` opens on the main
menu with no world made (see "The main menu" below), and New Campaign offers the New world screen (Classic first). The old
hand-made 224 × 160 map is gone (2026-10-22, D49). A save holds the world it was made in: a load makes that world again first
(`core/save.js` `loadGame`); a save of the old map is another world version and is refused.

## Independent army groups (save schema v2)

Start a **new campaign** for this version. Old saves are not loaded or migrated; old browser storage is left untouched.

- Each army has at most **10 groups**, including its hero/leader and companions. Wounded companions in your band reserve their slot; companions leading another army belong to that army instead.
- Each troop group contains one unit type. Duplicate types have persistent, distinct IDs. The existing total-headcount capacity remains a separate limit; no new per-group soldier cap is imposed.
- Open **Hero → Party → Manage** to name a group (up to 32 characters) or merge compatible groups (no splitting: the owner, 2026-10-31). **Reinforce** selects a recruitment target.
- Use **↑ / ↓** to reorder the entire army, including hero and companion groups. This persists into battle cards, default number keys and deployment lanes. Wounded companions reserve a slot but have no battle card until recovered. A troop group whose men are all wounded keeps its slot too (`ArmyRoster#held`, 2026-10-30): no new group takes its place, and its men come back into it when healed. Custom Ctrl+number selections still override the default keys within a battle.
- Each group shows its own wounded count. Groups with no fit soldiers appear under **Recovering groups** and retain their name when soldiers return. They do not take a battle card or an active slot until restored.
- Battles show one card per group. Shift-click adds cards to the selection. **1–9 and 0** select the ten cards; Ctrl+number stores a custom selection. Badges update immediately to the effective keys: a shared key appears on each member, an overridden default disappears, and multiple keys show two plus a remainder count (every key in the tooltip/long press). Card order stays fixed. **Home** resets the battle camera (0 is now the tenth card).
- The cards carry no words (`battlehud.js` `cardStatus`): key, portrait, count, shape and formation, the health/morale/stamina bars and up to `CARD.show` (4) status icons by rank: failing morale (with how many run or break), order (none while holding), pace while moving, an ability on, out of ammunition or exhausted, flags, a character falling back, low ammunition or tired, hidden or in woods, confident, weaknesses. The words are in the card's `title`/`aria-label` (a long press on touch), each icon's in its SVG `<title>`. The All card: the troops icon, the total, and running/breaking counts.
- Every card status icon is a round illustrated badge (`moraleicons.js` `badgeSVG`: shared material art over a dark inside), as in the details and beside the banners; its rim shows its kind (`BADGE_KIND` → `BADGE_CAT`): order gold, pace pale grey, ability on green, warning amber, bad red, flags steel blue, sight teal; morale and weaknesses keep their own rims. The order bar resolves `ORDER_ICON`/`ABIL_ICON` to the same illustrated family. Tactical markers share the illustrated atlas crops, with the small glyph registry as an image-loading fallback.
- The banners over the groups (`moraleicons.js` `iconLayout`) each stay over their own group, eased after it on the battle clock (deploying, with the clock frozen and groups placed at once, simply put over it: `tests/dragcancel.js`); they may overlap (selected, then hovered, then yours over theirs; one underneath faded). Only overlapping banners of groups at blows with each other fan out side by side, eased over `ICON_FAN.ease` (0.25 s); `reducedMotion()` places them at once. A click picks the topmost banner; a second click on the same spot cycles to the next one underneath (`iconPick`).
- Orders, formation, facing, waypoints and capability-filtered ability toggles are independent for each group. Enemies and allied armies use the same grouping model; reinforcements keep their own army budgets and source identities.
- Fought-battle casualties and wounded returns retain the originating troop-group ID. Merging groups transfers wounded ownership. Wounded whose original group cannot fit wait rather than reinforcing an unrelated duplicate.
- Fought-battle results include a per-group report in battle-card order: starting soldiers, fit survivors, new wounded and killed. Medicine returns count as fit; extra rearguard deaths after a failed escape belong to the actual group that lost them. Destroyed groups retain their name in this report. Earlier wounded are excluded. Hero/companion conditions appear separately and never count as soldier deaths. Auto-resolve and non-field encounters retain their aggregate losses summary rather than inventing a field breakdown.
- **Town → Recruit** lets you choose a destination group (or one new group), enter a soldier quantity, review total gold/headcount/slots, then confirm. Orders are all-or-nothing. Single-soldier quick-hire buttons remain available.
- Click a training-tree row to **upgrade a whole group**: select the source group, review its total gold and pooled XP cost, then confirm. Its ID, soldier count and slot are preserved, even in a full ten-group army; formation resets to Line. Groups with wounded waiting to return must heal first. Individual **Train 1** buttons remain available and may need a free slot for a new type.
- Recruitment, transfers and training reject operations that need an unavailable slot. A changed price or unavailable order is rechecked at confirmation rather than silently charging different terms.
- Open a companion’s details → **Orders** to select the exact source formation, quantity and destination (compatible group or a separate new group). Review and confirm the whole order; capacity, proximity, leadership and army slots are rechecked. Only fit soldiers move, leaving wounded with the source army. A whole fit formation moved to a new group keeps its name, formation and frontage; a partial new group uses defaults, and reinforcement keeps the destination’s settings. IDs are local to the receiving army.
- Taking the last soldiers back reserves a slot for the returning companion as well as any new troop group. Automatic **Come back** preserves separate formations and their settings too; if the entire returning army plus its leader cannot fit, no soldiers move. Automatic return retains the prior over-headcount-cap policy; explicit transfers cannot exceed headcount capacity. Giving to a distant companion remains allowed, but taking soldiers back requires being within 60 pixels. Neither direction may change a party currently in a battle. Garrisons still convert stationed soldiers into generic defenders.
- Individual attacks, fatigue, weapon rules and morale remain the existing systems. The proposed shared group-resolve / unanswered-missile-fire morale redesign is **not implemented** by this change. Troop XP is still pooled by unit type.

`core/army.js` owns group identity and mutations. `P.troops` and NPC `tc` remain count views/interchange records for economy and world simulation; `ArmyRoster` updates them together with `army.groups`. Battle controls reference unique IDs rather than fixed Infantry/Archers/Mages/Cavalry buckets. Tactical role is a separate field used by the captain.

Focused regression suites: `armygroups`, `armyedges`, `groups`, `battlesetup`, `battlecommands`, `rts`, plus the existing recruitment, roster, laboratory, combat and morale suites. Test-only `testGroup(role)` resolves role-based execution fixtures to real group IDs; it is not a gameplay or save-compatibility API.

**Army progression** (units, 2026-10-31; the owner: "a 10 unit is actually consist of xx amount of individuals"): start with 3 unit slots, counting the hero and companions. Each trained Leadership level above 2 adds a slot, up to 10, and so do Followers (I and II), Guard and Quartermaster General (+1 each; Lone Wolf one fewer: tree.js `slots`). A unit holds its kind's size including its wounded (`data/units.js` `UNIT_SIZE`: foot 15, ranged 10, mounted 6; `unitSize(k)`, a kind's own `ST[k].size` first). How many you may lead is everyone now and the room your units still have (`PlayerParty#cap`, `ArmyRoster#room`): no other cap. Lords hire the same way: their unit slots are their old men limit over `LORD_RECRUIT.men` (12); resting at home they refill their short units a man at a time, then buy one whole unit into a free slot, from the holding's willing; now and then one unit is trained up whole, only into a kind whose unit holds all its men (`world/realms.js` `lordRecruit`). A holding's willing pool is a share of its people (`world/economy.js` `VOL_POOL`: a quarter at the start, at most a third, a city of 320 about 107). Men who join one at a time (prisoners, volunteers, the road's deserters, a pressed villager, refugees, an outcome's troops) refill your short units of their kind first, else start a new one. Army and recruitment windows show the unlocked limit and unit-group occupancy. Circles scale with army size and formation footprint. Monster packs share contact circles, physical battle entry and same-kind reinforcements. Normal world battles walk into position, then pause with Start Battle in the screen center.

**Travel controls:** a click or tap travels there, a second on the same spot too; a party clicked opens its ring. (Hurry, a forced march on a double click, key R and the ring, was removed 2026-10-31: the owner, "remove hurry features"; an army marches at its men's pace.)

**World-map combat (first playable version):** Army → **March formation** (its tab), Field, sets the place of each group and of your hero round your army's middle, and each troop group's formation (Line, Column, Loose or cavalry Wedge). Army and March share deployable group numbers. March circles preview fixed-size unit sprites in each draft formation, with numbers above the preview. The formation drawing fills the available left pane. Apply formation saves both placement and formation for travel and world-battle entry; Discard drops the edits. Soldiers use their actual battle sprites and walking poses at world-combat scale, with faction cloth colors and individual troop races. Army leaders use their battle character sprites too, including hero and companion equipment, drawn directly from full-resolution assets. They follow individual paths around blocked ground and building feet, compress through narrow crossings, and reform afterward. Their actual positions carry into and out of combat. Leaders keep moving while soldiers turn and reform at ordinary marching speed; stuck followers do not stop the army. Blocked formation slots settle on the nearest reachable standing ground, including riverbanks and cliff-aware paths. Turns ease smoothly and each visible army has an outer circle and dashed interaction ring. Party contact opens the usual encounter; Fight on the road locks a circle on that actual world terrain. Dialogue changes to combat at the same world position and native camera scale, without a separate arena zoom or loading screen. Use the normal battle commands inside the circle. Eligible armies physically approach and join; routed soldiers choose reachable exits and can regroup once while allies still fight, preserving health, ammunition and fatigue. A full rout or the last fighter falling settles victory/defeat without a rally delay. Combat has no fog within the circle/apron; campaign fog remains outside. Sprites and formation spacing are 3× larger, with native-pixel movement and distance-based walk poses. World-battle screen shake is reduced to 20% of regular battles. World combat shares the campaign 1×/2×/4×/8× speed and movement clock, including the hero; hero impact effects do not freeze other armies. Unreachable battles disengage without a false victory or retreat penalty. Formation footprints grow the army radius when needed. Tournaments and settlement assaults retain their existing fields. The campaign clock pauses during combat; arriving armies advance on the shared-speed combat clock. Design, ownership and tuning: [`WORLD_BATTLE_DESIGN.md`](../doc/WORLD_BATTLE_DESIGN.md).

`wiki.html` is the game reference: world map, biomes, races, realms, holdings, the economy, battle, troops, gear, monsters and life on the map. It loads the game's own data and sprite scripts, so it updates itself whenever the data changes.

## Layout

```
Ashmark.html          page markup; loads style.css, the PX engine (../engine: pixel, buildkit, buildmodels (every building as a model at the owner's scale: objects 16 px, buildings 32, big ones and centres 64, a capital 128: the world map's buildings, sites and camps; the old hand-drawn buildings.js retired at stage 3), build-vale, build-karn, build-ashgor, build-sylvan (each culture's building style: Vale with the Salt League, the Clans, the Horde, the Court), pieces, ground, worldnature, beasts,
                      model), then the scripts below (the full order is the <script> list in Ashmark.html)
art/portrait/         121 source portraits; game/ has 30 compact named-character and animal portraits; review gallery and identity manifest
art/portrait/gamecopies.py  the troops' portraits for the game: a 256 × 256 WebP in art/portrait/game/ of each and js/data/unitportraits.js (UNIT_PORTRAITS)
art/tracks.py         the tracks' art, drawn once: writes art/tracks.svg (the sheet: boots, bare foot, hooves, paws, troll, talon, dragon, ruts, a drag, sample trails), art/tracks-px.svg (the same pixelated at their size in the game, each of the 8 ways) and js/data/trackart.js (TRACK_PX, the pixel prints the game draws: gfx/tracks.js trackPic; data/tracks.js TRACKS.art says who leaves which and how big)
style.css
fieldbook.css        scoped management-window theme and responsive layouts (doc/FIELDBOOK_UI.md)
js/core/              core.js (DOM, canvas, constants, helpers, the action registry), state.js (state factories, G, P; your numbers' older names),
                      roster.js (TroopRoster, count/list helpers), army.js (ArmyRoster, persistent groups and slot budget), ranks.js (a group's rank: rankOf, rankGain, rankReady; data/ranks.js RANKS), garrison.js (GarrisonService),
                      items.js (the item kinds: Gear, Mount, Quiver, Good, Trophy, Valuable, QuestItem; their categories; itemKind),
                      inventory.js (Inventory: the bag, its overflow slots and their burden; bag operations),
                      equipment.js (EquipmentService), recruitment.js (RecruitmentService: hiring, prices, upgrades: one full unit of the new kind),
                      talents.js (TalentService and character talent commands), session.js (GameSession and new game), save.js,
                      systems.js (Systems: the world's day and frame, each system's work registered in its own file with its order),
                      saves.js (Saves: the world part of a save, each piece registered with its key, order, save, load and defaults)
                      audio.js (the sound: context, buses, synth parts, the SFX cues), music.js (generative music, its moods, audioWatch);
                      the core parts (doc/CORE_DESIGN.md, after data/): clock.js (Clock: the world's time), timed.js (Timed: states that
                      last days), bus.js (worldOn/worldEmit), rng.js (rngOf: named random streams), pronouns.js (genderOf, pr, prFill:
                      he or she), spatial.js (Spatial: nearest, within), assign.js (assignSlots: places to men by the least
                      walking, SLOTFILL)
js/data/              tables only, no game logic: races (and WILD_RACES: those no one plays, the goblins), factions, units,
                      goblins (GOBLINS: their switch and every number, their troops), monsters (incl. mythic ones like the dragon), critters,
                      kits (a unit's kit, its terms: ORDERS, STANCES, KITS (each kind's ladder; RANK_SKILLS its old name), the skill bar's
                      SKILL_KEYS: doc/UNIT_KITS_DESIGN.md), abilities (the characters' skills, HERO_SKILLS, and the traits, TRAITS), matchups, gear, progression (pasts, skills, perks, companions), tree, holdings (the kinds of holding and their
                      classes), places (goods, holdings, camp spots), items (the item categories and their rules, the bag's tabs
                      and burden, valuables, quest items, the heirlooms),
                      economy (estates, walls, production, needs, taxes, caravans), conduct (what deeds cost),
                      scenes (who you meet inside a place, buildings, building styles), world (the world's scale: its distances,
                      the movement lever, and the wild things each world holds, DENSITY), chains (the story chains' numbers,
                      HIDDEN_RATE, the steps' STEP; the rescue's and Ander's wandering numbers; Ander The Fallen as a companion),
                      quests (the boards' JOB_OFFERS and JOB_REFRESH, QUEST_LATE, QUEST_CLOSED, QUEST_ABANDON (a job given up),
                      JOB_NEEDS (what a job asks of you), the journal's QUEST_CATS, QUEST_STATES and QUEST_ARCHIVE),
                      names (NAMING: a name's length and your band's default; the word tables of a random band name),
                      tracks (TRACKS: the prints every band leaves, how clear on each ground, how long they last, who sees them),
                      weather (WEATHER: its regions, each climate's kinds and weights, each kind's hours, effects and art),
                      dialogue (DIALOGUE: a speaker's first words by role; TALK_PIC: each role's portrait, swappable),
                      prisons (PRISONS: each place's prison by kind, its name, break-out and bribe numbers, cells; walls and garrison; PRISON: the rules of world/prisons.js),
                      questbook/ (the hand-written quests, doc/QUESTBOOK_DESIGN.md: book.js QUESTBOOK (the entries' shape), QUEST_CHECKS,
                      QUESTBOOK_OFFER; samples.js and every themed file after it: Object.assign(QUESTBOOK, {...})),
                      dismount (DISMOUNT: a rider on foot's pace, the stun, mounting, a horse's health and its share of the hits, the throw; a thrown rider's mount bolting or fighting)
js/story/             the story system's shared parts (doc/STORY_DESIGN.md, S0–S3, S5, ST2): context.js (storyCtx: where, when, who, the
                      weather, once a frame), outcome.js (Outcome: what a choice does, declared, and its words), choice.js (Choice,
                      and ChoicePage: the one page for any happening's, thing's or place's choices), kinds.js (the kinds registry:
                      defineKind, kindOf, the triggers' buckets), conversation.js (Conversation, Speaker and its kinds, TalkMemory:
                      one window for talking with a lord, a townsperson, a guard, a worker; a lord's temper and checks:
                      praise, a favour, a threat; a companion's talk, wish and banter; your captor's
                      talk; a story page's speaker (page.who); doc/DIALOGUE_DESIGN.md), happening.js (Happening, the base of every shape), encounter.js (the encounters' Behaviours and Encounter),
                      incident.js (Incident: the incidents' shape, and their daily roll), quest.js (Quest: the jobs' shape, their deeds heard on the bus),
                      questchain.js (QuestChain: steps of happenings sharing a memory: the chains), gathering.js (Gathering: held at a
                      place on set days, announced ahead; its calendar; framework only), steps.js (a chain's reusable step
                      kinds: visit, deliver, lair, held (a rescue), on StepQuest; the escort's base), wounded.js (the first
                      chain, The Wounded Traveller, its rescue, and Ander The Fallen: its steps, words and outcomes; Ander
                      wandering the taverns, Wanderer)
js/world/             the campaign map and everything on it
  map.js territory.js   the tile kinds and climates and what they cost to cross; each holding's land
  fog.js                the fog of war: the land you have explored (W.seen) and what you see now (Sight: every man's of your army); every army's eyes (ArmySight,
                        armySight: its men's, for a threat on the march); the minimap's dark, by chunks
  worldmap.js           the WorldMap: its size and layers, one map in at a time (WM); battleSite, what a battle reads of its tile
                        (a generated world's river, crossing, height and high ground too)
  worldsettings.js worldgen.js worldplace.js
                        the world generator (doc/WORLD_DESIGN.md): the clamped settings (their presets, the
                        holdings' density, the world code), the terrain stages 1-6 and the New world screen's quick sketch,
                        the placement stages 7-9 (holdings, roads and crossings, each holding's rivers crossed near it:
                        placeHoldXing; every bridge on the roads; a clearing round a holding in the woods: placeClear;
                        big oases liked by desert peoples: PLACE_OASIS; ponds and oases never bridged; camps; the classic
                        seed's anchors and hints: tests/classicseed.js); seeded, never Math.random; placeWalk, the ground
                        walked to from a tile (a holding's gate) within a box, and how far
  router.js             every way over the land behind three calls: Router.travel (a step toward a point, or a holding's gate:
                        gateGoal; with road, keeping to the roads: caravans, WAY.keep; workers WAY.folk), Router.reach,
                        Router.days (and Router.close, Router.stats): the way graph (buildWayGraph, charted again with the
                        estates' tracks: wayRechart) and the two-level router over it, searches in a box for each leg;
                        each leg's tiles straightened (waySmooth, wayStraight: WAY_SMOOTH; no staircase, still round
                        woods, water and off-road ground where that is cheaper; tests/straightways.js)
  roadband.js           the road's band: a road's speed follows the road as drawn (gfx/worldart.js artRoadSegs), not its tiles;
                        blended to the land's over its shoulder; an estate's track a little slower (ROAD_BAND, RoadBand; read by
                        map.js speedAt and regionAt; loads after gfx/worldart.js)
  weather.js            the weather (Weather): the map's regions, each its climate's weather in fronts of some hours (the
                        Systems hour 'weather', the stream 'weather'; saved), read by ambWeather, speedAt and visMulAt
  realms.js siege.js    the realms (Realm: wars and peace, honour and hostility, holdings and lords) and their lords; sieges,
                        raids, captures and fiefs
  leaders.js            the Lord and Chieftain roles (characters who choose for themselves); the bandit chieftains
  characters.js         everyone who fights is a Unit: a Troop (one per kind of soldier) or a Character (one person who grows;
                        its role is its class: Hero here, Companion, Lord, Chieftain)
  relations.js          how each lord feels about you, and about each other (Relations)
  worldobject.js        what every thing on the map shares: WorldObject (pick, tip, meet, view), asClass, level of detail by chunk (WORLD_LOD), walk cycles
  holding.js            a holding: Holding and its kinds (Village, Town, City, Castle), on the map HoldingObject, and the factory
                        (makeHolding, setHoldings, TOWNS); what each kind has is data (data/holdings.js HOLDING_TYPES)
  parties.js            every party's soldiers as counts; its kind's class (look, sight, shelter); W.parties; your band (P: PlayerParty,
                        with your numbers: size, room, food, sight, skills, gear, vitals); a holding's defenders in a siege or a raid (GarrisonParty: its own flag and its realm's colours, never a bandit rag); a fight's own men under their place's flag (BandParty flagB, colB: a camp's or a hideout's men under its rag as on the map, a realm's real toll-men under their holding's flag and in its colours)
  brains.js             how each kind of party behaves on the map; the systems parties and patrol moves run them (updateParties: all at once)
  waylay.js             bands (often) and lords (rarely) lying in wait in the trees by a road; your sight sources spot them (WAYLAY)
  fieldloot.js          spoils left on the field: withdrawn or beaten with beasts holding it, the fight's spoils stay there (fieldSpoils, leaveFieldLoot; the pack's ground moved there), a side quest to take them back (FieldLootQuest, step kind 'fieldloot': there with no beast on the spot), a band near it may take them first (BandBrain, lootGo), gone in 5 days (FIELD_LOOT)
  captivity.js          beaten but alive: the escape roll, taken captive (ransom, persuade, serve, your realm pays, refuse), their cage a day at a time (break out, bribe, talk, wait; your companions' rescue), scattered troops finding you, their stash (what they took, in a camp or hideout of a band's or a holding of their realm; StashQuest, step kind 'stash': clear or raid it for 60% back, buy the gear back from a tavern's fence, sold off after 30 days), the traits that help (Slippery, Silver Tongue, Old Soldier: CAPTIVITY.traits); taken to their camp or holding and freed there (placeOf, release; a band takes you to a hideout of its own kind, the camp you were taken at first: goblins to a goblin camp, bandits never to one, `captor` band and site), no break-out too hurt or in chains, a band may sell you on to a ransom broker far off (CAPTIVITY.sold); prisons (prisonOf: the place's prison by kind, PRISONS) and the war rule (a lord or realm at war with yours: straight to the prison of its nearest walled holding at the war price, no persuading, serving or release by time; your realm's ransom from its treasury, a lord's parole, peace frees you: CAPTIVITY.war) (CAPTIVITY; doc/CAPTIVITY_DESIGN.md, doc/PRISONS_DESIGN.md)
  graveart.js (gfx)     a grave's place on the map (GraveArt): its ground; remains as they lay, a small grave, a headstone, a tomb; a cairn in a ring of standing stones, your weapon planted in it, a cloth of your colour; an heir's fenced plot, its carved headstone, flowered mound, candles and banner; fireflies by night
  lineage.js            the lords' houses (LordHouse, Houses, Crypt): a lord dead for good (put to death, now and then slain as his army is beaten) buried in his house's crypt at its seat; after mourning, one of his house takes his place
  family.js (ui)        the Journal's Family and Houses (a lord's house's tree, the unknown ??): your line as a chart of square portrait tiles (child below parent, sibling and cousin beside, heir a small tile); a tile clicked tells its life: people, past, days, level, renown, what killed them, their heir, where they lie
  legacy.js             a new life in the same world (Legacy; doc/DEATH_DESIGN.md §6): slain, a new race, name and past; the old hero's heir (the most loyal companion) a lord over your holdings, crowned or a vassal; the rest handed out, your hero, quests and map reset
  death.js              a fight lost to beasts (Death, ReunionQuest, Remains; data/death.js DEATH; doc/DEATH_DESIGN.md): your whole side down, the roll to live; dying, your remains on the map and the Slain page; living, a day gone, your army gone, your companions to holdings near (found, or won back), food, horse, passengers, the beast home, a man-eater
  hosts.js              armies that march together (Host, HOSTS; doc/HOSTS_DESIGN.md): a leader and the armies that follow him, in a column on his own trail (each army's ring behind the one ahead of it, `HOSTS.gap` px between the rings, straight round a bend too: `Host#back`, `Host#places`; the owner, 2026-11-01: "big army, bigger interaction touchpoint", armies never inside one another; a lagging one holds those behind it back; its distances (far, the pace, gone, a siege's strength, joining a battle: `joinReach`) measured past each one's place), at the slowest's pace, joining his battles; phase 1: your companions' parties with Follow; phase 2 (HOSTS.lord): a lord follows you (asked in his talk, or asking you; deciding each battle by its conditions; Host.decide), you follow a friendly lord (Host.under, the save piece hosts); phase 3 (HOSTS.camp): realm campaigns, a marshal (l.campaign) and his host (l.with) marching and besieging together, you summoned when you serve the realm
  stalls.js             a party still on its way for WORLD_STALL.hours sent another way, and kept to read (Stalls.seen)
  prisons.js            who is held where (Prisons, the save piece prisons): every holding's Prison on its rail (who is held; pay, bribe, visit, break out; from outside a hostile one's walls), lords taken in the realms' wars (their ransom, exchanges, peace, a prison broken open), your companions left behind when you break out alone, your own prisoners (lock up, ransom, mercy, exchange, execute; soldiers recruited) (doc/PRISONS_DESIGN.md)
  captives.js           leaders in battle; captured, ransomed, let go or escaped; a passenger of yours carried off (carryOff)
  patrols.js            every holding's guards ride its land: a day watch and a night watch (Watches, t.watch; class wave D)
  defence.js            the land defended: monsters in the wilds, bandits away from homes, the watch and lords ride out at them;
                        off their lands a beast falls on any party it dares (DEFEND.dare), a lord on the road turns on one near him he can beat (DEFEND.pass)
                        (the LandDefence service; class wave D)
  fights.js             fights between parties on the map, which take time and can be joined
  stay.js               staying in a holding (your band), and the shelter rules for you and your companions
  supply.js             your band's supplies (doc/SUPPLY_DESIGN.md): SUPPLY (every number), stamina, food and drink by the hour,
                        furs and the desert's water, the waterskins (full and empty: drunk, filled, bought, a recruit's), its
                        health, hunger and thirst on your hero and her death, the camp (Z) on
                        clear ground, the card's numbers and the action ring's map actions
  economy.js            a holding's Economy (stock, production, prices, people; its day) and the treasuries
  buildings.js          what a holding has built: its Buildings (estates' levels, walls, projects; its day)
  estates.js            each Estate (fields, guards, sabotage, its day's work) and the spoils of raiding one; its window is ui/estate.js
  lots.js               placed buildings on the grid: a holding's Layout (its lots), the grid's searches, placing by hand;

- **Horse legs and outline** (2026-11-04; the owner: "make sure the leg when walking or running is correct and fix the extra dots in the outer line"; `people.js` `horseLeg`, `drawHorse`; `tests/horselegs.js`): every leg drawn edge to edge (a diagonal stepped along an edge, never only corner to corner, which the outline broke into dots), the near leg 2 px its whole length, the far one 1 px and darker a pixel apart, so a pair reads as two legs; the tail joined the same way; the nostril inside the muzzle (no dot past it). The test: no pixel of the legs on its own in any frame. The rider (the owner: "a bit low", then "show more of him"): on the saddle, no longer sunk a px into it (`HORSE_ART.sized.sink` 0), and his seated leg drawn over the cloth, a short thigh, the shin slanting from the knee and a small boot, outlined on their own (`RIDER_LEG`), on every mount; in what he wears (`riderLegCol`): plate, steel greaves and sabatons; mail, chain links in two shades and iron-shod boots; hide, fur; leather or none, leggings and leather boots; your hero's and a companion's own greaves and boots (`legsC`, `feetC` from their gear) in their metal's colours.
                      figures.js (a figure's gear apart from its body: FigureGear, FigureRig, the GEAR table, the finished figures' LRU FIGURES),

                      marchgear.js (what a marching man carries besides his kit: the army's flags, torches after dark; MARCH_GEAR),

                      structures.js (placed buildings as models: lotModel, lotSprite, lotYard, lotsAhead; estates' ground, camps
                      and sites as models, flags), render.js (world and battle drawing, the armies' labels, HUD; the outline engine: outlineOf, a ring of one colour round any picture's silhouette, unitHighlight: your selected soldiers ringed gold, your group under the pointer white, a foe you point at red), stagger.js (the ways to be knocked down and rocked),
                      depth.js (what stands in front of a figure on the map, and its fade), vfx.js (battle effects), worldui.js (the map's
                      pointer marks, the crossed swords over a party you go to attack (wuiSwords), route trail (the way your hero walks: heroWay, the router's round water, as footprints: wuiFoot) and encounter highlights), mist.js (mist in the fog), weapons.js (battle weapons),
                      tracks.js (the tracks' prints, turned the way each band went, on the ground under all that stands: drawTracks),
                      weather.js (the weather drawn over the map and the field: rain, snow, sand, fog, lightning: WeatherArt),

                      and title, drawn in code: MenuScene, MENU_ART)
js/ui/                fieldbook.js (management frame, native-art cache, meters, draft choice pages), hiredesk.js (HireDesk: the hiring screen of the barracks and the travellers' sellswords), partypage.js (the Army window, C: Roster, March formation (ui/march.js), Army skills, Condition, Passengers),
                      companionpage.js (a companion's card detail: its sections, equipment and reviewed orders),
                      mainmenu.js (the main menu: the page's first screen, no world behind it), ui.js (panels, title, the cards' banner tab, its Settings page (the Game Menu's too), Esc, the game-over window), loading.js (the loading overlay; runSteps runs long work in steps), worldsetup.js (the New world screen: a world's settings before you forge it), names.js (your hero's and your band's names: the New game page's fields, the Names window from the band card, random names), character.js (the Army window's commands, your hero's sheet, perks), journal.js (the Journal window, J: its sections, defineJournalSection), questwindow.js (your Quests, a window of its own, Q: Main, Side, Jobs and the Archive; the list and the one chosen in full, its Show on map, Track and Abandon under the objective, in its page), chronicle.js (the Journal's Chronicle, L: lasting events by day, its filters and links), lordsrealms.js (the Journal's Lords and Realms), hub.js (the Game Menu, Esc, Tab and the bar's Menu: the world and its seed, a tile per window (defineMenuEntry), Save, Load, Settings, Main menu, Exit; the game paused), talents, inventory, town (a holding's
                      places: Place, definePlace, PLACES; class wave D),
                      window.js (rail windows: a rail of places beside the chosen one's window; the same place drawn again changes in place, morph, its buttons kept), townui.js (a town's places, its overview, your

                      M or a click on the minimap), loot.js (the spoils and the battle report, army by army), icons.js (shared transparent UI illustrations and atlas crops), rankbadge.js (a group's rank badge), battlehud.js (battle HUD and army relationships), characterportraits.js (named faces), faces.js (the generated faces: the portrait engine's, the face maker), moraleicons.js (morale
                      badges and marks), orderpaths.js (where your groups are going; the withdrawal's preview and strip: WithdrawView), unitcard.js (UnitCard: the unit card, as Total War: Warhammer III's, in the Army window, the battle's panel and the barracks), developer.js (developer console),
                      ring.js (map hold progress and round action buttons: ActionRing)
js/main.js            game loop and boot
archive/              art.js (the sprite-sheet mapping for the graphics/ pixel-art pack, set aside; the pack is not in the repository)
audio/                offline music scripts (Python: a MIDI/WAV soundtrack generator); the game's own sound is core/audio.js, music.js
```

The scripts are plain `<script>` files, not modules, so the page still works from `file://`. Their top-level `const` and `function` names are shared across files. The rules that follow (checked by `tests/structure.js`):

- **One name, one place.** Every top-level name is global; a second `function` with the same name would silently replace the first.
- **Load order.** `core/core.js` first, then all of `data/`, then the systems (story, world, battle, scene, gfx, ui). Code that runs at load time (data tables, event listeners, `onAct` registrations) goes after everything it uses. `wiki.html` loads the same list (minus `ui/input.js` and `main.js`); keep the two in step.
- **Data, not special cases.** Tunable numbers and kinds of things live in `js/data/`. A new monster is a `MONSTERS` entry; one that roams the whole map, like the dragon, adds a `mythic` block. The cost of a deed is a `CONDUCT` entry.
- **Systems and saves.** A system's daily and per-frame work is one `Systems.add({name, order:{day, frame}, day, frame, ends})` line in its own file; `world.js` `dayTick` and `updateWorld` only run `Systems.day()` and `Systems.frame(d, dt)` (a frame with `ends` returning true ends the frame). The order is everything (one shared `Math.random`): `tests/structure.js` pins it and the world fingerprints hold it. A piece of the world's save is one `Saves.add({key, order, save, load, settle, defaults})` (`save` returning undefined leaves its key out); `save.js` `worldSave` / `worldLoad` run them. Keep the saved keys in their order (W7, B2 hash the save). Design: `doc/CORE_DESIGN.md` K1.
- **Actions.** Every button names an action `prefix-name` (`data-act="cv-buy"`). A system claims its prefix once with `onAct('cv-', (a,arg)=>G.cv?asParty(G.cv).act(a,arg):true)`; a second claim throws. Actions without a registered prefix go to `act()` in `ui/input.js` (the menu, encounters); a town window's own (`tab`, `bld`, `hire`, `buy`, `contract`, `ransom` …) go on to `townAct` in `ui/town.js`, which hands each to the place whose verb it is (`Place` `verbs`: `PLACES`, `definePlace`).
- **Settings.** Each remembered setting is one entry of `PREFS` (`ui/ui.js`): its default, how its browser-storage key `ashmark.<key>` reads and writes, and what a change does (`apply`). `prefSet(k,v)` changes and stores one (storage may be blocked: the value still changes for the session); the globals other files read (`GRID.on`, `BSPEED.v`, `GUIDE.on`, `HPBARS.on`, `BLOOD.on`, `BDEPLOY.on`, `AUTOSK.on`, `BAUTO.on`) are views over it. The Settings buttons go through `settingAct`. A new setting is a `PREFS` entry (plus its row on the Settings page). Tests: `tests/uiprefs.js`.
- **Encounters.** A new kind of fight adds its entry to `ON_WIN` in `battle/engage.js`: what winning it pays and changes.

### Shared helpers and cleanup

**The core parts** (doc/CORE_DESIGN.md, K2; loaded after `data/`, before `core/state.js`; checked by `tests/coreparts.js`):
- `core/clock.js` `Clock`: the day, `hour`, `frac` (the share of today), `isNight`/`isDay`, `part()` (Dawn… Before dawn), `darkness()`, `worldTime()` (clock units), `hours()` (hours, the road's clock), `hoursSince`, `daysSince`, `every(n)`, `year()`/`dayOfYear()` (`CLOCK.year` 120 days), `passHours(h)` and `advance(u)` (updateWorld's step: `dayTick` on a new day, then `Systems.hour(h)` on each new hour). It reads `G.day`/`G.clock` in the old helpers' very arithmetic; `timeOfDay`, `darkness`, `worldTime`, `roadNow`, `roadDay`, `roadHours`, `dayPart`, `watchNow` stay as its aliases.
- `core/timed.js` `Timed.of(record, field?)`: `has`, `end`, `daysLeft`, `set(state, days)`, `extend` (only later), `clear`, `onEnd` + `check()`; a view over the saved end-day fields (a holding's `fear`, `raided`, `sacked`, `turmoil`; `P.hostile[fac]`; `t.mem`), so saves are unchanged. `until(t,k)` (siege.js) is its alias.
- `core/bus.js`: `worldOn(type, fn)`, `worldOff`, `worldEmit(type, data)`, the world's twin of `battleOn`; the world's events are named `noun:verb` and listed in its header (who emits each, its data, who hears it): your deeds (`band:beaten`, `monster:slain`, `site:cleared`, `lord:beaten`, `holding:razed`) heard by the quests; what happens in the world (`siege:laid`, `siege:broken`, `holding:taken`, `holding:raided`, `estate:burnt`, `lord:routed`, `caravan:robbed`, `caravan:beaten`, `fight:ended`, `trace:left`, `land:threat`) heard by the news; `encounter:ended`, `incident:ended`, `quest:ended` (nobody listens yet). Listeners run at once, in the order added (S3: `questNotify` and the hooks' `newsRecord` calls are gone into it). The HUD is still redrawn by hand (`updateHUD(true)`): the suites call it and read `#stats` at once.
- `core/rng.js`: `rngOf(name)`, a mulberry32 stream per name seeded by the world, the game's number (`Rng.game`: one draw of Math.random as a new game begins, `Rng.newGame`; saved) and the name (`next`, `int`, `chance`, `pick`, `weighted`; `Rng.states()`/`restore`; `Rng.use(name, fn)`: a shared helper's Math.random draws taken from the stream while fn runs). S4: the story and the content that gets tuned often draw from their own (`road`: the encounters and their choices' rolls; `letters`; `incidents`; `quests`; `critters`: the wild game and the herds; `monsters`), so adding a kind or changing a weight never moves the world's Math.random nor the fingerprints; the world's simulation and the battles stay on Math.random. Saved last (`rng`: the game's number and the streams' states), started afresh by a new game.
- `core/spatial.js` `Spatial`: `nearest(list, x, y, filter?)`, `nearestHolding(x, y, filter?)`, `within(list, x, y, r)`, and `index(list, cell)`, a grid that answers as the full scan does (ties too). `nearestOf` (realms.js) is its alias. `ChunkGrid`: the world's movers filed by the 512-px chunk they stand in (`file(lists)`, each with its order `ord` and list `tag`; `box(x0, y0, x1, y1, out)` in that order), a snapshot asked with a margin.
- `core/assign.js` `assignSlots(n, k)`: n men to k places by the least walking in all (the sum of squared px), into `FILL.out`; positions written into `FILL`'s buffers first (`fillRoom(n)`), men in a stable order (by id). Up to `SLOTFILL.exact` (30) men the optimal (Hungarian); more, nearest first and up to `SLOTFILL.swaps` passes of swaps. Its buffers grow once and are kept. `fillWalk` weighs a share in px. Used by the march and battle (below, "Filling places").
- **Simulation by chunk** (`world/worldobject.js` `WORLD_LOD`, doc/WORLD_DESIGN.md §3.5): every half second `lodTick` files every mover in `WORLD_LOD.grid` and gives each a tier by the ring of chunks round yours: near (1 ring) every frame, mid (to 4) every 4th frame with 4× d, far beyond: its kind's `farStep` (beasts and herds hold where they are; a mythic beast and the parties, caravans and ships think as mid). What asked of every mover each frame now asks only the chunks near you: travel awareness (`TravelAwareness.observers`: those within reach of your groups, plus any still suspicious, the workers and refugees), the herds' threats (`WildlifeThreats.rebuild`), the beasts' prey (`beastPreyGrid`), the minimap's dots (`render.js` `mapDots`). Each answers as the old scan did (W1–W8 identical with `WORLD_LOD.on` false); a mover come into the world since the last filing is seen from the next. The grid is filed again at once when a list grows shorter (`lodGrid`) and after a load. The frames are counted from a new world or a load (`lodForget`: `WORLD_LOD.n`), so the same game thinks its mid tier on the same frames (`tests/streams.js`). `tests/lod.js`, `tests/visual/chunksim.js` (the costs at Large and Huge).

**The story parts** (doc/STORY_DESIGN.md, S0–S3, S5, ST1–ST2, ST4; `js/story/`: storyplace, context, outcome and choice loaded right after `ui/window.js`; kinds, happening, encounter, incident, quest, quest chain, gathering, steps and wounded right before `world/incidents.js` (a Happening is a `WorldObject`, parties.js; the road events are Incident kinds); checked by `tests/story.js`, `tests/kinds.js` and `tests/questchains.js`):
- `story/storyplace.js` `StoryPlace`: what a spot on the map is (terrain, climate, land, road, cover), each worked out once; `story/context.js` `storyCtx()` (`Story.ctx`): a `StoryContext` (a `StoryPlace` where you stand) for you: where (`terrain`, `climate`, `land`, `owned`, `nearHold`, `nearest`, `nearestDist`, `byRoad`, `byMountains`, `landKind`, `woodsEdge`, `cover`, `by(type, r)`; worked out once, when first read), when (`day`, `hour`, `isDay`, `isNight`, `dark`: the Clock) and who (`troops`, `size`, `cap`, `power`, `renown`, `gold`, `morale`, `food`, `skill(k)`, `honor(f)`, `rep(name)`: read live), and `weather`. Inside `Story.frame(fn)` (the road's moment, `updateRoad`) one is made and shared; outside, each call is fresh. `Story.at(tx, ty)`: a tile's where-part (the spot rules, `ENC_ON`). The road's kinds' `ok(c)` read it (`roadPick` passes one for the whole roll).
- `story/outcome.js` `Outcome`: an outcome is plain data (`{gold:20, honor:[fac,-5], morale:-3, say:'…'}`), each key done by its handler in the order written: `gold` (or `{n, min}`), `food`, `goods`, `gear`, `troops`, `lose`, `wounded`, `rep`, `honor`, `renown`, `morale` (or `{n, min, max}`), `xp`, `train`, `sp`, `hours`, `vitals`, `mood`, `wrong` (a CONDUCT entry), `log`, `news`, `fight`, `chase`, `end`, `gone`, `start` (another encounter), `band`, `call` (a hook's own step). `Outcome.apply(o, {e})` (`applyOutcome`) returns what came of it (`res.goods`, `res.gear`, `res.cost`… and `res.say`, the line); `Outcome.facts(o)` / `describeOutcome(o)` its words. No handler draws Math.random.
- `story/choice.js` `Choice` (from its spec: `icon`, `label`, `tip`, `when`, `need`, `ask`, `again`, `odds` (an `ENC_STAT` check: `chance`, `roll`, `read`, `helps`), `out(s)` (its outcome, shown and done; its facts written from it, `also(s)` adding the lines no outcome can say after them) or `run(s, choice)` (a hook returning an outcome or a line; `facts`, its words by hand)); `perform(s)`, `button(s, act, i)`, `Choice.list`. `CHOICE_PAGE` (`ChoicePage` on `PageScreen`): `show` (header, banner, the last result, text and facts or a body, the choices as key actions, `nav()`: Back left, the way out right), `buttons`, `choose`, `confirm`. On them: the road's encounters (`ROAD_ENC[k].acts`, `showRoad`, `roadAct`), the sealed letter's verbs (`LETTER_VERBS`, `letterPage`) and the shrine (`SHRINE_CHOICES`).
- **Dialogue, improved** (doc/DIALOGUE_DESIGN.md, 2026-11-09): a lord's audience opens on his topics (`Speaker#topics`; Talk, Ask, Sway, Work), each its replies and its bar's Back; what cannot be chosen now folded into one *Not now* line; odds as a chip by Insight (`Conversation.odds`). He remembers (`LordSpeaker#remarks`, `DIALOGUE.remember`): your line, his house's grudge, mourning, weddings and births, your last deed in his words, the war. An aim opens its ways to put it (`DIALOGUE.ways`: praise, honour, gain, friendship, fear), his temper taking to each; won, won in part (`DIALOGUE.part`), or lost. A story's page keeps its exchange as talk (`ChoicePage#exchange`; your `words`, `DIALOGUE.says`) and takes number keys (`CHOICE_KEYS`).
- `story/kinds.js` `Kinds`: `defineKind(shape, id, spec)` (the spec is the kind, plain data with hooks; `cls`: its own subclass, only where it has behaviour of its own; `trigger`: when it may begin), `defineKinds(shape, common, specs)`, `kindOf(shape, id)`, `Kinds.all(shape)`, and each trigger's bucket, filled at load in order: `Kinds.on(shape, trigger)` (a list), `Kinds.view(shape, trigger)` (by id: `ROAD_ENC` is the road's, `travelHour`).
- `story/happening.js` `Happening` (a `WorldObject`: `id`, `k`, `kind`; `marker()`, `page()`, `say()`; `ends`, `announceEnd()` (`<shape>:ended` on the bus); `save()`, `Happening.as(rec)` (its kind's class put on it in place), `Happening.load(rec)` (its kind's `defaults` filled in; a gone kind: null)); `Behaviours`, the encounters' small strategies chosen by `beh` (`approach`, `stay`, `scene`, `flee`, `walk`, `leave`, `bolt`, `graze`, and the `chase`: `step(e, m)`, `ends`, `due`, `settle`); `Encounter` (the road's: its moment `step`, `over`, `goOff`, `timeUp`, `drop`, `off`, `arrive`…; `RoadEncounter` and `asRoadEnc` its old names). Its own classes: `Shepherd` and `Stray` (`world/encounters.js`).
- `story/incident.js` `Incident` (S2): what befalls you with no one to meet; its kinds (`world/incidents.js`: fever, storm, blizzard, sandstorm, flood, lightning, frostbite, lost, heat, fineday, mirage) plain data with the trigger `day` (`EVENTS` is that bucket): `w`, `ok(c)` (the context), `run(c)` returning an outcome (its log line in it, any roll first); `Incident.pick(kinds, c)` (one by weight, one draw), `Incident.befall(kind, c)` (done, logged, `incident:ended`). `roadEvent` is the daily roll (the system `road events`).
- `story/quest.js` `Quest` (S3): a job you carry; the record is `P.quests`' as ever (`type` its kind, `id` its number), the class put on it in place when made or used (`Quest.as`). Each kind a subclass (`world/quests.js`), overriding only its own rules: `setup(t, lord)` (the offer), `begin(t)` (taking it), `seek()` (where the deed is), `deed` and `hits(d)` (the world event it waits for), `ready()`, `progress()`, `todo()`, `handsOver` and `handOver(t)`, `arrive(t)`. Shared: `accept()`, `goal()`, `finish()`, `pay(where)`, `turnIn(t)`, `reportableAt(t)`, `fail(why)`, `lapse()` (the day's check), `earned()` (the gold it pays now); its words for your Quests and the boards: `givenBy()`, `timing()`, `reportLine()`, `steps()`, `abandonCost()`; `Quest.listen()` hears the kinds' deeds on the bus. **The quest model** (QM, 2026-10-23; doc/STORY_DESIGN.md QM), read the same way by every quest and story: `cat` (`job`, `side`: a step kind, an escort from the road; `main`: a story kind marked `main`), `state` (`locked` (an offer's `needs()` unmet) or `available`, `active`, `completed` (ready: report it); over, its archive entry's `done`, `failed` or `abandoned`), `story` (the live quest chain it is a stage of: listed and archived as that story), `abandonable` (a job only, never a story's stage; `q-abandon` refuses the rest), `penalty(fate)` (what failing it costs, an outcome; a job's grows with its size). Over, it is kept in `QuestArchive` (`P.archive`, the last `QUEST_ARCHIVE.keep` (60), small records `{k, cat, title, giver, state, day, got}`; no field till the first). **The jobs** (`JobQuest`, `world/quests.js`, QU 2026-10-23): no cap on how many you hold (the boards still offer `JOB_OFFERS` each `JOB_REFRESH` days, a lord one at a time; `Quest.jobs()` is a plain count), and **none fails for time**: bounty, beast, clear, hunt, troops, lordkill and raid have no timing (each records the day `taken`); courier and supply (`timed`) have a best-by day (`due`, their `days` from taking), paid in full by then and `QUEST_LATE` less after (10% a day, never under half). The day's check closes one the world has ended (`gone()`: its band broken or its beast slain by another, its site cleared, its lord or village gone, its giver's holding or realm fallen): `QUEST_CLOSED`'s share of the gold and a line, `quest:ended` `{how: 'closed'}`. Chain steps and escorts keep their own due days. `makeQuest`, `acceptQuest`, `questGoal`, `questReady`, `questProgress`, `payQuest`, `turnIn`, `canReportAt`, `failQuest` are its names. **Your Quests** (`ui/questwindow.js`, the Character window's Quests tab, the journal): its tabs Main, Side, Jobs and the Archive; the list (the tracked one first in its tab; each row its state: Active, "Return to X for your reward", Locked greyed with what it requires; a story once, its stage's quest its objective; in Jobs, the offers you know of) and the one chosen in full: its category and state; given by, taken, timing, turn in (as `canReportAt`), its steps, its reward (`questFacts`, which the boards' offers and a lord's show too, with what a locked one requires); a story's who, objective, progress, where and stages; an archive entry's end and what it paid or cost; Show on map (the full map centred on its goal, a step closer, Back returning), Track (one, `P.track`: `'q'+id` or `'t'+` a story's number, no field when none; `QuestTrack`; its goal the strongest mark on the map, the minimap and the full map), Abandon (a job only; asks first, its real cost: `QUEST_ABANDON`, its size's reputation (a lord's: honour and his regard) and no work from its giver for 3 days, `P.shut`, the board saying so). On a small window (a phone on its side) the tabs and the list, then the chosen one alone, Back to the list. Its end on the bus says how (`quest:ended`, `{e, how: 'paid' | 'failed'}`; an encounter's `{e, how}` from its one `finish(how)`: `done`, `left`, `timeout`, `caught`, `escaped`, or its fight's `won`, `lost`, `fled`, begun with `encounter:fought` and settled by the battle's `battle:ended`). A quest's quarry (`Quest.quarry(id)`: the parties and beasts it marked, roving or sheltering in any site) is unmarked once it is done or failed. A lord's report pays where `lordPayAt` says (his realm's nearest holding; none, the nearest not hostile to you; none, the lord himself).
- **Search, comparison and dragging** (`ui/invtools.js` `Finder`, `GearCompare`, `SlotDrag`; `tests/invtools.js`): a search box over the bag and over the market's two panes (`Finder.html(key, hint)`; what does not match hidden as you type, no redraw, its category headings with it; kept in `G.find` across redraws); a piece against what it would replace, side by side (each one's picture, name, rarity and every stat, better ▲ or worse ▼): with a mouse a small box beside the bag piece you point at, else beside the one picked (no window of its own), a popup only when a piece is tapped on a touch screen (Equip or Swap, Off hand, Close; Esc closes it); dragging any slot onto another with a mouse or a finger (a long press, `SLOT_DRAG.hold` ms, so a swipe still scrolls), its ghost under you, the slot under it lit, the inventory's own move on letting go (wear, take off, swap, stack; the native drag and drop is gone). On a phone held sideways the bag has the room: the detail pane only once something is picked, the tabs, categories and search tighter (on a phone the equipment is its own page: tap a piece, Equip in its popup). A right click on a slot (`SlotMenu`): a small menu by the pointer of what it can do there (a bag piece Equip or Swap, Off hand, Drop; a worn one Unequip; a letter Read; any other thing Drop; and Sort bag), greyed with why where it cannot; Drop asks once more on the menu; closed by a choice, a click elsewhere, Esc or a scroll.
- **The market's trading screen** (each row and bargain line with its item's picture: `TradeScreen.ico`, drawn by `loot.js` `fillIcons` after the town window, `town.js` `renderTown`; `ui/trade.js` `TradeDeal`, `TradeScreen`; `world/economy.js` `quote`, `goodsQuote`; `tests/trade.js`): your pack on the left (the goods you carry, the things of yours a market buys), the merchants' stalls on the right (their goods, empty waterskins under Water, quivers), the bargain between: what you sell and buy (− takes back), each line quoted as its price would move unit by unit (the market unchanged), what each side pays, the balance and your gold before and after; Confirm (greyed: not enough gold, or no room) strikes it, your sales first, then your buys, as the old buttons did (standing, Trading); Clear empties it. `G.town.deal`, never saved. The verbs `deal`, `undeal` (kind:key: `s` your good, `o` a bag slot, `b` theirs, `k` waterskins, `q` a quiver), `deal-clear`, `deal-ok`; the old `buy`, `sell`, `sell-own` still act at once (console, tests). Your terms (`world/economy.js` `TRADE_TERMS`, `tradeTerms`, in every good's buy and sell price): your Trading as before, your standing there (from −50 to +100: up to 6% better both ways, worse under 0), and a haggle once a visit over a bargain of 60 gold or more (`deal-haggle`: `QUEST_CHECKS.haggle`, the road's stream; won 5% better, lost 3% worse for the rest of the visit, `G.town.haggle`; Trading learned either way); the bargain shows them, with the haggle's odds as Insight reads them. The things a holding's merchants buy and sell (gear, mounts, quivers, trophies, valuables) by your standing too, inside a holding only (`itemTerms`; never a haggle). The same thing both ways nets out: buying what you are selling takes it off your side first, and selling what you are buying the same (goods, waterskins, quivers: `TradeDeal#add`). Three side by side down to a phone in landscape (tighter, arrows only), the bargain on top in a narrow window (container queries).
- **Quests from what is true** (doc/QUESTS_DESIGN.md Q1–Q3; `tests/questcauses.js`): `world/troubles.js` `Troubles` answers what troubles a holding now (bands in its lands, packs whose lair is by its fields, hideouts and camps near, game grazing near, goods short, a thin garrison, the news near it), read only. `world/givers.js` `Givers`: its people who give work, a row of `GIVERS` (data/quests.js) each (the reeve, the herder, the captain of the watch, the steward, a merchant, the tanner, the innkeeper), named from the holding (no draw), their regard for you in `P.grel` (`GIVER_REGARD`); a village's pay part of a job's gold in the holding's goods (`pays`, `GIVER_PAY`). The board: each giver offers one of their kinds whose trouble is true; none, "Nothing troubles {town} these days". The jobs: a bounty on the band that is there (else one out of the nearest hideout, working the holding's roads: `haunt`, `world/brains.js` `HAUNT`; no hideout, none); beasts at the folds, the pack by its fields (else one where such beasts live, by its habitat); a hunt for game that grazes near, named; a supply run with its reason, its days by its stock, its pay by the price there (`SUPPLY_JOB`); troops only for a thin garrison; a clearance only of a lair whose bands or beasts trouble it. Each offer shows its giver and its cause. **Leads** (Q4, `tests/questleads.js`): a bounty's band and a beast hunt's pack are not pinned till found: where they were when taken, a dashed circle on the map growing `LEAD.grow` tiles a day (to `LEAD.max`); the talk at a holding within `LEAD.ask` tiles of them sharpens it (`JobQuest#hear`, on arrival), tracking inside it narrows it (an hour's `QUEST_CHECKS.track`), seen they are found and followed. **Turns and marks** (Q5, `tests/questmarks.js`): a band gone to ground is followed to its hideout (`BountyQuest#hideOf`, said once); a shortage filled by others or a garrison filled closes its job (a share paid); a holding that changes hands keeps your job; paid, a bounty or a beast hunt quiets its holding a while (`Quiet`, `QUEST_MARKS`, `W.quiet`: no band out of a hideout, no pack moving in, for a job there) and the talk says so (`news.js` `quiet`); a hunt's meat goes into its stores. **New kinds** (Q6, `tests/questkinds2.js`): a courier's letter is a real one in your bag (`LETTER.make`, its realm's real news; `job` set, so it is delivered on arrival, not by hand), paid less read (`COURIER.opened`), failed once gone from your bag (sold, burnt); a lord's war work adds `relieve` (a holding of his realm under siege: its besieger beaten; closed once the siege is over) and `scout` (an enemy lord's army: a lead, done once seen). The caravan escort and the missing (§5.6, §5.9) are not built yet. **JobChains from jobs** (Q7, `story/jobchains.js` `JobChains`, `tests/jobchains.js`): four stories begun by how a real job ended, each on the world as it is (one of a kind at a time for a holding): *The chief who ran* (a bounty paid while its chief got away, `W.exiles`: he is the nearest hideout's chieftain now; clear it; a real lord's letter among his plunder, given to the holding or sold back to him); *Something bigger* (a beast hunt paid with a cave near: tracked or searched blind, killed or smoked out); *The empty larder* (a supply run paid whose caravan was robbed: the informer found by talk, or the goods back from the hideout into its stores); *A sealed hand* (a courier's letter read that orders an attack: the holding warned, its garrison readied, `JOB_CHAINS.gar`). **The questbook on the world** (Q8, `tests/questbookworld.js`): a story's `beat` is the band that troubles its holding (`world/quests.js` `bandFor`: else one out of its nearest hideout, `bandOut`; none, not begun), its `hunt` the pack by its fields (`packFor`: else one where such beasts live, by its fields or within `PACK_FAR` tiles), a rescue's `band` one out of a hideout; an entry's conditions may name the world (`threat` band or beast, `news` a kind, `short` a good: `story/forms.js`), as the miller's (`threat:'band'`), the shepherd's (`threat:'beast'`) and the poorhouse's (`short:'grain'`) do. **The notice board** (`ui/noticeboard.js` `NoticeBoard`, `NOTICE`; style.css "The notice board"; `tests/noticeboard.js`): a cork board in a wooden frame, each offer a paper pinned to it, tilted its own way: its kind's word, its title, its cause, its words, its reward, timing and where it is turned in, and who posted it and where to find them ("Seek out Jory the merchant at the market square": `GIVERS` `where`; a questbook notice's `giver.name`, `giver.where`); a locked one faded and stamped; nothing offered, one paper says why. Medieval (the owner: "make it medieval"): an oak board of planks under a carved plaque ("Notices of {town}"), each notice a ragged parchment held by an iron nail, the captain's and the steward's sealed in red wax (`NOTICE.seal`), a crier's words (Wanted, Beware, Sought, Hear ye; "For the deed"), a drop cap, and a book hand (a serif) for the notices' writing only. Old (the owner: "make the board and papers look old"): the planks weathered grey-brown, grained, knotted and scratched, torn corners of old notices still nailed up; the sign a plank of its own with the letters cut in and two nails; each parchment yellowed and foxed (`--fx`, `--fy`: its stain's place, by a hash), creased where it was folded, its edges browned, its nail rusted; the textures SVG noise as data URIs. A holding's givers never share a name.
- **The caravan escort and the missing** (doc/QUESTS_DESIGN.md §5.6, §5.9; `tests/questkinds3.js`): `world/quests.js` `ConvoyQuest` (`convoy`, the merchant's) is offered only while a caravan was taken on the holding's roads lately (news `caravan`). Taken, a real caravan leaves its gate for a holding 10 to 40 tiles off that is not hostile to you, its goods from the holding's stock, marked with the quest (`qid`); the band troubling the holding works its road (`haunt`). It is paid at the gate when it arrives (`caravan:arrived`, `world/caravans.js` `arrive`) with you within `CONVOY_JOB.near` tiles; taken, robbed by you, or there without you, it fails. `MissingQuest` (`missing`, the priest's at a holding with a temple, or the reeve's): a band that beats a caravan carries off one of its people (`Missing.take` on `caravan:beaten`: a `carry` entry with their name, trade, home and day, named by a hash, no draw). Offered while a band, roving or in a camp, carries one of the holding's; the band is a lead, as a bounty's; broken (`band:beaten`, also when its camp is cleared), they are freed and ride home with you to report. Its band gone: closed. The notice board calls them "Guards wanted" and "Missing".
- **Quests and encounters that make sense** (the owner, 2026-11-05: "recheck all quests ... and all encounters"; `tests/escorttrips.js`, `tests/newencounters.js`, `tests/questmarks.js`): a job whose need passed before you take it is not given (`Quest#needless`, each kind's: a garrison filled, goods restocked, a siege over, a lord taken or at peace); a holding or lord turned hostile ends its jobs; a courier is never sent to a holding at war with you or besieged; a hunt counts only meat you hunted (`meat:hunted`). Every road escort (refugees, the lost child, the wounded traveller, the false toll-man) goes 14-60 tiles (`ESCORT.trip`), reachable, to a place not against you, or is not offered; the campfire's bandits rob a band too small to fight instead of trapping it; the shepherd points at the nearest real band (none made); a saved caravan thanks you (`ENC_TUNE.caravan`); messengers ride for living realms not at war with you; gamblers and deserters are of the land where they stand; no toll on your liege's land; praying pilgrims only at shrines you can reach.
- `story/questchain.js` `QuestChain` (S5; its first kind, the chain `wounded`, ST2): a story of several steps sharing a `memory`; in your Quests one quest (QM: `cat` its kind's `main` or `side`, `state`, `title`, `who`, `stages()` done; a step's `stage` its name, `fail` the keys that fail it; a main story's failed stage falls back to its last `checkpoint` (`cp`, `setback`), never ending; its end archived, but for a kind's `resumes`). A kind is data, `defineKind(QUESTCHAIN_SHAPE, id, {n, who, steps, first})`; a step `{id, start, until, how, remember, then, note}`: `start` one of `QuestChain.starters` (`{quest: kind, town, lord, spec}` (spec: an escort's, `world/escort.js`), `{step: kind, town, spec}` (a chain's step kind, `story/steps.js`), `{encounter: kind, x, y}`, `{incident: kind}`, `{page: {ico, title, text, choices}}`; a field may be a function of the quest chain), `note(th)` what to do now (your Quests' Stories: `QuestChain.journal`), `until` the bus event that ends it (none: the end of what it began, matched by shape and id; or `{on, match(d, th)}`), `how(d, th)` the branch key (none: the event's `how`), `remember(d, th)` into the memory, `then` the next step's id or `{how: id}`. `QuestChain.start(id, memory)` (or an outcome's `start: {quest chain, memory}`); it advances only on bus events (`QuestChain.heard`, listening only to what its steps wait for), never per frame, and ends with its last step (`quest chain:ended`, `{e, how}`). A page step shows its `ChoicePage` when you are free on the map (`thr-` actions, `G.mode` `story`; `page:chosen` on the bus), else waits in `QuestChain.paged()` and is shown the next moment you are (`QuestChain.showWaiting`, from `Steps.frame`). Each end is kept (`W.story.threads.ended`, the last `STEP.keep`); what may come once a game, `W.story.threads.once` (`QuestChain.once`, `QuestChain.mark`); what a chain keeps between its quest chains, `W.story.threads.aside` (Ander wandering). `QuestChain.start(id, memory, at)` may begin at a later step (a chain picked up again: freed from captivity, Ander's job). A quest chain never loops back on itself (the generic walk in `tests/kinds.js` walks every way through it): a chain that goes round again ends and starts a new quest chain. Saved in `W.story.threads` (key `quest chains`, once one has begun or something came once).
- `story/steps.js` a chain's step kinds (ST2): quests a story begins, of the shape `step` (no board offers them; `Quest.make` and `Quest.as` find them), each in your Quests with where to go and on the map, saved in `P.quests`, ended on the bus (`quest:ended`, `e.fate`). `StepQuest` (not a job, never reported, no due day unless its spec's `days`; `pay(fate)`, `fail(why, fate)`: its prize, its forfeit (`forfeits` by fate), its lines (spec `say`, else its kind's; `STEP.say`); its place `StepQuest.place(to)`: a holding, a house or an estate (`homeMark`: a house on the map), or a spot (the quests' "!")); `visit` (`VisitQuest`: go there), `deliver` (`DeliverQuest`: bring goods there, handed over), `lair` (`LairQuest`: clear the hideout (or camp) its spec binds, `site:cleared`; cleared by another or gone, the day's tick binds the nearest of its band within `STEP.retarget` tiles, else it is over, `gone`); `held` (`HeldQuest`, a rescue, no due day: free a captive of yours from where he is, `held.k`: the band that carries him (beat it, `band:beaten`; after `WOUNDED.rescue.home` days it takes him to the nearest hideout of its kind), a hideout or camp (clear it), or a holding's gaol (at its gates on the map, the gaoler's page, `rs-` actions: pay his ransom; or storm it, a siege won there; it changes hands to you or your realm: freed); his holder destroyed by another: `gone`; its own ears, `HeldQuest.heard`); the escort (`world/escort.js`) is one too. `over(fate, k)`: a step over neither done nor failed. `Steps.frame` (each moment on the map, from `updateRoad`): a waiting page shown, else a step at its place reached.
- `story/forms.js` `Stories`, `defineStory` (doc/QUESTBOOK_DESIGN.md §10): one way to write any story: its steps as a goal (`defineGoal`: visit, deliver, escort (its `on: {captured, lost}`), track (a trail to the nearest hideout of a band: `memory.site`), clear, beat, hunt, rescue, kill), a choice (with checks, `QUEST_CHECKS` in data/jobchains.js) or an end, beside the quest chain's own steps; any field a function of the quest chain, `remember` on any step; conditions (`defineCond`); outcomes with plain numbers and `once`; a story's deadline (`fail`: `Stories.day`, the system `deadlines`) and `resume` (picked up again at a step). The questbook's entries and the Wounded Traveller are written with it.
- `story/questbook.js` `Questbook` (doc/QUESTBOOK_DESIGN.md phase 1): every entry of `QUESTBOOK` (data/questbook/) defined as a story `book:<id>` at load (`Questbook.compile`, story/forms.js): a goal step starts an existing step kind (visit, deliver, clear: `lair`, escort, rescue: `held`, and its own `band` (`BandQuest`: a band set near), `beasts` (`BeastsQuest`: beasts set near) and `foe` (`FoeQuest`, kill: a lord to beat), story/steps.js), a choice step is a quest chain page (its options' checks put in `ENC_STAT` as `chk:<name>`, rolled on the road's stream, read by Insight; won or lost, its `win` or `lose` step), an end pays its reward (`rep`, `honor`, `rel` as plain numbers at its holding, realm and lord). Three registries, a new kind one call: `defineGiver` (board: `jobsBody`'s rows; tavern: a card; lord: a reply in his talk; road: a day's chance, `QUESTBOOK_OFFER.road`, never while `G.quietEvents`; temple: the priest in the streets; comp: a companion's talk), `defineGoal`, `defineCond` (level, renown, honor, rel, realm, war, kind, culture, day, has, done, comp, approve, gold; `not`). Phase 2: an entry's deadline (`fail.days`: its days left on its note; passed, `Questbook.expire`: failed), a step's `stage` name. Offered by its giver while not under way and not done (`once`) or cooling (`cool` days: the day it ended, `QuestChain.once('book:<id>')`), the same few a holding a week (a hash, no draw); taken (`qb-take`), a story in your Quests. The console: `book`, `book <id> [step]`. Tests: questbook (every entry linked and walked through every branch, saved and loaded at each step).
- **The poorhouse** (2026-11-04; the owner: "make sure that it happen at poor village or city ... click at the house and submit"): `Bread for the poorhouse` is offered only at a poor holding, a village, town or city with prosperity under `STORY_POOR` (40: the questbook's `poor` condition, `story/forms.js`). Its grain goes to a house named the poorhouse (a spec's `to.name`: `StepQuest.place` label). A delivery to a house is handed over by your hand (`DeliverQuest` byHand): walk to the house, click it, and its page (`ui/house.js`, titled "The poorhouse") has **Deliver 10 grain**, greyed with what you carry till you have it all. Test: `tests/questbook.js`.
- `story/wounded.js` The Wounded Traveller and Ander The Fallen (ST2; numbers: `data/jobchains.js` `WOUNDED`, `HIDDEN_RATE`): the road's wounded traveller, tended, stands bandaged and offers on his page (`Wounded.offer`): Join you (a first-tier soldier of his people), Take him home (the quest chain `wounded` begins), Refuse (he limps off; reputation −1 at his home). The quest chain: `escort` (to his family's house or estate; late and left end it, each its line and cost; captured, the rescue below) → `family` (a page: the gang that robbed him) → `tracks` (a visit to where you found him; the trail binds the nearest hideout of their band, marked) → `trail` (a page: ride for it, let it lie; no hideout: cold) → `clear` (a `lair` step) → `back` (a visit) → `reward` (a page: the heirloom, `ITEMS.hearthring`, once a game, else gold; reputation, renown). Ander The Fallen: `WOUNDED.ander.p` × `HIDDEN_RATE` of wounded travellers (a hash of the encounter's number and seed: no draw), never twice a game (`QuestChain.once('ander')`, from his healing); no Join; at the end he joins as a companion (outcome `companion`, `world/companions.js` `compJoin`; `COMPS.ander` unlisted, `ST.ander`, his look `PEOPLE_LOOK.ander`: a scar, a dark cloak). **The rescue** (ST4, the owner 2026-10-23, no deadline): carried off when a battle is lost (`world/captives.js` `carryOff`: the band that beat you carries him, its `carry`, told on its tip; the hideout or camp you stormed; a realm's men take him to their realm's nearest holding; beasts take no one), the `held` step frees him (beat the band, clear its hideout once it goes home, pay `WOUNDED.rescue.ransom` at the gaol's gates or take the holding); freed, the quest chain ends `freed` and picks itself up at the escort, from where you are with a fresh due day (`Wounded.resume`), or, with no passenger room, the `call` (he goes home alone, the chain resumes at his family's page); his holder destroyed by another or no one took him: the `call`; given up: over. **Ander is never lost** (but dying unhealed): no due day on his escort; no passenger room, he offers to meet you later; every end of his chain but the reward sends him wandering (`Wanderer`, kept in `W.story.threads.aside.ander`): a tavern every `WOUNDED.wander.days` (a hash of the world and his leg, no draw), told by every other tavern's rumours (`world/news.js`, the far kind `ander`), his own card in his tavern (`ui/town.js` `Tavern`, never the hiring list): finish the job (his chain picked up at the tracks; his attackers dealt with already, he joins at once) or pay `WOUNDED.wander.price` (300), with room in your band. Tests: `tests/wounded.js`.
- `world/escort.js` escorts (ST1): a quest kind, `escort` (`EscortQuest`), begun by an outcome's `{passenger: spec}` (the lost child, the refugees), a quest chain's step `{quest: 'escort', spec}`, or offered at a holding (a traveller to another). `spec`: `{who, n, eat, to: {town, lot ('house' or an estate key), i}, days, prize, forfeit, at}`. The passenger rides in your band: the card's Escort tag, the Army window's Passengers, its food in the band's hour (`bandFoodHour`), no slot, no fighter, no map figure; at most `ESCORT.max`, not counted as a job (`Quest.jobs`); a side quest (QM): never yours to abandon, still failing by the world (late, captured). Its place has its house mark (`gfx/worldui.js` `wuiEscorts`) and the quests' minimap dot. Ends: arrival (within `ESCORT.reach`, `Steps.frame` from `updateRoad`, or entering its holding: prize, page, paid), its due day (`late`; none with `open`: Ander), a battle lost (`captured`; a `rescue`'s holder kept on its record, `by`), abandoned (`left`): the record's `fate`, on `quest:ended`. Numbers and lines: `data/encounters.js` `ESCORT`. Tests: `tests/escort.js`, `tests/lifecycle.js` (every encounter's endings, the lord's report, a bounty's quarry).
- `story/gathering.js` `Gathering` (S5, the framework only: **no gatherings or tournaments yet**): held at a place on set days, announced ahead. A kind: `{n, ico, text, places(), schedule: {every: N} | {day: D}, announce, lasts, fee, choices}` (`every`: at each place on the days of its own offset, `Rng.seedOf(kind, place)`, no draw; `day`: that day of the year, `Clock.dayOfYear(d)`). The calendar (`W.story.gatherings`, key `gatherings`, saved once it holds any) is sorted by each one's next due day, so the day's check (the system `gatherings`, day order 240, after every other) reads only its head: planned → `gathering:announced` (N days ahead) → `gathering:opened` → `gathering:closed` (`{e, at, day}`), the next then planned. `entrants`, `enter(who)`; its page (`show()`, `gth-` actions: Enter, the fee). `Gathering.at(place)`: those coming there. The test kinds (one quest chain, one gathering) live only in `tests/kit.js` `Kit.storyKinds`.

`core/core.js` owns the generic numeric helpers (`sum`, `seeded`, `angGap`); the seeded RNG sequence and angle wrapping are shared unchanged by simulation, rendering and combat. `core/state.js` keeps `partyRoom` alongside party size/capacity, as the older names of your band's methods (`P.room()`, `P.headcount()`, `P.cap()`: `world/parties.js` `PlayerParty`). It deliberately returns a negative value while over capacity. `core/roster.js` owns count/list conversion; companion and garrison displays reuse `countsOf` without modifying saved counts.

Before removing a global, check both HTML entry points, registered action callbacks, the save format and tests—not only direct function calls in gameplay. `FORM_ORDER` and `LCAP`, for example, are still read by the wiki. Talent, equipment, site and party compatibility helpers with live consumers remain supported. Unused legacy controls and rendering helpers were removed in cleanup step 4; manual hero control and archived/reference assets were not removed. `tests/helpers.js` checks the shared helper contracts and grouped displays; `tests/wiki.js` loads the actual wiki and checks completion, sprites and search, rather than only checking its script list.

## What is on the map

Things in the world are plain data (so a save is plain JSON) read through a class for their kind. The class is put back on load.

- **The classic seed's land** (the default world, seed `CLASSIC`; doc/WORLD_DESIGN.md §8.15): Ashmark drawn by the generator, steered by `CLASSIC_HINTS` (`data/places.js`), soft warped nudges read by the terrain stages (`world/worldgen.js`): frost in the north, desert at home in the south-east, the League's ports round one bay of the southern sea, Mirefen, Tidewatch and Brinewatch in fen, peaks over Karn Hold and in the north, and a great river from the northern peaks past Stonebridge to that sea (`genValleyHint`); land round every holding's anchor and no peak near one.
