Checked by `tests/worldgen.js` (the land at the anchors), `tests/rivers.js` (the great river) and `tests/classicseed.js` (the holdings on it).

- **Holdings** (`world/holding.js`; the data in `data/holdings.js`): `HOLDING_TYPES` says what each kind has (people, garrison, patrol, walls, tabs, troops, horses, needs); `Holding` → `Village`, `Town`, `City`, `Castle` (→ `Fort`: a small walled frontier post, `tests/forts.js`). Code asks the holding (`t.T`, `t.label`, `t.has(tab)`, `t.hires(race)`, `t.takesGuests(free)`), never `t.kind==='…'`. Its behaviour is its own (class wave B): `t.walled()`, `t.besieged()`, `t.besiegers()`, `t.closed()`, `t.defenders()`, `t.siegeLv()`, `t.garCap()`, `t.ruined()`, `t.fiefIncome()`, `t.draft(n)`, `t.fleeReason()`, `t.refuge()`, `t.setUp()`, `t.landDay()`, `t.refillGuards()`; and its parts, kept aside (never saved: they work on its plain fields): `t.econ` (`Economy`, `world/economy.js`: `foodDays()`, `need(g)`, `price(g)`, `tax()`, `day(ly)`, `buy`/`sell`, `lacking(mats)`…), `t.buildings` (`Buildings`, `world/buildings.js`: `cap(k)`, `day()`, `start(k, lv)`, `burn(n)`, `breach()`, `build(k)`…), `t.layout` (`Layout`, `world/lots.js`: `add(l)`, `drop(l)`, `at(k)`, `housing()`, `sync()`…) and `t.estate(k)` (`Estate`: `guards()`, `produce(…)`, `fields()`, `sabotage()`…). The older loose names (`foodDays(t)`, `bldCap(t, k)`, `lotAdd(t, l)`, `besieged(t)`…) are one-line aliases; `bldLv`, `wallOf`, `garList` stay small readers of any record. Tests: `tests/economy.js`, `tests/estates.js`, `tests/lots.js`, `tests/guards.js`, `tests/siege.js`. A new kind is an entry and, if it behaves differently, a class; `holdingOf(t, kind)` changes a holding's kind.
- **Hamlets and small free villages** (2026-11-04; doc/WORLD_DESIGN.md §6.3, §8.9; the owner: "implement the hamlet and small villages, natural hamlet or village"): a stage of the generator after the realms' holdings (`world/worldplace.js` `genHamlets`, `PLACE_HAMLET`; stage `hamlets`, its own stream) settles the free folk in the land past every holding's reach, each its spacing apart (`PLACE_GAP`), best by a river (never on its bank: 7 tiles off it at least), the water, the woods or the hills; how many by the map's area and the Hamlets setting (New game: None, Few, Normal, Many; Classic takes Normal), at most one a 900 tiles of that land; a quarter of them a small free village, the rest hamlets (`HOLDING_TYPES.hamlet`, `class Hamlet extends Village`: 30 to 70 people, a militia of 3, no walls, a yard at its heart, a field and the one estate of its trade, `Buildings#init`). No realm holds them (`owner` null, "the free folk"): no lord's hall, walls or prison in their window (`ui/town.js`), no watch on their land, no building projects of their own; the tavern says no realm rules there. Attacking one (`world/siege.js` `foeAt`, `FREE_FOE`): −3 honour with every realm and they remember it 60 days; raided, −40 reputation there; a misdeed against them (`wrong`) makes them hostile too. Roads reach them as tracks (not counted as travelled road: `worldRoads` skips them by `WM.freeHoldings`). Mended with them (`world/lots.js`): every holding's centre, and its gate's row and the one before it, kept clear of others' lots and tracks; a new world's estate tracks laid once every holding stands, as a load lays them; a holding's lots walked to on foot (no ferry), a hamlet's on its own bank; a spur left in a centre's ring opened; a herder's round on legs he can walk (`world/folk.js`); a column's goal behind its head counted as reached only beside its file (`world/march.js` `marchByFile`); a lord you have not met named in a lord's work only once taken (`world/quests.js` `ScoutQuest`). Not yet: realms annexing them, bands raiding them, your Offer protection (the design's politics). Test: `tests/hamlets.js`.
- **The humble start** (2026-11-05; doc/START_AND_GATES_DESIGN.md §1; the owner: "start from nothing: you spawn in a small hamlet far away", every past with its reason): a new game puts you in a hamlet or small village by your past (`world/start.js` `StartPlace`, `data/progression.js` `PAST_START`): a Nobody wakes in a natural hamlet, the farthest from the capitals; a discharged soldier at a realm's village by a castle; a guard where a caravan broke up on a great road; a woodcutter by deep woods; an exile in a free hamlet far from home; a runaway apprentice a little way from a town; a hedge mage by old stones; a merchant's child on a trade road. Fair (`HUMBLE_START`, `data/world.js`): a quarter day at least from every capital, no bandit camp or hideout within 25 tiles, no wild land within 15, a realm's holding 2 days off at most by road (not shown: you find it). Your first line says why you are there; the place is your home (`P.home`, reputation 10 there, a bed for nothing). Nowhere fits: your people's capital, as before. The harness turns it off (`HUMBLE_START.on`), so the older suites start at the capital. Test: `tests/humblestart.js`. Your own people's first (2026-11-07; the owner: "make the starting city align with race"): each of a past's tries looks first among holdings of your race (`StartPlace.ours`), then anyone's, as before.
- **Places you must earn** (2026-11-05; doc/START_AND_GATES_DESIGN.md §2; the owner: soft locks, "all depends on situations", lords too): `world/gates.js` `Gates`. A town, city or castle refuses you until your renown or your honour with its realm is enough (`GATES`, `data/world.js`: a town renown 10 or honour 10, a city 30 or 25, a castle honour 20); always open: yours, your service's, your home, a holding a quest of yours takes you to. Refused: its gate page (the way in, told; the market outside its walls, its window with the market alone; Leave), and a small lock under its name on the map. A hamlet's and a village's always open. The ways past a gate (`GATE_WAYS`): a letter of introduction from a reeve or innkeeper whose work you did, or a merchant's guild token, for the nearest town or city that refuses you; a writ bought at the gate (a town 60 gold, a city 160); a bribe (odds by Persuasion and Charisma; caught, honour and reputation lost and the gate's ways barred 3 days); slipping in by night (by Scouting and Agility; caught, a fine of half your gold). Passes kept in `P.passes`, barred gates in `P.gateBan`. A quest can seal a holding to all (`Gates.seal`, saved as `sealed`). A great lord (renown 32 or more, `LORD_AUDIENCE`) hears you only with half his renown, honour 15 with his realm, his regard 20, his service or his work; else his steward's word. The harness turns both off. Test: `tests/gates.js`.
- **Provinces** (2026-11-05; doc/START_AND_GATES_DESIGN.md §2.3, G3): `world/provinces.js` `Provinces`. Every city and castle a seat, every holding in the province of its nearest seat (a realm's own first). A province's danger: the beasts, bands, bandits' lairs and lords at war with you on its land (within 12 tiles of its holdings) against your army's strength (`PROVINCE`, `data/world.js`: calm, watchful, dangerous, deadly). The full map shows each seat's danger and a lock where its gate refuses you, the province's name and what makes it dangerous in the tooltip, and a legend. Setting out for a deadly province warns you once a day ("Few your size come back from the Province of Granitegate…"); the road stays open. The harness turns them off. Test: `tests/provinces.js`.
- **Beasts in the wilds** (2026-11-05, the owner: "monsters should spawn far away from holdings. And they tend to avoid holding territories"): a beast lairs 16 tiles at least from every holding (`WORLD_SCALE.spawn.monsterHold` 256 px, half that at its last tries; `world/monsters.js` `spawnMonster`) and never steps from the wilds onto a holding's land, wandering or hunting you (`beastKeepsOut`: it turns for its lair at the border; a holding's land is a refuge); a mythic one roams as before. The way's footprints shorter (`gfx/worldui.js` `WUI_FOOT`: 4 px long, 10 apart). Test: `tests/beastland.js`.
- **Sites** (`world/sites.js`): every place off the roads. `Site` → `Cave`, `Ruins`, `Shrine`, `TravellersCamp`, and the bandit lairs (`Lair` → `Hideout`, `BanditCamp` on the fixed `CAMP_SPOTS`). Where a kind may stand is static (`ground`, `clear`, `fits`, `point`); what it does is the instance's (`enter`, `panel`, `act`, `day`, `tip`, `box`). A new kind of place is a `SITE_TYPES` entry and a class in `SITE_CLASS`; `siteOf(s)` puts the class back on plain data. Searched `Ruins` fill with occupants from `RUIN_OCC` (seeded by ruin and cycle); bandits, creatures and travellers there are a `Hideout`, `Cave` or `TravellersCamp` of their own (`occ.s`), shown and acted on through it. What you can tell before you act is `insight()` (ui/window.js, by Scouting: `INSIGHT`). A site opens only when clicked or tapped (every kind): beside it at once, further off when your band reaches it (`world.js` `clickAt`, the chase in `marchStep`); walking past or stopping by one opens nothing. `updateSites` only spots them and forgets `G.lastSite` once you are 28 px away. Tests: `tests/siteopen.js`.
- **Discovery** (2026-11-08, the owner: "you may not know the name right the way but once you near it then you'll have a text animation like You discovered xxxxxx"; `world/discovery.js`, its numbers `data/world.js` `DISCOVER`; `tests/discovery.js`): a holding, or a named site (a cave, ruins, a shrine, a hideout, a goblin camp; not a bandit or travellers' camp), is known by its kind only until your band comes within `DISCOVER.r` tiles of it (`site` for a site): its tip, its lands ("Unknown lands") and the full map's name. (No place has words on the map itself: 2026-11-08, the owner, "remove text on holding or places in the world map"; `holdingLabel` keeps the siege mark, the padlock and the lords' pips.) Then `Discovery.discover`: its name, its title over the map (`DiscoveryTitle`: "You discovered", the name, its kind; it rises, its letters drawing together, holds and fades: `style.css` 6b), a line in the log, Scouting practice (`DISCOVER.scout`) and `place:discovered` on the bus. What a game begins near (`DISCOVER.start` tiles) is known quietly. Saved (`found`). `DISCOVER.on` off (the test suites') every name is known.
- **The medieval look** (the owner, 2026-11-11: "redesign the game ux/ui to have more quality and better user experience, the ui should medieval looks"; `style.css` section 1's tokens, 3, 4 and 6; `fieldbook.css`): the main menu's own look taken through the game. Dark oak and iron for every surface (the cold greys of both sheets warmed); a thin gold frame with a black hairline and a dark band inside it on windows, HUD cards, buttons and cards (`--plate`, `--plate-sm`), a second line round a window; the title row's and the action row's edge a gold rule fading at its ends (`--rule`) with a diamond at the title's middle; a grain on a window's face (`--grain`); the way on gilt (`.btn.primary`, `--button-gilt`); bars and chips cut in; a row's line fading at its ends; labels in gold. Titles (a window's, a pane's, a card's, your band's name, a place discovered) in an old book's face where the device has one (`--display`: Palatino, Book Antiqua, a serif else); everything read or pressed stays Arial; `--display:var(--font)` takes the titles back to Arial. Sizes, borders' widths and the page's structure as they were (the UI suites pass as before). The discovery title no longer lies over an open window.
- **A holding's bar, a plate of the HUD's own** (the owner, 2026-11-11: "the holding or castles name ribbon seems a bit off and not like ui"; `gfx/structures.js` `HOLD_PLAQUE`, `drawPlaques`, `drawPlaquePlate`, `plaqueFont`; `sprites.js` `mapTxt`'s own face and size): the bar over a holding was the map's own pixels, a cloth ribbon behind a plank, so it grew with the zoom (huge and blocky on a phone, its tails off the screen). Now a dark plate in a thin gold frame with a black hairline and a dark band inside it and a diamond at each end, as the main menu's buttons, drawn on the screen's pixels at one size whatever the zoom (30 px high; down to .7 of it as the map is zoomed far out); in a row its owner's shield, the padlock, its name in the titles' face (`--display`), a crown a lord inside, the siege's mark. W6 re-recorded (tests/worldprint.js). **Discovery** from 4 tiles of a holding's edge, not 6 (`DISCOVER.r`; the owner: "a bit shorter distance").
- **Goblins** (2026-11-07; the owner: "Goblin enemy should also be added, similar to bandits but can use building sprite from orc factions for their camps"): every number in `data/goblins.js` `GOBLINS`, `GOBLINS.on` the switch (off, nothing is placed or sent and the world is as before). Troops `gob_stab` (knife and round shield), `gob_spear`, `gob_bow`, `gob_boss`, of the race `goblin` (`WILD_RACES`, `raceOf`: not playable; their look is `gfx/people.js`'s): frail, quick, morale `GOBLINS.morale` so they break early. Camps are `GoblinCamp` (a `Hideout` of their own, `world/sites.js`) on the hideouts' wild land, `DENSITY` `sites.goblins` of them, drawn as the Ashgor Horde's yurts in a ring of stakes (`gfx/structures.js` `SITE_CULTURE`); scouted, stormed or parleyed as a hideout, their plunder a pile of stolen goods. Warbands are `GoblinBand` (a `BandParty`, kind `goblins`, `world/goblins.js`): `GOBLINS.bands` out of each camp, bigger than a bandit band, timid (`nerve`: brains.js judges you by it), haunting their camp (`hauntR`), sheltering in it by night (`nightCamps`, `shelters`), raiding hamlets and villages they pass (`goblinRaid`: `frighten`, food taken, news). Their placing and sending draw from the stream `goblins` (`Rng.use`), so the world's Math.random is untouched. Tests: `tests/goblins.js`.
- **Their tongue** (2026-11-10, the owner: "Goblin cannot be negotiating if you cannot speak their language"; `data/goblins.js` `GOBLIN_TONGUE`, `world/goblins.js` `goblinSpeaker`, `goblinTongueWhy`, `goblinTongueSay`): no terms with goblins without one who speaks their tongue: your hero, or a companion with you, of a people that does (orcs, their kin; goblins), or a goblin among your men to speak for you. Without one: a cornered warband's surrender (`parley.js`), a goblin camp's price and its surrender (`sites.js` parley: their jabber not understood) and goblin captors' mercy (`captivity.js`: held, you are alone, so only your own tongue counts) are shut, with why; fighting and letting them go stay open. With one, the page says who speaks for you. Tests: goblins.
- **A band strips you** (2026-11-11, the owner: "when you captured by bandits normally you'll lose all your golds or inventories which then you can get back what you lose about 75-100% depends on day passed ... so after you lose you may not select to pay them gold"; `world/captivity.js` `CAPTIVITY.band.strip`, `Captivity.strip`, `tookSay`, `stashShare`, `StashQuest#share`; `data/dialogue.js` `captor.robbed`): taken by bandits, raiders or goblins, your purse and your bag's gear are theirs at once (`held.took`), but the coin you hid (`prison.hidden`, a quarter now, 15% before). So they ask no ransom: no Pay on their page nor in their cage. The ways out: talk them round (no fine: nothing left to fine), ride with them (your gear given back, your gold theirs), your realm pays, a guard bribed with what you hid, a break-out, or their cage till they tire of you or sell you on. What they took is their stash from the day they took it. A stash, any captor's: all of it there the first day, 75% from day 10 on (`stash.back`, `stash.fade`; a flat 60% before), sold off at 30 days as before, never fewer than 7 days to go for it once you are free (`stash.least`); its quest tells the share left. A lord or a realm's men are no robbers: their ransom as before. Off (`band.strip` false): a band's ransom as before. Tests: captivity.
- **Bands rob caravans** (2026-11-08, the owner: "bandits or goblins, looters should rob caravans"; `world/brains.js` `BandBrain#hunt`, `data/outlaws.js` `BAND_HUNT`): a band (bandits, looters, goblins: every party on the band brain) with a caravan in sight (`see` px) whose guards it thinks it can take (the caravan's power at most `dare` × its own by its nerve) rides it down before anything but its fear of you (not lying in wait, not cooling from a fight); the caravan's own brain starts the fight when it is upon it, and the winners plunder it as before (`fights.js`). Off: bands come on caravans only by chance. Tests: `caravanparty`.
- **Goblins and bandits at feud** (2026-11-08, the owner: "Make sure that bandits and goblins are hostile to each other"; `world/brains.js` `BandBrain#feud`, `data/goblins.js` `BAND_FEUD`): a goblin warband and any other band (bandits, looters, raiders) are enemies. On the map each rides down a rival in sight (100 px) it thinks it can take (the rival's power at most 1.1 × its own, by its nerve), unless it fears you or cools from its last fight; two rivals within 10 px fight whichever is the stronger. In a fight (`fights.js`) `battle/sides.js` `armyRelation` already put bands of different kinds on opposite sides, so one riding in joins against the other. Two warbands stay friends. `BAND_FEUD.on` false: they pass each other by. W7, W8 re-recorded (91 bands left after 600 frames, not 96; with the switch off, the old values). Tests: `goblins`.
- **The goblins' warchief** (2026-11-08, the owner: first "should be hobgoblin not human", then "replace the hobgoblin (will use later as a special) with the goblin leader, just make it look different from normal goblins"): a goblin band's or camp's chieftain is a goblin of his own look (`gfx/sprites.js` `chiefLookType`: battle's `riderLook` and the map's `marchSprite`; `gfx/people.js` `gob_chief`: a skull for a helm, his side's cloak over a pelt, a crude blade, no shield), called Warchief (`world/leaders.js` `Chieftain#roleName`); a bandit chief still a man. The hobgoblin is kept for a special to come (`hob_chief`, the race look `hobgoblin`, `engine/doll.js` `RACES.hobgoblin`: a man's height, broad, ruddy, long-eared). A goblin's long ears and an orc's tusk are drawn again (`engine/doll.js`: a `//` on their line had hidden them since d909f51). Tests: `goblins`.
- **Goblins in rags** (2026-11-08, the owner: "goblins should not wear full armor, make them realistic, same goes for weapons"; `gfx/dollfigs.js` `dollParts`, `gfx/people.js`, `data/goblins.js`): a goblin wears no soldier's kit: barefoot, ragged breeches, a scrap of a leather vest (the boss a pelt), bare arms, his side's colour only a rag at his waist, under his hood; crude arms of his size: a knife and a round shield, a short spear (a javelin's length, not a man's spear), a short bow, the boss a hand axe (no great axe) and no mail (hide). In their variants (`people.js` `lookOf`: `bareTop`, by the man's variant) every other one bare to the waist in his leather (or dark) breeches only; the boss and the leader always clothed. The hobgoblin in a leather jerkin, a fur under it and a fur cape. Tests: `goblins`.
- **Goblins after the owner's reference** (2026-11-08, a picture of a goblin): bald and bareheaded (the archer and the boss still hooded), his long ear out behind his head at his eyes' height, its tip up, a hooked nose (`engine/doll.js` `goblinEars`); his legs wrapped in brown, a ragged fur loincloth under a leather belt, bare-chested under a studded strap or in a scrap of a vest, no side's colour on him (`gfx/dollfigs.js` `dollParts`); the stabber a crude cleaver (`gfx/people.js` `WPN.cleaver`, `gfx/weapons.js` `drawBlade`: its edge steel, its back rusty) and a shield of planks bound in iron (`gfx/figures.js` `SHIELD_ART.plank`: no device). Tests: `goblins`.
  - **Outlines and seams** (2026-11-08, the owner: "the outer line should be darker shading not completely black"; "inside the structure itself it looks like merging together, sometimes I cannot tell what is this part"): a building's outline is a deep shade of what it borders (`engine/pixel.js` `PX.buildRim`, `PX.BUILD_RIM`: ×.66, ×.8 by a dark body, no navy, never under a floor), and inside it a seam where two parts meet, the one behind a shade darker (`PX.SEAM`, `engine/model.js`): a column's plinth, shaft and capital, a roof and its wall, a crag and the boulder before it.
  - **Centred figures** (2026-11-08, the owner: "check whether the unit and player hero is centered? Check the marching preview as well"): every man's picture is centred on his body (`figures.js` `FigureRig.frame`'s `ax`, `people.js` `FIG_AXIS`: a doll's middle 6 from its origin, a rider's mount's legs), not on the grid's middle 2 px aside, so he stands on his point on the map, in battle and in the march preview, and no longer jumps 4 px as he turns. The estates' names are gone from the map too (no words on places).
  - **Buff glow** (2026-11-08, the owner: "remove unit outer highlight, instead make that as a buff effect for certain skills"): no outline for a selected, pointed-at or targeted unit; a man lifted by a war cry, a banner, standing firm or a rally glows round his silhouette in its colour, pulsing (`render.js` `unitGlow`, `outlineOf`; vfx.js `STATE_FX`).
  - **Site looks** (2026-11-08, the owner: "add more variations for caves, hideouts, places with more detail; currently stone pillars look not like stone pillar"; `engine/buildmodels.js`): each of four by its seed. Ruins: broken walls and an arch; a colonnade of real columns (`column`: a plinth, a round shaft, a capital, or snapped and mossed) under a lintel; a round tower's stump; a temple's stepped platform with a column gate. Shrines: rough standing stones round a glowing altar; an obelisk on a stepped plinth; a column gate round a glowing stele; a cairn. Caves (and so the bandit lairs and goblin warrens in hillsides): an outcrop; a cliff face hung with ivy; a low mound; a mouth between twin crags, bones before it.
  - **Building looks** (2026-11-08, the owner: "improve the building sprites like holding, estate and other as well, using the same concept"; `engine/build-vale.js`, `engine/buildmodels.js`): a holding's and an estate's buildings each in several looks by their dice, as the sites. The Vale's (and the League's, on them) smithy: the oak shed with its brick stack, a stone forge under a hip with a coal store and quench trough, or a farrier's, its gable to the street, a shoeing frame and grindstone; barn: the long thatched one, a tall plank barn gable-on with its loft hatch and hoist, or the long barn with a cart shed leaning on it and a raised grain bin; granary: on its tall plinth, on staddle stones, or round under a cone; warehouse: the framed store with its loft, a stone store gable-on with a loading dock and hoist, or one with a covered wagon bay. Every culture without its own: a chapel with its bellcote, a little square tower over its door, or a rounded apse and a yard of headstones; a windmill: the round tower mill, a post mill on its braced post with its tail pole, or a smock mill on a stone footing with a gallery; a watermill in stone, or framed with its wheel in a timber-lined race and a sluice. The cultures' most common buildings too, three looks each: the League's townhouse (three storeys under a hip with a balcony; two gable-on with a shop's arch, awning and planter; or with a little belvedere on its roof); the Court's elf hall (slender gable-on; broad with a taller gabled door wing; round under a leaf cone with a turret), treehouse (as it was; a round pod ringing its trunk on a railed deck; a pod slung between two young trees) and lumber shed (as it was; a woodwright's hall with seasoning boards under a lean-to; a round store with stacked boards and cradled logs); the Horde's yurt (as it was; on a wagon; two-tiered), hide hall (as it was; a long felt hall under a hide gable with horned poles; a great yurt between two vaulted wings) and barn (as it was; a log store beside a hay barrack; a tall log barn gable-on beside a stake pen); the Clans' townhouse (as it was; a granite tower-house on a cellar with its stair; a smith's house, its forge glowing, copper vents through its slate) and stone hall (as it was; on a terrace with a corner tower; tall and gable-on between two aisles, banners and braziers). Every look meets the models' rules (`tests/buildmodels.js` sweeps ten seeds of each varied type).
  - **Warrens** (2026-11-08, the owner: "add goblin warrens near hills"): a goblin camp among hills (`GoblinCamp` `hilly`: `HIDEOUT_LAIR.hills` hill or peak tiles within `r`) is a warren, its mouth a cave in the hillside (`lairLook` 'cave', `lairSprite` with its green banner, `GOBLINS.warren.banner`), its type `GOBLINS.warren.T`, stormed inside with a small party as a bandit lair is (`battle/interior.js`). Elsewhere a camp of yurts, fought in the open. Each named in its own word ("X Warren", "X Camp"). `GOBLINS.warren.on` the switch.
- **Wild things per world** (`data/world.js` `DENSITY`, `density(m)`): the base counts (`DENSITY.classic`, the old hand-made map's: bands, camps, beasts and game at the start and at most, sites by kind, caravans, refugees, trade ships, and the tries a day to bring bands, beasts and game back) grown to the world by `DENSITY.grow`: each by its measure against the base's, land (32,042 tiles), realm holdings (37) or ports (9), to a power (1: today's per tile of land; the bands .8), and the sites a rule a kind (caves, ruins and shrines .35, the travellers' camps by the world's road, `worldRoads`, the hideouts by its wild land, `worldWild`). Hideouts stand deep in the wilds (`sites.js` `HIDEOUT_PLACE`, `wildLand`: 22 tiles or more from every holding, 8 from every road, track and ford, 20 apart, most in a clearing among woods and hills or deep in the swamp or desert), with a bigger garrison, a stronger chieftain and richer plunder (`HIDEOUT_BAND`; bandits in ruins as before); `tests/hideouts.js`. `density(WM)` is the world in's, `density()` today's (so the generator never reads the last world's). Beasts meet bands from a grid of them (`monsters.js` `BAND_GRID`), the watches their prey from one of the threats (`defence.js` `LandDefence.grid`), both `Spatial.index`. On a map bigger than Normal the LOD tiers are bounded (`worldobject.js` `WORLD_LOD.reach`), and a generated world packs its beasts' and herds' records in a save (`packRec`, `unpackRec`). `tests/wilddensity.js` checks a Large world.
- **Parties** (`world/parties.js`): lords, bandit bands, patrols, caravans, your companions' parties and your own band (`P`) keep soldiers as counts (`o.tc`; yours are `P.troops`). `o.troops` (and a band's `list`) is a read-only view; change soldiers with `add`, `remove`, `removeAny`, `cull`, or by setting `troops`. Each kind's class says how it looks (`look`), whether you can see it (`inView`: nearer in cover) and where it may shelter (`shelterWhy`, `enter`, `leave`; `inside` is the holding's name). Plain data becomes its class with `asParty(o)`. `W.parties` lists every party on the map.
- **Reinforcements** (`battle/engage.js` `nearAllies`, its numbers `REINF` in `data/factions.js`; the owner, 2026-10-23: armies on neutral or better terms help). Armies within `REINF.r` of your fight join it, by whom you fight. Against a common enemy (a band, a camp or hideout, a beast, a lair or a ruin): your friends always (your liege's men, its allies', a realm that honours you, a lord who likes you); a neutral lord by a chance from how he feels about you (`REINF.chance` at 0, sure at `RELATION.helpAt`), drawn once for the encounter (a hash, never `Math.random`: `e.rolls`) and only at fair odds (`REINF.fair`); a holding's watch always on its realm's land, off it as a neutral lord; a caravan's guards only for their own caravan (the road's caravan under attack, or a fight on the map you join). Against a realm's men: only your friends at war with it, and the watches of a realm at war with it on their own land. At most `REINF.max` (3), the nearest first, of those about as near the strongest; your companions' parties come too. The pre-battle page gives each a reason ("At your side: Ser Corwin of the Vale Crown (10), a friend; Day watch of Ashford (6), riding in to clear its road."). Won: honour from each realm beside you (`REINF.honor.beside`), more for a common enemy on its land (`land`), the lords like you more, the renown is shared (yours × `REINF.renown`), and each lord or watch takes his share of the gold by his men (`reinfCut`, shown on the victory page). They fight as allied groups under their own captain, as before (`tests/reinforce.js`).
- **Caravans** (`CaravanParty`, `world/caravans.js`) save only how many guards they have (`guards`); their kinds and formations are rolled from that and kept off the record (re-rolled when `guards` is set; a change to their soldiers sets `guards`). Behaviour is methods: `meet`, `act`, `demands`/`demandAct`, `fears`, `price`/`pays`, `loot`, `seize`, `arrive`/`endLeg`, `look`. They are not in `W.parties` (they are drawn and picked as their carts: `draw`, `pick`), but they are in `worldObjects()`. Tests: `tests/caravanparty.js`.
- **A caravan's picture** (the owner, 2026-11-01: "the caravan sprite looks out of place not match with battle style"; `world/caravans.js` `caravanSprite`, its numbers and palette in `CARAVAN_ART`): built at the battle figures' density (a man 16 px) and drawn on the map `WORLD_BATTLE.sprite/scale` as big, as its guards are (`drawCaravanArt`). A covered cart a man and a half tall (the cover on its hoops in its owner's colour mixed toward canvas, two wheels, the far one in shade, a planked bed, a crate and a sack at the open back), its ox a battle beast (`engine/beasts.js`'s kit `quad`, `oxShape`), its drover a battle figure (`peopleSprite`, a goad on his shoulder: `figures.js` `GoadGear`), lit from the top left in `PX.ramp` ramps (the beasts' and the buildings' timber, plank and iron), one outline round it all. The ox's and drover's legs walk at the march's cadence by its own time walked (`caravanGait`, `MARCH_MOVE.cadence`), still when it stands; the wheel turns by the way it rolls (`caravanRoll`). Each picture is built once into `spr` (5 walk frames × 3 of the wheel a colour, a 64 cell); `caravanPic` is it at the map's own size, for a click and the banner's height. The cart alone, left behind (its shafts on the ground) or the burnt wagon (charred, its cover gone to its hoops), is the same art (`how`: `'left'`, `'burnt'`; `gfx/encounterscene.js` `roadCart`, `ROAD_ART`). Tests: `mapfigures` (its density, kept, drawn as big as its guards, its gait), `smoothmove`, `worldscale`; report `visual/caravansheet` (old and new beside a guard, each way, its walk, colours, left behind and burnt, on the march, at battle size; its IMG line a PNG).
- **World objects** (`WorldObject`, `world/worldobject.js`, with `asClass`, the level-of-detail ticking and walk cycles): everything on the map says what it is (`mapKind`: `town`, `estate`, `lord`, `band`, `patrol`, `caravan`, `cparty`, `monster`, `site`, `critter`, `refugee`, `road`, `worker`, `ship`, `fight`; your band `you`), whether it is still on the map (`here`: in its kind's list), whether a chase after it goes on (`chaseable`), whether it is met as soon as you reach it even just after a fight (`meetsAnyTime`), whether you see it (`inView`; the shared rule is `spotted(o,V)`, nearer in woods and hills; a mythic beast from afar), where a click answers on it (`pick(at)`), how you meet it (`meet`), what the pointer shows (`tip`), how it is drawn (`draw(ents,cam,V)`) and its minimap dot (`mapDot(V)`). `pickAt`, `objKind`, `interact`, `tipFor`, the chase and the minimap go through them. `worldObjects()` (`world/world.js`) lists everything in the order a click weighs two that are as near: each holding then its built estates, the parties, beasts, sites, herds, caravans, refugees, workers, ships, fights (no pick, no dot: a click on one of its parties meets it). The minimap's dots come from `worldObjects()` by layer (`MAP_LAYER`, `gfx/render.js`). Holdings are `HoldingObject` (`world/holding.js`: `Holding extends HoldingObject`; it and `worldobject.js` load before the world map, which makes the classic holdings as it loads); an estate is one `Estate` per holding and kind (`estatePick`, never saved); then `Site`, `Monster`, `Herd`, `Refugees`, `EstateWorker`, `Ship`, `Fight` (`world/fights.js`). Behaviour only: saves stay plain JSON; plain data (a save's, a test's) is made its kind in place when the map next asks (`asClass`, `asWorldObject`). `drawWorld` still calls each kind in its old order (ties in depth keep it). Tests: `tests/worldobject.js`, `tests/worldobjects2.js`.
- **Encounters on the road** (the kinds: `world/encounters.js`; their numbers: `data/encounters.js`; the road: `world/road.js`; drawn by `gfx/encounterscene.js` `EncounterScene`; doc/ENCOUNTERS_DESIGN.md): the people you meet travelling (a wounded traveller, a peddler, hunters, a caravan under attack, an ambush…) are `Encounter`s (`story/happening.js`; `mapKind` `road`) in `W.road.on`, each a registered Encounter kind (`defineKinds`, trigger `travelHour`; `ROAD_ENC` their view: its rule `ok`, ground `on`, behaviour `beh`: approach, stay, flee, scene, walk, the strategies of `Behaviours`; its scene, page text, Insight facts and choices). They come by a pity timer (`ENC_SPAWN`: the hours travelled since the last met, `W.road.c`; at most 2 a day, a kind not again within 5 days), last by `ENC_LIFE`, are drawn from their pieces by `EncounterScene` (today's map figures, beasts, cart, tent, fire, shrine, mine), highlighted while unmet (`gfx/worldui.js` `wuiRoadUnder`, `wuiRoadOver`: the blue "!", sound waves rippling out, the call-out, and waves rolling in at the screen's edge while it is off the screen) and met on a `PageScreen` page (`enc-` actions). Nothing ends while you can see it: its time up in your sight, each ends its kind's way (`ROAD_ENC` `ends`, else its behaviour's: `Behaviours[beh].ends`; walking off along the road, `ENC_OFF`) and is gone once out of your sight. A few leave a trace (`ROAD_TRACE`). `W.road` is saved once there is one. `world/incidents.js` keeps the instant weather, sickness and spring/mirage events. The **abandoned wagon** is a silent, stationary roadside encounter (`ROAD_ENC.wagon`, `ENC_TUNE.wagon`): Search for 15 gold once, or Leave and return while it lasts (24 game hours). It has no call, notice sound or sound waves (`silent`); sight and the usual encounter mark reveal it. The searched cart remains in sight and ends outside it. Tests: `tests/wagon.js`. **The small encounters** (ST3, IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN §10.3; their numbers `ENC_TUNE` `campfire`, `herbalist`, `toll`, their checks `ENC_STAT` `tracks`, `watch`, `nimble`, `forage`, `herblore`, `writ`, `talk`, `haggle`; helpers at the end of `world/encounters.js`): a **warm campfire** beside a road (read the tracks: who left it, how many, where they went, marked as a rumour's "?"; lie in wait for bandits; rest, and they may come back; search the ashes, a find or a trap), an **herbalist** at a wood's edge by day (remedies that mend your wounded, the fevered first; a tonic; help her gather; learn from her: Medicine; rob her), and a **toll on the road** on a friendly realm's land by day (springs as you pass; the lord's own men or impostors: pay, ask for their writ (Scouting 3 sees through them at once), talk your way through, refuse, fight; false ones reported: their ringleader rides bound with you to the reeve, an escort, `ESCORT.who.impostor`; the news `toll`). **Ambushes by the woods** (the owner, 2026-11-01: "should happen near wood and should make the spawn bandits not look like they are out of no where"): the **fake wounded** (`ROAD_ENC.lure`) lies only beside a road, out of the trees himself, with woods within `ENC_TUNE.lure.woods` (40 px) behind him, on his side away from the road (`world/road.js` `roadLie`: rays within `arc` of straight away from the nearest road tile; none, no lure there, and the weighted pick tries another spot or kind). His band lies hidden in those trees, `hide` (12 px) past the tree line (`e.lie`, `e.edge`), never drawn. Scouting `warn` shows a rustle in the trees at that spot (`rustle`, drawn over the trees), `count` a glint of steel there too. Sprung, the battle on the map opens with the band where it lay (`roadFight` places its party at `roadLieOf(e)`), so they come out of the trees at you; called out, they stand at the tree line and fight from there; left be, they slip away. The road's **ambush** (`ROAD_ENC.ambush`) keeps its band in the woods by it the same way (its own spot when no woods are near). Waylaying bands (below) already wait as real parties in the trees. Tests: `tests/ambush.js`. A kind's `vs(e)` names its fight (`roadFight`). Tests: `tests/encounters.js`; every registered kind generically: `tests/kinds.js`; the small encounters: `tests/newencounters.js`.
- **Weather** (2026-11-07; the owner: "add weather to the game like raining, sunny days, sandstorm etc. some events may relevant to them";
  `world/weather.js` `Weather`, its numbers in `data/weather.js` `WEATHER`, drawn by `gfx/weather.js` `WeatherArt`; `tests/weather.js`):
  - The map is cut into regions (`WEATHER.cell`, 24 tiles a side), each with its climate (the most of its tiles') and a front: one
    of clear (sunny by day), overcast, rain, storm, fog, snow, blizzard, sandstorm or heat wave, for some hours (`hours`). Each hour
    (Systems hour `weather`, first) a front that has ended gives way: now and then a neighbour's (`spread`), else one by the climate's
    weights (`climates`: no sandstorm or heat in the frost, no snow or blizzard in the desert). Drawn from the stream `weather`
    (core/rng.js), never Math.random. Saved (`weather`, order 370); an old save makes it afresh.
  - `Weather.at(x, y)` is the weather there; `ambWeather` returns it, so the birds, the tracks (`TRACKS.weather`, every kind) and the
    story's `context.weather` read it. As it changes where you are: a toast and `weather:changed` on the bus.
  - On the map: travel × `speed` (`speedAt`; at home in the climate, half the loss) and sight × `sight` (`visMulAt`). In a battle (the
    weather where it is fought, `B.env.w`): pace and reach (`envStats`), arrows' aim and scatter (`Missile.fire`), each man's sight
    (`B.sightK`; in a battle on the map, the map's sight). No weather in caves and cellars, nor at a tournament.
  - Drawn over the view, under the fog of war (2026-11-08, the owner: "make weathers under the fog"): on the map before
    `drawWorldFog`, in a battle before its `fogDraw` (a battle on the map: over the field, under the HUD). The sky's shade, falling
    rain, snow or sand, a haze in soft bands (`WeatherArt.band`), lightning; fewer and slower, no lightning, with reduced motion.
    The day bar shows the season, its icon and name.
  - **Seasons** (2026-11-08, the owner: "make it into a seasons"): the year's 120 days in four (`WEATHER.seasons`: spring, summer,
    autumn, winter; a game begins in spring). Each weighs its climate's kinds (`mul`) and may add one (`add`: winter's snow on the
    grass and in the swamps, summer's rain in the frost, winter's in the desert): `Weather.weights(c, d)`, `Weather.season(d)`; the
    story's `context.season`.
  - **Gradual** (the owner: "weather should gradually change not just so sudden"): drawn, the old weather fades out as the new fades
    in over `WEATHER.ease` s (`WeatherArt.layers`); a new front builds to its strength over `WEATHER.build` hours
    (`Weather.strength`: drawn only; its start kept in memory, not saved).
  - **A fair start** (2026-11-08, the owner: "why always start with raining?"): the first fronts are drawn by chance alone, not by
    how long each lasts, so in spring on the grass a third of games opened in rain or a storm and two in three under a grey sky. A new
    game now opens under a clear sky where you stand (`WEATHER.start`, `Weather.fair`, from `session.js` `startGame`) for a clear
    front's 6-14 hours; the other regions as drawn, and then the weather turns as ever. `WEATHER.start` null: as drawn.
  - **Skills in the weather** (2026-11-08, the owner: "make weather effect battle and some of the skill such as rain affect fire arrow
    which cannot be used"; `WEATHER.skills`, `Weather.skillBan`, `skillMul`): no Fire arrows in rain, a storm, snow or a blizzard (the
    troops' skill refused with its reason: `unitabilities.js` `abilityWhyNot`, so the AI's captains never try it); a Fireball 30% weaker
    in rain (40% in a storm, 15% in snow, 30% in a blizzard), 15% stronger in a heat wave; a Frost Nova 20% stronger in snow, 30% in a
    blizzard, 25% weaker in the heat (`abilities.js` `kitOf`: your hero's and every champion's; one the weather forbids refused in
    `castAbility`, `chooseAbility` and your skill bar). What the field's weather does is told on the pre-battle page and as the lines
    meet (`Weather.battleText`), and named on the battle bar beside the ground.
  - The road's incidents (`world/incidents.js`) in their weather only: storm, lightning (men hurt), flood (rain), blizzard,
    frostbite, sandstorm, lost (sandstorm), heat, a fine day (clear, grassland).
  - `WEATHER.on` off (the test suites' default, `tests/harness.js`): the old way (the frost's snow, clear elsewhere), nothing kept.
- **Tracks** (`world/tracks.js`, drawn by `gfx/tracks.js`, the numbers in `data/tracks.js` `TRACKS`; doc/WORLD_DESIGN.md §12.7, the owner's 2026-10-23): every band that moves leaves a trail (`Track`, in `W.tracks`): your band, the lords' armies, bandit bands, patrols, your companions' parties, caravans, refugees, the beasts and the wild game (never the workers about their estates: to and fro on the same roads all day, they would bury every other trail there; nor ships, livestock or a mythic beast in flight). A point every `sample` 24 px walked (`Tracks.frame`, the frame's first system; a jump into or out of a holding starts a new trail); prints drawn every few px between them, each from its stretch's two kept points (so a print laid never moves or turns; a trail still walked shows up to its last point), turned the way the band went: footprints, hoof prints (mounted bands, deer), paw prints, a wagon's ruts, a wyrm's or a slime's furrow, wider for a bigger band (`size`). Clear on sand, snow and mud, fainter on grass and hills, very faint in woods (`ground` × `cover`); on roads (`road`) dust prints on a dirt road or an estate's track (0.5), faint on a great road (0.3); none on water, fords and a bridge's deck. They fade over their ground's `life` (grass 36 h, swamp and frost 30, desert 14; a dirt road 10, a great road 5), faster by its `weather` (the frost's snow 3x: 10 h); a stretch is as old as its newer end. Kept to `cap` 4,000 points (the oldest dropped first) and `keep` 48 h; saved under `tracks` (each trail its kind, size, mounted, name, bounty and its points as steps: about 15 chars a point, some 63 KB at the cap on a played world; none: no key). Your own you always see while they last; others' by Scouting: no older than 3 + 3 × level hours and no farther than your sight × (0.25 + 0.075 × level) (0: 3 h, a quarter of your sight; 10: 33 h, all of it), and only in your sight. Hover a trail, or Look on the action ring over it, for its card (`trackTip`): below Scouting 2 "Tracks"; from 2 "Tracks of about 8, on foot, heading NE"; from 5 "Bandits, about 8, a day old"; from 8 "The Red Vipers, 8 men, 5 hours ago"; yours "Your band's tracks". A first reading of each trail trains Scouting a little; a bounty's band's trails, once one is read, show faintly gold. **This is how a roving band, or its hideout, is found: walk along its tracks.** `TRACKS.on` false keeps, draws and saves none (every fingerprint as before it). Tests: `tests/tracks.js`; screenshots and the cost: `tests/visual/tracks.js`.
  - Seen from every man of your army (the owner, 2026-11-01), as your sight is (`world/fog.js` `Sight`): your own trail wherever any man sees, others' within each man's Scouting share of his sight (`TRACKS.see.range`), measured from him, not from your army's middle (`Tracks.sees`).
- **The world's log** (`world/worldlog.js`, the owner 2026-10-23: it works like rumours): every world event (a holding falls, a siege fails, a lord raises a new army, war, peace and alliances, a realm falls, a sally, lords taken inside walls, a lord routed, a caravan taken, battles between others; a siege laid, `world/brains.js`; walls begun, `world/buildings.js`; a lord riding out, `world/defence.js`; a lord growing in skill, `world/leaders.js`; people leaving a holding, `world/refugees.js`) goes through one gateway, `worldEvent({text, cls, kind, x, y, fac, towns, involved, mine, news | heard, seen, past, also, post, noise})` (`kind`: the Chronicle's, else `war`; `''` for the lines that had none), which picks its route: **you took part** (`involved`; your battle's aftermath, `worldPart`; you besiege or stay in one of its `towns`) or **you saw it** (within actual unit sight and woodland concealment; worded as seen, `seen`, where natural): into the log at once; **your realm's affairs** (one of `fac` your employer's or your own banner's; `mine`: your caravan, a battle your realm's lord fought): a rider from your realm's holding nearest it, a day per `WORLD_LOG.tiles` (60) tiles from it to you, at least `minHours` (3), then "A rider from Ashford: Thornford fell to Lord X of the Karn two days ago." (its record keeps the day it happened, `at`; the Chronicle shows "happened day N (2 days before you heard)"); **anything else**: news only (`world/news.js`), heard as a rumour. What you learn at once or by rider is heard already. The riders on their way are `W.news.post`, saved with the news (the hour system `riders`). New news kinds: `war`, `peace` (an alliance ended too), `ally`, `realm` (fallen), `army` (a lord's new army), `starving` (a garrison under siege, yours or another's), `walls` (a realm's new walls), `exodus` (people leaving a holding); the realms' affairs are proclamations (below). A lord riding out and a lord growing in skill have no rider and are no news (`post` false): seen, or nothing (a sortie's fight brings its own word). People leaving come by rider only from your own fief (`mine`). **Your own conquest** (`world/siege.js` `captureAct`): a `fell` proclamation like a realm's, your band the taker by name (`bandName()`; handed over, "in the name of" the realm: "Ashford has fallen to The Grey Company in the name of the Vale"), through the gateway with no `text` (its lines are logged where you did it, once): yours, so heard already, no rider; sacked and left, talk of a raid; your raid (`finishRaid`) heard too. The full map draws the rumours' "?" (`ui/map.js`). `WORLD_LOG.on` false: every line logged where it was before (the fingerprints' check). Known gap: the map's holding colours (and the territory) still change at once. Tests: `tests/worldlog.js`.
- **News in three classes** (`world/news.js`, `world/worldlog.js`, the owner 2026-10-23: news spreads the way it did in medieval times):
  - **Proclamations, free** (`PROCLAIM.kinds`: `war`, `peace`, `ally`, `realm`, `fell`): a wave from where it happened. The item keeps its origin and hour (`h`); `proclaimReached(it, p)` is a formula (`proclaimDays`): a day per `PROCLAIM.tiles` (60) tiles to a holding, a village `lag` 1.5 days later (a castle .5), anywhere else by its nearest holding and on over the land at `PROCLAIM.wild` (15) tiles a day, or straight over the land if sooner. Heard free from the crier of a holding it has reached (`proclaimAt`: entering it, `ui/town.js` `enterTown`, or each hour you are in, staying at or at the gates of one, the hour system `riders`), or from anyone met whose place it has reached (inside `rumourFrom`: travellers, caravans, the watch, lords, workers), never against a cap; "Proclamation (the crier of Ashford): War! The Vale and the Karn went to war two days ago." (log kind `war`). Then heard. Your realm's riders still bring their own (heard as they set out). Kept `PROCLAIM.days` (20); `NEWS.keep` counts only the rest. `PROCLAIM.on` false: every kind a rumour, as before.
  - **Common talk, free, capped:** the rumours as before (never a proclamation), vaguer as heard (`newsVague`, `NEWS_VAGUE`): a level for every 40 tiles from you and every 2 days of age (0–3), kept in `W.news.v`; places rougher (1 no estate, 2 "somewhere about Millbrook", 3 "somewhere in the lands of the Vale"), numbers swollen (× 1 + .5 per level: "a dozen", "a score", "scores", "hundreds"), "some days back" from level 2.
  - **Intelligence, paid** (`INTEL`, `INTEL_KINDS`): a lord's army, an army on the march, a siege's true state (besiegers, garrison, food, the days to the assault), a bandit hideout's place (on your map then), a caravan's road and guard, worked out from the world as it is. `intelGather(src)` (a source's places `at` and reach `r`: the tavern's innkeeper now; informants, Scouting and the bird later); `intelLearn` logs it ("Information (…): …", kind `rumour`) with a gold "?" on its spot. A tavern's **Buy information**: up to `INTEL.n` (3) of the 8 dearest and nearest within `INTEL.r` (640 px), chosen in a picker, once a day; each its value (hideout 40, siege 35, march 30, lord 20, caravan 15) × (1 + .25 × tier past a village's); greyed when nothing is known. Tests: `tests/proclaim.js`.
- **Brains** (`world/brains.js`): what each kind does each moment (`think`), on the shared steps of `Brain` (cool-down, sight, meeting you). `updateParties(d)` runs them all.
- **Frightened parties** (`battle/engage.js` `fearSeen`, `fearOf`, `safeRefuge`, `armyMid`; the owner, 2026-11-01: bands that fled his army walked back into it to reach their camp). One afraid of your army keeps fleeing it while it remembers it (a group of yours seen within `TRAVEL_GROUPS.memory`, `TravelAwareness`'s record), out of its sight too, straight away from your army's middle, and just after a fight too (its cool-down stops only hunting). A refuge is safe only if your army is neither at it (the reach to meet there plus `WORLD_SCALE.ai.refugeAt`) nor across the way ahead (your ring plus `refugeLane` of the line): by night a band takes the nearest safe camp in reach (`world.js` `campNear(b, fear)`), else flees, and comes back to its own once it has forgotten you (it runs again if it sees you there). A band that fought in your battle and is the weaker leaves the field remembering your army (brains.js, `battle:ended`). The same for beasts (not back past you to their lair), the watch (home only through a clear gate, else away) and caravans (afraid while they remember you). Tests: `tests/bandflee.js`.
- **Waylay** (`world/waylay.js`, the owner 2026-10-27): by day a band (`WAYLAY.band`, at most `share` of them) or a lord (`lord`, rarer, shorter) takes to a forest tile beside a road and waits, unseen. Only those who can see it can find it out (the owner, 2026-10-29). While it is inside your sight into the trees (`Waylay.sources`: your map sight × `spot.trees`), you notice it at a chance a second (`spot.rate`, plus `spot.scout` per Scouting level). Out of sight, you never notice it. Noticed, it is shown, "Lying in wait", and springs on no one you lead. Unnoticed, once you are alongside (`spring` px), it bursts from the trees, a battle with your army caught off guard (`Surprise`). Walking straight past at the usual pace, you notice one about 4% of the time with no Scouting and about 37% with Scouting 5. A party it would beat does the same with its kind's own sight (`see`, × `spot.trees`) and rate (`sees`): a patrol sees farthest and soonest, a caravan least. Unnoticed when it comes within `meet` px, the party is waylaid: a map fight, the ambusher `fight` times as hard for `fightFor` of it. Hunters that lose sight of you search where they last saw you (`TravelSearch`, `world/worldobject.js`).
- **Characters** (`world/characters.js`, class wave A): everyone who fights is a `Unit`: a `Troop` (one per kind) or a `Character` (one person). A character's role is its class, one step under `Character`: `Hero` (your hero, over `P`), `Companion` (`world/companions.js`, over `P.comps[id]`), `Lord` and `Chieftain` (`world/leaders.js`, over a lord's party and a chieftain's record). `charOf(o)` (`leaderOf`) makes the one Character over a record, kept aside, never saved: saves stay the plain records. One rule for all: `gainXp(n)` (level × 60 a level; `levelled`, then `grew`: a companion's perk every third level, news of a lord or a chieftain), `train(k, n)` (disciplines by use, as fast as `learn(k)`; `rose`), `trained(k)` (as trained) and `skill(k)` (with its tree), `kit()` (its tree, slots, fighting disciplines and gear, made once on the record), `cls()` (warrior, guardian, rider, archer, caster), `autoTree()` and `outfit()` (for those who choose for themselves, `aiOwned`: lords and chieftains), `meleeSkill()`, `gearMod(k)`, `treeMod(k)`, `hasPerk(p)`, and the numbers: `stats()` (`base()`, the one formula; your hero's are `P.heroStats()`), `fieldStats`, `actives()`, `card()`. A companion also has `inBand()`, `canLead()`, `partyCap()`, `equipment()`, `fits(slot)`, `equip(slot, i)`, `unequip(slot)` (`compOf(id)` gives it); a lord `won(foes)`; a chieftain `won(foes)`, `bounty()`, `holder()`. The older names stay as one-line aliases: `charStats`, `charKit`, `compSkill`, `compTrain`, `compXp`, `compHas`, `compInBand`, `compMeleeSk`, `compStats`, `compEquipment`, `compFits`, `compEquip`, `compUnequip`, `cpartyCap`, `canLead`, `lordWins`, `lordXp`, `lordTrain`, `chiefWins`. Tests: `tests/characters.js`, `tests/leaders.js`, `tests/companions.js`.
- **Your numbers** (`world/parties.js` `PlayerParty`; `P` is your band and your hero's record at once): `troopCount()`, `compCount()`, `woundedCount()`, `mountedCount()`, `headcount()`, `prisonerCount()`, `cap()`, `room()`, `prisonCap()`, `eatPerDay()`, `sight()`; your hero's `treeMod(k)`, `treeSkill(k)`, `hasPerk(p)`, `roleBonus(k)`, `gearMod(k)`, `gearSkill(k)`, `skill(k)` (with what your companions, gear and tree add; `charOf(P).skill(k)` is as trained), `heroStats()`, `maxSP()`, `maxMP()`, `vitals()`, `regenVitals(days)`, `restoreVitals()`. Their older names stay as aliases wherever they are used: `troopCount`, `compCount`, `woundedN`, `mountedN`, `partySize`, `prisonerCount`, `partyCap`, `partyRoom`, `prisonCap`, `eatPerDay`, `vis`, `tm`, `treeSk`, `has`, `roleBonus`, `gm`, `gearSkill`, `skill`, `heroStats` (`core/state.js`), `maxSP`, `maxMP`, `vitals`, `regenVitals`, `restoreVitals` (`battle/combat.js`). Small pure helpers over any record stay functions (`tmOf`, `treeSkOf`, `bountyOf`, `chiefLabel`, `lordStats`, `lordSkill`, `lcapOf`).
- **Leaders** (`world/leaders.js`): the hero, lords, companions and bandit chieftains grow by the one rule above; `leaderOf(o)` reads any of them. Leaders fight at the head of their men and can be taken captive (`world/captives.js`).
- **Items** (`core/items.js`, class wave C; categories: doc/ITEMS_DESIGN.md): a bag holds plain records `{k, id, n}` (a letter's with its record `L`, a quest item's with `by`, what it is bound to), saved as they always were; `itemKind(rec)` gives its kind, one object per kind over the data tables: `Gear` (`ITEMS`), `Mount` (a `Gear` for the mount slot: `stockAt(t)`, `canBuyAt(s, t)`, `ground(s)`), `Quiver` (a `Gear` for the quiver slot, `QUIVERS`: `shots(gear, shoots)`, `shootsWith(w, s)`, `stockAt(t, place)`), `Good` (`GOODS`; a holding's old arrows and bolts are no item), `Trophy` (`MATS`), `Valuable` (`VALUABLES`), `QuestItem` (`QUEST_ITEMS`: `give(id, e)`, `take(id)`, `boundTo(e)`, `ended(e)`), `Letter` (`world/letters.js`). Each knows `def`, `name`, `describe`, its category (`catId`, `cat`: one of `ITEM_CATS`, `data/items.js`, its data's `cat` or its kind's), `stack(id)`, `worth`, `price` (what a market pays), `fetches` (the whole slot), `cost` (what you pay), `slot`, `stats`, `colour`, its words (`title`, `some`, `sold`), its order among its own kind (`compare`) and on a loot pile (`lootRank`, `lootLine`), what coming into your stores does (`gained`: a drink's morale), and its `verbs`: Choices read with the bag slot's index (gear: Equip, Off hand, Sell, Drop; trophies and valuables: Sell, Drop; goods Drop; Sell and Drop each shown only as the category allows, so a quest item has neither; a letter's page has its own, `pageVerbs`). Gear also has `keyOn(id)`, `damageType(id)`, `armourClass(id)`, `label`, `rank`. The bag screen, the trade window and the loot pile read the kinds; the old names stay as one-line aliases: `itemDef`, `stackOf`, `itemPrice`, `gearPrice`, `matPrice`, `stableStock`, `canBuyMount`, `targetKey`, `itemDt`, `itemArmor`, `lootRank`, `lootDesc` (the pure helpers `itemRank`, `kindLabel` stay functions). A bag sorts by category (in `ITEM_CATS`'s order), then as each kind sorts its own. Tests: `tests/inventory.js`, `tests/mounts.js`, `tests/loot.js`, `tests/uiprefs.js`, `tests/itemcats.js`, `tests/quivers.js`, `tests/valuables.js`, `tests/burden.js`.
- **Item categories** (`data/items.js` `ITEM_CATS`; doc/ITEMS_DESIGN.md): Weapon, Armour, Quiver, Mount, Food, Water (water: full waterskins, empty waterskins, ale, liquor), Resource, Trade good, Trophy, Valuable, Quest item, each with its rules: `bag` (it takes bag room), `route` (where it goes when you get it: the bag for now; the pools and the pack train come with logistics), `sell`, `drop`, `tab` (`BAG_TABS`: All, Equipment, Supplies, Goods, Trophies & valuables, Quest), `icon`, `col`. The bag's tabs, the market (its goods under their categories, then its quivers, then what you carry that it buys) and the loot screen read them.
- **Quivers** (`data/gear.js` `QUIVERS`, `ARROWS`, `QUIVER.none`): arrows and bolts are no item. A quiver is gear in its own slot (`q_*`, unworn a bag slot), named with its arrows ("Hunter’s quiver · 30 bronze arrows"); your hero (`battle/combat.js` `heroShots`, `heroShotsLeft`, `heroSpendShots`, kept on her soldier's `u.ammo`) and the characters (their battle stats' `shots`) shoot as many as it holds, full at every battle's start; a bow with none, 5. Troops keep their kinds' quivers (`attacks.js` `AMMO`, `quiverOf`). Markets sell the worn and hunter's, smiths the soldier's, a city's smith the master's, a people's holdings their own.
- **Quest items** (`QuestItem`, `QUEST_ITEMS`): bound to the quest or quest chain that gave them (a quest chain an encounter of it began), neither sold nor dropped, a bag slot each and never refused; given and taken by the outcome `item: {give, take}` (`story/outcome.js`), gone on the bus's `quest:ended` or `quest chain:ended`. The sealed letter is one.
- **Valuables** (`Valuable`, `VALUABLES`, `VALUE`, `VALUE_FINDS`): 5 to a slot, sold at any market by your Trading, a city paying best; found in caves' and ruins' chests (`world/sites.js` `siteChest`, `valuablesFound`), bandit camps and the purses of the lords and chieftains you beat (`world/captives.js` `spoilsWon`, from `battle/resolution.js`), drawn from the stream `valuables`.
- **Realms** (`world/realms.js`, class wave C): `realmOf(fac)` gives the `Realm` over the plain saved maps (nothing of it is saved): its wars and peace (`relWith(g)`, `atWar(g)`, `enemies()`, `setRel(g, s, chain)`, `dayWith(g)`, `fall()`, `fallen`; `Realm.day()` is the day's diplomacy, run first by `worldDay`), how it sees you (`honor`, `addHonor(n, quiet)`, `hostility` (the `Timed` view of `P.hostile`), `hostile()`, `provoke(days, honour)`: turned hostile by an attack, `wronged(t, c, cost)`: a deed's realm part, from `conduct.js` `wrong`), its holdings and lords (`fiefs()`, `lords()`, `races()`, `army(n)`). Your own banner is a `PlayerRealm` (at war with whoever is hostile to you). The lords' own feelings are `Relations` (`world/relations.js`: `withYou`, `change`, `ripple`, `between`, `shift`, `friends`, `rivals`, `helps`, `spares`, `day`). The old names stay as aliases: `relOf`, `atWar`, `enemiesOf`, `fiefsOf`, `lordsOf` (new), `hostileTo`, `honorWith`, `addHonor`, `setRel`, `fallFaction`, `facRaces`, `armyOf`; `relWith`, `changeRel`, `rippleRel`, `lordRel`, `changeLordRel`, `lordFriends`, `lordRivals`, `lordHelps`, `lordSpares`, `relDay`. Tests: `tests/relations.js`, `tests/reputation.js`, `tests/factions.js`, `tests/conduct.js`.

## State and inventory

Inventory and equipment rules live outside the screens. What each bag record is comes from its item kind (`core/items.js`, above). `Inventory` operates on a record's current bag, with capacity and stacking supplied to it. Nothing is refused up to its limit (doc/ITEMS_DESIGN.md §4a): what does not fit goes into overflow slots past its `size`, up to `BAG.maxOver` (15), each costing `BAG.per` (4%) of your pace, down to `BAG.floor` (×0.4): `burden()`, read by `bandPace` (`bagPace`); past that `BAG_FULL` ("You cannot carry any more"), a quest item excepted (`add(k, id, n, force)`). `over`, `spare`, `settle()` (the overflow moved into slots freed since), `put(rec, force)`; the card's Burdened tag (`burdenTag`, `render.js updateHUD`); the bag window shows the overflow slots in red. It carries only what its category routes to the bag; `gained` tells an item's kind that it came in. `EquipmentService` operates on a wearer's gear and an `Inventory`, returning `{ok, reason, moved}` without touching the DOM or logging. Hero and companion commands use the same service; companion slot limits remain explicit. A failed transfer restores both records, including when removing a pack or mount would shrink a full bag. Equipment transfers always return existing items to the bag; only rewards use the loot pile.

`GameSession` resets the live `G`, `P`, and `W` records in place from factories. New game and load share this reset, so missing save fields use defaults and transient input, loot and battle state cannot carry over. Record identity and the player's `Party` prototype survive; saves still contain plain JSON. `session.js` loads after `world/realms.js`, which creates `W`; its commands run after boot has loaded every system. A new game (`begin`) and a load (`loadGame`) run as steps (`runSteps`, `ui/loading.js`): the overlay shows only past `LOADING.delay` (300 ms), `G.mode` is `'loading'` meanwhile (no simulation), and `LOADING.sync` (the tests) runs them at once. The world's part of a save is gathered by `worldSave`/`worldLoad` (`core/save.js`), each holding writing its own record (`saveRec`, read back by `loadRec`; the same tuple as before); a holding founded while the world ran also keeps its plain data, so a load makes it again (`Holding.fromRec`).

## Management windows

The field book (doc/FIELDBOOK_UI.md) brings the main menu's dark cloth, brass, native pixel art and parchment into Character, Party, Quests, Inventory and Skills. Army shows actual Fitness/Morale/Stamina, ordered troop cards, per-group wounded counts and a condition page. Companions use a portrait collection with Overview, Equipment, Skills and Orders. Inventory filters the bag, keeps all ten ring targets separate and pins item comparisons/actions. Quests show their objective, progress, timing and reward tiles; full facts remain in Details. Skills has My path, Paths, Fit and List, an explicit Learn action and ten skill-slot buttons.

The menus (the owner, 2026-10-23): one Game Menu (ui/hub.js; doc/FIELDBOOK_UI.md "The Game Menu"), opened by Esc with no window open, Tab or the bar's one Menu button, the game paused while it is open (in battle too). Its header: Paused, the day, the world and its seed (Copy seed, World). A tile per window, its icon, name and key: Army (C: the Army window), Inventory (I), Quests (Q), Journal (J), Map (M) and Camp (Z); in battle all are greyed, in the streets those that need the map. A window adds its tile with `defineMenuEntry`. Then Save game, Load game, Settings (the title's page), Main menu and Exit game; the last two ask first when play has moved on since the last save ("Unsaved progress since day N will be lost.": Save first, Leave anyway, Cancel). The bar's other buttons stay as shortcuts. Quests is a window of its own (Q: Main, Side, Jobs, Archive). The Journal (J, its last section; L, the Chronicle; ui/journal.js) has Chronicle, Lords and Realms; a section is one `defineJournalSection`. Lords and Realms reveal only discovered entries, including their counts, friends, holdings and realm relations. Unknown lord names display as ??? on map tooltips and court labels, streets, hall cards, People entries and profiles, following labels, fight previews, and unbought intelligence or war-work offers. Meeting, chosen information, heard news or accepting named work reveals the real name; the canonical name in saves and quest matching is unchanged. The Journal continues to omit undiscovered lords. A meeting identifies only its speaker. Merely seeing an army or visiting a holding does not identify its lord; sightings update Last known reports only for lords already discovered. A lord’s Ask for rumours and Ask about other lords are deliberate dialogue choices; opening, refreshing, gifts and troop requests reveal no extra names and consume no rumours. Social answers identify only the friends and rivals named, with whereabouts unknown. Other discoveries come from news and information actually heard; recording remote news or dispatching its rider reveals nothing. Locations say Last known with the event day (and hearing day if later): sightings, rumours, proclamations, arriving riders and bought information update the stored report, never an unseen army’s live position or size. Older news cannot replace a newer sighting. Realm holding lists keep last known ownership until seen again or a capture is heard; global treasury and territory totals are omitted. The optional save piece known defaults empty, and a new campaign resets it. Tests: lordnames, journaldiscovery, lorddialogue. The Chronicle (ui/chronicle.js) replaced the Message log: lasting events, kept for good and saved with the game (the save piece `chronicle`: [day, m, cls, kind, ref] each; past CHRONICLE.budget, 1.5 M characters, the oldest band and plain messages go first, said in the console only), newest first under day headers, one page of CHRONICLE.page (100) at a time; filters All, War & battles, Rumours, Your band, Story & quests (`log(m, cls, kind, ref)`); a quest's message opens it in Quests (Back to Chronicle), a rumour's shows its "?" on the full map. "Welcome back" is a toast only (`toast`). Tests: chronicle, journal, menu; screenshots: visual/chronshots (shot=all|war|hub|dock|size), visual/gamemenu (shot=menu|battle|settings). Back to the Game Menu (the owner, 2026-10-23): the top page of each window it opens (Army, Inventory, Quests, the Journal, the full map), however it was opened, has Back on its bar's left, to the Game Menu (`fbMenu(back)`, ui/fieldbook.js: the window closed by its way out, then `showEsc`); a sub-page keeps its own Back to its parent; Esc follows the bar, page by page, then the Game Menu, and one more Esc resumes. Tests: fieldbook ("Back to the Game Menu"), army, journal, menu.

The Army window (the owner's design, 2026-10-23; ui/partypage.js; doc/FIELDBOOK_UI.md "The Army window"): C, the Menu's Army and the bar's button open it; it replaced the Character window (Hero, Party) and the separate Skills window. Its header, on every tab, is the company muster (`partyHead`: the band's name, groups, fit and wounded, Fitness, Morale and Stamina; to read, not a link; on 844×390 one row, the bars % chips). Its five tabs are its top level, each its bar's Back to the Game Menu: Roster, March formation, Army skills, Condition, Passengers. Roster: one list of cards in battle order, your hero, your companions (those leading their own party under Away) and your troop groups, each its kind's icon (`rosterhero` crown, `rostercomp` star, `rostertroop` shield: ui/icons.js) and its experience bar (to the next level; a troop type's training, or Top tier). Every card opens the same troop-style inspector beside the cards (on a narrow phone, a page with Back): compact portrait and identity, Manage group / Reinforce / Development, Group counts, Combat, Condition and Progress. Fields that do not apply stay dimmed with dashes and reasons; real zeros remain visible. Development opens the character's tree (K for your hero) or the troop training path. Character details, Equipment, Company role and orders, and Unit details expand below, replacing the separate companion tabs. Hero equipment opens Inventory with Back to Army; companion equipment, roles, reviewed transfers and dismissal keep their commands and Cancel/Apply pages; troops show their fixed loadout. Tests: army, unitinspector. Army skills: the band's skills (your hero's: trained, a Scout's or a Surgeon's role bonus, gear, tree; `bandSkillFrom`), each its value, who gives it and what it does; the four roles, each Choose (one holder, Cancel/Apply). Condition: one scrolling area, Supplies in it; every Army page has one bottom bar (doc/FIELDBOOK_UI.md "One bottom bar a window"). Passengers: escorts, prisoners (Recruit, Release) and captives (Ransom, Recruit, Let go). Tests: army; screenshots: visual/fieldbook (scene=army|hero|companion|compskills|orders|troop|path|armyskills|condition|passengers|…, &w=&h= the screen's size).

**The unit card** (the owner, 2026-10-28: as Total War: Warhammer III's; ui/unitcard.js `UnitCard`): picture, name, class, damage type and armour; Manage Group and Development under the name; a group's strength (fit of its ten, hurt) and its training or experience; then only the numbers the unit has (health, armour by its value, morale, speed, melee attack, reach or missile attack, range and ammunition, charge bonus, vigor), each a bar against the best troop, now against its own (a red end: what lowers it, a green one: what raises it); its traits and skills as chips, strong and weak against, its upkeep (the wage rule in its tip). A hero or companion has Character, Equipment (only what is worn) and, for a companion, Role as tabs under it. In battle, a group's portrait in its panel opens its card with its men left and its morale and tiredness now.
Growth on the card (2026-11-01; the owner: "the unit detail should show which stat they grow from the original"): in the Army window each number is drawn from its original, the gain since in pale gold (`.gr`), and its state now over that (red, striped where it covers growth; green). `statGrowth(x)` (ui/unitcard.js) works it out for a troop group (its kind's own `ST` numbers, then your talents `tHp`/`tDmg`, its rank's proficiency and ladder (Long reach, Marksmen and their mastery), its upgrades `Buffs`) or a character (the one formula on its record stripped to its kind at its level, then its attributes, skills, talents and gear in turn, then its upgrades), as `{s, stats: {id: {base, now, parts}}}`. Each bar's tip names the sources and amounts ("original 16: Long reach +1.6"), and a "Grown by" line under the bars names them for a phone. The barracks' kind cards and the battle's panel show none. Tests: unitgrowth.

These screens use touch tabs, cards, full choice pages and quantity steppers instead of dropdowns. Choice pages commit only with Apply; army transfers still Review and revalidate at Confirm. Content scrolls inside the frame while actions and the existing navigation bar remain on screen. Portrait Army/Quests open a detail page with Back; short landscape screens share one row for title and primary tabs. Hero and companion skill cameras remain independent through resizing. Test sizes: 1600×900, 844×390, 390×844 and 320×568. Targeted checks: fieldbook, questwindow, inventory, equipment, companions, grouptransfers, talents, talentrules and armymanagement. Screenshot fixture: visual/fieldbook, with v=review and scene=army|condition|companion|orders|inventory|equipment|skills|journal.

## The band card and the names

The card at the top left (`gfx/render.js` `updateHUD`; the owner, 2026-10-23) is the band's: its name, big (a button: the
Names window), your hero's name (else your race and past), level and rank, then three bars in %: **Fitness**
(`world/supply.js` `fitnessOf(fit, wounded, hp)` = (fit + hp) / (fit + wounded + 1) × 100, a wounded man 0, your hero her share of
her health; `bandFitness()` yours, with its parts; red under 50%), **Morale** (`P.morale`; it left the chips) and **Stamina**
(`P.bsta`). Names (`ui/names.js`, `data/names.js`): typed on the New game page (each with Random: a folk name of your race with a
byname; a band name from `BAND_FORMS` and `BAND_WORDS`), at most `NAMING.max` (24) characters, trimmed; renamed from the card. Kept
as `P.name` and `P.band` only once chosen (none stored otherwise, so the W7 save is unchanged); put into HTML through `htmlText`.

## Gender and pronouns (2026-11-01)

Every named person is a he or a she (`core/pronouns.js`; the owner: "the people in the world should have gender").
`genderOf(o)` gives `'m'`, `'f'`, or null for no one or a group, from the first that answers, nothing drawn and nothing stored:
the record's own `gender` (the companions in `COMPS`, `data/progression.js` and Ander in `data/jobchains.js`), a realm's named lords
(`FACS[f].gender`, `data/factions.js`, by their portraits: Borka, Gimra, Ulgra, Neri, Mirelle, Maren, Rowena and Ithriel women), a
gendered title (`TITLE_GENDER`: Lady, Lord, Thane, Clanmother, Warchief, Warmother, Chief, Chieftess, Brother, Sister; Ser, Warden,
Admiral and Captain say nothing), a known given name (`GIVEN_GENDER`, filled by `givenNames` where the lists live: the folk names,
`world/folk.js` `FOLK_WOMEN`, so workers, trainers, sellswords and watch captains; the chiefs, `world/leaders.js`; the wounded
travellers, `story/wounded.js`), else a hash of the name past a neutral title (`genderHash`). The generator draws a lord's title as
ever, then swaps in a woman's form where the syllables hash to a woman's (`world/worldplace.js` `LORD_SHE`: Thane to Clanmother,
Warchief to Warmother, Chief to Chieftess). `pr(o)`: `{he, him, his, hers, himself}` and capitalised (they for null);
`lordWord`, `sirWord`, `manWord`, `genderWord('wife', o)`. `prFill(text, o)`: a text's `{he}` `{His}` `{lord}` tokens for o and
`{you.sir}` and the like for your hero; dialogue lines take them (`story/conversation.js` `Speaker.fill`: the speaker's), story texts
`{lord.his}` (`story/forms.js`: the person a memory field names). A choice's label and tip may be functions of its subject
(`story/choice.js`). Your hero: Man or Woman on the New game page (`ui/names.js`), kept as `P.gender` only for a woman (a save
without it is a man's, so W7 holds), shown on the hero's card in the Army window. Tests: `tests/pronouns.js`.

## Supplies

Travel is not free (doc/SUPPLY_DESIGN.md; `world/supply.js`, every number in `SUPPLY`). Your band's stamina (`P.bsta`, 0-100) is your
hero's: it is kept in her `P.sp`, on her scale (`maxSP`; when that grows, by a level, the share dips a little). Each hour (the system
`supply`, `Systems.hour`) the band:
- **eats** `eatPerDay() / 24`, ×1.5 on the march (more in the frost), fractions carried over; with no food it rests no more, loses
  2 morale an hour, and after a day men desert or fall sick by turns;
- **drinks** one of a drink a person a day (the owner, 2026-10-24: `SUPPLY.drink.perDay`; 20 men and your hero, 21 a day) on the
  march, half that stopped: water, then ale, then liquor (liquor first if you choose it), all of the category Water. Water and
  liquor are goods in the bag (`data/places.js` `DRINK_GOODS`) that no market trades: kept out of `GOODS`'s keys, so no holding
  stocks them. Water is carried in **waterskins** (`CONTAINERS`: today one kind; a full one is the good `water`, an empty one
  `skin_empty`): each water drunk leaves an empty skin, and a fill fills only empty ones (none: "No empty waterskins"). A new hero
  sets out with 4 full (`SUPPLY.skins`), each recruit brings 1 empty (`core/recruitment.js` → `skinsJoin`), markets (under Water)
  and the tapster sell empty ones at 2g (`skinsBuy`). Empty waterskins fill free at the water's edge (a drawn water pixel within half a tile: `waterEdge`) or at a
  holding's well (1.5 tiles of where you arrive or set out: `wellNear`), over game time (the owner, 2026-10-23: `SKINS`, a
  `SkinFill`; 15 game minutes a man's day of drink, 5 to 180 minutes, `SUPPLY.fill`), by the game clock (paused, it waits; faster
  at 2× and 4×), the water coming in as it runs; any order, a battle or an encounter stops it and keeps what is in. It starts
  by itself at a well or stopped at the edge; a bar on the card and a drop over your band show it. The tavern's tapster fills
  your empty skins at once and sells empty skins and liquor (`tapsterCard`: 2 liquor for 112g);
- **tires or rests**: −3 an hour on the march (×1.5 rough ground, ×1.3 at night, ×1.5 Forced March, ×1.5 with nothing to drink,
  ×2 in the desert with no water, up to ×1.5 short of furs in the frost; ×0.7 mostly mounted, −3% a level of Athletics or Riding,
  ×0.9 a Guide; every drink alike); +2 stopped, +8 in camp, +10 staying in a holding (half with nothing to drink; none with no
  food, nor in the desert with no water). `staminaRate(moving, drink, land)` says how much and why;
- **morale from drink** (doc/ITEMS_DESIGN.md §2a): ale and liquor lift it once, as they come into your stores (`drinkIn`,
  `SUPPLY.drink`: ale +2, liquor +6 each, a person's day of it; water none), not by the hour while drunk;
- under 20 is **Tired** (pace ×0.85: `bandPace`, morale −1 every 3 hours), under 5 **Exhausted** (pace ×0.6, a man falls sick every
  8 hours); the desert's thirst (after 12 hours) and frostbite (6 hours' worth of shortage) take men too, as wounded.
- **Your hero** (`SUPPLY.thirst.hero`, `SUPPLY.hunger.hero`, `heroRegen`, read by `parties.js regenVitals`): Parched or Hungry, her
  mana stops coming back; Dying of thirst, every thirst toll takes 10% of her health and mana; Starving, 5% of each every 6 hours;
  Starving or Dying of thirst, her health stops coming back. With no troops a toll that would make a man sick falls on her (10%).
  At 0 health off the battlefield (`heroHurt`, `heroDies`) **the game is over**: `ui.js showDeath` (what killed her and the day; Load
  last save, greyed with none, and Back to the title; Esc follows the bar); nothing runs on behind it (`G.dead`). A lost battle is not
  death. Tests: `tests/gameover.js`.
- **Time:** 1×, 2×, 4× and Travel fast's 8× (key 4, the bar's last button, a toggle) are on the top bar. (Hurry, key R, was removed
  2026-10-31; the Forced March perk stays.) Tests: `tests/timecontrols.js`.
- **By road** (key B, or the ring's By road; the owner, 2026-10-23: a toggle): a mode, `P.byRoad` (saved; absent = off). While on,
  every trip keeps to the roads and tracks (`world.js byRoadTo`: the router's road keeping, mild for you, `ROAD_GO.keep` 1.15: the shortest way, preferring the road; a caravan's `WAY.keep` is strict) when one is within
  `ROAD_GO.near` (8) tiles of you or the goal, decided once a trip (`G.byRoad`, never saved); otherwise it quietly goes the plain way.
  Under way (under the minimap) shows By road, with Off ("in column" while your army marches in one: Road travel by army type,
  below); the ring lights active modes (`active`: By road, Break camp, Water first, Stop following). Tests: `tests/roadband.js`,
  `tests/ring.js`, `tests/roadcolumn.js`.
- **Straight ways** (`world/router.js` `WAY_SMOOTH`; the owner, 2026-10-23: the way was a staircase): every walker that goes by the
  router (you, lords, bands, patrols, caravans, workers, refugees, companions' parties) walks each leg pulled straight
  (`waySmooth`): from where it is to the farthest tile of the leg the straight line reaches with every tile it crosses open and 2 px
  clear of any closed one (no corner cut), and costing by the same tiles' costs (off the roads × a road keeper's) no more than the
