  stretch it skips (3% more at most); a road keeper (a caravan, a worker, By road) never skips a stretch of road by a line that
  leaves it. A walker pushed off its way (a fight, a shove) finds it again from where it is when the line on is not clear.
  Legs over the land, a road's and the last stretch run on together up to 96 tiles (`wayStraight`); a crossing or a ferry is kept as it is. A way that keeps to a road, or goes round a wood, still does; By road and
  the caravans' strict keeping hold. About 0.1 ms a leg, never a frame. The drawn way shows it. `WAY_SMOOTH.on` false: every way as
  it was. Tests: `tests/straightways.js` (classic and Normal s1-s4: turns 5490 → 1716 over 200 trips, never more than 2.3% dearer).
- **Follow** (`world.js` `FOLLOW`; the owner, 2026-10-23): a click or a tap on another party (a lord, a band, a patrol, a caravan,
  refugees, a companion's party) opens its ring at it (`ui/input.js partyRing`, `supply.js partyRingItems`): Attack (hostile; crossed swords, `ICONS.attack`) or
  Meet (today's chase), Follow, Look; Esc or a press elsewhere closes it; a double click or a double tap approaches at once, as one
  click did. The hold ring has Follow on a party too. Following, your band trails it 2.5 tiles behind on its way (`followStep`:
  where it walked), standing when it comes within 1.5; never meeting it. It ends when the party leaves your sight, goes into a
  holding or a camp, or is gone; on any other order, camp, a meeting or a battle; or Stop following (lit on the ring). Under way
  (under the minimap) shows its name, with Stop; the footprints lead to it. An order: never saved. Tests: `tests/follow.js`, `tests/ring.js`,
  `tests/touch.js`, `tests/clicks.js`.
- **The road's band** (`world/roadband.js`; the owner, 2026-10-23: "my character stands on the road but the game still reads it as
  swamp"): a road's speed follows the road as drawn, not its tiles (a staircase on a diagonal). Within `ROAD_BAND.great[0]` (10) px
  of a great road's line (`track[0]` 8 of a track's) road speed 1.3 (humans ×1.1), blended to the land's over 6 px more, the land's
  beyond; an estate's track 1.15 (`trackSpeed`). Water and peaks stay closed, a ford keeps its pace, a road's own tile its road's.
  The same for everyone who moves by `speedAt` (you, lords, bands, patrols, caravans, refugees, beasts); not rough ground for riders
  (`roughFor`); `regionAt` names the road (Road, Causeway…, Track) there, `regionAt(x, y, true)` the tile's own. A byte per 4 px
  square, kept only for the tiles a band reaches (about 430 KB on the classic world), made again when the ways change (`WAYV`).
  Tests: `tests/roadband.js`.
The band's own record (its camp and the hour's counters) is `BAND`, beside `P` (every battle snapshot holds `P` whole), saved as
the world piece `band` once it is not fresh. The band's own numbers are `PlayerParty`'s: `staminaState`, `staminaPace`,
`foodHour`, `drinkHour`, `furNeed`, `fitness`.

- **Into battle** (`battle/fatigue.js`): your troops, companions and hero start at the band's stamina (never under
  `SUPPLY.battleMin` 25; at full, as ever); when the field is done (`fieldDone`) the band's stamina is the average your side still
  standing ended with. Not in a tournament or the lab.
- **Make camp** (Z, the dock's Camp, the action ring): stamina +8 an hour, the wounded mend ×1.5, food and drink at the rest rate;
  any travel order breaks it; faster than 1x, anything hostile in sight brings the speed back to 1x; a band that finds you in camp
  catches you unready (morale −8). Only on allowed ground (`campSpot`, `campGround`, numbers in `SUPPLY.camp`): the tents and 2 px
  round them on no water (ponds, oases), ford, peak, wood, swamp, road or track (its tiles and its band as painted: the art's kept-clear
  mask), crossing, brook, lot or field, holding's ring (centre and houses, a tile round) or site, nothing standing there
  (`WorldArt.standing`); where you stand if it is, else the nearest you can reach within 12 tiles (6 before 2026-10-30; the woods' glades, `GEN_GLADE`, give it ground: deep in a wood a band now finds a camp about 7 times in 10, before 1 in 9) (water between counts 4 tiles more):
  within 2 tiles the band steps there and pitches at once, farther it walks there and pitches (`G.pitch`, `campArrive`); none near:
  "No clear ground to camp here". The tents (`bandCampEnt`) are drawn by their feet among the trees, those in front drawn whole over
  them, your banner planted beside them; your figure and host are hidden while you camp.
- **The action ring** (`ui/ring.js`): hold the left button or a finger still on the map 0.35 s. A press does nothing yet (the owner,
  2026-10-22, mouse and touch alike): let go sooner and it is a click, travelling there or meeting what was clicked (on another party,
  its own ring: Follow above); a move first calls the ring off and the release still travels; a right-click travels (or chases) at
  once. Its buttons, only those that apply (`mapRingItems`): Make camp or Break camp, Follow (Stop following), Travel here, By road,
  Fill waterskins, Drink liquor, Look (a card, for touch).
  A gold circle fills clockwise at the pointer during a hold, after 90 ms (18 CSS px radius for mouse/pen, 34 for touch), reaching full at 350 ms. It stays the same size across zoom and display scales; quick clicks show nothing. Release, movement, pointer cancellation, leaving the canvas with a mouse/pen or losing focus removes it. Rendering: ring.js drawMapHold, called after the frame; tests: holdprogress and visual/holdprogress.
  Let go over one, or let go and tap one; Esc, a right-click or a tap outside closes it. So does anything else opened (2026-10-31; the
  owner: it stayed open under other menus): every window (`ui.js` `showPanel` closes it: the Army, the full map, the Game Menu and
  their keys), a press on the HUD outside it, and, by the next frame, the screen it opened on gone or under a window. Tests: `tests/ringclose.js`.
- **The card** (`render.js updateHUD`, `supplyNow`): the band's stamina bar (green, amber Tired, red Exhausted, ▼ ▲, its rate and
  why on hover) and health (fit of the whole band, red under half, what hurt it lately on hover), the tags (Tired, Exhausted,
  Thirsty, Hungry, Cold, In camp), a thin Filling waterskins bar in % while they fill, food and drink in days at the
  current rate, furs in or near the frost. Your hero's health and
  mana are in the Hero window (C). Tests: `tests/supply.js`, `tests/camp.js`, `tests/ring.js`.

## Recruitment and talents

**Ranks** (2026-10-30, doc/RANKS_DESIGN.md phase 1; the owner: "each level a rank with a badge, capped at 5, bronze, silver, gold for each tier"; "once you have max rank you can upgrade to higher tier units"): experience and rank belong to each troop group (`core/ranks.js`: a group's `xp` and `rank`, 1 to `RANKS.max`; its next rank's need by its tier, `data/ranks.js` `RANKS.xp`). A battle gives each group its men's average experience (`attacks.js` `u.xp`) and `TROOP_XP.fight` and `.win`, Leadership 5% more a level (`battle/resolution.js` `groupRanks`; auto-resolved: `groupRanksAuto`); a group that ranks up says so in the messages and the report. The rank stays whoever joins (Total War): refilled, split (both halves) or merged (the higher), and its men all wounded, their record keeps it till they come back. Only at rank 5 a group upgrades, as a whole, keeping its place, and starts again at rank 1 (`RecruitmentService#quoteGroupTraining`/`trainGroup`, gated by `rankReady`): below tier 3 and not into a caster, free and anywhere (the Army window's Rank and path: Upgrade to …); tier 3 and casters at a holding that trains, for `trainCost` a man (the Barracks' Upgrade tab). Peasants have no rank: `RANKS.peasant` experience to their first upgrade. The badge (`ui/rankbadge.js`): a pixel shield, steel (bronze, silver, gold with mastery, below; iron for peasants), a chevron a rank; on the Army window's cards, its Rank and path, the battle's unit cards, the March formation's buttons and the Barracks. The old pooled experience by kind (`P.txp`, Train 1 / Train all) is gone. Tests: ranks, recruitment, grouprecruitment, army, rosters, factions, uiprefs.

(Since unit kits step 2, 2026-11-01: ranks give stats only, mastery has five levels with a pick each from the kind's menu; the rank ladders and three metals below are step 1's, kept behind `RANKS.statsOnly` and `RANKS.mastery5`: see **Unit kits** below.)

**Rank skills, Spear and Bow** (2026-10-30, doc/RANKS_DESIGN.md phase 2; the owner: "each rank has certain skills, passive or active, limited to their proficiency and play style"; today's abilities "moved to the ladder for unlocking"): `data/kits.js` `KITS` (was `data/ranks.js` `RANK_SKILLS`, its old name), a kind's five rungs, rank 1 to 5; `battle/rankskills.js` reads them through its kit (`battle/unitkit.js` `UnitKit`). Rank 1 is the kind's own trait (`traits` in `RANK_SKILLS`: the spear's Set spears, the trait once called Brace; the bow's Longshot or, an elf's, Elven aim), shown on the ladder and not again with its traits, the rungs above building on it (the owner: skills and traits "not overlapping", "relevant to each other"). Spear: Set spears, Brace (rank 2: Set spears on the flanks while it lasts), Long reach (3: +10% reach), Rally (4), Hedge of spears (5: braced from the flanks too, Brace or not). Bow: Longshot (or Elven aim), Fire arrows (2), Steady aim (3: shots 10% truer), Rapid shot (4: a new skill, the skill bar's slot 2 (X): 30% faster shots for 8 s; the AI's captains use it whenever a mark is in reach), Marksmen (5: +10% range). An ability on a role's ladder only from its rung (`rankAllows`, in `groupHasAbility`); one another ladder holds and its own does not, none (bowmen have no Rally); one on no ladder yet (Spur, Scout ahead) and a role not on one (swordsmen, crossbowmen, riders…), as before. A battle group's rank: yours from its roster group (`battleRank`), an AI army's or a lab's `RANKS.ai` (2) till AI armies rank (phase 5). Passives on stats (reach, range) on each soldier once (`rankUnit`, each battle step), the rest read where they act (`rankSkill`: aim in `aimErr`, the hedge in `spearBraced`). The Army window's Rank and path lists a role's rungs, each lit once reached. Tests: rankskills, unitabilities.

**Proficiency and mastery** (2026-10-30; the owner: "each rank should also increase unit proficiency a bit", "1% per rank"; "the bronze, silver, gold badge means mastery: once your xp is at max and you do not or cannot upgrade, you gain mastery xp"; each mastery "may select which skill to improve its effect"; upgraded, "reset, but converted to exp for the new unit"): each rank above the first gives a group `RANKS.prof` (1%) more damage, health and aim (`rankProf`; on each soldier once, on the health he has (a lab's own too), `battle/rankskills.js` `rankUnit`, `rankAim`; an AI army's at `RANKS.ai`). At rank 5 a group's experience is mastery's (`mxp`): bronze, silver, gold at `RANKS.mastery.xp` (300, 700, 1200 more). The badge is steel with its rank's chevrons till it masters, then bronze, silver, gold (the metal no longer the tier). Each mastery is a pick in the Army window's Rank and path (`masteryOpts`, `masteryPick`, `picks`): a reached skill of its ladder made better (its `up` in `RANK_SKILLS`: Brace +3 s, Long reach +5%, Rally +5, Fire arrows +5%, Steady aim 5% truer, Rapid shot +2 s, Marksmen +5%; `rankUp`, `rankDur`), or a drill (`RANKS.drill`: one more step of proficiency); a role not on a ladder has the drill. Upgraded, a group starts at rank 1 again, its mastery and picks gone, `RANKS.mastery.carry` (half) of its mastery's experience its new kind's (`ArmyRoster#upgrade`). Tests: mastery, ranks, rankskills.

**Trainers** (2026-10-30, doc/RANKS_DESIGN.md phase 3; the owner: "like Skyrim when some NPCs can teach you"; "about 5 times a week" for the whole army; a trainer expert in some kinds of unit, his level capping the training; yours in your camp, otherwise gold at a holding; "the player may need this trainer to upgrade some specific units"): `world/trainers.js`, its numbers `data/trainers.js` `TRAINERS`. A trainer: his roles (spear, infantry, bow, crossbow, caster, cavalry, horse archer: `trainRole`), his level (journeyman, expert, master: he trains a group to rank 2, 4 or 5 at most), a lesson's flat experience (15, 25, 35), `TRAINERS.week` (5) lessons a week. A holding that trains keeps a few (`trainersAt`, `W.trainers`, made the first time asked from the 'trainers' stream, their roles from the troops it raises, their level by its kind; saved), each lesson for gold (20, 40, 70 times the group's tier); yours, free and only in your camp: your hero with Drillmaster (`TRAINERS.hero` roles, his level by Leadership) and a companion as Trainer (a new company role, `ROLES.trainer`; his roles by his kind, his level by his). A special upgrade (to tier 3, or into a caster) needs an expert or more of its role (`upgradeTrainer`: the holding's, for `trainCost` a man; yours in camp, free), in place of any holding that trains. The Barracks' tab is Train & upgrade (its trainers, a lesson button for each of your groups of theirs: `trainerRows`); the Army window's Rank and path, your own trainers' lessons. Tests: trainers, factions.

**Wandering trainers** (2026-10-30, doc/RANKS_DESIGN.md phase 4; the owner: "some may stay at a fixed place, some may travel around holdings or places, so it will work with news and rumours"): `world/trainers.js` `wanderers`, made on the first day (`TRAINERS.wander.levels`: five, two of them masters), each a few days (`TRAINERS.wander.stay`) at a holding that trains, then on to the next of the nearer ones, known beforehand (`trainersDay`, a day system after quests). While there he is one of its trainers (the Barracks: "passing through, till Day n"). Each move is news (`news.js` kind `trainer`, heard anywhere: where he teaches, where next). Those you meet at a Barracks or hear of in a tavern are kept in `P.trainersMet` (`trainerMeet`, `trainerHeard`) and listed in the Journal's new Trainers section (`ui/trainerlist.js`). Saved with the holdings' trainers. Tests: wandering, news, journal.

**AI armies' ranks** (2026-10-30, doc/RANKS_DESIGN.md phase 5; the owner: "they should have ranks too and should grow realistically"): `world/armyranks.js`, its numbers `data/ranks.js` `RANKS.aiStart`, `aiFight`, `aiDrill`. Every group of an army on the map ranks on its own record. Its first rank is set when first asked (`aiRankInit`, from the 'army ranks' stream): a band's 1 to 2 (its chieftain's own group one more), a patrol's 1 to 3, a caravan's 1 to 2, a lord's 1 up to 1 + his level ÷ 4 (at most 4). It grows by what it lives through: a battle with you, its men's average experience and the fight's, the win's when its side held the field (`aiBattleRanks`); a fight on the map, or one auto-resolved, 14 won or 6 lost (`aiFightRanks`, `fights.js` `endFight`); a lord at rest in a holding drills, 6 a day up to rank 4, and where it trains his rank-5 groups upgrade into the first of their path (`aiRanksDay`, a day system). In battle an AI group's rank, ladder and proficiency are its own (`battle/rankskills.js` `battleRec`); a holding's walls and the lab keep `RANKS.ai`. A party's Look card says how seasoned it is (`rankTip`). Tests: armyranks, rankskills, mastery.

**Volunteers** (2026-10-30; the owner: free recruits "if you have a good reputation and renown", "at holdings, or when you set up camp near holdings", "replenish your army until max and should affect population as well", hiring staying "like an instant hire"; "within territory", "tier 1 only or peasants", "refill existing groups only"): `world/volunteers.js`, its numbers `VOLUNTEERS`. Staying in a holding, or camped on its lands (`landHold`), with standing there of 20 or more and its realm not hostile, the Under way panel offers Recruit. Each hour some join, free: 0.5 an hour at standing 20 up to 1 at 80, up to half again more with renown (`volunteerRate`), each out of the holding's willing (`t.vol`) and its people (`t.pop`). They only refill your groups of a tier-1 or peasant kind it raises (and you may hire there: `canHire`), never a new group; it stops when none has room (or your band is full), when you leave its lands or break camp. Under way: "Recruiting · X · +n" with its own icon (`ICONS.recruit`, a raised banner and a plus, pulsing while it runs; on the camp's line too), Stop. Tests: volunteers. Always shown (2026-10-31; the owner: "I don't see the free recruitment options"): the barracks' Hire tab opens with a Free volunteers line (take them, stop, or why not: stay here or camp on its lands, too little standing, no group to refill), and Under way shows its Volunteers row whenever you camp or stay at a holding, its Recruit greyed with why in its tooltip.

**Houses** (2026-10-31; the owner: "make buildings like houses actually clickable and inspectable like estate buildings, instead of pointing to the holding"): `world/houses.js` `House`, one world object per house lot (kept aside, never saved), picked on its building in its holding's order of lots (`HoldingObject` pick, `housePick`), answering while you can see it (as every map object); its tip its own (its kind, its households, a quest that leads to it). Its page (`ui/house.js`, the page frame `PAGE_SCREEN`): its kind (cottage, townhouse, stone hall…), a picture of it on its ground, its households and about how many live there, its holding's people and homes, burnt or still being built, your quests that lead to it (`House#quests`: a step quest's house, `StepQuest.place`); a delivery's Deliver; Back to map (no Visit of its holding since 2026-11-04: the holding is its own place on the map). Tests: houses, clicks.

`RecruitmentService` owns troop hiring, training, tavern companion hiring, prisoner recruitment, prices and pooled troop XP. Its `quoteHire`, `quoteTraining`, `quoteCompanion` and `quotePrisoner` results drive the buttons and the commands. Commands recheck eligibility before spending anything. A recovering holding cannot recruit, training needs a barracks and a valid upgrade, troops must belong to the holding's roster, and a tavern companion must be available there. These checks also apply to direct action calls that bypass a disabled button. Existing price formulas and normal transfers are preserved.

**The level up's box** (2026-11-04; the owner: "a level up button at the bottom left corner ... like a square box and animated in blinking"): talent points to spend (`P.tp`) show as a blinking square at the bottom left, the left panel's own group (`#lgroup`, `gfx/render.js` `lvBox`), its count on it; a click opens your Skills. The party card no longer carries the badge. The messages move over beside it while it shows; on a phone it sits above the bottom bars and hides while the party card is open; never in battle. Test: `tests/levelbox.js`.

**Redraws that keep your taps** (the owner, 2026-10-28): a town's, holding's, estate's or site's window drawn again for the same place changes in place (`ui/window.js` `morph`: its text, attributes and rows; what is the same, kept: the button under a finger, its focus, the scroll, a field you type in), so a quick second tap is not lost to a redraw; another place is drawn afresh. The management windows (the army, the inventory, the march, the skills: `ui/fieldbook.js` `fbShow`) the same: `showPanel(html, key)`, the same window drawn again while shown, in place. What is redrawn on a clock (the party card and the day four times a second, the battle's unit panel and its Armies) is written only when it changes (`ui/ui.js` `setHtml`). Tests: redraw.

**Unit upgrades** (2026-10-31; the owner: the tech tree "tied to holdings, you have to visit the workshop/smith or relevant buildings for upgrades", "tied to unit group not individual unit (except group of 1)"): `data/buffs.js` `BUFFS` (empty till the tech tree: each its name, the building that offers it, `at`: smith, stable ...; what the holding must be, `need`; its gold a man, `cost`; the kinds it fits, `who`; its mods: health, damage, speed, reach, range and swing time as shares, armour and morale as points). `world/buffs.js` `Buffs`: each troop group keeps its own (`g.buffs`: which, and where bought), your hero and each companion theirs (`P.unitBuffs`); a building's window lists what it offers with a button for each of your groups it fits (`ui/town.js` `buffShop`: the smith and the stable for now), bought for the whole group at its price a man; refills pay the new men's share (the hiring desk); merged groups keep only what both had; an upgraded group keeps them, idle where they no longer fit its kind; in battle each soldier takes his group's (`buffUnit`, after his rank's); listed under each kind's traits in the Army window (Upgrades, where each came from). Tests: buffs. Only a purchase keeps a list on a group or on `P.unitBuffs` (`Buffs.rec(id, make)`): a look, as each battle's `buffUnit`, leaves none, so a band with no upgrades saves and fights as before (2026-11-01).

Any building sells the upgrades whose `at` is its id (2026-11-01; the owner: "dynamic enough so that I can just switch building type"): `ui/town.js` `Place#page` adds the shop under any place's own content, `Place#act` buys there, `placeActing` asks the place you are in first. So moving an upgrade (Fire arrows: `BUFFS.fireArrows.at`, the smith for now) is its `at` alone. Tests: `buffs`.

Three named women lords carry the female titles (2026-11-01): `data/factions.js` `LORD_RETITLE` (Thane Borka → Clanmother Borka, Thane Gimra → Clanmother Gimra, Warchief Ulgra → Warmother Ulgra), by `lordTitled` where lords are made (`realms.js` `addMissingLords`) and loaded (`core/save.js`); the list's name stays the key (portraits: `NamedPortraits.of` via `lordListed`, gender, an old save's lord). Empty the table and every name is the list's again.

**The troop tree** (2026-10-31; doc/TROOPS_DESIGN.md, the owner: "every tier after levy require enlist"): the humans' Peasant Levy (tier 0: every estate trade recruited as one kind, `peasantLevy`, a unit of 20) trains into Spearman, Bowman, Footmen or Crossbowman; Spearman into Pikeman or Lancer, Bowman into Longbowman or Mounted Archer, Footmen into Shield Men (then Shield Guard) or Squire (then Knight), Crossbowman into Arbalester (`data/units.js` `UP`). Every upgrade at a barracks for its price, never in the field (`core/recruitment.js` `quoteGroupTraining`: barracks; a tier-3 or caster one an expert trainer there too); into a mounted kind a horse a man (`horsesFor`: `ST[k].horse`, a riding horse, the Knight a war destrier; your spare ones first, the rest at that holding's stable at its price). An upgraded unit keeps what it learned (`core/ranks.js` `rankLadders`, `rankLearn`: its ladders' best rungs and mastery picks; `battle/rankskills.js` `rankRungs`; a foot drill, Brace or the hedge, off while it rides). **An upgrade moves only what fits** (the owner, 2026-11-01; `core/army.js` `ArmyRoster#upgrade`, `upgradeCount`, `upgradeSplits`): at most one full unit of the new kind (`unitSize`: foot 15, ranged 10, mounted 6). More men than that: that many go into a new unit beside the old one (its own rank, part of the mastery carried, the picks kept), the rest stay in the old unit with its rank, mastery and picks, to refill with Replenish; a split needs a free unit slot (the Barracks' button "No free unit slot"). Fewer: the whole unit converts, under-strength. The price and horses for those who go only (`quoteGroupTraining`: `count`, `stay`, `split`; the button "Train 6 of 15 into Lancers"). Lords the same (`world/realms.js` `lordRecruit`: a split with room, else not that kind); an AI army's rank-5 upgrade too (`world/armyranks.js`, through `ArmyRoster#upgrade`). A Battle Mage is no longer trained from archers: a rare hire at a large, prosperous holding (`world/holding.js` `MAGE_HIRE`, `mageHere`). Swordsman, Sword Sergeant and Greatsword are out of the tree. Tests: troopstree, upgradesplit.

**The dismount** (2026-10-31; doc/TROOPS_DESIGN.md step 4; `battle/dismount.js` `Horse`, `Dismount`; its numbers one table, `data/dismount.js` `DISMOUNT`):
- **Dismount** (J) and **Mount** (P) are unit abilities. Dismount: the riders step down where they stand, their horses left standing there, and stay on foot. Mount: each walks back to his own horse and mounts, enemies near or not.
- On foot a rider has his kind's stats on foot (`Dismount.footOf`: no charge, a man's pace by his armour), so a Lancer is a spearman again: Brace and the hedge of spears work (`rankRungs` reads him live). Remounted, his stats are the ones he stepped down with.
- **Thrown:** a charge, a hard blow (`physics.js` `physThrow`, `Dismount.throws`: harder than `DISMOUNT.seat` × his own weight) or braced spears (`physBraced`, `DISMOUNT.brace` × the momentum) unhorse a rider. He is down a moment ("Unhorsed"), then goes back up on his own unless an enemy is within `DISMOUNT.near`; then he fights on foot.
- **His mount, thrown** (2026-11-08; the owner: "horse or sheep should run away. Warg may fight back"; `Horse#loose`, `Horse.step` in `battle.js` `battleStep`; `DISMOUNT.temper`, `bolt`, `fight`): a horse, a destrier, a stag or a ram bolts away from the nearest enemy and stands 150-230 px off, where he walks to catch it. It runs to open ground only (`Horse.open`: a cell riders may enter, clear of rocks and trees; turned aside by `bolt.turn` when straight away is not), round rock, water and cliffs as a rider goes (`terrain.js` `steerDir` on the riders' grid), kept off them as every soldier is (`Horse#hold`), and within the field or a map battle's ring (`Horse.inside`); held up `bolt.stall` s, it stands. A wolf or a warg turns on the enemy nearest him (within `DISMOUNT.near`), biting (`fight.dmg`, every `fight.cd` s, his group's kill), and takes a share of each bitten man's blow back (`fight.back`); killed, it lies where it fought. With no enemy left near it comes back to his side and he mounts. Ordered down (Dismount), a mount stands quiet.
- **His horse** has its own health (`DISMOUNT.hp`, by its breed). A share of every hit on a mounted man lands on it (`Dismount.share` in `Fighter.wound`: missiles and spears more). Killed, it lies where it fell and he fights on foot to the end ("Horse down").
- A riderless horse stands saddled, a cloth of its side's colour under the saddle (`sprites.js` `horseSprite`, `people.js` `drawMount` `saddled`). It is kept in `B.horses`, never `B.units`, and drawn with the living (`render.js` `battleEnts`). A rider on foot is drawn without his mount (`unitSprite`'s `afoot`, `unitLook`).
- The group card shows a count while some are on foot ("4/6 mounted").
- **Your horses** belong to the group (`core/army.js` `groupHorses`, `horsesShort`; `g.horses`, kept only once it differs from one a man). After a fight the hurt ones come back and the dead are lost (`Dismount.settle`). Men without one start the next fight on foot (`Dismount.muster`). Men refilling a unit come without horses. A holding's stable sells them: **Horses for your riders** (`ui/town.js` `horseRows`; `horsesFor`: your spare horses first, then its price). Lords' armies keep no count: every fight they ride out whole, and every rule in battle holds for them.
- Set on a soldier only when it happens (`u.horse`, `u.afoot`, `u.remount`); `tests/harness.js` turns `DISMOUNT.on` off, so the older suites and fingerprints are as they were. Tests: dismount.

**The barracks** (`ui/town.js` `Barracks`, the owner 2026-10-28: casual recruiting; 2026-10-31: whole units and a section to replenish): two tabs. **Hire** (the hiring desk, `ui/hiredesk.js` `HireDesk`, the same at a travellers' camp: its **Sellswords** place, its men by name, the first in line hired; `world/sites.js` `TravellersCamp#deskOf`), over it your units in their slots and your men, the willing and your standing. **New units**: a row for each troop it raises (face, tier, role, its unit's men and price; i for its card, a row press only picks it), **Recruit unit** one whole unit of its kind's size into a free slot, greyed with why (no free slot, too few willing: they can only refill, gold, standing). **Replenish your units**: the free volunteers first, then each of your units of a kind it raises that is short (its bar, its wounded, the men it needs), **Refill** as many as the willing and your gold allow, **Refill all**. Each press asks once when it spends more than half your gold (`RECRUIT_UI.ask`). A camp's 2-4 sellswords are too few for a unit of their own: they refill yours. The picked one's card beside the list on a wide screen or over it on a small one (Back returns to the list where you were). **Train**: its trainers and a whole group's upgrade keeping its place. Tests: barracks, grouprecruitment, recruitment, townui, estateui, sites, unitcard, uiprefs.

`TalentService` owns tree connectivity, attribute effects, point spending, refunds, skill slots, finishing-style choices and ability modifiers. `talentsOf(record)` caches one service per live character record. The service receives its nodes, skill definitions, wallet, price and effect functions; its methods return outcomes without logging or touching the DOM. Hero and companion trees remain separate, while refunds retain their existing fee from the player's purse (`15 * P.level`). Learning fills the first free skill slot, and refunding an active clears every slot containing it. UI renderers do not initialize saved slot arrays.

The existing helper names (`takeNodeOf`, `refundNodeOf`, `canTakeOf`, `abilOf`, `hireCost`, `txpGain`, etc.) delegate to the services. The talent command helpers handle messages and hero vital refresh outside the service. Character AI and new-game setup can use those core helpers without depending on the Skills screen. The services hold record references rather than snapshots, so the session reset and save schema from step 1 continue to work.

## Troop operations

`TroopRoster` is the count-based component behind the existing `Party` classes, not a replacement for `Troop` (a unit definition) or another inheritance tree. `partyRoster(party)` follows that record's current `tc` field; the player still stores counts in `P.troops`. Adds, removals, casualty sampling/application and transfers share this implementation. Quantities must be positive safe integers, empty kinds are deleted, and transfers return the number actually moved. `partyTransfer`, `partyTransferAll` and `partyTransferAny` operate on plain or initialized parties, invalidate cached lists/power, and cannot move a roster into itself.

Transfer callers supply the receiving room: player capacity includes companions and wounded, companion parties use their own cap. Automatic companion disband/return intentionally retains the existing policy of bringing everyone back even over the player cap; it drains the old source so it cannot be replayed. Giving soldiers to an already distant companion remains allowed, as before; taking them back still requires proximity. XP stays in the hero's pooled-per-kind system, not a companion/garrison XP pool. Every player roster mutation clamps unusable XP, so stationing, donations, desertion and casualties follow the same rule; load also clamps stale saved XP.

`GarrisonService` owns stationing, local-guard call-ups and paid guard drafting. A holding still saves `gar` as a number: stationing intentionally turns any soldier into one generic defender, and calling one up gives its local melee unit. It does not preserve the stationed unit's type or XP. The last defender remains on the walls, normal capacity checks apply, and partial volunteer drafts are charged only for those actually hired. UI commands only dispatch and describe the result.

## Battle setup

`startBattle(opts)` remains the entry point. It creates a short-lived `BattleSetup` coordinator in `battle/setup.js`: spawn units and leaders, assemble command groups, create fresh battle state, initialize formations/morale/stamina/camera/deployment, then present the HUD. Unit definitions and character stats still belong to `Unit`/`Character`; group commands still belong to `BattleControl`. The coordinator does not enter saved state or introduce another unit inheritance tree.

Construction order matters. Spawning consumes the existing random sequence; a chief already among the enemies is promoted in place; field bounds are measured before obstacles are placed; formations precede deployment. The old global `B` is replaced only once the new state is fully constructed. Simulation remains in `battle.js`, player command routing in `commands.js`, and interactive deployment in `deploy.js`. Both HTML entry points load commands after `battlecontrol.js`, then setup and resolution; calls run only after all scripts have loaded.

`tests/battlesetup.js` compares nine fixed-seed snapshots captured before this extraction, including random-call counts and the subsequent random values. It covers ordinary fights, allied/enemy reinforcements, chief reuse, siege penalties, monsters, tournaments, deployment settings and field-width limits. Fixed geometry isolates structural parity; a separate check uses the actual HUD measurement.

Forward ranged groups keep `face` as their ordered heading and `currentFacing` as their simulation-time firing heading. Their at-rest shooter bodies follow `currentFacing`; slot movement does not steer their firing arcs. A hero's explicit ranged attack updates its group's ordered heading, while ordinary soldiers cannot rotate a group toward a rear target. Pausing and deployment freeze turns; deployment placement snaps to the order. The HUD shows one indicative envelope per group at its median inferred slot anchor, widened by ideal formation frontage. It uses the shortest range in a mixed-range group, while all-around groups retain a circle. The envelope is not a promise that every soldier can hit every point: each attack still checks that soldier's position, ammo, range, arc and obstructions, and hover counts use those actual checks. Formation slots and existing melee, shield-wall and wedge facing rules continue to use the ordered `face` in this stage.

`tests/formationfacing.js` covers simulation-time turns, angle wrap, pause/deployment behavior, isolated duplicate-group envelopes, grouped firing/arc agreement, rear-target discipline and mounted all-around coverage. `tests/visual/formationfacing.js` prepares two same-type archer groups at different headings and a scattered formation for visual review.

### Formation attack targets

An explicit attack selects an enemy formation by its battle group ID, not the lifetime of the clicked soldier. Duplicate troop types remain independent. Soldiers choose nearby opponents within the selected formation; ranged soldiers prefer members in their own range and current firing arc. Immediate close-range self-defence remains possible without replacing the group's order, and crowd alternatives stay within the selected formation. Character duels do not override an explicit formation focus.

The group keeps its attack after the originally clicked member dies. Explicit orders pursue routing or withdrawing members while they remain on the field. Once the entire target is dead, off-field or removed, the attackers Hold at their current position instead of automatically charging another formation; normal local defence/free fire still applies, and an AI captain may issue a fresh order. Ground movement, Hold, withdrawal and general Charge cancel explicit focus. Hero/companion formations follow the same identity rules; genuinely ungrouped actors can still be targeted individually.

`BattleControl.targetGroupId` and `targetUnitId` own the transient order identity. `targetMembers()` resolves living on-field enemies, and `tgt` is a derived representative retained for tactical AI callers, not the authoritative soldier target. Order lines and captain/hero aiming use the surviving formation's centre. Per-soldier range, arc, ammo, projectile collision and morale rules remain unchanged. These battle-only fields do not alter campaign saves. Focused coverage: `tests/grouptargeting.js`; `tests/visual/grouptargeting.js` previews an attack after the clicked soldier has fallen.

## Battle resolution

`battle/resolution.js` owns the short-lived `BattleResolution` coordinator. `finishBattle()` carries the finished field into campaign state: survivor troop XP, recovered ammunition, hero condition, morale context, tournament results and release of `B`. `concludeEncounter(win, lost, kills, auto, slain, retreat, txp)` remains the compatibility entry point for campaign settlement, including auto-resolve callers. A field result hands off to the same settlement implementation.

Settlement preserves its ordering: start collecting loot, synchronize reinforcements, apply medicine and roster losses, settle victory/retreat/defeat, then apply troop XP and show the summary. `heal`, `takePrisoners`, `battleMood`, `BATTLE_MOOD` and `MOOD` live with resolution. Encounter-specific victory rules remain in `engage.js`'s `ON_WIN` registry and their existing world-system handlers; auto-resolve still computes its result there. Shared XP and loss/text helpers remain available. The coordinator is not saved and does not replace the existing roster, character or encounter models.

`tests/battleresolution.js` captures 15 pre-extraction outcomes: field victory/defeat, escaped/caught/carried retreats, both tournament outcomes, siege and lord victory/defeat, monster loot, direct automatic settlement and the actual auto-resolve entry point. Snapshots cover campaign records, captures, loot and summary HTML, world consequences, messages, random-call counts and subsequent random values. Explicit field fixtures isolate settlement from combat simulation; existing combat and integration suites exercise that connection.

## Player battle commands

`battle/commands.js` owns the existing player-facing selection and command entry points: `rtsUnitAt`, `rtsSelect`, `rtsOrder`, `selectGroup`, `cycleGroup`, `ctrlGroup`, `setOrder`, `toggleFlag`, `setForm` and `formDragTo`, plus their selection helpers and flag labels. These are stateless functions over the active battle, not a second group class. `BattleControl` still implements movement, attacks, queues, pace, flags and formation changes for both players and AI.

The command layer forwards formation eligibility/layout to `formations.js` and zone placement to `deploy.js`. It keeps existing HUD refresh and log calls. The renderer retains `setOrderUI`, drawing, speed and pause controls; `ui/input.js` retains pointer/keyboard events, coordinate conversion, double-click detection, touch routing and camera interaction. No control binding, click threshold, formation rule, AI takeover policy or save field changed during extraction.

**Order paths** (`ui/orderpaths.js`, drawn from `render.js` `drawBattle` over the men, trees and rock): the planned route of each group's order as a dashed line with chevrons, only the part still to walk. A move follows the group's way round cliffs, rock and deep water (`g.way.p`), then each queued waypoint's leg (routed too); an attack goes to the target's seen men, or under fog to where it was last seen (`sightGoal`, read without its cache); a withdrawal goes to its exit. Yours are blue, your allies' muted, the enemy's never drawn. Settings → Order paths: Always, Selected (the default: the selected groups, the one whose card or icon you point at, and for 1.5 s any you give an order) or Off (the old straight lines, `battlehud.js` `drawOrderLines`). Drawing only: it writes no group, soldier, sight or route-cache state and draws no `Math.random`; the dashes stand still with reduced motion. `orderPath(id)` returns the points for tests (`tests/orderpaths.js`).

`tests/battlecommands.js` captures 19 pre-extraction scenarios with explicit field fixtures: unit/icon picking, Shift selection, boxes, control groups, relative movement, queues, attack targets, pace/hold/withdraw, shooter flags, formations, dragged lines, deployment and empty selections. It also dispatches real browser pointer/touch/keyboard events through the unchanged input listeners. Snapshots include selection, group state, target identities, unit positions, HUD button state, messages and calls to the existing BattleControl methods. Fixed geometry and seeded input isolate commands from combat timing and generated world actors. `tests/battleplacement.js` checks effective hotkey badges and recall, shared/All/empty/dead bindings, focus and card identity, hero-inclusive lines in both directions, the canvas ghost, actual walking and deployment. `tests/visual/battleplacement.js` prepares desktop/phone screenshots.

## Attack and projectile execution

`battle/attacks.js` owns `Fighter` (class wave E3: doc/STORY_DESIGN.md §7), every soldier's blows and wounds, the base his `Actor` extends: `wound` (was `applyDmg`), `hit`, troop kill XP (`earnXp`), the attack's cooldown and damage (`attack`), melee follow-through (`follow`, was `meleeFollow`), sidearm blows (`blade`, was `bladeBlow`), dragon breath (`breathe`), the aim (`aimErr`) and the quiver (`useAmmo`, `outOfAmmo`). What differs for a character or your hero (a roll, armour and talents against a wound, a fall, a guard, their aim and skill, their quiver, XP) their `Actor` kinds override (actors.js). It also owns the ammunition/accuracy helpers and their existing constants (`AMMO`, `AIM`, `BLADE`, `quiverOf`, `spreadOf`). All existing function names remain available as one-line aliases to actors, character/manual combat, abilities and effects. Damage still invokes the existing morale, fatigue, roster, character, HUD and VFX systems in its original order.

`battle/projectiles.js` owns the missiles (class wave E3): `Missile.of(a)`, one for each plain record in `B.arrows` (the record stays as it was: the art and the tests read and make it), by its kind an `ArcMissile` (arrows, javelins, stones, webs), a `Bolt` (crossbows) or a `MagicBolt` (through the trees, a burst where it hits); each `step`s itself and shares one `strike`. It owns `Missile.fire` (was `fireBolt`), `arcZ`, launch constants and `Missile.update(dt)` (was `updateProjectiles(dt)`; `fireBolt`, `arcStep`, `updateProjectiles` and `flyArrows` stay as aliases). The update contains the former inline projectile loop unchanged: arc and flat stepping, tree checks, blocking, web effects, hits/splash and final lifetime/bounds filtering. `updateBattle` calls it after unit movement/collision/clamping and before morale/end checks, inside the same pause/deployment/hit-stop gates. Target choice, shooter draw/reload decisions and monster activation now live in `actors.js`; no actor or commander policy was changed. `combat.js` retains your hero's own attack/ability mechanics: `HeroHands`, which is `B.hx` itself (the same fields in the same order, made by `HeroHands.of()`, was `heroState`): `spend`, `guard`, `hitFoe` (was `heroHit`), `swing`, the bow (`drawStart`, `drawHold`, `drawRelease`, `shoot`), `shove`, `dodge`, `cast` (was `castSkill`), `actions` (was `heroActions`), `drawHud`, `updateBar`.

Both HTML entry points load attacks and projectiles after resolution and before character/manual combat, and must: `Actor` (actors.js) extends `Fighter` (attacks.js) as it loads. These are plain scripts with runtime cross-system calls, not isolated modules. Save formats and transient field records are unchanged.

`tests/attackrules.js` captures 26 pre-extraction combat snapshots with explicit units, fixed geometry and fixed random streams. It checks damage reduction/invulnerability, casualties/XP, companion and lord losses, melee/follow-through/sidearms, launch physics, ammunition/accuracy, arc and flat impacts, trees/elf arrows, magic splash, webs, expiry/bounds, pause/deployment/hit-stop gates, guarding and shooter-phase handoff. Snapshots include state, effects metadata and random-call sequencing; generated bystanders are excluded. Existing attacks, manual combat, RTS, biome, matchup and XP suites provide broader integration coverage.

## Actor execution and targeting

`battle/actors.js` owns local target queries, firing arcs and `Actor` (class wave E3): `Actor.of(u)`, one for each soldier, holding nothing but its man, his kind looked at each time: a troop or beast (`Actor`), a lord, chieftain or companion (`CharActor`), your hero (`HeroActor`). Its `step` (was `aiUnit`: troop movement and strikes), the shot cycle (`shot`, `shotKind`, `shotTimes`), crowd retargeting (`crowd`), an order at a target gone from sight (`lost`), relocation, monster activation (`ability`), each turn (`turn`) and the retained manual movement driver (`HeroActor.manual`, was `updateHero`). Pure target queries stay functions (`nearest`, `nearestSeen`, `meleeTarget`, `shooterTarget`, `orderedTarget`, `roomyFoe`, `targetMembersOf`, `targetStands`, `inArc`, `relocating`…). Existing names (`aiUnit`, `shotStep`, etc.) remain available as one-line aliases to commanders, groups, attacks, effects and tests. Units stay plain records in `B.units`: the class adds no field to them.

`Actor.turns(dt)` (was `updateNonHeroActors(dt)`) contains the former non-hero unit loop: decrement cooldown, then each one's `turn`: a character's is its mind's (`CharacterMind.of(u).auto`, charmind.js), a troop's or beast's its step, monster ability and breath timer in their existing order. `updateBattle` calls it at the same point after knockback/buffs/slow/regeneration and before collision/projectile/morale processing. The hero still runs earlier on `rt * HERO_PACE`, while non-hero turns use `rt * BATTLE_PACE`; group stepping and commander decisions keep their original ordering. Pause, deployment and hit-stop gates remain in the coordinator.

Commanders and `BattleControl` retain authority over orders, group facing, formation and tactical choices. No targeting priorities, activation thresholds, random sequencing, pacing or saved/transient record formats changed. Both HTML entry points load actors after projectiles and before character/manual combat. Stale comments describing old hotkeys and an obsolete shooter-target heuristic were corrected without changing executable code.

### Battle boundary audit

`battlecontrol.js` owns `moveOrder`: issuing a new relocation increments the group's `mvId`. `actors.js` owns `relocating`: each actor compares its completed generation with the group's current one. The global helper name remains available; moving its declaration does not change the command API or transient state.

The actor cleanup removed only unreachable code: an always-null automatic-sidearm branch and an always-false face-the-foe movement branch. Moving actors still face their travel direction; explicit Melee Mode still executes `bladeBlow`. Small helpers such as `meleeTarget`, `meleeFoe`, `blowsToFell` and `arcZ` stay because they have test, commander or group consumers. The unused `BattleControl.suggest` method was removed: nothing called it or read the `prefer` field it wrote. Commanders still focus fire through `attack`; otherwise actors select by range and arc. No new targeting policy was introduced.

These files still share live `B`, `G` and `P` records and runtime callbacks. This is an ownership split, not an acyclic dependency graph:

| Owner | Main runtime connections |
| --- | --- |
| `commands.js` / `commander.js` / `charmind.js` | Player orders (`commands.js`, one `playerOrder` path) and AI captains (`commander.js`: one `Commander` for each side, `Commander.of(ck)`, `EnemyCommander` the enemy's; each think `orders()` reads the battle with `read` (a shared context), then `infantry`, `rally`, `shooters`, `cavalry`, `hero`, and `finish`; each captain's memory is one object, `captainMem(ck)` = `B.captains[ck]`) all issue orders through `BattleControl`; the character mind (heroes, companions, lords, chieftains; fall-back) is `charmind.js` (`CharacterMind.of(u)`: `HeroMind`, `ChampionMind`); beasts' ways (packs, rushes, ambushes: `PackMind.think`, called as `captainBeasts`, handed the beast groups by `orders` through `beastsOf`; the ways the table `BEAST_WAYS`) are `beastmind.js`. The old function names stay as aliases (doc/STORY_DESIGN.md §7, wave E2). |
| `battlecontrol.js` | Owns group order state and relocation generations; uses actor target queries and formation helpers. |
| `actors.js` | `Actor` (and `CharActor`, `HeroActor`): reads orders; delegates character turns, attacks, fatigue and rout/withdraw movement. |
| `attacks.js` | `Fighter`: executes damage/strikes, launches projectiles, and calls morale, XP, character and visual helpers. |
| `projectiles.js` | `Missile` (`ArcMissile`, `Bolt`, `MagicBolt`): advances projectiles and calls damage, guarding, XP and visual helpers on impact. |
| `combat.js` | `HeroHands` (`B.hx`): your hero's swing, bow, shove, dodge, casts and their costs; called by HeroMind and the manual control. |
| `battle.js` | Sequences group/commander turns, actors, collisions, projectiles, morale and resolution on the existing clocks. |

Keep those callbacks inside runtime functions: load-time calls into scripts loaded later would fail. `tests/structure.js` now checks ownership of the main extracted entry points and the supported battle script order, in addition to duplicate globals and wiki parity. Behavior remains covered by the actor/attack/command snapshots and RTS/character integration suites.

`tests/actorrules.js` captures 23 pre-extraction scenarios for local targeting, arcs, shooter phases, stun/rout/withdraw, ordered-target self-defence, firm hold, relocation, fatigue, crowding, melee mode, cease-fire, regeneration/pounce, manual movement, hero-versus-troop clocks and frame gates. It uses explicit units and fixed geometry/random streams; unrelated generated world state is excluded.

## Formations and unit abilities

**The arena** (2026-11-01; the owner: "can you have some small arena battle screen instead of open green field?"): every bout in a town's lists is fought in a small walled ring (`gfx/tourney.js` `TourneyArena`; any fight may ask for it with `opts.arena`). Its field is `FIELD_SIZES.tourney` (448×256), the fighters 140 px apart facing each other, the camera fitting it as any field. The wall is the field's own edges: the ring is the field's width, its rows (92–216 inside the HUD's measured edges) `B.ftop`/`B.fbot`. Its look follows the holding: its culture's wall (Vale and League stone, Karn granite, Ashgor stakes and bone, Sylvan living wood, else timber), that culture's gatehouse in the far wall, the lord's hangings, pavilion, banners and pennants, and sand by climate (raked, frost-dusted, desert-pale, marsh-damp); the fight's rules stay the open field's (climate the look only). The stands hold the town's peoples in mixed clothes. Painted once and kept by its key. Each frame the flags flutter and the crowd raises its arms in a wave from a hard blow (12% of a fighter's health or more), a knockdown or a man felled, and roars at the end (audio.js `cheer`, `roar`). Drawing only: a hash of seat and clock, never `Math.random`. `TOURNEY_ARENA.on` false: today's open-field tournament. Tests: `tourney`; picture: `visual/tourney`.

**Battle screen drawn sharp** (2026-11-01; the owner: "why separated battle screen seems not sharp like the world screen?"). A battle fought apart draws at the screen's own pixels, as the map does (core.js `bview`, `RENDER.sharpBattle`; the Resolution setting caps it too). Its view is still FIELD_H px down, k device px each, so every click, the camera and the HUD read the same view px. The camera's stops (camera.js `camZooms`) are whole device px per field px from the whole field in, so sprites are even squares at rest; the whole field is its exact fit, or the whole number just under it when that shows at least `BCAM.snap` (.85) of it. A wheel or key step glides onto its stop (`BCAM.ease`, at once with reduced motion); a pinch settles on the nearest when let go; a resized window keeps a whole number. `camPx` and `camView`: the camera's device scale and the field in view, for whatever draws or culls by them (loose horses too: dismount.js). Icons, health bars and fog ghosts on whole device px; battle text at the screen's resolution. Test mode (`FIXED_VIEW`) and the switch off keep today's canvas (BVW × BVH, stretched by the browser) exactly. Cost (headless, 150 men): a phone at Fast as today (4.8 ms), Balanced +10-20%; a 1080p desktop 5.0 → 5.8 ms seeing the whole field, 2.8 → 6.6 ms zoomed in. Tests: `sharpbattle`; picture and cost: `visual/sharpbattle`.

**A fighter holds at his foe** (2026-11-01; the owner: "the fighter that I fight in battle map seems to move weird, jiggering left right"; `battle/actors.js` `MELEE_HOLD`, `MELEE_HELD`). A man-sized fighter who has reached his foe holds there till the foe is `slack` (1) px past his reach, no further than he strikes, as a shooter keeps his range (`inRng`); before, a body's push or a blow's knock took him just out, he stepped in, and over again (the arena's champion: 49 steps back and forth in 6 s, now 15, its blows and lunges). A big beast holds none: it wades on, through a line. Kept off the unit (a WeakMap), so the battle snapshots are as they were. His lean as drawn (physics on: `physics.js` `physLeanDrawn`, `PHYSICS.feel.leanStep`; the owner: "I think it's because the sprite is stretching in the animation"): eased toward `physLean` over `leanEase` .2 s and stepped so his head stands whole pixels off his feet (a step kept till `leanHold` .75 px past it). Before, each step's jolt of acceleration flickered the skew, his head 0, 1, 2 px off his feet 17 times in 4 s, so the sprite seemed to stretch; now once. Drawn only. Tests: `duelhold`.

**The circle and the Cantabrian circle** (2026-11-01; the owner: "the circle shape where you can order unit to form in circle, the circle size will determine whether to have unit stack by layers ... the maximum circle size is where your unit can fill a whole circle without gap otherwise you may have a gap in circle"): two formations in `battle/formations.js`.
- `FORMS.circle`, a schiltron for foot who fight hand to hand (`FORM_WHO.ring`). Every man faces out. Its outer ring holds `files` places (radius files × `gx` ÷ 2π, at least `CIRCLE.minR`); the rest stand on rings a rank `gy` further in, as many as fit, the innermost spread evenly. Files past its men: one ring with gaps. Too few: as few more as hold them all (`circleRings`).
- A man of a formed circle (`ringed`: near his place round where its men stand) has no flank or rear. Every blow lands on his front (`morale.js` `sideOf`; an arrow keeps its side, `CIRCLE.arrows`), so Brace covers him all round. He faces out (`ringFace`; `physics.js`, `actors.js`) and never steps out after a foe. It walks at `CIRCLE.spd` of a walk and never runs. Charge, an attack or Withdraw break it into a line (`breakRing`).
- `FORMS.cantabrian`, for horse archers (`FORM_WHO.hbow`): one ring, single file, its places turning round its middle (`battlecontrol.js` `loop`, `CANTABRIAN`), its riders shooting on the move. A move order moves its middle, Hold stops it (`still`), Charge, an attack or Withdraw break it. On the map it marches as a loose clump (`mapForm`).
- Both take their size from `files`: a drag (the line is the ring's width across, `dragFiles`; its ghost draws the ring) or the March formation tab's drawn formation. O (`KIT_KEYS` `ring`) or the ring's button (`#bRing`) puts each selected group in its own ring; T cycles through it.
- An AI captain rings a spear group against riders on more than one side (`CIRCLE.ai`, `ringWanted`).
- Tests: circleform, cantabrian.

**March formation previews** (2026-11-01; the owner: "previews in march formation not showing flag", "by road and in field they face different direction"): both previews fly your banner as the map does (`ui/march.js` `marchFlagMen`, `marchFlagDraw`: a fifth of each group, a spearman's on his spear, anyone else's at his back). Both face right: By road's head is on the right (`ColumnEditor#xAt`). Tests: marchpreview, columnorder.

Positioning formations are Line, Loose and Wedge. A dragged line includes the hero as a one-person group alongside troops and companions, in selection order; All uses battle order. Preview and destination use the same plan, including deployment. Selecting only the hero still places them at the midpoint. Loose changes spacing only: no arrow resistance or melee vulnerability modifier. Shield wall is a separate **B** ability toggle; old `setForm('wall')` command callers forward to the ability rather than assigning a formation.

Group flags request actions, with eligibility checked per soldier. Shield wall requires shield-bearing, non-ranged, non-mounted, non-charge infantry. An active soldier needs a nearby active-requesting eligible ally within 16 pixels, facing within 60 degrees; dead, routing, stunned, withdrawing or running soldiers cannot support it. Supporting shields may belong to another friendly group. Supported soldiers take 35% less frontal damage and move 40% slower; unsupported shields keep their normal personal frontal missile blocking. Charge and running orders lower the toggle. Other group members gain no wall protection or speed penalty. Shield-wall users hold their ground instead of chasing nearby enemies.

Wedge positioning can be selected below six riders, but the charge bonus starts at **6 formed cavalry: +5%**, increases by **2.5 percentage points per additional rider**, and caps at **+30% at 16**. Only living, non-routing, non-stunned cavalry aligned with the wedge and within 28 pixels of their relative slots count or receive it. Inferred median anchors let the formation move without counting distant stragglers. Casualties remove bonus without forcing a formation change. Existing formation-depth charge resistance remains unchanged.

The HUD shows eligible/active shield counts and the current wedge bonus. Tuning lives in `WALL` and `WEDGE` in `battle/formations.js`. Focused coverage: `tests/formationabilities.js`, existing RTS/command/actor/attack regressions and unit-card/wiki checks. This does not introduce dynamic squads or change the existing rank-based depth model.

## Group morale, confidence and routing

Morale is owned by the **formation**, not the soldier. `BattleResolve` (`battle/morale.js`) holds one morale value, confidence, cohesion, recent-loss strain, missile pressure, breaking timer and route/rally/shatter state per group (`g.resolve`). Ungrouped breakable soldiers get a one-man resolve; `resolveOf(u)` finds either. Soldier fields (`u.mor`, `u.rout`, `u.confidence`, …) are **mirrors** written by `sync()`; writing them changes nothing. HP, armour, shields, ammunition and stamina stay per soldier. Heroes, companions, lords, chieftains and beasts (`steady`) have no resolve and never break.

**Inputs.** Wounds (`hurt`), deaths (`death`), flank/rear blows and charges on any member feed the group. Ratios use the group's **starting** headcount and health, so the same share of losses shakes small and large groups equally. Casualties and wounds also lower the recovery `ceiling()`. Deaths in a short window build **recent-loss strain** (up to `GROUP_RESOLVE.strainCap`), which decays with `LOSS_MEMORY.life`; bursts hurt more than spread-out losses. **Unanswered missile fire** (missile damage in the last `window` seconds, while dealing back less than `answer` of it) drains up to `missileRate` a second and blocks confidence. Per-soldier flank and charge shocks are divided by the group's starting size.

**Context.** Every 0.2 combat seconds (sooner after a death) each group samples three members (left, centre, right along its front) and averages their view: visible, distance-weighted strength within `CONFIDENCE.radius` gives **Confident** or **Outmatched**; close strength or threats on opposite sides give **Locally overwhelmed**; aligned, settled neighbours within `COHESION.radius` give **cohesion**, which softens ordinary and routing shock. Trees and rocks block the view. Confidence (1.25:1 to 3:1) adds up to +12 above the starting value and 30% shock resistance; a withdrawal order, missile pressure, a nearby rout or a fallen leader suppress it.

**Breaking and routing.** At 0 or below the group is **Breaking**: it routs after `RESOLVE.breakDelay` (0.6 s), `severeDelay` (0.25 s) at −10 or worse, or `leaderDelay` (0.2 s) during leader panic. Positive morale resets the episode. All break decisions are made before neighbour shocks are applied, so routs cannot domino in one update. On a route the group's order becomes `'rout'`, its target and queue are cleared, and every `BattleControl` order method returns early; each soldier still escapes on its own (`routStep`). A group left quiet for `rallyQuiet` seconds can rally once (back to Hold); a second rout shatters it. A nearby rout shocks other groups within `routeRadius` at most once per second. Leader death shocks every group of that side and suppresses confidence. Nearby enemy deaths reassure by `killGain`, capped at `killCap` per rolling second and at the ceiling.

**Readers.** The HUD (`grpMorale`, unit cards, badges), the battle lab's morale history and the developer panel all read the resolve. The developer panel only allows editing `group.<id>.resolve.mor`. The enemy commander reads it too: once its strength is under `FOE_CMD.beaten` (0.4) of yours and its formations' shared morale, weighted by men, is under `FOE_CMD.broken` (30), the whole enemy army **falls back** (withdraws off its own edge, logged "The enemy falls back.") instead of fighting to the last man. Once begun it does not turn round; withdrawing groups are left alone by `captainFinish`. Escaped enemies are not kills, as with a rout. Your army (even under AI command), allies, beasts and morale-free battles never fall back on their own (`beatenArmy` in `battle/commander.js`, tests in `tests/groupmorale.js`).

**Unit-level AI morale.** Every captain (the enemy, your allies, your army under AI command) also reads each group's own resolve, tuned by `UNIT_AI` in `battle/commander.js`. (1) An infantry group under `pullAt` (25), with another infantry group of the same captain still steady (`backAt`, 45, not routed, not recovering), **pulls back** `pullDist` (90 px) straight away from the foe's middle and stands there (`g.recover`) until its morale is back to `backAt` or `maxRecover` (15 s) pass; a time-capped group cannot pull back again for another `maxRecover` s. A group with no steady sibling fights on. (2) The nearest steady sibling not already fighting **plugs the gap**: it is ordered once onto the foe nearest the recovering group (`g.plug`) and keeps at him while the other recovers. (3) Cavalry under `chargeAt` (40) **start no new charge** (from the flank, the regroup, a rescue or the ranged-duel ride): they stand off as after a charge until they recover, or at most `cavRecover` (4) s (losses lower the ceiling morale recovers to, so worn riders would otherwise stand off for ever); a charge under way goes on. (4) Cavalry target choice adds `prey` (2) and shooters' focus fire `preyShot` (1.5) for a foe whose group is **wavering**. Groups without a resolve (heroes, companions, leaders, beasts) count as steady and are never weak prey; with morale off (`B.noMorale`) none of this applies. Tests: `tests/unitmorale.js`.

**The word of a lost group** (the owner, 2026-10-20; `MORALE.army`, `armyWord`, `armyHear`). When a group routs or is cut down to the last man, every other group of its side feels it, each later the farther it stands, as word spreads: after `delay` (0.5 s) + `per` (0.5 s) a 100 px, at most `max` (4.5 s; `B.armyWord` holds the word on its way). The hit: `hit` (6) × the lost group's starting size against its side's average group (clamped 0.4-2) × `elite` (1.4, its men tier 3 or more on average) × `lead` (1.5, a hero's or leader's own group) × nearness (1 beside it, down to `far` 0.45 at `span` 900 px) × `fade`^n (0.7; n: the word that group has heard already, `r.armyHeard`, so losses stack, each a little less) × the hearer's steadiness, as an ordinary shock (confidence, cohesion and Stand Fast! soften it). **No double count:** a rout's neighbours within the cascade's `GROUP_RESOLVE.routeRadius` (140 px) take the cascade (`MORALE.cascade`, at once, with its panic) and never the word; a group cut down has no cascade, so its neighbours hear the word too; a group is lost once (`r.armyLost`: routing, then cut down, is one word). A routing group hears nothing. A hit of `say` (3) or more puts the word over the group that hears it (`moraleSay`, the same word said once there): "The left flank is broken!", "The right flank has fallen!", "The centre is broken!", by where the lost group stood in its army facing the enemy. `MORALE.army.on` false: only the cascade, as before. Tests: `tests/armymorale.js`.

Tuning lives in `MORALE` (with `MORALE.army`), `CONFIDENCE`, `AWARENESS`, `COHESION`, `LOSS_MEMORY`, `RESOLVE` and `GROUP_RESOLVE`. Coverage: `tests/groupmorale.js` (shared state, normalisation, routing as a group, rally/shatter, HUD/lab agreement), `tests/moralebalance.js` (kill reassurance, breaking timers), `tests/confidence.js`, `tests/moralecontext.js`, `tests/moraleresilience.js`, `tests/armymorale.js` (the word of a lost group), and the reports `tests/visual/moraleaudit.js` and `tests/visual/confidenceaudit.js`; footmen against berserkers in `tests/moralebalance.js` (the Footman's own morale is 45, under his tier's 50: the owner, 2026-11-01; a hundred now break with about 30 standing, not 11-16).

## AI tactics

**Shooters lead, or play safe.** Every captain picks a posture for its shooters each think (`rangedMode`, stored in the captain's memory `captainMem(ck).rmode`): **aggressive** when attacking, not outnumbered, out-ranging (×1.1) or out-shooting (×1.25) the other side, with little fast melee against them (riders or speed ≥ 60 under 15% of its shooters' strength), quivers and nerve whole and no melee foe closing. The shooters then lead: the army line puts the infantry `screenGap` behind their full reach, and they go up (at a run from over 80 px) and open fire first. When the captain turns **safe** (defending, outnumbered, cavalry, worn, a foe closing), shooters that led run back behind their infantry. The margins relax while aggressive, and a 2 s wait applies before leading again.

**Charges by stamina** (`runFor`): fresh infantry runs from 240 px and charges from a 140 px gap (not at riders or beasts, nor with a charge coming at it, when it braces instead); fresh riders gallop all the way, start a charge from 160 px, and pull back 150 px to charge again; less fresh, `CAPT.runAt`; tired, 130 px; spent, a walk. Tired riders stand off after a charge until stamina 55, or 5 s.

**Characters fall back** (`CHAR_FALL`): one who rolls out of a press ("Falling back") goes to cover behind its own troops, heals if it can, and goes back in once its breath is back and the place is no trap (at most 2 foes near the target, or as many of ours there): your hero to your order, AI characters to the safest foe near; after 12 s, or with no troops of its own left, it goes in anyway. Any order of yours to its group (a move, an attack, Hold, pace (Charge/Advance or R), a formation or a flag, a Shift waypoint) ends it — a unit ability does not (`playerOrder` in commands.js; tests `tests/orderfallback.js`) — and with Auto skills off your characters never fall back on their own. Back in at its own pick, a character isn't drawn into a duel for 2 s (`CHAR_FALL.keep`). Health is not a gate (it doesn't regenerate in battle).

**Auto (A):** A hands the selected groups to the AI (`g.aiCmd`, this battle only), or with nothing selected your whole army (`B.auto`, remembered: `BAUTO`); `deploy.js` `aiHas`/`aiAll`/`aiAny`. Your captain orders only the groups it has (`captainGroups`), counts the rest as friends (`assess`) and never orders them; the commander powers are its only with the whole army. Handing groups over starts its captain afresh (`aiReset` clears every AI note); any order, formation, flag or ability button of yours takes back the groups it is given to (`takeCommand(gs)`); groups you sent withdrawing keep withdrawing. Posture keeps attacking down to `attackAt − FOE_CMD.hold` and is remembered per captain (your army and your allies apart). Cavalry pick a flank once and keep it; riders 3 s in melee on any charge, a rescue included, pull back to charge again; hunting archers head home for at least 3 s without focus-firing at a run. Only a wavering group not engaged hand to hand pulls back (an engaged one fights on), and a braced group is never sent to plug a gap. Tests: `tests/controlaudit.js`, `tests/aishare.js`.

**Attack-move (Ctrl + right-click, or D / the button then a ground order):** `BattleControl.attackMove` marches to the place; `amStep` turns the group on the nearest foe its side sees within `AMOVE` reach of its march (melee 90 px from any man, riders 120, shooters their shortest reach + 30): fighters attack his group, riders charge, shooters halt and shoot at will (just short: on into reach); none left (off × the reach, no flicker) and it marches on to the same place, its waypoints kept, then holds; runners are chased `AMOVE.leash` px from where it turned at most. Shift queues one; any new order of yours (`amEnd`) ends it, the pace does not. No field on a group never given one (the fingerprints stay as they were). Tests: `tests/attackmove.js`.

**Keep formation (Shift+K, or the button after the formations; on by default, a setting kept: `PREFS.keepform`):** with two or more groups selected, they go as one block, as Total War's formation lock (`formations.js` `GroupBlock`, tunables `KEEPFORM`). The block's centre is the groups' places (`ax`/`ay`, so stragglers do not drag it), its facing theirs averaged; each group keeps its place ahead or behind and to the side. A right-click turns the block to face the way it is sent (further than `KEEPFORM.turn` 40 px; nearer, it keeps its facing), each group's own facing turning with it; Shift queues the block from its last waypoints (each waypoint notes its facing, `w.face`, so the next turns from it; the groups do not yet turn to it on arrival, which waits on a line in `battlecontrol.js` `step`), Ctrl the same as an attack-move. A right-drag makes the line the block's new front: the groups within `KEEPFORM.rank` 24 px of its front share the line by their numbers, as a drag does; the rest keep their files and their places behind, spread as the front's width changed and set back (or up) as its depth changed, so the gap behind the front rank holds. The drag's ghost shows where each group will stand (`formPlan`). An attack on an enemy is unchanged. Deploying, the block is moved whole into your zone as far as it fits, then each group into it. Off: each group as before (offsets unturned; a drag lays every group side by side). K stays Auto skills: every letter is taken in battle. Tests: `tests/keepform.js` (`battlecommands` and one `rts` check run with it off, as they were recorded).

**Side colours:** one table, `battle.js` `SIDE` (yours blue, your allies' green, the enemy's red; a paler shade routing), read by the soldiers' cloth (`spriteTeam`), health bars, icons, the small map, order paths, words and callouts.

**Race fighting styles** (`TEMPERAMENT` in `battle/commander.js`): each captain (the enemy, your allies, your army under Auto) takes a temperament from its troops (the race of 60% of those that have one, else *mixed*; beasts, monsters and generic levies get none), chosen once at its first think (`captainMem(ck).temper`): multipliers on existing tunables, read through `tun()` at the captain's decision points. **Human** is the base itself. **Dwarves hold the line** (attack only from a higher strength ratio, hold longer, run in from nearer, shield wall against riders earlier, rotate a wavering group out sooner). **Elves skirmish** (shooters lead on smaller margins, a wider screen behind them, infantry goes in after longer volleys, riders swing wider round a flank). **Orcs come on in a frenzy** (attack sooner, charge and run from further, pull back only when nearly broken, fight on long after a human army would fall back, riders rest less). **A mixed host** favours wavering prey. The enemy's style is logged as the battle opens. Balance: in human-troop mirror labs over seeds 1–8 each style wins 38–50% against human. Tests: `tests/aitemperament.js`.

**The captains read the ground** (`TERRAIN_AI` and `TERRAIN_TEMPER` in `battle/commander.js`; `tests/aiterrain.js`). With no terrain the captains are unchanged.
- **Defence:**
  - A defending army takes the crest of a top near its line on its own half, if it can get there before the enemy's fighters could run there.
  - Its infantry stands on the rim and its shooters behind it on the height; elves may hold a wood.
  - A river between the armies makes a defence more likely, and defenders hold their bank at the crossings.
- **Attack:**
  - Infantry does not run uphill into a held crest: it walks up after longer volleys and runs only the last 40 px.
  - Riders wait rather than charge foot holding a hilltop, ride round woods to an open flank spot, and pass over marks standing in woods, marsh or water.
- **Shooters** stand on the highest ground near their place, out of woods (elves stand under the trees).
- **Rivers:** moves over a river go by the nearest ford or bridge, with files narrowed to it.
- **Races:** dwarves look further for high ground and hold crossings longer; orcs charge up anything short of a two-level climb.

**Beasts in battle** (`BEAST_TACTICS` in `battle/beastmind.js`; `tests/beastai.js`). Under an AI captain (the enemy's, your allies', yours under Auto) each group of beasts fights its kind's way, taken out of the captain's arms first, so in a mixed horde wolves circle while trolls hit the front. Beasts never rout; your beasts follow your orders.
- **The look:** no ranks: every group of beasts stands in the ragged `pack` spread (`FORMS.pack`, not one of your formations). At the start an AI group of wolves splits into packs of 3–5 (`id~2`, `id~3`…), boars into twos and threes, bears and trolls into loners or pairs.
- **Wolves (`pack`):** the packs pick one prey together (routing, shooters, wounded, alone; distance counts most) and circle it, each to a side of its own from its rear round, the shorter way and only where a way can be walked (`canReach`: no stall at a cliff). All on their sides (or 3 s), they bite at once; after 4 s at grips a pack pulls back 60 px and comes round to a fresh side, unless the prey's morale is under 50 (then it worries on). A prey bitten from two sides at once is **surrounded**: 6 morale a think. A pack that loses 75% of its health in one bite breaks off 140 px and gathers; too few left, it joins another pack.
- **Boars (`rush`):** headlong at the nearest, 2.5 s at grips, 70 px back (a run-up: the next charge lands with full momentum), again.
- **Bears, trolls (`brute`):** straight at the thickest fight (most of a group's men at grips with theirs), else the strongest group; the dragon at the most crowded one (its breath as ever).
- **Spiders, scorpions, the tiger, the wyrm (`ambush`):** into the nearest spot deep in a wood (the wyrm: where it is), unseen (`seenBy`); a seen foe within 130 px (tiger, scorpion 110; wyrm 150), a wound while waiting, or 15 s (tiger 20, wyrm 8), and they strike; sprung, they are seen by those they fight.
- **Griffins (`swoop`):** mark the shooters (else the foe's edges and rear), circle to the mark's rear, dive, wheel 160 px away, come again from the other side. **Slimes (`blob`):** a slow advance.
- **Close in:** within 70 px of its mark a group charges, each beast at the foe nearest him (one set on a single man of a crowd would stand idle behind the others); one that spits (spiders, slimes) stands and spits once its mark is in reach (on a charge it would back off a foe closing on it, into a corner).
- **Off:** `BEAST_TACTICS.on=false` (the test harness, like `TERRAIN.on`), beasts fight as before: one group, a line, the captain's arms. The fingerprint suites run with it off and stay byte-identical.

Balance (`ai:true` lab, terrain and fog on, 500 px apart, seeds 1–8; men's wins of 8, length, men lost, before → after): wolves ×10 vs 10 foot + 6 bows 4, 19 s, 52% → 7, 29 s, 46%; boars ×4 vs 8 + 4: 8, 19 s, 25% → 8, 18 s, 15%; trolls ×2 vs 8 + 4: 8, 37 s, 28% → 8, 39 s, 33%; spiders ×12 vs 6 + 4 in a forest: 6, 50 s, 19% → 7, 36 s, 21% (every strike from hiding); griffins ×2 vs 8 + 10 on hills: 2, 26 s, 78% → 2, 42 s, 77%; a mixed horde (8 wolves, a troll, 3 boars, 4 spiders) vs 10 + 6 + 2 knights: 7, 45 s, 34% → 8, 37 s, 37%. A wolf battle sees about 4–5 bites from 3 packs, 3 regroups and pull-backs, and 2–3 surrounded thinks; boars rush twice, griffins dive twice.

Tunables: `AI_TACTICS` in `battle/commander.js`, `CHAR_FALL` in `battle/charmind.js`, `BEAST_TACTICS` in `battle/beastmind.js`. Tests: `tests/aitactics.js`, `tests/charfallback.js`, `tests/beastai.js`. Balance: with the AI rallying, equal infantry fights rout at ~46–49% losses (~8 s).

## Charges, bracing and morale-off rules (effect audit, 2026-10-17)

- **Spearmen brace** only against a charge on their **front**, and only while fighting (not routing or withdrawing): no charge bonus, push or shock. Brace (the ability) extends that to the flanks; a charge into the **rear** always hits home (`spearBraced` in morale.js).
- **Depth and wedge scale only the charge bonus**: a block 4+ ranks deep takes 70% of the bonus (not from behind), so a weak charge never does less than no charge; a full 16-rider wedge is about ×2.17.
- **Charge stamina** is spent only when the charge actually earns its bonus (cavalry from `CHARGE.min`, foot from `CHARGE.foot_min`; characters likewise).
- **Morale off** (some lab/test battles) skips only morale effects: flank/rear damage and foot-charge damage still apply. Tournaments still have no flank or foot-charge bonus.
- Morale boosts above the recovery ceiling (Rally, war cry, an enemy leader's fall) fade at `GROUP_RESOLVE.fade` per combat second. Tests: `tests/effectaudit.js`.

## Battle physics

`battle/physics.js` (`PHYSICS`, one tunables table; `PHYSICS.on`, off in `tests/harness.js` so every older fingerprint is unchanged, on in `tests/physics.js`). Every soldier has a **mass** by kind and armour (a man by his armour class, ×1.1 heavy; a rider adds his horse, 3.2; beasts by kind, a wolf 0.9 to the dragon 20), a **heading** and a **speed**. He speeds up at his kind's acceleration (less tired, on bad ground or uphill: `groundMul`), slows at its braking and turns at its rate, slower the faster he goes: foot get going in a quarter second, heavy foot in twice that, riders need a ~40 px run-up, overshoot by ~30 px and wheel round wide; a line ordered to face a flank turns in time, and the side a blow lands on follows the heading (`facing`).

- **Charges:** the damage bonus is still `CHARGE`'s, by momentum, now the run that built it capped by the speed he still has at the blow (no double count). The old push and jolt (`chargePush`, `chargeJolt`) give way to an impact by the two masses: the one struck is thrown back (`u.kx`, the slide) and, hard enough for his weight and from a true charge (movesets below), **knocked down** for 0.5–1.4 s (no blows; then he gets up), which shakes his unit a little; ranks close behind him and a shield wall add weight; a wedge rider hits with the wedge's weight; the attacker keeps only the speed the two go on with.
