- **Staggering** (`gfx/stagger.js` `STAGGER`, `Stagger`; drawn only, `tests/stagger.js`): knocked down, a man goes one of six ways by his build (light, foot, heavy by his mass) and a hash of his id and how often he has gone down: reel (rocked back on his feet, stumbling), back (staggered back, over onto his back), face, spin (spun round, then down), knee (driven to one knee), sit (thrown onto his seat); the heavy never spin. He goes the way he was thrown (from behind, onto his face). A throw too light to fell him but over `STAGGER.reel.min` of what would (`physics.js` `physReel`: `u.reelAt`, `reelK`, `reelA`) rocks him: a flinch, leaning back from it and a step or two back. Riders, beasts and monsters too: a rider struck hard (a charge, braced spears) is rocked in the saddle, his mount rearing on its hocks; a beast knocked down goes one of `STAGGER.beasts` by its weight (low, pressed flat then onto its side; skid, braced, skidding back; tumble, bowled onto its back, legs kicking; roll, right over backward onto its side); a big one never goes down but a heavy blow makes it recoil, lurching back (a throw weighed against `STAGGER.reel.cap` of mass at most).
- **Braced spears** (`spearBraced`: spearmen facing it, Brace for the flanks) stop a charge dead, throw the rider back, stagger him and hurt him ("Braced!").
- **Pushing:** the jostle shares the overlap by mass (the heavy shove the light, a troll walks through a line), and one pressing into a foe is slowed by him; a heavy one's blow (a big beast's, a rider's) pushes men, and only a hard attack throws them down (movesets below). Routers wander and stumble (the battle's own seeded stream, `physRng`).
- **Safety:** rock, cliffs, water and the edges hold as before (`terrainHold` after every move); one who runs into a wall loses his speed (`physPost`). Feel: riders throw dust braking or wheeling hard, men lean into a run, the knocked-down lie and get up, a big impact jolts the view (never with reduced motion).
- Balance (`tests/visual/physicsbalance.js`, AI on AI in the open, 8 seeds, off → on): cavalry vs light foot 2/8 → 4/8 wins; cavalry into braced spears still loses every time and harder (riders lost 80% → 100%, spears 42% → 24%); heavy vs light infantry, archers vs cavalry, a troll vs a line, wolves vs foot, knights vs militia keep their winners.

## Movesets: who knocks down (2026-10-22)

`battle/movesets.js` (`MOVESETS`, one tunables table; `Moveset.of(u)`, by kind). The owner: "several units can knock you down every attack… only a hard or charge attack can knock down". Before, every blow of anything as heavy as a mass of 4 (a bear, a troll, every rider) could throw a man down: your hero (no armour) went down 37–49 times a minute against two bears, a troll or six knights.

- **Normal blows** (most swings, bites, claws): damage and, from a heavy one, a push (`MOVESETS.push` of the old throw). Never down.
- **Hard attacks** (`MOVESETS.hard`): heavy blow (two-handers, maces and hammers by the kind's look; your hero with a two-hander or hammer, champions with the heavy combo), maul (bear), club slam (troll), stomp (wyrm, dragon, any big one; it strikes those round the one hit too). Wound up first (`wind`, standing: a ring at his feet and its name over him; a stun, a fall or a move order spoils it), then ×`dmg`, a throw by `kb` against the masses (his ranks and shield wall behind him), down only if that beats his footing (a shield toward it ×1.5, a braced spear ×2) and then its chance (`down`). Not again for `cd` s. The AI uses one when ready on a worthy target (a shield toward him, a character, a big one, two or more foes together), else on anyone 3 s later; a charge's blow comes first. Characters wind up their combo's finisher. With physics off they still wind up and land (nobody goes down without physics).
- **Charges** (riders, boars, griffins, the bear's rush ×1.6): the impact throws down only from a true run, momentum ≥ `charge.speed` (0.6); a rider swinging from a standstill, or at the gallop but only just turned on his man, does not. Foot running in never knocks down. Braced spears stop a charge as before.
- **Grace:** nobody goes down again within 1.75 s of getting up (`u.upAt`), nor while rolling clear. A character down lies still (no skills or moves).
- **Your hero's controls are unchanged:** under RTS command your hero fights on their own, and with a two-hander or a hammer winds up their finisher as a hard attack. The manual control (set aside) has no hard-attack button; its finisher stays as it was.
- Balance (`tests/visual/physicsbalance.js`, physics on, `MOVESETS.old` then new): cavalry vs light foot 4/8 then 2/8 wins (men knocked down a battle 17.9 then 4.1); knights vs militia 8/8 both (16.6 then 5.3); archers vs cavalry 0/8 both (15.9 then 9.1); a troll vs a line 0/8 both (6.9 then 0.1); braced spears, heavy vs light infantry and wolves vs foot as they were.
- Measured (`tests/movesets.js`, your hero with no armour, 3 seeds × 60 s): knockdowns a minute against two bears 48.3 → 4.7, a troll 36.7 → 4.0, six knights in a wedge 48.7 → 2.0.
- `MOVESETS.old` (the proof switch): every blow as before. The battle fingerprints (`actorrules`, `attackrules`, `battlecommands`, `battleresolution`, `battlesetup`, `aishare`, `groundhash`, `rts`) hold both ways: they run with physics off (no push or fall changes), and none of their fights ever winds up a hard attack (checked: with `windUp` made to throw, every one of them still passes).

## Defence: blocks, parries and dodges (2026-10-29)

`battle/defence.js` (`DEFENCE`, one tunables table; `Defence`). The owner: battles about 1.5 times as long, every man blocking, parrying or dodging in his stance (never rolling away, which stays your hero's and the champions'), no words over him, only the look.

- **The roll:** each melee blow of a troop, a character or your hero at a foe. Met, it does nothing. Chance: `base` 22%, +4% a tier past the first, +10% for a shield toward the blow (not on a mount), −3% a tier of the attacker's, ×0.55 from the flank, ×0.1 from behind, ×0.8 tired, ×0.6 spent, ×0.5 against a big one's blow, at most 60%.
- **How:** a shield toward it, a **block** (the shield raised before him: `DEF_POSE`, sprites.js); else a **parry** (his weapon raised across: `HOLD.*.parry`, weapons.js), or, in leather or cloth, a third of the time a **dodge** (a sway and lean back on the spot: render.js). A spark or a puff where it met, the block or clash sound; struck, a one-pixel flinch.
- **Never met:** a hard attack, a combo's finisher, a charge's blow, a shove or bash, an ability, a missile (a shield's front already turns those); nor a blow at your hero (yours to block), a man down, stunned, routing or winding up. Big beasts never defend; the rest dodge 12%.
- **The auto-resolve** counts it: a kind's health is raised by its expected share met (`Defence.expect`, engage.js `power`).
- Measured (`tests/defence.js`, 8 v 8 to the last man, 4 seeds, off → on): levies ×1.53, spearmen v shield orcs ×1.46, swordsmen v hammerers ×1.51, shield guards v black orcs ×1.61, levies v swordsmen ×1.34: ×1.49 on average.
- `DEFENCE.on` false: every blow lands as before. `tests/harness.js` turns it off for the older suites, so their fingerprints hold; `tests/defence.js` turns it on.

## Duels: two men fighting each other (2026-10-29)

`battle/duel.js` (`DUEL`, one tunables table; `Duel`). The owner: units fight each other with more animations, as in Total War; orders still send them where they are told.

- **Paired:** a soldier's blow at a free man, himself free, pairs them (`u.duel`). Each meets his partner's blows better (+30%, at most 75%: `Defence.chance`), and a block or parry is answered at once (his next blow within 0.3 s). Another man striking one in a duel catches him on his blind side (×0.35). It ends when either falls, runs, or is taken more than his reach away (an order, a push). While it lasts he keeps to his partner (`actors.js` step), unless ordered at others; any number may strike him, and with his partner down the next free man who strikes him is his new one. Your hero and the characters duel too (the owner's: like a normal attack, with more animations): struck, paired, meeting blows in their stance, your manual block on top. Not a shooter with his bow, a beast or a big one.
- **The looks** (drawn only; the rules keep `troopStyle`): each blow by his weapon and what he fights (`DUEL.moves`: blade, heavy, pole × man, beast, big; a shield adds a bash): a swing, a low cut, an overhead, a thrust, a thrust from above, a shield bash, each with a lunge. Each defence by the blow it meets: a shield high or low (`DEF_POSE`, `DEF_LOW`), a parry high or low (`HOLD.*.parry`, `parryLow`), a dodge back or a step aside; a heavy blow blocked pushes him a step back; a wound of a quarter of his health staggers him.
- **Future:** looks against beasts and big ones are their own rows of `DUEL.moves` (the owner's: different attacks by the enemy's kind).
- Measured (`tests/duel.js`, 8 v 8 to the last man, 4 seeds): melee ×1.96 as long as with neither (blocks and parries alone ×1.49). A whole battle (38 v 38, the lab's AI on both sides, 10 seeds): the middle one about 71 s against 57 s.
- `DUEL.on` false: none of it (`tests/harness.js` turns it off for the older suites; their fingerprints hold).

## Bodies: room for each man (2026-10-29)

`battle/battle.js` (`JOSTLE`, `roomOf`). The owner: "each unit not to collide or collapse to each other". Each man keeps his body's room, as wide as he is drawn: two of a side the mean of theirs apart (foot 11 px, a rider 16; big ones 18 as ever, was 8 for all), foes face to face 0.8 of it (8.8 px for two on foot: inside every reach); two passes a frame, so a pile settles. On the world map (world battles) each is drawn `WORLD_BATTLE.sprite` (3) times as big, so his room is 3 times as much (33 px for two on foot, as the formations' slots are already), and foes stand as far apart as 0.85 of the shorter reach allows (they still strike each other). Each fighter's melee reach is scaled the same on the world map (`WORLD_BATTLE.melee`, `meleeK`: worldbattle.js `WorldBattleSetup#spawn`, with a duel's slack, the riders' contact and a bowman's blade), so foes in melee stand about 26 px apart, not 9 (the owner: "the units overlap in world battle"). Each fighter's melee reach is scaled the same on the world map (`WORLD_BATTLE.melee`, `meleeK`: worldbattle.js `WorldBattleSetup#spawn`, with a duel's slack, the riders' contact and a bowman's blade), so foes in melee stand about 26 px apart, not 9 (the owner: "the units overlap in world battle"). Measured (38 v 38 with spears, lab AI both sides, 8 seeds): men within 10 px of another 56% → 5% of the time, riders within 9 px of someone 31% → 0%; the battles' length about the same; about 6% more time a frame. And a beaten army's last few fall back whatever their morale once under `FOE_CMD.hopeless` (15%) of your strength (commander.js `beatenArmy`: two riders no longer circle twenty men for ever). `JOSTLE.on` false: 8 px and one pass (`tests/harness.js`; the older suites' fingerprints hold). Tests: bodies.

## Phones: resolution and frame rate (2026-10-29)

The owner: "it lags on mobile". Settings → **Resolution** (core.js `RENDER.cap`, `dprOf`: Full the screen's own density, **Balanced** 2 (the default), Fast 1.5) and **Frame rate** (`RENDER.fps`: 60, or 30; main.js `loop` skips a frame sooner than .65 of its interval, so a 120 Hz screen draws 60, a 90 Hz one all of its own). The art is whole pixels, so fewer canvas pixels shown at the window's size (pixelated) look the same, the text a little coarser; every place that read `devicePixelRatio` reads `dprOf()`. And: `reducedMotion` (vfx.js) asks the browser once, not each call; the travel-detection eye (render.js `travelDetectionState`) is worked out at most every 100 ms, drawn only; a multi-army battle's `relation` (sides.js) is remembered while the jostle runs. Measured (headless, 844 × 390 at density 3, a world battle of about 90): the canvas 2534 × 1176 → 1692 × 783; the map's frame 13.1 → 5.0 ms to draw, 7.0 → 5.4 ms to update. Tests: render.

## Figures and their gear (2026-10-31)

The owner: "a body sprite plus separately drawn gear ... for ALL figures". Drawn only: no rule reads it.
- **Body and gear:** every figure, the battle's and the map's small people, is its body (`people.js` `drawFigureBody`, `drawMapBody`: template, clothes, helm, cape, limbs) and its gear apart (`gfx/figures.js`): the weapon in the hand (`HandWeapon`, drawn as the battle's: `weapons.js` `drawBlade`, tools' heads too), a bow, crossbow, sling or staff by the body's frame (`ShooterGear`), a shield (`ShieldGear`), a quiver or bolt case (`QuiverGear`), a bow put away in melee mode (`SlungBow`), a drover's goad (`GoadGear`, `o.goad`: the caravan's), and the map's held item, shield and load (`MapHeld`, `MapShield`, `MapLoad`). Each hangs from the body's anchor for the pose: `hand`, `off` (the shield arm), `guard` (a shield raised), `back`.
- **Layers, one table** (`GEAR.layer`): below 0 behind the body (drawn only where the body is not: a slung shield on the far arm, a quiver, a bow on the back, a load), above 0 in front (the weapon, a raised shield). Sprites face right and are mirrored, so the near side is always the picture's own. In battle the weapon in hand goes behind the body while it points up and back over the shoulder (`GEAR.behind`: a sword on the march, a wind-up; `weapons.js` `heldNow`, `render.js` `drawUnit`), but only while at least `GEAR.peek` (0.4) of its pixels still show past the body (`weapons.js` `peeks`, kept on the body sprite by weapon and place); else it goes in front.
- **A weapon in every pose (2026-11-01):** the owner: "the hero battle unit should also draw a weapon from start to finish". Two causes found: a sword, greatsword or hammer on the shoulder (walking, running, climbing) or in a wind-up went behind the body, which hid all but its hilt (fixed by `GEAR.peek`); knocked down, the figure was the corpse's, its weapon taken off as if dropped (now `sprites.js` `downedOf`: the same man standing and lying with his weapon in his hand, kept apart from the corpse's record, `render.js` `drawDowned`). The same holds for companions, lords and troops. Bare hands still draw none.
- **Nothing cut off:** a finished figure is body and gear laid in one grid `GEAR.room` px bigger and outlined once (`FigureRig.frame`), cut to the old frame (24 × 19 on foot, 32 × 32 riding) grown only where gear reaches past it, evenly about its middle, so its feet stay the cell's bottom middle and callers need no change. A pike, a levelled lance, a dismounted rider's lance stand their full length; on the map a pole stands `GEAR.rise` px over a small figure's head.
- **What a unit carries:** a shield in your hero's or a companion's off hand shows (`lookOf` `offShield`: drawn only; the look's `shield`, which `CharacterMind` reads, is untouched); an archer in melee mode has his bow on his back; a rider off his horse carries his lance.
- **Kept:** the battle's bodies by pose in `spr` (the weapon in hand drawn live, as before); the finished figures (the map's marchers and small people, the cards, the march preview) in `FIGURES`, an LRU of `GEAR.cap` that never lets go of one drawn this frame or the last (`render.js` counts frames), so a map frame still draws each marcher once. Your hero's and a companion's battle body is keyed without the melee weapon held apart: one body for every sword.
- **Health by group:** no bar over each man unless the HP bars button is on (your hero, companions and leaders included); each group's health stands as a strip under its flag (a bar on the left of its icon before 2026-11-10), its men's health now over theirs as the battle began, every side's (`battlehud.js` `drawGroupHealth`, `GROUP_HP`).
- **Group flags** (2026-11-10, the owner: "make a unit flag scale based on camera zoom? And make it a bit smaller and more flag like", then a page of hanging standards: "The flag should be like this"; `ui/moraleicons.js` `flagPic`, `FLAG`, `iconZoom`, `UICON`): the marker over each group is a banner hung from a crossbar: its cloth taller than wide in its side's colours, edged (gold when selected), a gold trim inside its edge, its kind's glyph on it and a gold fringe at its foot; the crossbar's ends gold knobs, on a pole through its middle from a gold finial down to its men; its health a bar standing on its left, as tall as its cloth (`battlehud.js` `drawGroupHealth`; the owner: "HP should still at the left side"), its badges beside it as before. The men's own flags too (the owner: "do this kind of flag for a faction flag on units as well only for a large one. But the pole should still at left side"; `gfx/marchgear.js` `hangBackFlag`, `BATTLE_BANNER.hang`): a realm's banner at a flag man's back hangs from a crossbar out from his pole (the pole where it was, at the cloth's side), 9 × 11 with a field's pixel each side of its device (the owner: "Can you make a faction flag not cut the emblem off?"; then "May need a bit more area at the end?": the holdings', spearmen's and army cards' banners 13 × 9, their device two columns from the pole, plain cloth after it, then their fly's wave and a deeper swallow tail: `BATTLE_BANNER`, `BANNER`), gold-trimmed and fringed, the realm's device in its middle, its foot swinging a pixel as he moves; in battle and on the march alike. A rag, a pennant and a spearman's cloth as before. A quarter smaller (`UICON.k` 1.5, was 2), and it scales with the camera: its zoom against its first view (a field's fitted view, the map's own zoom), held within `UICON.zoom` (0.6 to 1.6), each art pixel a whole number of device pixels. Tests: battleui, figures, worldbattleedges, marchgear.
- **The banner on the card** (the owner, 2026-11-01: "remove a floating banner, show the banner detail only when you inspect them along with the detail window"; `sprites.js` `bannerSprite`, `BANNER`): no banner flies over an army. A party's banner is a flag tab on the top left corner of its hover card and long-press card (`ui.js` `tipFlag`, `TIP_FLAG`: cut to its pixels, 2 CSS px a pixel, `pixelated`; `style.css` `.tipflag`), its pole down the card's edge, the header clear of it; none for a beast or a place. Over each army only its label (`render.js` `armyTagAt`, at `armyHome`: the point its rings are drawn on); your eye and badges over your hero (`heroTagAt`). The one banner left on the map is yours, on its pole in your camp; a world battle's arrival marker keeps its crest. Tests: `bannercard`, `bannercardphone`.
- **Flags and torches on the march** (2026-11-01; the owner: "20% of unit for each group", torches "every army can use during night time"; `gfx/marchgear.js`). A fifth of each group (at least one) flies the army's colours (`o.banner()`): a spearman's on a longer spear of his own, the cloth hanging under its head, anyone else's on a short pole at his back; leaders carry none. After dark a fifth of the men with a free off hand hold a torch, lit (`LIGHTQ`), fading in with the dark; an army of shields lights one all the same. The same men keep them (a hash of the marcher id within his group, never a dice roll). Shares, sizes and the two switches in `MARCH_GEAR`; pictures cached in `MARCH_FLAGS`. Figures keep `hand`, `free` and `back` points (`FIG_ANCHORS`); `weaponSprite`/`drawBlade` take an optional length. In battle too (the owner: "the flag should be at the battle as well?"): the same share of each group in its own army's colours (`u.sourceArmy`, yours `P`), a spearman's cloth under his spear's head as he holds it (`weapons.js` `heldNow` gives the weapon and its angle), anyone else's at his back; drawing only (`battleFlag`, `battleFlagBack`, `battleFlagFront`, drawn by `render.js` `drawUnit`). Tests: `marchgear`; the picture sheet `-Suites visual/marchgear`. In battle (2026-11-07; the owner: "not too square and big enough to be distinctive") each flag is the army card's banner in small (`sprites.js` `bannerCloth`, `BATTLE_BANNER`): a 10 × 8 swallow-tailed banner, a rag or a pennant, its folds lit and the army's device in its field, on a back pole tall enough to fly over his head, or under the head of a flag man's spear drawn `BATTLE_BANNER.pole` longer (drawing only); no outline. The same flags everywhere (2026-11-07; the owner: "align flags use in all place. Camps, building"): the map's marchers and the army screen's previews fly the battle's (`poleFlagSprite`: the spear and its cloth as one picture; `battleBackFlag`), and a flag on a pole (`structures.js` `flagSprite`, `drawFlag`, `FLAG_POLE`) is the same banner flying from a 15 px pole: over a holding's centre its owner's (`holdingFlag`: the realm's device, yours the crown), over a bandit hideout a black rag with a skull, over a travellers' camp their people's pennant (`Site#flag`), over your siege camp yours, on the loading screen and as a realm's mark in the windows (`window.js` `facFlag`). An army's map banner (`bannerSprite`) is outlined inside too. A building's painted wall hangings stay its culture's (the owner's colour is the building redesign's stage 3).
- **Outposts** (named standards in the code until 2026-11-12, the owner: "refactor the name standards to outpost"; their sites' saved type is still `'standard'`, `data/outposts.js` `OUTPOST_TYPE`, so older saves load) (2026-11-10, the owner: "every factions will also have a static flag in their territory, and around that they may have tower or buildings to defense ... This tower and flag can be captured"; doc/OUTPOSTS_DESIGN.md, phase 1; `world/outposts.js` `Outpost`, `Outposts`, `outpostSprite`; `data/outposts.js` `OUTPOST`, `OUTPOST_PLACE`): each realm's standards stand on its land, at its deposits (the land's natural resources), road crossings, fords and passes, placed when a game begins by a pure reckoning of the map (no random draw: the same every time, every other site where it was), the best of each province in turn (`Outposts.place`: by why, more on a border, a kind it already has worth less), clear of anything built and of each other. A standard is a site of its own kind: a stepped stone plinth, an iron pole with a gilt finial, a crossbar with two banners of its realm's colours and device and a rope's tassel; level 2 a watchtower beside it, level 3 a stone tower, in its realm's builders' style; its guards by it (3, 6, 10). Its page: whose it is, what stands there, its guards; Attack its guards (an act of war on a realm at peace with you: encounter kind `standard`, `ON_WIN.outpost`), then stay by it 4 hours (`Outposts.hour`) to raise your banner: for the realm you serve (`P.fac`), for yourself once you hold a holding, else hand it to one of the realms near it or leave it to no one; renown +4 and the realm's thanks. A realm's guards come back one a day. Its tiles are kept from every holding's building (`Outposts.reserve`, `lots.js` `occReserve`: occupancy -3); a realm fallen, its standards are no one's; two of one name, the second named by its compass from its holding. Phase 2: its sentries (up to three of its realm's foot soldiers) walk their beat round it in your sight, by the game's clock (`Outpost#draw`, `OUTPOST.sentries`); held in peace it grows a level (`OUTPOST.grow`: 8 days and 14 timber to a watchtower, 14 days and 8 timber and 20 stone to a stone tower) paid from its realm's nearest holding's stores (`Outpost#work`, `supplier`), or waits on them (its page says on what); taken, a tower lies broken (its guards one level fewer, its top gone in its picture) and is mended 4 days on for 6 timber and 6 stone (`OUTPOST.repair`). Phase 3 (`Outpost#contest`, `taken`, `raid`, `watch`; `Outposts.day`, `guarded`; `OUTPOST.capture`, `lair`, `own`, `sight`, `safe`): a lord at war with its realm with twice its guards by it takes it; a band by a weakly held one makes it its lair (their rag, their men its guards) and raids the roads round it from there; you drive them out as you take one; a lair counts in its province's danger (`provinces.js`); yours and your employer's lift the fog round them; no band is raised by a held one (`world.js` `spawnBandit`); guards you slew in a lost fight stay dead (`engage.js` `encLost` now hands its kind the slain); yours are manned from your nearest holding's garrison. Phase 4 (the owner: "can we rename it to something more straightforward?": an outpost to the player, `OUTPOST.T.n`, each one's name ending "outpost"; the code keeps standard): quests (`world/quests.js` `OutpostQuest`, `OutlairQuest`, `MendQuest`, `OUTPOST_JOB`): a lord at war asks you to take an outpost for his realm, the nearest held by a realm at war with his or by a band, or to retake one of his realm's own (`home0`, the realm it began with); done once its banner is his realm's by your hand, by serving him or handing it to his realm (it is always among the realms you may hand it to). A captain of the watch asks you to drive a band from the outpost near that it made its lair; a steward asks you to mend the broken tower of one of his realm's: an outpost's page now has Mend its tower (6 timber and 6 stone from your packs, at once). Phase 5, its tower in battle (`OutpostTower`, `OUTPOST.tower`): a fight at an outpost is fought on the world map (`worldbattle.js` `worldBattleEligible`); a standing tower's footprint is raised (`OutpostTower.lay`: 2 levels a watchtower, 3 a stone tower), sheer but its three-cell ladder on the side away from you; up to 2 or 4 of its archers posted on its top (`man`, `post`: group `e0_tower`, `g.post`, skipped by the captains in `commander.js` and by the muster), drawn up on its platform (the field's `lift`, read into `u.z` by `BattleTerrain#update`), seen over the woods under it, shooting from its height, their arcs loosed from there (`projectiles.js`); no blade between its top and the ground (`BattleTerrain#apart`, `actors.js`), so you climb or shoot them down; no sentries drawn on the map while a battle is fought there. Its land (`OUTPOST.land`, `Outposts.claims`, `landKey`, `live`; `territory.js` `landCompute`, `landFresh`): each held one claims the land round it for its realm (reach 5, 7, 9 by level) after every holding's own claim and only where its reach beats theirs, its tiles its realm's nearest holding's (`landRun` from its tile), so borders follow the outposts and move when one is taken; none for a lair, one no one holds or one not yet handed over, and none while a new world's first land is reckoned (`live` off till this game's are placed). More quests (`world/quests.js` `HoldQuest`, `OutscoutQuest`, `OUTPOST_JOB.hold`, `.scout`, `outpostJobsHour` from `Outposts.hour`): hold an outpost until relief comes (12 hours by it, three waves of the foe's men marching on it over the map from its foe's way, in the bands' list with `wave` (`holdWaveStep`, from `brains.js` BandBrain), met as a party encounter with `hold` and `post` and the outpost's guards as your allies (those who fall taken off its guards after: `HoldQuest#ended`, their party's start `n0` less what is left), their archers on its tower (`OutpostTower.of`, `man`, `group`: the ladder away from the attackers); their end heard on `battle:ended`; a wave at it with you away takes it; left too long the job lost, beaten the outpost theirs), and scout up to three of the foes' outposts unseen (within 6 tiles to look, never within 2.5 of a guarded one by day). In battle its tower's ladder is drawn (`OutpostTower.draw`, `ladderSprite`, `OUTPOST_ART.ladder`: a pixel ladder made once a height, its rails lit and shaded and narrowing up from splayed feet, knots in the grain, a rung every 3 px with its top lit and its joints shaded, rope lashings, now and then a snapped rung, leaning out from the wall with its shadow on it) and its archers fall back from it once a foe is up (`OutpostTower.step`, `OUTPOST.tower.look`). Tests: standards.
- **A force's own flag** (2026-11-10, the owner: "When siege or battle with garrison the flag seems to be bugged and use bandit flag"; `world/parties.js` `forceKindOf`): men who come into a fight as a plain record (a holding's garrison riding out, a patrol, band or companion party joining, `fights.js` `forceOf`, `garrisonForce`; a village's folk and an estate's guards, `folk.js`, `estate.js`) fly the flag of where they came from: a free hamlet's or village's men its grey banner (they read as a band before and flew the bandits' red rag), a patrol its pennant, a raider or goblin band its own rag. Their kind is as before, so nothing else changes. Tests: parties.
- **The map's people** (2026-11-01; the owner: "can you update encounters and old sprites to look like current sprite designs?"; `people.js` `MAP_FIGS`). Every person on the world map is a battle figure (`peopleSprite`, its gear apart: figures.js), drawn `WORLD_BATTLE.sprite/scale` as big, as the marchers are (`drawFig`): road encounters' people standing, walking, kneeling (`figKneel`) and fallen (`figDown`, in blood), a rider and a horse alone, soldiers and bands (`figFollower`), workers and their loads (`figWorker`; one carrying to his holding: `figCarrier`), a builder on a building site (`structures.js` `drawSite`), refugees of their own people with their bundles (`figRefugee`), and ships' crews (`figCrew`, painted at double density: `shipSprite(...).hi`). Loads and a bard's lute are battle gear (figures.js `BackLoad`, `LuteGear`); a tended man wears a `bandage`. They walk the marchers' four steps (`figPose`). Each is kept in `FIGURES`, marked `dense`; `figPic` gives its map-size picture for clicks, boxes and banners. `MAP_FIGS.on` false (or the march off) brings back the old small figures (`mapPerson`, `mapFigure`), which the menu art and cards still use. Tests: `mapfigs`; the sheet `-Suites visual/mapfigs`.
- **A building site's crew** (`gfx/structures.js` `drawSite`, `SITE`; `tests/buildsite.js`): of the holding's people; builders walk the scaffold's planks to a spot and hammer there, the soldiers' overhead blow three times with a spark at each strike, then on to the next; a small site has one, a middling one a builder and a carrier bringing planks in over his shoulder (`LOADS.builder`, `LOAD_ART.planks`), a big one two builders and the carrier. Off the battle's figures, the small builder as before.
- **Horses** (2026-11-02; the owner: "can you fix the mounts sprites? i mean the horse legs", then "make sure to make it look like horse"; `people.js` `HORSE_ART`, `drawHorse`, `horseLeg`). A horse (and a stag, its antlers on its ears) is a horse's shape in the same 32 px cell: an arched neck rising from the withers, a wedge head with ears and an eye, a deep chest, a barrel, rounded quarters, the tail from the croup; the rider's seat, the saddle, the stirrup and a knight's barding (laid over its body) where they were. Its legs bend: a forearm, knee and cannon before; a gaskin and a hock angled back behind; a fetlock and a hoof; the near pair lighter. One table of each frame's joints (hip, knee or hock, fetlock, hoof, in px above the ground), drawn by one helper: standing square (0-1), a four-beat walk (2-5: far hind, far fore, near hind, near fore lifted in turn, the body's bob as before), a gallop (15-18: reaching, the push off, gathered off the ground, the hind landing; the neck stretched out, the body highest gathered and lowest landing, the head against it, the tail a frame behind the hip, the rider's seat a frame behind the horse). A loose horse (`horseSprite`, `figHorse`) and a fallen one (`drawMountDown`) are the same horse. The ram and the wolf keep their own; the map's small horse (`MAP_HORSE`) is as it was. Cached as before (same `spr`/`FIGURES` keys and cells). `HORSE_ART.on` false: today's horse, pixel for pixel. W6 re-recorded (the mounted men in its views). Tests: `horselegs`; the sheet `-Suites visual/horses` (old beside new at 8x, 2x and 1x, and the silhouettes). Second pass (2026-11-03; the owner: "a second pass for leggier horses", +1 px, then "the head when running is too small", "not too square", from his photos): `HORSE_ART.leg` (1) px longer legs under the same body (`horseBuild`, `sizedHorse`): the body and saddle that much higher, each joint's height × (8+leg)/8 so the gaits keep their shape, the tail with the croup, the fallen horse's legs as much longer, the rider sunk a px into the saddle (`sized.sink`), so he, his anchors and his height are where they were. Its own neck and one head (`sized.head`, hung at `sized.poll` by shape, the same pixels in every frame): a thick neck from the shoulders at about 45° with a maned, arched crest (lower and forward at the gallop), a long tapering head from the poll (two ears pricked forward, the eye high, a rounded jowl over a dark jaw, a rounded muzzle and its nostril). The gathered gallop (pose 17) is the references' suspension: the fore legs folded back under the chest, the hind reaching forward. `HORSE_ART.tall` false: the first pass, pixel for pixel. The sheet `-Suites visual/horses` shows the head before and after at 6×, and before, after, the first pass and the old horse side by side, in battle and on the march, at 1× and 2×. W6 re-recorded. Detail (2026-11-07; the owner: "make sure mount have more details. Current one looks really ugly"; `people.js` `horseDetail`, `HORSE_TACK`): a thick mane in strands down the crest, a fuller tail, the quarters and shoulder rounded by a light patch and a shaded line; a horse's bridle (the headstall down its cheek, a steel bit, the reins back to the withers) and a studded breast strap; under every saddle, ridden or not, a cloth of the side's colour and the girth over it (the ram's and the wolf's too). A stag has no tack but its saddle and a small lyre of antlers rising from its poll, every pixel joined by an edge. Proportions (the owner: "the mount portions still weird, check distort pixel"): the barrel two rows deeper, as deep as the legs below it are long (no table on stilts); a barded horse's caparison down to its belly, its hem dagged and gilt-pointed; no reins (they read as a stick across the neck) and no light patches on shoulder and haunch (blotches); the ram's curls two-pixel arcs, never a lone dot; `horselegs` checks no mount has a lone speck but its eye. Coat (the owner: "can you add detail like furs, or shading to mounts"; `people.js` `mountCoat`, `MOUNT_COAT`): every mount's main colour rounded down each column of its body in tones between its ramp's (a sheen under its top, a shade over its lower third, a deeper foot), a wolf's, a warg's and a ram's shade edge ragged in tufts with two-pixel strands of fur down the flank, light and dark; never a lone pixel. `MOUNT_COAT.on` false: flat as before. The ram's fleece is cream in curls (`PPAL.fleece`), a wolf's and a warg's coat a dark saddle down the back and a pale chest (`engine/beasts.js` `MOUNT_ART`).
- **Deaths** (2026-11-03; the owner: "make sure all sprites has correct dead sprite and more variant of dead sprite", then "make sure that not all monsters have red blood"; `sprites.js` `DEATHS`, `deathOf`; `vfx.js` `drawFallen`): every kind falls into one of several deaths, picked by a hash of its id. People have five (face down, on his back, curled, on his knees, arms flung), with the shield beside him and sometimes the helm rolled off. A rider is thrown clear of his mount, lies across it, or is pinned under it; the mount lies on its side or its belly, a wolf as a wolf. Each beast has three of its own (`engine/beasts.js` `PX.BEAST_DEATHS`), and the hunt's game has three (`critters.js` `critterDead`). Blood is by kind (`DEATHS.blood`): ichor, slime, murk, fire with embers, none for the undead, red otherwise. Drawn only and cached; `DEATHS.on=false` draws the one corpse of before. Tests: `deaths`, `visual/deaths`.
- **Fire** (2026-11-01; the owner: "can you change a fire effect to be more realistic?"; `gfx/fire.js`, one table `FLAMES`). Every flame is the engine's `PX.fireFlame`: a white-yellow heart, orange, deep red tips, tongues licking unevenly, each frame (8) drawn once by kind, frame and lean and kept. The frame shown is noise of the clock and the fire's id, so no two fires keep step, never `Math.random`. A camp fire sends up embers and thin smoke and casts a light (`fireCamp`); a carried torch's flame trails back as he walks. Lights are only light (`fireLights`: an uneven stepped glow, a pool on the ground; each fire draws its own flame, none doubled). A fire arrow streams its flame behind it, trails smoke and throws sparks where it strikes (`fireArrows`, `fireSparks`); a spell's explosion leaves a blaze (`fireBlazes`), drawn-only effects (`fxp`), never the battle's own. Nothing drawn off screen. `FLAMES.on=false` gives the old drawing exactly. Tests: `fire`; the sheet `-Suites visual/fire`, its cost `-Suites visual/fireperf`.
- Tests: `figures`, `heroweapon` (your hero with seven weapons in every pose, both facings, knocked down and on the march, lords riding and on foot: the weapon's pixels seen, drawn with it and without); `mapfigures`, `mappolish` updated; reports `visual/figuresheet` (a sheet of figures, gear and banners, its IMG line a PNG), `visual/heroweapon` (the audit sheet: every pose with each weapon, a cell with no weapon seen ringed red) and `visual/figureperf` (map and battle frame times, the pictures kept).

## Unit portraits and the sprite view (2026-10-29)

The owner: "change all unit icon to portrait and add a button to view unit in sprite in detail page".
- **Portraits:** every unit type shows its painted portrait wherever the windows show a unit: recruiting, the party page, prisoners, cards and the unit card. `ui/battlehud.js` `portrait()` takes the named face first (`NAMED_PORTRAITS`), then its kind's (`NamedPortraits.unit`, `js/data/unitportraits.js` `UNIT_PORTRAITS`), else the sprite. The copies are 256 × 256 WebP in `art/portrait/game/`, about 25 KB each, written by `art/portrait/gamecopies.py` from the PNGs. The battle's own cards (the group cards, the selection rows) keep the troops' sprites (`hudPic`), so you read the field at a glance; the customizable player keeps a generated one.
- **The sprite view** (`ui/unitcard.js` `SpriteView`): on the unit card, View sprite swaps the portrait for the unit's battle figure, standing, enlarged and in whole pixels (still for now: the owner). View portrait brings the portrait back.
- Tests: `portraits`.

## Proficiency (2026-11-08)

doc/PROFICIENCY_DESIGN.md. The owner: "your proficiency grows as you do certain actions ... and the level progress will just based on your proficiency progress"; "a proficiency cap for each at 99".
- **Proficiencies** (were disciplines; `SKILLS`, `P.sk`, numbers in `data/progression.js` `PROF`): from 0, with no top for now (the owner, 2026-11-08: "remove the proficiency cap for now, may be max at 9999"; `PROF.max` 9999, was 99), rising by use as before; a made character (a lord, a chieftain, a companion, a past) starts at no more than the old 99 (`PROF.made`, `SK_MADE`), and the effects made for the old top (prices, Survival's food, Blocking's stamina, sight) read a proficiency only to 99, its gear and tree on top as ever (`skillSafe`), so none turns over; the rest (damage, health, stamina, healing, the trees' points) grow on with it (`Character#train`; a level takes `SK_XP` = 10 + 0.3625 × level: 2.5 times the 4 + 0.145 × level first set, the owner, 2026-11-08, "proficiency increase too fast": a small battle won now gives about 1.5 levels of your weapon's at 20-30 and 1 of Leadership, was 3.5 and 3; 0 to 99 about 2750 practice; your level follows). Their power is level / 9.9 (`trained`, `skill`), so 99 is the old 10; the Tree and gear add in power (+1 is ten levels). A past starts its own free (`PROF.pastPer` 10 a point, `P.sk0`), not counted toward your level.
- **Level from them** (`Character#profProgress`, `relevel`): each proficiency's weight × L(L+1)/2 (`PROF.weight`: the easy ones count 0.8 or 0.6), less the past's start; level n needs 50 × n(n+1)/2 (`PROF_NEED`). Each level is a choice of health, stamina or mana (below). The same practice past 6 uses in an hour gives half, past 12 a quarter (`PROF.burst`, `damp`).
- **Rewards are practice** (`gainXp(n, kind)`, `REWARD_TRAIN`, `QUEST_TRAIN`): a battle trains your weapon, Leadership and Tactics; a tournament your weapon and Athletics; a quest what fits its kind. Companions grow the same way, from level 0; one who joins grown in a story (Ander, `compJoin`) keeps his level and gets what he was good at free (`sk0`). Lords and chieftains (`aiOwned`) keep their experience levels; their proficiencies are about half their level in power (`realms.js` `lordStats`, `leaders.js` `newChief`).
- **The combo's upgrades are nodes of the weapons' trees** (`data/tree.js` `combo3`, `fin`): a Third Blow at One-Handed, Two-Handed or Unarmed 15, a Stunning or Crushing Finisher at 60 (a finisher brings its third blow; the first finisher learned rules). `battle/combat.js` `comboBlows`, `heroFin`.
- **The skill page** (`ui/talents.js`): Proficiency (the level bar, the level's choice when one waits, then each proficiency by group: `PROF_GROUPS`, `profBody`), Skill trees (`skTreeBody`), Trait (your attack, the moves everyone has, your race and past: `HERO_TRAITS`, `traitBody`), Skill bar.
- **Saves:** `SAVE_V` 4; older saves are refused.
- Tests: `proficiency`.

### Skill trees by proficiency, and the level's choice (2026-11-08)

The owner: "make each skill tree separated by proficiency"; "when you level up you choose to upgrade health, stamina or magic"; asked, milestone picks and only the choice at a level; then "freely spend": points.
- **A tree for each proficiency** (`data/tree.js` `PROF_TREE`, `TREE_AT`): its milestones (15, 35, 60, 90 for One-Handed, Two-Handed, Archery, Spellcraft, Unarmed and Leadership; 15, 35, 60 for the rest), two nodes at each. Its points (`TREE_PT`: one each 15 levels of it, 6 at 90; the owner: "player can freely spend based on their preferences") are spent as you like on any node its milestones have opened that grows from a node learned on the tier below (`core/talents.js` `points`, `canTake`; `pending`: a proficiency with a point and a node to take). Unlearning costs gold (15 × your level), gives the point back, and is refused for a node a higher one grows from (`canRefund`). A big tree's 8 nodes for 6 points: a choice still. The old radial tree (`TREE_BRANCHES`, hybrids, the brawler's chain, `TREE_COMBO`, the Crossroads) is gone and its nodes rehomed: the actives (Whirlwind, Blade Dash in One-Handed; Leap Slam, Earthquake in Two-Handed; Rain of Arrows in Archery; Fireball, Frost Nova in Spellcraft; Healing Light in Medicine; War Cry, War Banner in Leadership), the old perks, the keystones (Berserker's Oath, Lone Wolf, Arcane Surge, Warlord, Open Hand). Your past's origin stays (`ORIGINS`: no tree, its small head start).
- **The level's choice** (`data/progression.js` `LEVEL_UP`, `core/state.js` `levelPick`, `lvPicked`): each level a choice waiting (`P.lvUp`), +10 health, +10 stamina or +10 mana (`P.lvPick`); a level gives nothing else of these (it gave 10 health, 2 stamina and 2 mana by itself). Made on the Proficiency tab. A save from before: every level a choice waiting.
- **The level box** (`gfx/render.js` `lvBox`): the choices waiting, a level's and a tree's; its click opens the level's choice, else the tree that waits.
- **The page** (`ui/talents.js` `skTreeBody`, `treeSvg`, `treeNodePane`, `TREE_ART`; `fieldbook.css` "skill trees"; the owner: "not an actual tree but a tree node path with more cool animation"): the tree the whole page, and under it the switch between the proficiencies (the owner, 2026-11-08: "the selection between proficiency should be at the bottom and have an arrow to click or swap between them"): the one before faded, ‹ this one (its level, its points to spend) ›, the one after faded, round and round (`tree-step`; ← and → too); a tap on its name opens the list of them all, each with its gold count of points to spend (`tree-list`, `G.skTreeList`); on an upright phone the chosen node's words sit under the tree, as large as the page's (15 px; its Learn the pane's width), at most half the stage, scrolling (the owner: "the description is too small"); the shown one as a path of nodes: its root (the proficiency's initials) at the foot, each milestone a row of two nodes above, every node linked to both of the row below; a rail at the side fills with the proficiency's level, a mark at each milestone. Links learned gold with light flowing up them, the ways open now dashed and flickering, the rest dim; nodes hexagons (a keystone a diamond, an active ringed with a turning ring), learned gold and glowing, open pulsing, reached bronze, not yet dark; faint motes twinkle behind. Choosing a node shows it beside the path with Learn (a point) or Unlearn (gold) and why not. Switching trees, or opening the page, the nodes fly out of the root to their places and the links and the rail draw after them, row by row; a node just learned bursts. Nothing moves with reduced motion.
- **Companions** spend their own trees' points, by their own proficiencies (their page, as yours). **Lords and chieftains** spend theirs themselves (`characters.js` `autoTree`, `CLASS_PICK`: the nodes a class takes first, else the lowest it can), their trees as if each proficiency they have were 8 × their level (`AI_TREE`), re-spent as they rise (`AUTO_LV`); a caster has Spellcraft.
- Fingerprints: W7, B2, the battle suites and `aishare` re-recorded (your record's fields, the others' trees, the level's numbers); with the others choosing no node and the old tp and level numbers, the old code and the new give the same. Free spending: W7 again (the lords' trees; with `autoTree` off, the same as before).
- Tests: `talents`, `talentrules`, `levelbox`, `characters`, `proficiency`.

### Skyrim's loop, no attributes (2026-11-08)

doc/PROFICIENCY_DESIGN.md §11. The owner: "a scale like Skyrim: XP from quest, battle, etc. and from proficiency; a level a skill point for the trees"; no milestones, no top, smaller buffs, pasts at 15 at most, attributes into the trees' bonuses. This replaces the level from progress, the level's choice and the trees' own points above.
- **Level** (`data/progression.js` `LEVEL`, `characters.js` `gainXp`, `addXp`, `xpNeed`): experience from deeds and from each level a proficiency reaches (that level's number); the next level 75 + 50 × level; a skill point each. Your damage +1 a level. The Proficiency tab shows the level, its XP and the points to spend; the level box (`lvBox`) the points.
- **Points** (`core/talents.js` `points`): one pool, any tree, no milestone (a node grows from the tier below). **A node's strength** by its proficiency (`treeScale`: a quarter at 0, whole at 100, slowly more; slots and prisoners whole: `TREE_SCALE.whole`). The tree's rail shows its proficiency against 100; the node's pane how strong it is now.
- **Proficiencies** have no top (`PROF.max` Infinity); their power `profPow` (6 at 100, 10 at 500); the old-top effects read them to 99 (`skillSafe`); the made ones' levels give their old power (`PROF.per` 16.7).
- **Attributes gone:** past, race and nodes give their bonuses (`ATTRS[k].fx`, `attrMods`, `TREE.o_<past>`, `TREE.r_<race>`); a companion's own (`o.own`); `HERO_BASE` your hero's start; checks read the relevant proficiency (`ENC_STAT`, `QUEST_CHECKS`, `attrCheck` for a story's condition); the Character tab shows what the trees give (`ui/character.js` `treeTotals`).
- **Pasts** at 15 at most (`PROF.pastPer` 7.5). **Lords** spend a point a level (`autoTree`: class nodes, else their strongest proficiency's tree); **companions** yours to spend, theirs while away.
- Tests: `proficiency`, `talents`, `talentrules`, `characters`, `levelbox`, `progression`, `races`, `encounters`, `newencounters`, `leaders`.
- Fingerprints: W7, B2, the battle suites (actorrules 23, attackrules 26, battleresolution 15, battlesetup 10) and `aishare` re-recorded with the reason (your record, everyone's numbers and trees; no battle rule changed); W1-W6, W8 and `rts` hold.
- **The round tree** (step 2; the owner: "one big circle node tree spans across each proficiency"; doc/PROFICIENCY_DESIGN.md §11): one wheel round your origin, a slice a proficiency (`data/tree.js` `TREE_RING`), a run of two bonus nodes between each two tiers and two past the last (`PATH_RUN`, `PATH_TAIL`, `TREE_PATH`, `PATH_NAME`; `<prof>_p<n>`, each on its `ring`; the owner, 2026-11-09: "add more attribute nodes to each proficiency"), the run's first joining both below, its last both above; 252 nodes; a node opens once one linked to it is learned (`core/talents.js` `reach`, `canRefund` keeps the rest reached, `pathTo`). The page (`ui/talents.js` `treeSvg`, `treePos`, `TREE_ROUND`) turns the shown slice to the top (the wheel spins to it), names beside its nodes, the rest dim; ◎ the whole wheel. Lords walk the way to their class's nodes (`CLASS_PICK`, an active first). Under your hands (step 3): a drag pans it, two fingers or the wheel zoom about the point under them, + and − a step under ◎ (`TREE_PAN`, `twSet`, `twZoom`); the view kept while the same slice is shown (`G.skVB`), another its own (`treeView`); a drag is never a tap. Tests: `talents` (the wheel and its gestures), `talentrules`, `characters`, `fieldbook`, `proficiency`, `rts`. W7, `aishare`, attackrules 1, battleresolution 8, battlesetup 8 re-recorded (the lords' trees).

### Drops in battle (2026-11-09)

doc/ACTION_DESIGN.md §0. The owner: "it drops something on the ground like just cool dot effects or small icons for exp and items"; "can you make this drops in the other battle type too?". In the battle map and the battle on the world map (`battle/drops.js` `BattleDrops`; its numbers `data/drops.js` `DROPS`):
- **Dots:** each foe of yours that falls bursts into gold dots that fly to your hero ("+8 XP" over him: what a kill is worth, paid at the end as ever) and blue ones into the soldier who struck it (attacks.js `earnXp`, `BattleDrops.struck`: his own experience, as ever).
- **On the ground:** a man may leave coins (`DROPS.coin`: 60%, 1-4) and a piece of gear (`DROPS.gear`: 10%), a beast its hide (`DROPS.beast`: 60%), bobbing and glinting where it fell; your hero or a companion within `DROPS.pick` draws them in, into purse and bag (a line over him; a full bag, overflow too, leaves it, not picked again till he steps away). Won, the rest is gathered; lost or fled, it stays.
- **Fair:** what the field gave (`tally`) comes off the fight's own spoils: its gold off the end's gold, gear in the field and no end gear roll, hides off a beast fight's (`resolution.js` conclude, `monsters.js` `monsterLoot`); the report lists it ("From the field: …": `fieldLine`). A group ranked up flashes in the report (`style.css` `.rank-flash`).
- **Apart:** its state is its own (kept by the battle, never on it or its men), and the tests' harness turns it off (`DROPS.on`), so no battle fingerprint moves. Tests: `drops`.

## Death to beasts, phase 1: living (2026-11-09)

doc/DEATH_DESIGN.md. The owner: "When you lost to the beast, or monster, you should be dead. But there might be a chance that you may survived which would be wounded but the day should be passed and the monster should not be there anymore." (`world/death.js` `Death`; its numbers `data/death.js` `DEATH`.)
- **When:** your whole side down to beasts alone (`beastLoss`: no one of yours or an ally still standing or fled off the field; no man against you, a band come into it). Else as before: carried off, or the men's capture rules (`Captivity.defeat` asks it first).
- **The roll** (`odds`): 30% to live, moved by Medicine, your armour, daylight or night, a holding or a road near, the beast's level, a great or mythic beast, a frost night, being alone; kept within 10–60%. Dying is phase 2: until then `DEATH.die` is off and every roll lives.
- **Living** (`fall`, then `after` on the report): a day passes (`Clock.passHours`); you wake where you fell at a fifth of your health, weak for 3 days (Timed `weak`: less damage and speed, `parties.js` `heroStats`); your army is gone for good, the wounded too; the beasts eat 60% of your food; your horse may bolt.
- **Companions:** each wakes hurt in the tavern of one of the three nearest holdings (`c.lost`; out of your band: `companions.js` `inBand`), a quest each (`ReunionQuest`, kind `reunion`). Found there, they are back. Left past 7 days, they leave your service for another holding (approval −2): win them back in its tavern, a talk (Persuasion, approval; one a day) or their pay (`cards`, actions `death-`). The Army window lists them apart.
- **Passengers:** each lives (50%: waits at the holding near, `EscortQuest` `wait`; found there, rides on) or dies.
- **The beast** goes home to its lair (away from it, if it fought you there), and may become a man-eater (60%, `eater`): a name, two levels and one beast more, a quest's bounty raised, talked of (`news.js` `maneater`), named on the map; slain, renown.
- **Quests** hear it (`hero:fell`), their days running on. The tests' harness turns it off (`DEATH.on`). Tests: `death`.
- **Dying** (phase 2, `died`; `DEATH.die`): your remains where you fell (`Remains`, a world object, saved as `remains`: everything you wore and carried and 60% of your gold, never fading; a band near may carry it all off, `plunder`, given back when you beat it; whoever comes to them takes what is left); the beast a man-eater with a bounty on it (`DEATH.eater.slayer`, paid when it is slain); a line in the chronicle; the Slain page (your days, level, renown, experience gained; its way out a new person, a save or the main menu, and it comes back while you are slain).
- **Graves** (phase 4, `Grave`, a kind of `Remains`; `DEATH.grave`): as you go on (`Legacy.reborn`), your remains are buried (`bury`): at your heir's holding (safe from robbers while it is theirs: `robbable`) or under a cairn by where you fell. It holds half the experience that hero gained (`P.xpEarned` × 0.5) and their grave goods. While it holds anything a light breathes over it, motes rising (`draw`); folk speak of it (`news.js` `grave`). Its page tells their life; **Pray** (`pray`) and the light flows over the map into you (`drawFlow`), the experience gained as it arrives (`flowed`: `addXp` as a gift, not counted toward your own grave); its goods can be taken. Spent, it stays.
- **Its place** (the owner: "Can the grave be a special area? And more detail"; `gfx/graveart.js` `GraveArt`, `DEATH.grave.art`): a ground of its own under everything (trodden earth, tufts, flowers); a cairn in a ring of standing stones, moss on its stones, your weapon planted in its top and a cloth of your colour on the hilt, or a fenced plot with its gate and a path of stones, a carved headstone, the mound in flowers, two flickering candles and your heir's banner; fireflies by night. Found as a place is (`world/discovery.js`: its title rises, "Cairn of …"), then on the minimap.
- **How you are laid to rest** (the owner: "if you died whatout much renown or if you died far away then you may have a grave or remains"; `Grave.tierOf`, `DEATH.grave.tiers`): far from anyone (over 22 tiles from a holding, off the roads), no one buries you: your remains lie where you fell (bones, a skull, a torn cloak, the weapon fallen by them), never talked of, or a cairn if you had 50 renown. Near people (the owner: "If you death near town, someone may burry for you right? But if you have more renown you may have a good looking grave"), someone buries you at the nearest holding, your heir at theirs, the finer the more renown you had (`DEATH.grave.tiers.near`): a small grave (a mound and a cross of sticks), from 50 a headstone over a flowered mound, from 200 a fenced plot with candles and a banner, from 500 a tomb (a tall stone on its steps, railed in iron, four candles). Each can be prayed at.
- **Your family's plot** (the owner: "Yes, make a family plot"; `Grave.inPlot`, `Legacy.plot`, `DEATH.grave.plot`): the first of your line buried by people makes it, by that holding, out past its lots and its estates' fields on clear ground, the nearer the woods the better (`Grave.ground`, `Grave.clear`; the owner: "Can you make it away from holding or estates? At least make it in forest or some where"; the lords' crypts likewise); every one buried by people after lies there too, in rows (4 to a row), a low stone wall round them and a gate under a wooden arch (`GraveArt.plot`); their pages say so. Those who fell far from anyone stay where they fell.
- **The lords' houses** (the owner: "Create a family system for lords as well. Lord die may also have grave but may be different grave"; `world/lineage.js`, `DEATH.house`): every lord is of a house (named for its founder, its seat the realm's holding nearest him). A lord dies for good put to death, or now and then (6%) slain as his army is beaten (`defeatLord`). His house buries him in its crypt at its seat (`Crypt`: one per house, a stone mausoleum on steps, its pediment and columns, the realm's banner over its door, candles; its page names its dead). Pay your respects there, once a day: its living lords think the better of you (+3). After 5 days of mourning, one of the house takes up his place (a new lord of its people, two levels less, a few men, at its seat; a Lord's heir may be a Lady). With the rule off (the tests' harness) no lord dies.
- **Burial takes time** (the owner: "What times when remains turn into grave or is there a chance to let the remain stay there"; `Grave.bury`, `Grave.daily`, `Grave.rest`, `DEATH.grave.find`): as you go on, your body lies where you fell, unburied, a place of its own ("Remains of …"): you may pray there, and bandits may loot it. Your heir comes for it within 3 days. Each day someone may find and bury it: 30% near people (within 22 tiles of a holding, or on a road), 4% far from anyone, more with renown (up to 90%); found near people they go to your family's plot, the finer the more renown; found far off, a cairn if they were known, else a small grave there. Your new hero may bury them too, from the place's page: here, or carried to the family plot. Remains no one finds stay as they lie. Never before your eyes (the owner: "Will it going to disappear right in front of the eye"): a burial, or a band picking them over, waits while you see the spot or stand at its page (`Death.unseenAt`), and happens on a day you are away.
- **Your family and the lords' houses** (the owner: "Go with 3, 1, 10 and 7 first"):
  - **Kin is a past** (2026-11-10; the owner: "remove who are you since it redundant with background"; "to be able to select you need to be eligible ... you should be a new one that just born and grow"; `Legacy.page`, `kinWhy`, `kinHome`, `kinOf`, `DEATH.kin.past`): the new life page has no "Who you are" of its own. Kin of the one who fell is the first past card: a young one of the family just come of age (a Nobody's start: bare hands, 20 gold, a little food and Athletics; never an old hand of another past), of their people, which kin (child, sibling, cousin) by chance from the fall, never chosen; their keepsake, a share of their renown and half their lords' regard and grudges; a young face, no beard; they begin where the family is known, so the card is greyed out with its reason while no home of the family stands. Every other past is a stranger to them: any race (the race tabs say so), nothing of theirs. Tests: death.
  - **A family name** (`Legacy.family`): the new life page asks it (its guess: the fallen's own last name); your line, its graves and its plot go by it ("Thornwood Family Rest"), kept from life to life.
  - **Kin** (the owner: "1. Small 2. Ok"; `world/legacy.js` `KIN`, `DEATH.kin`): the new life asks who you are: the child, sibling or cousin of the one who fell (of their people; any past), or a stranger (any people, nothing of theirs). Kin carry a little: a keepsake of their gear (their weapon from their remains, if no one took it: `Legacy.heirloom`), a tenth of their renown, half what their lords thought of them, their feuds at half (`grudge.half`), and begin where the family is known (its plot's holding, or their home).
  - **A family tree** (`ui/family.js`, the Journal's Family; `Legacy.line`, each with whom they were kin of; the owner: "Make family tree in a hierarchy", "Make the tree clean, with the sprite in the square box and click to show text"): a chart of square tiles (style.css `.ft-chart`), each one's face in its box (`Legacy.record` keeps the face they wore; an older record without one gets a face drawn by its name), their generation and name under it: a child below the one they came from, a sibling or a cousin beside them (a cousin's line dashed), a stranger who took up the name a root of their own; the heir who kept their company a small tile beside them. A tile clicked (`fam-pick`, `G.famPick`: runtime) tells its life beside the chart: their people and past, days, level, renown, what killed them, where they lie; an heir's whose company they kept and how they stand now. You, until another is chosen.
  - **A face of your own** (`Faces.next`, ui/faces.js): each new life gets one (a stranger's drawn afresh; kin's like the one who fell: the same shape of face, nose and ears where their people allow, the same skin, hair and eyes); the world's other faces kept as they were.
  - **Grudges** (`LordHouse.fell` byYou, `DEATH.house.grudge` −40): a lord you put to death, or who falls in the fighting against you (6% as you beat him: `lordWon`; or when you rout him: `defeatLord(l, true)`), leaves his house holding it against you: its living lords and the next of it. A new person is a stranger to them: the grudge goes with you.
  - **Houses shown** (`Houses.tagOf`): a lord's house with him in his tip on the map and in the Journal's Lords (its seat, its dead in the crypt, a grudge); looked up in the Lords, a lord's house is founded if he has none yet.
  - **Houses' trees, discovered** (the Journal's Houses, `ui/family.js` `housePage`; `LordHouse#knows`; the owner: "only appear once you know them or someone tell you about their family (only when very friendly)"): a house is set down once you know one of it (a lord met or named to you). Its tree: its dead and its living, each below the one whose place they took up (`succeeded` on a lord, `of` on the dead); one you do not know a ?? tile. You learn them by meeting them or hearing them named, by reading the names on its crypt (`Crypt#meet`), or from a close friend of the house: a lord whose regard is `DEATH.house.tell` (50) or more answers "Tell me of your house" in his audience (`LordHouse#tell`) with all of it, living and dead. What you were told is saved with the house (`known`). A successor is never given the name of one of the house's dead.
  - **Lords' families** (`LordHouse#kin`, `seed`, `daily`, `heirOf`, `raise`; numbers `DEATH.house.fam`; the owner: "Give lords spouses and children so houses have real families"): a lord's spouse and children are kin of the house (not lords: no army). As a house is first known, its lord is wed (75%) with up to 2 children of up to 200 days; of age at 120 days. Each day one of age and unwed may wed (1%: a spouse of their people from outside), a couple may have a child (1%, up to 4), no more than 24 of a house alive. One dead is followed by their eldest grown child, else a grown brother or sister, else their spouse, else the eldest grown of its blood, else a cousin from afar (dashed in the tree); one of its kin raised keeps their id, name and face (`kinFace`) under the title. In the Houses tree each child is below the one born to, the one wed a tile beside them (a double line); kin are learned only from a close friend's telling.
  - **Edge cases** (checked 2026-11-09): a title passes as a woman's or a man's (`DEATH.house.titles`: Thane/Clanmother, Warchief/Warmother, Lord/Lady, Ser/Dame); a lord with no title is followed by their own name and the house's after it (`nameParts`, `LordHouse#styled`: "Rukk Bonecleaver", "Krenna the Red"; a house named so: "House Mok" for Mok the Red); no two lords alive of one name ("of" the seat after it when the names run out), no two kin of one if it can be helped; a spouse is of age before their eldest child was born; a lord who rides with you (`world/captives.js`) or whose realm falls (`world/realms.js`) is gone from his house (`Houses.away`: with its dead, not in its crypt), the next taking up the place of one who rides with you; a fallen realm's houses have no more weddings or births; your heir raised a lord has no family made for them; one who took up the place of one wed into the house is drawn below that one's spouse.
- **Edge cases** (the owner: "Check for edge cases"): praying gives its experience at once (a save or a load while the light flows loses nothing; the flow is not saved); Esc on the Slain page goes on as someone new, never out of the game; a family plot whose holding is gone stays a place of its own and the next burial begins a new plot by the nearest holding still standing (graves keep their plot's holding and family: `g.plot`, `g.family`); a lone grave keeps its place when buried or carried off (`RestSite.keyOf`); resting places number from 100000, never reused (`Legacy.restN`); no two living lords share a name; a new life only for the dead.
- **Resting places** (the owner: "Can you make it a actual places?"; `world/lineage.js` `RestSite`, a site of type `rest` in `W.sites`): every grave belongs to a place: your family's plot ("Rest of …"), a house's crypt ("Crypt of House …"), or a lone grave, cairn or remains. Named, found as places are (its title rising), on the minimap and the full map (its finest grave's picture), clicked and walked into: the site window, its banner the place as it stands, the dead who lie there (their day, level, which of your line, what lingers), and what you do there: pray for each who still holds anything, take their grave goods, pay your respects at a crypt. `RestSite.sync` makes the places for the graves and keeps each where its graves are (after a load too).
- **Its light while anything is there** (the owner, 2026-11-09: "if money, items, etc still there then can have glow"; `Grave#glows`): a grave's or remains' glow, column and motes show while its experience waits to be prayed for or its gold or goods are still there; prayed at and emptied (or holding nothing), it lies dark. Its candles are only candles. Tests: death.
- **Many lives** (the owner: "Bear in mind, player may died a lot"): graves are never laid on one another (`Grave.spot`: rings out from the spot, `DEATH.grave.apart`), however often you fall in one place or at one heir's holding; one grave talked of an hour at most, however many there are; your line counted (`Legacy.fallen`: "the 6th of your line to fall" on the Slain page and the grave); an heir's realm left with no holding and no lord drops out of the realms that act (`Legacy.prune`: out of `FIDS`, its wars ended, its name kept for whatever names it), so many lives make no crowd of dead realms; a realm's name taken already gets a number ("The Grey Company II").
- **A new person** (phase 3, `world/legacy.js` `Legacy`): from the Slain page, a new race, name and past (`page`), and you go on in the same world on the same day (`reborn`: `session.js` `resetPlayer`, then `heroFrom` and `heroHome`, the new game's own steps). What the old hero left (`handOut`): their heir, the most loyal companion (Loyal, `DEATH.heir.ap`), becomes a lord (`raise`, `l.heir`): over a realm made for them (`found`: your banner's name and colour, at war with your foes; saved in the piece `legacy`, before the holdings and lords) if you held holdings of your own, else among the lords of the realm you served; the companions content with them ride along; your holdings and caravans' purses theirs. With no heir, each holding goes to the realm nearest it. Every other companion is free in their tavern, hired again as they were (`kept`, `rejoin`). Your hero, quests (and their marks on the world), map, names and stories reset; the chronicle, your remains and the man-eater stay. The company ends with its lord (`hour`).

## Bare hands and the Nobody (2026-10-29)

The owner: "a new origin where you start from nothing" and "an unarmed attack: mostly a one hand punch, two hands to grapple, a kick"; only your hero for now.
- **The Nobody** (`data/progression.js` `BGS.nobody`): no weapon, 5 gold and 3 grain, no troops, no attribute bonus; Unarmed and Athletics learned faster. Their origin (`data/tree.js` `ORIGINS.nobody`, +10 stamina) joins the Crossroads itself and the brawler's chain. A card of its own on the New game page.
- **Unarmed** (`SKILLS.fist`): your hero's proficiency with no weapon in either hand (`core/state.js` `unarmed`, `meleeSk`); it rises with each blow landed. The combo's third blow comes from the Tree, as for every weapon.
- **The fists** (`battle/combat.js` `FISTS`): ×0.6 damage, −8 reach, ×0.75 swing time, blunt. The combo (`COMBOS.fists`) is a Jab, a Cross and a Kick, and each strikes only the nearest foe. The hard attack (`battle/movesets.js` `MOVESETS.hard.grapple`) is a Grapple: wound up, then the foe is seized in both hands and thrown (`HeroHands#grab`), staggered and perhaps knocked down.
- **The look** (`gfx/people.js` `FIST_RIG`, `rigLimbs`; `gfx/sprites.js` poses 29–40 from `FIST_POSE`): a small rig, so the whole body moves. In each frame the head and the body move apart, and both arms (shoulder, elbow, fist) and both legs (hip, knee, foot) are drawn from their joints. The guard hand stays at the chin while the other strikes; the knees bend; the hips turn and the rear heel lifts on the cross, thrown with the rear hand across the body; the body leans back over the kick, and crouches, rears and bends over the grapple. Parrying bare-handed, both fists come up.
- **The brawler's chain** (`TREE_FISTS`, nodes `fist0`–`fist3`, below Command, from the Nobody's origin or from Sage Counsel): Bare Knuckles (+15% unarmed damage, +1 Unarmed), Iron Shins (a harder kick, +8 health), Grappler (grapple 30% more often, a surer throw), Open Hand (keystone: +35% unarmed damage and 10% quicker, but 10% more damage taken). (Now the Unarmed tree, 2026-11-08: `PROF_TREE.fist`, its nodes `fist_*`.)
- Tests: `unarmed`.

## Tracks: pixel prints from the art (2026-10-29)

The tracks are drawn pixel only, in 8 directions (the owner). `art/tracks.py` draws each print once as art (art/tracks.svg) and pixelates it at its game size (`TRACKS.art.px`), turned each of the 8 ways (art/tracks-px.svg; `TRACK_PX` in js/data/trackart.js). It keeps as many pixels as the art's ink fills, the most covered first. The straight ways are one print turned and the slants another, pixel for pixel, with no light on them, so each way looks the same. gfx/tracks.js `trackPic` lays them in whole game pixels with the map's ground (render.js `drawWorld`, under everything built). Boots have a wide heel and a clear arch, and bare feet their real breadth, so at 6–7 px they still read as feet. Change a print or a size there and run it again. Tests: tracks (each art's 16 prints as long as the art, each way the same turned, drawn on the map).

The prints are at the figures' scale (the owner, 2026-11-01: "make it realistic with sprite scale"): a man on the map some 10 px tall, a boot print 3 px long, lanes 3 px apart, a stride 6 px (were 7, 7 and 13); beasts in proportion (`TRACKS.art.px`, `lane`, `stagger`, `step`; `art/tracks.py` run again).

## Battle speed (spatial grid)

`battle/spatial.js` (`SpatialGrid`, open as `SG` only during the soldiers' turns, the separation pass `jostle()` and arrow flight) answers neighbour queries from per-team buckets of `B.units` indices: `nearest` searches rings outward from the soldier's cell; box queries serve `shooterTarget`, `roomyFoe`, `withdrawThreat`, charges, projectile hits and splash; `moraleView` has its own grid per morale tick. A reset empties only the buckets filled since the last one (`used`, marked by `stamp`), and where cells outnumber filed men `SPATIAL.sparse` times (a world battle's wide field) `nearest` looks at each filed man once instead of walking rings: the same pick either way (tests/battlecrowd). **Exactness rule:** every query returns exactly what the old full scan did (same set, same `B.units` order, same ties and float arithmetic), so battles are byte-identical; outside those phases queries fall back to the full scan. It depends on one invariant: during the soldiers' turns only the acting soldier changes position (moves through `knock`/velocity are safe) and nobody revives mid-turn. `targetMembersOf` caches a target formation's members per turn (`targetTest` must match `battlecontrol.js` `targetMembers`). Timings (`tests/visual/battleperf.js`, ms per frame): 200 soldiers 3.9 → 1.3, 400 9.3 → 2.4, 800 38.8 → 8.2, 1200 79 → 12.9.

**The soldiers' picks, found faster and the same** (a code review's measure, 2026-11-11; `battle/actors.js` `nearestSeen`, `nearWithin`; `terrain.js` `BattleSight#shown`): two sifts ran every frame. A soldier with no foe in sight (armies closing in the fog) looked for one each frame, the whole battle sifted each time: now only the soldiers his side is not hidden from are sifted (`shown`: kept till a sight reckoning or a blow that shows its striker changes who is hidden, `hv`), and with none of them no one is looked for. A soldier sent at a foe looked over the whole field for the foe right on top of him: now only within that reach (`nearWithin`, the grid's buckets his box touches). Both pick exactly as before (the battle fingerprints held; `tests/targetpicks.js` sets each against the old rule for every soldier through a fogged fight, 11,000 picks). Measured on 301 soldiers, all systems on: the battle's update 6.7 ms a frame to 2.8 as the armies close, 6.3 to 3.3 over the next 9 s. Tried and not kept: a siege captain massing his groups on the least defended ladder (in six simulated assaults it never did better than dealing them out one a way, and twice worse).

**The same world, reckoned with less work** (a code review's measure, 2026-11-11; `world/worldobject.js` `WORLD_FAST`; `brains.js` `BandBrain#rival`, `near`, `moved`, `updateBands`; `territory.js` `landHeldFor`; `outposts.js` `Outposts.posts`; `monsters.js` `updateMonsters`): a Huge world's moment was 16 ms on the map, a fifth of it the bands' feuds (each band looked through every band for a rival) and a twelfth the land looked at afresh by every beast (each holding's owner and reach and every site, twice a beast). Now a band looks only through the bands filed in the cells near it (128 px cells, filed once a pass over the bands, each band filed again after its own moment), in the bands' own order, so its pick is the old one; the land is looked at once before the beasts' moments and held through them (none of them changes it; a holding added or lost is still seen at once), and an outpost's part of its key comes from the outposts alone. Measured, the map's update on a Huge world: 15.8 ms a frame to 9.3 at 1x (284 frames of 1200 over 16 ms to 40), 16.5 to 12.4 at 8x; the roaming parties 8.0 ms to 2.6, the beasts 1.6 to 0.6; a Normal world 3.6 to 3.1. `tests/worldfast.js` runs a seeded world for days and asks the old rule beside the new at every step: 59,883 feud looks pick the same rival, the land is unchanged after each of 37,943 beasts' moments, the key the same number. Off (`WORLD_FAST.feud`, `.land` false): the old reckoning. Left for later: a far party's cheap moment (bands, lords and patrols think in full every fourth frame however far), and the spikes (a worst frame of 47 ms at 1x, 273 at 8x: the parties', the land defence's).

**No gold from nothing** (a review of the rules, 2026-11-11: six readers over the world, the battles, the money, the quests, saves and travel; `tests/nogoldloops.js`, `tests/drops.js`). The loops that paid without end are shut: a unit sold to a market is priced with it already in the stock and a gold under its price back (`world/economy.js` `TRADE_FAIR`, `Economy#sellPrice`: it was sold at an empty stall and bought back for less); a caravan pays under what it charges for the same good (`caravans.js` `pays`); the market of a fief of yours buys no goods of you (`TRADE_FAIR.own`: what the steward gave free was sold to it and lay in its stores again; store goods at the steward's); a holding lying sacked or raided gives no sack gold, no raid and no renown again (`world/siege.js` `PLUNDER`, `plunderLeft`, `sackGold`); remains keep only what your bag did not take (`death.js` `Remains#take`); a drink lifts your band once, not again when you set it down in a fief's or castle's stores or on a battle's pile and take it up again (`supply.js` `drinkIn`, `keptIn`, `DRINK_KEEP`; `ui/loot.js` a stack's `mine`; first built as a memory of the most held, which wrongly gave no cheer after ale left the bag another way: fixed 2026-11-12); a foe who left the field alive, or has not come onto it, leaves no coins or gear (`battle/drops.js` `step`). Each has its switch (`TRADE_FAIR.on`, `.own`, `PLUNDER.once`, `SUPPLY.drink.once`). Also re-recorded: `battleresolution` field_loss and auto_loss, moved by the band's stripping (2026-11-11) and missed then.

**Rules that gave the wrong result** (the same review, 2026-11-11; `tests/rulesright.js`, `tests/siegewalls.js`). No autosave is written over a death by beasts (`core/save.js` `saveGame`: a load of it had the hero alive again with his remains at his feet); a passenger the beasts kill ends his quest `dead`, a story's bad end (`world/death.js`, `story/forms.js` `STORY_BAD`: `slain` is a beast hunt's done, and his family welcomed him home); a band that moves into an outpost is not told as beaten by you, and a quest's quarry is not taken off the map so (`world/outposts.js` `contest`); yours on the field are your own band, not an ally's leader, and one routed off it is no withdrawal (`battle/morale.js` `yourOwn`, `leaveField`, `retreating`; `attacks.js` `fell`); defenders who fled a storm are not counted slain (`battle/resolution.js` `wallsFell`); a deed that costs standing by a holding costs honour with its realm, not +1 (`world/reputation.js` `repNear`); at the market outside a gate that will not have you there is no lodging and no streets (`world/stay.js` `yourShelterWhy`, `ui/townui.js`); a sally out of a besieged holding you stayed in is left at a flight's odds (`stay.js` `sally`, `battle/engage.js` `fleeOdds`); the banner taken, the defenders are broken for good (`battle/siegeworks.js` `takeFlag`); no siege is kept by a captive or by no one (`world/siege.js` `siegeDay`, `captivity.js` `defeat`); a storm you joined and left leads no later taking (`G.capWith`); a lord slain neither gets away nor is beaten twice (`world/realms.js` `lordWon`).

**No one stuck for good, and the world's lords by their own rules** (the same review, 2026-11-11; `tests/nostuck.js`, `tests/captivity.js`).
