A fight on the map sets no party down on water (`world/fights.js` `fightPlace`, `unstick`: a fight on a bridge left its winner in the river for good); your band left on ground it cannot stand on makes for the nearest bank (`world/world.js` `heroAshore`: a ferry's crossing given up mid-water); dead in hours passed off the map, the death's page comes up, not a frozen map (`updateWorld`; `captivity.js` `day`, `death.js` `after`), and your captors keep the frost off you (`supply.js`: frostbite not while held); your place in a lord's host is kept till he can be followed again (`hosts.js` `watch`: his fight's page shut while he fought made you a deserter); a new life is none of the old one's host, keeps counting quest numbers, unmarks the old quarry in camps and caravans, and sets out with its waterskins (`legacy.js` `reborn`); its heir is a companion still with you (`heirOf`); a free holding's anger at you and a sabotage's days are saved (`holding.js` `saveRec`); the fence sells back only what your bag takes, at its share (`captivity.js`). The lords: a campaign calls no lord out of a besieged holding he defends (`hosts.js` `defending`), and its end does not begin a member's siege again (`part`, `disband`); a beaten lord's new army is raised at a holding not besieged, and a weak one rides home to one (`realms.js`); a lord lying in wait springs only on an army he can beat (`waylay.js` `springs`). Quests: a job done whose giver's realm fell or whose holding is gone closes paid (`quests.js` `JobQuest#lost`); a Hold job whose foe has no holding left sends no wave and relief comes (`attack`); a lord's stale offer is dropped (`q-lord`). A beast's meat comes from all slain, whatever hides the field gave (`monsters.js` `monsterLoot`). Fingerprints hold.

**Money and men by their own rules** (the same review, 2026-11-11; `tests/fairpay.js`). A pulled-down building gives back half its cost, at most half of what you paid for it (`world/buildings.js` `REFUND`, `paidFor`, `refundOf`, `t.paid` saved with the holding: a holding taken was pulled down for thousands); a man called up from a garrison is the cheapest kind you stationed there (`core/garrison.js`, `t.garKinds`: a peasant came back a soldier); your garrisons are paid, 0.4g a man a day as a realm pays (`world/world.js` `BAND_PAY.garrison`); a realm's contract pays 125% of your soldiers' wages from its treasury, none when it has no gold (`BAND_PAY.merc`, `mercPay`: it paid a gold a man from nothing, more than a levy costs); new men refilling a group pay their share of its upgrades however they come (`core/recruitment.js` `reinforce`: auto-refill paid only the hire); a giver who pays in goods cuts the gold only by what they are worth (`world/quests.js` `purse`); the heirloom is marked given only when it fits your bag (`story/wounded.js` `gift`); a bribe the guard takes leaves your purse (`captivity.js`); fed, the hungry hours wear off 4 an hour, not at once (`supply.js` `hunger.recover`, `BAND.lean`); a job you ruined (a post left, or lost while you held it; guards that saw you; the convoy you seized) is failed, not paid for your trouble, and one closed by a realm at odds with you pays nothing (`JobQuest#ruined`, `close`; `caravans.js` `seize`). Fingerprints hold.

**The battle and the world by their own rules** (the same review, 2026-11-11; `tests/worldrules.js`). A camp or hideout you withdrew from or lost to keeps its losses for a week (`world/sites.js` `CAMP_WORN`, `Lair#worn`, `keep`; `battle/engage.js` `ON_WIN.camp.lost`: it stood at full strength again at once); an ambush on the world map is sprung once, so run down after it you are not caught off guard again (`battle/worldbattle.js` `initialize`); an army waiting at a full field's edge turns away after the stall, so the battle can end (`reinforce`); a besieged holding makes a quarter of its yield and its gate is barred to caravans, which go on to another of their realm (`world/economy.js` `SIEGE_SHUT`, `world/brains.js` `CaravanBrain`: its farms fed it, and it never starved); a truce ends the two realms' fights on the map (`world/fights.js` `truceFights`); a battle of many armies takes what the field gave off its gold, as a two-sided one does (`battle/sides.js` `settle`). Only forest hides you; the comments that said hills did are corrected (`map.js` `hiddenAt`). W7 re-recorded for the siege rule (`tests/worldprint.js`, with its proof).

**An outpost's sentries keep a watch** (the owner, 2026-11-11: "patrol around the flag and stop, dont make them always walk, and they should go rest in the tower like garrison"; `world/outposts.js` `Outpost#posts`, `door`, `sentryAt`, `around`; `data/outposts.js` `OUTPOST.sentries`; `tests/outposts.js`). In place of a ring walked without end: four posts in an arc in front of the flag (the map draws them over the outpost, so none stands behind it), walked at `pace` and a stand of 20 to 40 game minutes at each; after a round, back in by the tower's door to rest `rest` game minutes, unseen, each sentry a third of the round behind the next, so one is always out; with no tower standing (a flag alone, or its tower broken) they rest at the flag's foot. A way that would cross the plinth or the tower goes round its corners. Worked out from the time alone: nothing saved, no fingerprint moved.

**Quest chains and job chains** (the owner, 2026-11-12: "refactor thread, threads to something else such as questchain"). A thread is now a quest chain: `story/questchain.js` `QuestChain` (was `story/thread.js` `Thread`), `tests/questchains.js`, its end on the bus `questchain:ended`, an outcome's `start: {questChain, memory}` (an older `thread` read too), `G.questChain`, `QuestTrack.questChain`, the console's `questchains` and `questchain`. The four chains that grow from jobs are now job chains, so the two do not share a word: `story/jobchains.js` `JobChains`, `data/jobchains.js` `JOB_CHAINS`, `tests/jobchains.js` (were `chains.js`, `Chains`, `CHAINS`). What saves keep is as it was, so an older save loads: the save key `threads`, `W.story.threads`, and the shape `'thread'` in what waits on one and in what one gave (`QUESTCHAIN_SHAPE`).

**Closer, shorter footprints** (the owner, 2026-11-12: "make a step guide path a bit more closer for each foot", "make the footprints track a bit short right now it's like a long dash"). The guide path's prints 7 px apart (were 10), each 3 px long, its ball, a gap and its heel (was 4: `gfx/worldui.js` `WUI_FOOT`); a man's prints on the ground 2 px long (were 3: `data/tracks.js` `TRACKS.art.px` hobnail, boot, barefoot), `art/tracks.py` now giving a print `PX_SMALL` px or shorter exactly its length in pixels (its rounding's two half pixels made a 2 px one 3 long), `js/data/trackart.js` written again.

**Gold where it is spent; places found nearer** (the owner, 2026-11-12: "why show gold in this menu?", "still discover too easy need a bit more near holding"). An estate's window shows your gold, in its header and its rail's, only on an estate of yours, where building costs gold; on another's (sabotage, plunder, burn) none (`ui/estate.js`, `ui/window.js` a rail head's `gold:false`). A holding's name is learnt within 2 tiles of its edge (was 4, first 6), a cave's, ruins', a shrine's or a hideout's within 3 (was 4) (`data/world.js` `DISCOVER`).

**No "you cannot tell"** (the owner, 2026-11-12: "remove, you cannot tell your odds"). A Scouting reading too dim to give shows nothing, not a line saying so (`ui/window.js` `insight`: a null reading; `andRead`, a label and its reading after a comma, the label alone with none): your odds to haggle, break out, bribe a gaoler, persuade your captors or pass a story's check; a camp's or hideout's strength; whether a band would yield; what lairs in a cave. From Scouting 2 the rough reading shows as before (`tests/conduct.js`).

**No party stuck for good on its way** (the owner, 2026-11-12: "when the world is simulated far away it seems like we will end up having a lot of entities stuck at certain places"; `tests/stalls.js`). Measured first: 25 days far from you and 8 days at a castle's gate, three seeds, showed few stuck. One kind was real: a lord sent to the nearest holding of his realm, or the nearest foe's, picked by distance alone, and one across the water walked straight at it and stood at the shore for good (one lord in 3 of 5 seeds at the start). Now a lord chooses only holdings he can walk to (`world/realms.js` `walkable`, `nearestReach`; `lordThink`, the fleeing and dreading turns in `brains.js`, a campaign's target and its members in `hosts.js`); none of his own in reach, he rests and thinks again each day. And a safety net for what was not seen (`world/stalls.js` `Stalls`, `data/world.js` `WORLD_STALL`): a lord marching, going home, relieving or on patrol, a realm's caravan or a holding's patrol still for 12 game hours with somewhere to go is sent another way (a lord thinks again, that holding put aside; a caravan to another holding of its realm, else home; a patrol back inside), each kept in `Stalls.seen` (who, where, what it was doing and does now) to trace a crowd. The developer console (F2) reads them: `stalls` (how many sent on, the last ten) and `nearby` (every party within 15 tiles of you, what it is doing, where to, and how many hours it has stood still). Not watched: a siege laid, a campaign's column, your own caravans. Fingerprints hold.

## Terrain

**Terrain** (`battle/terrain.js`, `doc/TERRAIN_DESIGN.md`; phase 1: hills, woods, marsh; phase 2: rivers, fords, bridges; phase 3: depth-sorted trees and cliff faces, concealment; phase 4: impassable water, cliffs, one-way hills, mountains, routing). A field fought on the world map gets a static 16-px grid `B.terrain` (kind, level 0–2, slope direction; a `BattleTerrain` since class wave E1: the ground's rules are its methods, `speedFor`, `climbOf`, `blocksView`…, its routing `B.terrain.routes` (`TerrainRoutes`) and the sight `B.sight` (`BattleSight`); the old names, `groundMul`, `seenBy`, `sightStep`…, are aliases; doc/TERRAIN_DESIGN.md §8, "Classes as built") laid out from the world tile and its 8 neighbours with its own seeded stream (the same place always gives the same field), fair to both sides (woods ≤25% of each deployment zone, the halves score within 15%, a 96-px open lane edge to edge, hills centred or mirrored); none in tournaments, sieges or raids. Layouts vary per biome (wooded hills and ridges, copses…). A field with terrain has **no scattered trees or rocks**; deserts get no woods and water only as an **oasis** beside a water tile; their sand stands **cacti, prickly pear, dry and dead shrubs, small rocks and now and then bones** in sparse clusters (greener round an oasis), decoration only: nothing collides (`engine/ground.js` `PX.desertSprite`).
- **Field variety** (2026-10-18; `doc/TERRAIN_DESIGN.md` §8, "Field variety as built"; `tests/fieldvariety.js`). The field reflects the world map.
  - **Several layouts per tile kind**, picked by the tile's seed:
    - plains: open ground, copses, a low rise;
    - hills: rolling terraces, ridges, more cliffs and ramps;
    - **peaks** (a hill among peak tiles): a pass between mountains, a saddle, a gorge;
    - swamp: marsh, a fen with dry islands and a causeway, marsh pools;
    - desert: dunes, an erg, rock spires;
    - frost: snowy copses and a frozen river even away from water (`TERRAIN_RIVER.frostP`).
  - **A forest tile is a real forest:** dense woods over 50–65% of the field (`TERRAIN_DENSE_LAYOUT`: deepwood, stands, hillwood, glade; elvenwood near an elven town; the mire wood in the swamp).
    - Each army gets a deployment glade.
    - A road crosses (a road tile), else a deer path or a chain of clearings.
    - The fairness rule replaces the 25% zone rule: both zones' cover and open ground within 8 points, the glades open, an open way from glade to glade, and the zones reaching each other.
  - **The neighbours shape the edges** (`terrainEdges`): north and south their own edge (woods, a ridge, crags, reed marsh); west and east both ends alike, so that neither army is favoured.
  - **A road tile's road** crosses the field and is a little faster (×1.1, `TERRAIN_ROAD`; the HUD shows "On the road"). Roads and paths are laid like real ones (`wayPlan`, `TERRAIN_WAY`): the least-cost line over the cells (`route.js`: woods and marsh dearer, a plateau and above all a slope band dear, rock and deep water closed, cliffs closed both ways, so a way climbs only by a slope or a ramp and crosses a river at its bridge or ford), simplified, smoothed (Chaikin) and swayed by seeded noise of varying wavelength and strength (no sine) inside its corridor; the same tile, the same road. The lab's own grounds take `road` (`true`, `'ns'` or a height share).
  - **Roads and paths are painted** (`engine/ground.js` `paintWays`, from `recipe.ways`; `WAYPAL` by climate): a road is a worn cart track 20 px wide, low in contrast, with two faint wheel ruts that wander and fade, a patchy grassy crown and a soft, ragged verge (packed snow in the frost, sand in the desert, mud in the swamp; puddles, or ice, in the ruts now and then); a deer path is a trodden strip 8 px wide, a little lighter than the ground. A hill's climbable edge is a smooth rise (its band 1 cell in from the rim: `TERRAIN.slope`), grassy, earthy or stony by stretches; only a real cliff is a grey rock face. A road runs up a rise or a ramp; trees keep their crowns off its line (a gap in the canopy zoomed out). A cliff-ringed hill's ramp is purely natural: the cliff breaks and the hillside slopes down through the gap. Where marsh lies round it, a road runs on a causeway (raised earth, logs along its sides) and a path on a boardwalk (planks across, a beam each side). Details, lone trees and reeds keep off them. An ambush's road is painted too (art only: it is no faster).
  - **Any field size:** `buildTerrain({W, H, top, bot})`. Feature counts and sizes scale with the playable area (`TERRAIN_SIZE`); dense woods lay by share. On a sized field (`BFIELD`) the ground keeps to its real deployment zones (`terrainZone`, from `fieldZone`): each army's glade lies in its zone, the fairness checks measure those zones, rock keeps out of them and the ground behind them, and a dense field's clearings spread over the half outside its zone. A mountain pass's crags grow with the field's height (`TERRAIN_ROCK.pass`) and go on along both edges.
  - **On a dense field, captains** may hold a glade (`TERRAIN_AI.gladeHold`), and riders keep to the ways (`wayRide`), on their rides and, for a mark over `TERRAIN_AI.wayCharge` (160) px off, their charges too.
  - **Big woods zoomed out** (`render.js` `LOD`): on a field of more than 2,000 trees, under zoom ×0.9, the trees are drawn as one canopy layer (`PX.battleCanopy`, painted once and kept) under the men instead of thousands of sorted sprites; zoomed in, the sprites as before (depth order, the fade over your men). The two cross-fade over 0.2 s. A huge forest's prop drawing at the whole field goes from 16–18 ms a frame to under 1 ms. Art only: sight and concealment are untouched.
  - **Lab ground keys:** `layout`, `edges {n, s, w, e}`, `road` (`true` or `'ns'`) and `elven`; `'auto'` on a forest is a forest tile.
  - **Reports:** `tests/visual/fieldbalance.js`, `tests/visual/fieldperf.js`.
- **Range:** a shooter reaches 7.5% farther per level he stands on (replaces the old whole-field +15% hill rule).
- **Charges:** the charge bonus ×1.35 downhill, ×0.65 uphill; halved against a foe in woods. **Melee:** +10% against a foe at least one level lower.
- **Climbing:** on a terrace's slope band (1 cell in from its rim), foot ×0.5, riders and big units ×0.4 (dwarves climb freely), descending ×1.15; running uphill doubles stamina drain; climbing breaks cohesion. Climbers lean forward and step short (`climbState(u)`, drawn only).
- **Woods:** foot ×0.8, riders and big units ×0.55 (elves ×1); charge momentum builds ×0.35; each wood cell stops a low missile 8%, an arrow coming down into woods 35% (elves and magic pass); three wood cells block morale sight unless the viewer stands higher; no cohesion, shield wall or wedge in woods.
- **Marsh:** foot and big ×0.7, riders ×0.5; momentum ×0.3; stamina ×1.5; blows ×0.9 (orcs and swamp beasts exempt).
- **Rivers (phase 2):**
  - **Where:** a field beside water on the world map gets a river down the middle: 48 px of deep water, meandering.
    - 1–2 fords (64 px), or a bridge (32 px) and a ford when a road bridge is within 2 tiles.
    - Frozen in the frost (all ford, crossable anywhere). A ford-only stream in half of swamps away from water.
    - None in deserts (oasis only), against monsters or in caves.
  - **Fair:** deployment zones stay dry; crossings lie near the middle, ≥120 px apart (less on a short field), and the open lane runs over one.
  - **Deep water** is impassable (phase 4): only fords and bridges cross it. `TERRAIN.wade` brings back phase 2's wading: ×0.35 speed (big ×0.5), no momentum, stamina ×2 running and spent even walking, no shooting, blows dealt ×0.75 and taken ×1.25, no cohesion, shield wall or wedge.
  - **Fords:** foot and big ×0.7, riders ×0.5, momentum ×0.3, stamina ×1.5, blows ×0.9 (orcs exempt), no shield wall or wedge.
  - **Bridges:** open ground, but narrow.
  - **Crossing:** groups stream over a bridge or ford as a column and re-form beyond it. A line holding a bank does not step into the water after a foe (`holdsBank`).
  - **For the AI:** captains read `B.terrain.ai.crossings` (`{kind, x, y, w, y0, y1, x0, x1}`) and `B.terrain.ai.river`.
- **Art:** `engine/ground.js` `PX.battleGround` paints the field once per battle (`B.ground`): terraces as cliff-faced plateaus, species-mixed woods, marsh, oases, rivers with banks, fords, bridges and ice, per-biome painted detail; the minimap shows it (`B.groundMini`).
  - `PX.battleWater` animates the water (glints and current, still with reduced motion).
  - Waders are sunk to the waist inside a ring of ripples; fords splash.
  - **Depth:** trees (32-px sprites, `PX.battleTree`) and the plateaus' south cliff faces stand in `B.ground.props` and are drawn in the same depth order as the soldiers (`render.js` `battleEnts`).
    - A man behind a trunk's foot is under its crown; one behind a rim is under the rock.
    - A tree or face covering one of your or your allies' men fades (`PROPFADE`); the enemy's get no fade.
- **Sight (phase 3):**
  - **Woods:** a soldier in a wood is unseen by a side with no soldier within 64 px of him (96 px from higher ground), unless he struck or shot at that side in the last 3 s.
  - **Raised ground** at least a level above both viewer and target hides the target too (`riseBlocked`). A `TERRAIN_GROUND` kind with `blocks:true` hides at any height, ready for mountains.
  - Sight is reckoned every 0.25 s (`sightStep`, `SIGHT`).
  - **For you:** unseen enemies are not drawn, not on the minimap, have no icon and cannot be clicked. Free fire skips them.
  - **For the AI, the same rule:** captains (`assess`) and characters (`liveFoes`) only count foes their side sees, and aim at a hidden target's last-known place (`sightGoal`).
  - **Lost targets:** an attack on a group gone from sight marches to where it was last seen, and lapses there if nothing is found.
  - **Ambush:** a group unseen until its charge lands shakes its foe ×1.5, once ("Ambush!", `AMBUSH`).
  - API: `seenBy(team,u)`, `visibleFoes(team)`, `lastSeen(team,u)`, `sightGoal(g,team)`.
- **Fog of war** (`battle/battlefog.js`, `FOG`; `B.fog`; `tests/battlefog.js`). Total War's line of sight, in every field battle (all grounds, rivers, a held bridge, an ambush; terrain on or off); none in tournaments, sieges or raids.
  - **The rule:** a side sees an enemy soldier only when one of its own (living, not routing) is within his sight range with a clear line. Ranges: foot 220 px, shooters 260, riders 300, characters 280 (the most that fits him; `FOG.char` times `data/world.js` `SIGHT_KIND`, the map's table too); a scout (`SCOUT_KINDS`) ×1.6 (a mounted archer 480), and ×1.6 again while scouting ahead. Big units are seen ×1.5 farther. +25% range per level the viewer stands higher. Rises and rock block the line as above; woods keep their 64/96 px rule; a blow or shot reveals the striker for 3 s.
  - **How:** `sightStep` builds each side's grid of 16-px cells (`fogGrid`): one disc per viewer cell and range, marked whole where nothing in its box stands a level above the viewer, else cast by rays (`fogCast`, the ray templates laid once a radius). Casts are kept per cell and range for the battle (the ground never changes). Cost at 800 soldiers marching: under 0.1 ms/frame (open ground and crags alike); a cold first step on crags about 10 ms.
  - **What you see:** ground outside your side's sight under a soft dark (`fogDraw`: a cell a pixel, blurred and smoothed up twice, each cell easing over 0.3 s), the same on the small map; unseen enemies undrawn, without icons, not clickable. A grey **ghost** of an enemy group (its icon, a dashed ring) marks where it was last seen; it fades over 15 s and goes when the group is seen again or its place is seen empty. All from the deployment on; the top bar keeps both armies' counts.
  - **The AI is bound the same way** (its foes are `seenBy`'s). A captain that has never seen the foe advances its army as a line after 3 s (`captainSearch`, `FOG.scout`, `FOG.probe`); its lull and posture clocks start when it first sees them.
  - **Switches:** `FOG.on` is a view over the Settings' **Fog of war (battle)** (`PREFS.bfog`, on by default; `tests/harness.js` turns it off for the older suites). The lab's `fog` key (true/false) overrides it; left out, the lab follows `FOG.on`. Scout ahead (a scout unit's ability) pushes it back round that group for a while: see Unit abilities.
- **Cliffs and rock (phase 4):**
  - **Sheer hills:** cliffs all round, one or two ramps.
  - **One-way hills:** one ramp; men may drop down the cliffs but climb only by the ramp.
  - Both are commonest on hill fields and in the frost.
  - **Mountains and rock spires** (`TKIND.rock`) are impassable and hide what lies behind them. Deserts get spires only.
  - **Fair:** each zone reaches the other and every hilltop; no rock in a zone or the open lane; ramps face both sides alike or are mirrored.
  - **Data:** `B.terrain.wall` (edge bits E 1, S 2, W 4, N 8; one-way on the low cell only) and `B.terrain.rockH` (levels of `PX.ROCK_PX` = 14 px).
- **The way round (phase 4):**
  - Every group move, chase, charge, rout and withdrawal routes round rock, cliffs and water (`route.js`, grids in `B.terrain.route`).
  - No step ever enters rock or deep water or climbs a cliff (`terrainHold`, one guard pass after all movement).
  - A charge builds momentum only on a clear last leg.
  - Captains measure tops by the way round (a ramp on the far side is far), and go looking when they have seen no one for 10 s (`captainSearch`).
  - Cost: +0.6 ms/frame at 800 soldiers on a cliff, mountain and river field; none on fields where nothing blocks.
  - Tests: `tests/routing.js`, `tests/route.js`. Reports: `tests/visual/terrainperf.js`, `tests/visual/terrainbalance.js`.
- **The world's ground (World Phase 2; doc/WORLD_DESIGN.md §13, §15.2.1):** the world fills its `BattleSite`s (`WorldMap#battleSite`: `river`, `crossing`, `elev`, `terrace`, `rough`, `high`); the lab's are null unless its spec asks.
  - **River width:** the world's width 1, 2, 3 gives 3, 5 or 8 cells of deep water (`TERRAIN_RIVER.widths`, half 24 / 40 / 64 px; `BattleRiver.half`). A held bridge keeps it (`spec.wr`, laid over the river spec).
  - **Crossings** (the nearest within `WORLD_SCALE.battle.bridge` tiles): the world's bridge is the field's one bridge (and a ford), stone or wood as on the map; a ford's river has fords only; a ferry's one ford at its landing; none near, fords as before. *Hold the bridge* (`bridgeNear`) reads the world's bridges.
  - **Wet ground:** a delta or floodplain lays marsh beside the river in mirrored pairs, a delta's channels too (`TERRAIN_WET`, `FieldPlan#planWet`), never in a zone or the open lane.
  - **High ground** (D41, the tile's `high`): a rise is never cliffed, a cliffed hill always, a steep hill steep (`planFeatures`, `planSteep`); none: the recipe's chances.
- **Steep hills** (D41; `STEEP` in `battle/terrain.js`, `B.terrain.steep`; `tests/steephills.js`):
  - **The bank** is 2 cells a level (`STEEP.band`), climbed anywhere but slowly: foot ×0.32, riders ×0.3 (the floor: they crawl); dwarves as up a rise. Running up it tires ×3.5, walking up it tires too.
  - **Ranks break** on it (no cohesion, no shield wall or wedge) and for 2 s after the climb (`steepRanks`, morale.js).
  - **Momentum:** none builds or is kept going up; a charge down off it hits ×1.7 (a rise ×1.35). **Height:** range +15% a level (a rise +7.5%), blows down +25%.
  - **Scree:** riders keep no momentum on it; shooters on a scree hill reach 5% a level farther.
  - **A mixed hill's shoulder** is a rise; it faces up or down the field on a lone hill and is mirrored on a pair, so both armies reach it alike.
  - **Routing** prices the banks (`STEEP.route`: foot ×3, riders ×4.5 the open cost; `route.js` `routeGridOf`, the grids' `wcap`), so groups go up by a shoulder or a ramp when one is near. A steep field routes as a cliff field does.
  - **The AI** holds a steep crest before a rise (`TERRAIN_AI.steepHold`) and never runs up at one, whatever the odds.
  - **Look:** a darker stony bank, broken ledges and outcrops; scree loose grey stone; the shoulder a rise (`engine/ground.js` `slopePx`). Awaiting the owner's approval.
  - **Lab:** a hill's `steep`, `scree` and `shoulder` (degrees); `site: {river: {w, delta, flood}, crossing: {kind, stone}, high: {kind, scree, mixed, shoulder}}` (`labSite`) as a generated world's tile.
- **Battle follows the world** (a generated world's fights; `WorldMap#siteNear`, `TERRAIN_WORLD`, `TERRAIN_MIX`, `TERRAIN_BUILT`, `TERRAIN_POND`, `TERRAIN_LANE` in `battle/terrain.js`, `CLIM_GROUND` in `battle/setup.js`; `tests/battleworld.js`; doc/TERRAIN_DESIGN.md §8 "Battle follows the world as built"): at a climates' edge the field's ground (`B.terrain.clim`) is mixed by the map's own covers, the other climate's share the world's, both armies' halves alike (mirrored across the middle line, the owner's "half for now": `TERRAIN_MIX.mirror`; off, more of it toward its side), with its small things, trees by the ecotone and the desert's life; snow, mud and the grass's gallop follow each soldier's cell (the weather and the heat stay the tile's). A holding's buildings, crop rows and fences by the fight stand on their side of the field, between the deployment zones (`B.terrain.built`: buildings solid and hiding, crops and fences slowing); buildings at twice the world's model size (`TERRAIN_BUILT.scale`: a 32 px cottage stands 64 px, its footprint too), never within a building's width of either zone, set in the field's own ground (the model's ragged yard and a soft shadow, `engine/ground.js` `houseOf`), facing the nearest way or the middle; a brook is a stream crossed anywhere, a pond its pool in a marsh, a lane's trees close along its road. A field of one climate with nothing by it is as it was. Shots: `tests/visual/battleworld.js` (`&at=x,y`, `&off=1`, `&mirror=0`).
- **Battle types** (`BTYPE` in `battle/setup.js`, `B.btype`; triggers in `battle/engage.js`; only with terrain):
  - **Hold the bridge:** a fight with a lord, band, patrol or caravan on a road bridge or next to one (not in the frost, where the river is ice, nor the desert).
    - The field: a river of impassable deep water with a single bridge in the open lane near the middle; 40% of places (by the tile) also have a ford far off it, when the field is tall enough (≥100 px between).
    - The side set upon holds it: its foot on its bank by the bridge, its shooters behind, its riders further back on the flanks. You hold it unless you rode at them; holding it, your deployment zone runs to your bank (`B.dzone`).
    - The other side comes on from its own edge and must cross.
    - **AI holding:** defends while the river lies between (no lull or idle shooters send it over, until 60 s pass with no blow struck). Its foot stands at the bridge, the bridge first, 30 px back from the water until their fighters come within 260 px. It never follows anyone over the water. Its riders ride only at those who come over (`typeHolds`).
    - **AI taking:** picks the crossing least held. Its foot and riders hold back out of reach while its shooters shoot (2 volleys), then gather at the near mouth, then go over and form up 50 px beyond the far mouth. Only once the foot is formed (or 20 s pass) does the army go on as ever; its riders cross after the foot (`typeTake`).
    - The panel and the battle's start say **Hold the bridge** or **Take the bridge**.
  - **Ambush:** bands (bandits ×1, raiders and bog raiders ×1.2) or a hostile lord's army (×0.5) that come at you where there is cover spring one on 35% of days in a forest, 30% on hills or peaks, 15% where the field has woods or rock (`BTYPE_WORLD`).
    - The draw is from the tile and the day (`hash`), never `Math.random`. Patrols, caravans and fights you start never ambush you.
    - **Caught:** no deployment, and no fleeing. Your band starts in march order, 4 files wide, on a road down the middle (`ambushLayout`).
    - **Their side:** each group stands in a wood of its own, on one flank or both (deserts, and half of hill fields: behind a low ridge), its front 84 px off the column, beyond sight. Their first charge out of hiding gets `AMBUSH`'s shock and the "Ambush!" callout. For 6 s they go straight in.
    - **Spring an ambush:** riding at a band, lord, patrol or caravan where there is cover, the panel offers it beside Fight. Your band starts hidden beside the road and theirs in march order on it, marching on west till it sees you. Neither side deploys.
    - The **ambush on the road** (only where there is cover; an encounter on the map, `world/encounters.js` `ROAD_ENC.ambush`, unseen below Scouting 2, springing as you pass): fight (the ambush above), or cut the baggage loose and run (−4 food, −15 gold, −4 morale).
  - **Lab:** `type: 'field'` (default) | `'bridge'` | `'ambush'` | `{kind, side, ford}`. `side` is the side set upon (holding the bridge, or caught on the road), `'left'` by default. A typed battle with `ground:'none'` gets `'auto'`.
  - **Balance** (`tests/visual/btypebalance.js`, mirror armies of 20 foot, 12 bows and 6 riders, seeds 1–10, each side holding): see `doc/TERRAIN_DESIGN.md` §8, "Battle types as built".
  - **Tests:** `tests/battletypes.js`.
`TERRAIN.on` is the game's switch; `tests/harness.js` turns it off, so older suites see today's field. The lab's `ground` key takes `'none'` (default), `'auto'`, `{recipe, seed, oasis, river}` (recipe `'river'`: open with a river; `river: true | {bridge, frozen, stream}`) or explicit `{hills, woods, marsh, rivers:[{x, meander, crossings:[{kind, y, w}], frozen, stream}], rocks:[{x, y, r, h}], seed}` (hills take `cliff`, `oneway`, `ramps:[degrees]`; recipe `'crags'`: hills with cliffs and mountains). Balance: the hill holder wins ~69% of mirror battles; woods cut archer damage ~27%; hill battles run ~44% longer than flat (harder climbing; `HILL.climb` is the knob). Tests: `tests/terrain.js`, `tests/concealment.js`, `tests/desertlife.js`.

## Battle feedback

**Battle events** (`battle.js`): the fight never calls the HUD or the lab. It tells what just happened with `battleEvent(type, d)` (`rout`, `rally`, `flank`, `leader`, `down`, `left`, `ambush`, `fallback`, `btype`, `power`, `ability`, `withdraw`), and whoever shows or records it listens with `battleOn(fn)`: the banners and cards (`battlehud.js`), the commander bar's cue (`commandpowers.js`), the lab's telemetry (`lab.js`). Listeners run at once, in the order they were added, and never change the fight.

**Callouts** (`callout*` in `ui/battlehud.js`, tuned by `CALLOUT`): a short side-coloured banner at the top middle of the field for the big moments: a group routs, is shattered, rallies or is flanked; a leader falls; the enemy falls back (told by `battleEvent('fallback')` from `commander.js`, heard here). Each moment is called once (the same key stays quiet for `same` combat seconds; a flank at most once per group every `flankEvery` s; arrows and steady units never trigger it). At most one new banner every `gap` real seconds, at most two on screen; a burst is cut to a short queue, minor callouts first. Your groups' routs and shatters and a leader's fall also go to the battle log; the enemy breaking is logged once at the end. No callouts in tournaments.

**Screen shake** is drawn only (`shakeView` in `gfx/vfx.js`, tuned by `SHAKE`): `frame()` offsets the view by a sine wobble, never moving `B.cam` and never drawing random numbers. A rider or big beast charging home at momentum over `SHAKE.charge` jolts the view (capped at `SHAKE.max` px, fading over `SHAKE.life` s); one of your groups routing gives a lighter jolt. All of it, and the finisher's flash and zoom-punch below, shows only up close (`screenFx`): the view zoomed in very near (an arena camera at `SHAKE.near` or more, a world battle's map zoom at its closest step), and either an epic skill just now (Quake, Leap Slam's landing: `epicShake`) or a horde of `SHAKE.horde` beasts or more against you. Settings → Screen shake (`SHAKE.on`, the `bshake` setting) or the system's reduced-motion setting turns it off.

**The fighters' states, on them** (`STATE_FX` in `gfx/vfx.js`, drawn only: marks kept off the battle in `STATE_MARKS`, particles in `FXP` aged by the real clock, randomness from its own stream, so no fingerprint moves): a pulsing ring at a man's feet for War Cry (orange), the War Banner's reach (blue), Stand Fast! (gold) and a fresh rally (a gold flare); wavering, he flickers dim and trembles now and then; routing, his sprite goes pale and he kicks up dust; his group shattered, grey; stunned, stars circle his head; spent, a drop of sweat. A group breaking shudders and leaves a grey ring; a rally throws gold sparks from its men; a flank flashes red on the side struck; a leader falling, a red ring. Settings → **Battle effects**: Full, or Light (the rings, colours and stars only). Tests: statefx, uiprefs.

**The hero's moves, made flashy** (`HERO_FX` in `gfx/herofx.js`, drawn only in the same way, told by the battle's events `swing`, `blow`, `cast` and `land`): a ribbon of light along each swing's arc (a thrust's streak), wider on the finisher; the finisher landing gives a zoom-punch and a white flash (up close only, as the screen shake); a crit, a star of light; a crit or a kill of your hero's, a beat of slow motion (at most one every 3 s, on the loop's clock in `main.js`, never the battle's own; none in world battles or with reduced motion); Whirlwind, after-images all round; Leap Slam, a shadow that shrinks as he rises, then debris, a shockwave and a flash; Blade Dash, a streak; a spell, sparks drawn into the hand and a burst (spells still fire at once: the gathering is drawn, not waited for); a faint gold glow under your hero. Light keeps the ribbons, streaks, stars, shockwaves and flashes. Tests: herofx.

**Livelier bodies** (Wave 3, drawn only): a man on foot no longer swings from a still body. Each of a blow's nine frames (`people.js` `BLOW_RIG`: swing, thrust, overhead; wind-up, strike, follow-through) turns his body back into the wind-up and forward into the strike, bends the knees and steps the front foot in, and the fore fist carries the weapon (`c.rig`: `weapons.js` `drawHeld` puts it in that fist). The free arm swings back for balance, a two-handed weapon has both hands on it, a shield stays on its arm, and a pole's swing is a thrust from the hips. Riders keep their standing body. Struck, a man flinches (`FLINCH_POSE`, `BODY_LIFE.flinch` s); standing about he now and then glances back or shifts his weight (`LOOK_POSE`, `SHIFT_POSE`, from his id and the clock). The fallen go down one of three ways (`vfx.js` `FALLS`: over forwards, over backwards, to the knees then over), and the weapon is flung from the hand and lands beside them. A spin (Whirlwind, a spin blow) turns him round on his feet (`herofx.js` `spinFace`). Tests: bodylife, sprites.
**Blows and shots with weight** (2026-11-07; the owner: "make unit animations more realistic when battle", "archers bow shooting position is not correct"; `gfx/dollfigs.js`). A blow's weight is in his legs, his head and body never apart (`DOLL_LUNGE`): a cut steps his front foot out and lifts his back heel as it lands; a thrust lunges, his front leg reaching forward from the knee; a chop sinks all of him above the knees a pixel into it. None for a rider or a shooter. A shooter's arms are his own (`DOLL_SHOOT`, `shootArms`): drawing a bow, the bow arm rises to his shoulder and out toward his mark, the drawing hand forward across him to his chin, the string drawn back to it in a V (`figures.js` `BOW_PULL`, `bowPull`, `bowLine`; the owner: "fix posture"), the arrow from it to its head past the bow; the quiver slung close behind his shoulder, mostly hidden by him, its fletching by his head (`QuiverGear`; it was a box at his hip); the reload, a hand back to the quiver over his shoulder; a crossbow raised to his shoulder to aim and lowered to span it. Weapons: a melee weapon is drawn apart from the body in battle and on the march, turned to its angle (`render.js` `drawUnit`: the bare body, then `heldNow`, `weaponSprite`); a bowman's bow is drawn apart too in battle, aimed (the owner: "make bows aim up and down too"; `figures.js` `bowPic`, `BOW_AIM`; `render.js` `bowAim`): the body drawn without it (`unitSprite(..., 'bow')`: `bowApart`), the bow, string and arrow turned about his hand to the nearest of 0, 15, 30 or 45 degrees up, by the shot's rise (`battle/projectiles.js` `arcRise`, `ARC_RISE`: with the square of the distance, higher for a mark up a slope, flatter for one below him; `BOW_AIM.k` how steeply), level for a near shot, 45 only for a long one, drawn as lines so it never breaks into dots; and the arrow loosed flies by the same rise (`battle/projectiles.js` `Missile.fire`, `arcDh`: his ground less the ground where it falls), lobbing up to a mark above him or over a long way and flying flatter down to one below or close by (level ground as before); the reload level, a hand back to the quiver. A weapon carried on the move moves with his stride, never turned (the owner: "make weapon at least shake or moving a bit with unit walking animations", then "shift from up to down or left to right instead of changing the angle"; `weapons.js` `WEAPON_SWAY` (a frame's [dx, dy]), `swayOf`, `poseStride`; `heldNow(u, o, sp, pose)`, `mapHeld(L, sp, walking, step)`): a pixel forward and up as he passes, back and up on the other, its own angle kept; his weapon hand goes down with him as each foot lands; in the saddle only at a run (his hand bobs with his mount's gait already), none with a foe at hand. The walk's legs scissor (the owner: "make the left legs cross and go back, same with right leg"; `engine/doll.js` `LEG`, `STRIDE_SPLIT`): apart as the near foot lands, together under him as they pass, crossed as the far one lands, each leg bent forward or back a pixel more at each joint down to its foot. His steps go by the way he has walked, a step each `WALK.foot` × `WALK.step` px (a long stride, at most `WALK.cap` frames a second: the owner, "it seems too fast") (render.js `walkPhase`: a battle's own field × `WALK.field`, a battle on the map × its scale; the march's `marcherStep` by `u.walk`), so his feet keep to the ground at any speed (the owner: "the sprite seems like they are floating"). A bowman is lean: his arms in at his sides standing and walking (`dollfigs.js` `DOLL_LEAN`; the owner: "make an archers unit sprite less bulky"), his quiver a pixel thin. Nothing on the map is ringed in ink (the owner: "some world map sprites still has black outer line instead of darker shading colour"): every outline a deep shade of what it borders (`engine/pixel.js` `PX.rim`; by a dark body, `PX.RIM.dark`, a lighter shade of it and no navy, so dark timber or a shadowed wall keeps its own colour), `PX.outline` (the map's props, pieces) shaded so unless given a colour, ships, carts and an encounter's horse edged in their own tones (`g.edge`), a building's outline shaded from its body as it finally stands (`engine/model.js`). A worker's load sits in against his back, its top at his shoulders, behind his body and his back arm (`figures.js` `LOAD_AT`; the owner: "their item in the bag looks separated from their back"). Off the field a sword, a dagger or a one-handed axe is worn out of sight (the owner: "make weapon sheathing ... only when non battle", then, a sheath a few pixels big tangling with the body at the map's size, chose it hidden: `weapons.js` `SHEATH.show` false; true draws its scabbard behind his hip, `sheathSprite`); great weapons and bows in hand, a rider's too; a spear or a pike on the march sloped over his shoulder, its shaft behind it and its head over his back, its butt at his hand (`weapons.js` `SHOULDER`; the owner: "make spearmen carry spears over the shoulder on the march"), upright before him standing, a javelin in hand. In battle every weapon is drawn. Weapons' lengths beside a spear's 12 (`people.js` `WPN`; the owner: "some of the weapon is longer than it has to be if compared with spear"): a sword and a one-handed axe 5, a dagger 3, a great sword and big axe 8, a maul 7. Nothing touches the ground on the move (the owner: "when walk it touches the ground"): a sword walking and running point up before him (`HOLD_DOLL.blade.march`), low at his side standing; a one-handed axe head up (`holdKind`: `hafted`); a lowered weapon at most `DOLL_DROP` (2) px under his hand; a pole a pixel higher all the walk long (`WEAPON_SWAY.pole`). A great sword, big axe or hammer is carried on the move in both hands, up before him as in the grip (`dollfigs.js` `DOLL_CARRY2`, `DOLL_GRIP.grip`), his legs walking. A shooter on foot stands braced as he shoots, his front foot forward and his back foot set behind him (`dollfigs.js` `DOLL_SHOOT_STANCE`). A crossbow, sling or staff stays the figure's own gear layer (`figures.js` `ShooterGear`); a horse archer's bow stays on his figure. Test: `bowaim`. A realm's armies are its own peoples (`world/realms.js` `Realm#races`: its holdings' races that are the realm's, the Salt League's elves, humans and orcs, never the dwarves of a town it holds).

**Hands and legs** (2026-11-07; the owner: "the hero and unit sprites hand looks longer than legs"; `engine/doll.js` `SKEL`, `RACES`, `SEAT_HANDS`). A man on foot has his hands at his hips, a row higher, and legs a row longer (a man 16 px of body, an elf 17, a dwarf 15). A rider keeps the first cut's legs and hands (`spec.seat`, from `o.mount`), so he and his lance sit the horse pixel for pixel as before. A braced pike's front hand is a pixel further out (`DOLL_GRIP.brace`), still before his resting hand. A figure's shaded edge (`people.js` `prim`) is drawn only where it is 3 px or more across, so a leg, an arm or a bow 1 or 2 px wide keeps its own colours (the owner: "no need darker shader if that area is only 1 px").

**Cleaner figures** (2026-11-07; the owner: "why shield float with no hand grab it?", "avoid using too many shading to make unit sprite looks clean and distinguishable for each body part", with a reference sheet). A shield comes before his chest in a blow and his fist shows on its rim at rest (`figures.js` `SHIELD_HOLD`). Cloth is no longer mottled (`engine/doll.js` `LOOK`). The side's colour is on his chest only: a tabard to the hips, a jerkin with his shirt's sleeves showing, his hose by the man (`dollfigs.js` `DOLL_TABARD`, `DOLL_LEGS`). Knights and champions wear a white surcoat with a cross in the side's colour (`o.surcoat`, `PARTS.cross`). A robe falls whole to the feet, its hem flared and darker (`PARTS.robe.skirt`). In the frost a man wears a wolf's or a bear's pelt over his head and a fur mantle, a helm of rank kept (`people.js` `WINTER`, `winterLook`; `sprites.js` `DRESS`, set by a battle's ground and a march's place). Goblins are their own race: small, green, long ears out behind the head (`RACES.goblin`). Every head is side-on (the owner: "there should be no eyes and the head should look like others, facing a bit to the left or right not center"): a hood, a coif, long hair and a pelt are open before the face, never framing it full-face, and an elf's ear is behind it. A blow's arm is as long as his arm at rest, which ends at his hips (the owner: "the hand is too long"): the rig's joints drawn `DOLL_REACH.k` (.7) of the way out from his shoulder, bare fists `.fist` (.6) (`dollfigs.js`). Every figure is one piece (the owner: "recheck whether the body composition still match with the new sprites"; `tests/figurepieces.js`, 3600 pictures: each kind standing, walking, running, striking and guarding, bare and whole, folk and workers with their loads, kneeling and down): the weapon hand the inner cell of the hand, as before the arms were shortened; a cape a small one (the owner, 2026-11-08: "just a small pixel and look like cape"): fastened at the neck, a pixel's fall behind his far shoulder, a darker hem at the knee, joined to him only at the neck and shoulder; a whole figure's blow (the map's, the cards') with its striking arm and the weapon in its fist, not hung beside him.

**The AI by the ways there are** (2026-11-08; the owner: "fix the auto AI and AI in battle map, especially in the interior maps such as caves, fort, etc. make sure they order the unit based on available path"; `battle/commander.js` `PATH_PLACE`, `SIEGE_AI`; `battle/battlecontrol.js` `standAt`). Every captain (the enemy's, your allies', yours on Auto) placed its line by straight geometry (the foe's x less a reach), which inside a cave or a lair's cellars sent groups into the rock or left them waiting at the mouth. Now, on ground that blocks and with the straight way barred: its shooters' and its infantry's places are their reach (or the screen's gap) short of the foe along the walking way to him, a shooter's where he sees him (`Commander#pathPlace`); going looking in the dark, the army steps on along the way toward the far side from its middle, together (`pathStep`); and any place given in rock (by a captain or a click) is the nearest point its men can walk to, on their side of it (`standAt`). At a fort's wall the attacker's foot work the ram (`SIEGE_AI.ram` of its foot groups, in 2 files at the gate's face) and the rest go to the foot of the ladders and up at the nearest defender, till the gate is down (`Commander#siege`). Measured with the game's own switches on, the AI on both sides (`tests/interiorai.js`): orders into rock in caves and lairs 498 to 27, first contact a few seconds sooner, and a fort's gate down at about 40 s, never before. Those they come for wait to be found (the owner: "the player party may be the one who will search while those goblins, bandits or monsters may stay at their place if not alerted or detected"): a cave's, a lair's or a fort's men, and now a camp's on the open field (a goblin camp, a hideout's tents: `INTERIOR.holdCamp`), stay at their posts till roused (one of yours seen within `INTERIOR.notice` px, one of theirs hurt, or a roused group of theirs within `INTERIOR.alarm` px: `insideRoused`); the stall watch no longer sends them out (`stallWatch`); your party goes looking, chamber by chamber at a walk.

**Outlaws** (2026-11-07; the owner: "add more variations for bandits not to use the soldier knight sprites"; `data/outlaws.js`). A band's men are its own: looter (hatchet), cutthroat (sword), brigand (axe, round shield), highwayman (spear), poacher (bow), each as strong as the kind it stands for, rolled as before (`outlawOf` maps `makeEnemies`' roll). Each man is dressed his own way (`people.js` `OUTLAW_DRESS`: a headscarf, a hood, a cap or his bare head; cloth of the road), his side's colour on a headscarf or a sash, never a tabard; the chieftain keeps his horns and fur, in dark iron. Orc raiders and the bog's men stay orcs. `OUTLAWS.on` false: the old troops. Tests: `tests/outlaws.js`.

**Longer, steadier battles** (2026-10-31; the owner: "the battle seems to finish too fast", "routing happen too much", and a router that may rally "should stay in battle area only. Otherwise, they will be immortal beyond the boundary"): every fighter's health on the field is doubled (`world/characters.js` `FIELD_HP`; your hero goes in at twice his health and comes out at half his battle health, a lord the same: `battle/setup.js`, `resolution.js`, `sides.js`); every loss of morale (shocks, the strain of recent dead, being outmatched) × `GROUP_RESOLVE.grit` (.55). Measured, AI against AI in the lab (two seeds each): from first contact 11-17 s before, 34-79 s now; mixed armies' routs 4-5 of 6 groups before, 1-2 now. In a world battle a router who may still rally keeps inside the ring, running along its edge where he can be run down, and rallies there; only one broken for good leaves, and none comes back (`worldbattle.js` escape, `WORLD_BATTLE.returns` 0). Tests: worldbattle, worldbattleedges.

**A fleer out of a dead end** (2026-11-03; a code review's reproduction: a dead-end corridor whose way out lay past the flight's search, `FleeRoutes.plan` found nothing and the empty plan was kept for ever, the fleer standing still): `world/router.js` `FleeRoutes` keeps a search that found no way `FLEE_ROUTE.wait` of the fleer's steps (no search every moment), then makes it again twice as far (its radius and depth), up to `grow` times; a way found, or the threat or ground changed, and it starts again at its own size. Fingerprints held. Tests: fleedeadend.

**A way's goal and its outcome told plainly** (2026-11-03; a code review: "reject or explicitly clamp outside-grid goals, and keep the distinction between unreachable and search budget exhausted through the marching wrapper"): `battle/route.js` routePath takes a goal off the grid at the nearest point on it and says so (`clamped`), the way never ending off the grid (its cell was taken at the edge before, but a straight line could end the way at the goal itself); `world/march.js` `MarchGround#path` keeps `ok` (reached), `cut` (the budget ran out first), `n` and `clamped` on the ways it gives. Fingerprints held. Tests: routegoal.

**The path cache's key** (2026-11-03; a code review: "routes with zero versus nonzero body clearance can share a cache key"): `battle/route.js` routePath keyed a way with no room as `s*N+t` and one with room as `(s*N+t)*128+q`, so a pair without room could take the cached way of another pair with room (and a room that rounded to 0, a way without). One key for all now, `(s*N+t)*128+q`, q the room in quarter px (1-127; 0 only for none); the smoothing's half-width is done after the cache, on any, so it needs no place in it. Fingerprints held. Tests: routememo.

**A march's grid follows its buildings** (2026-11-03; the code review's finding: a building raised, finished or burnt moved no world version, so a march's grid was kept right by its 2 s timer alone): `world/march.js` `MarchGround#fresh` is stale when the world version moved or any building or site under it differs (`marchBlocksSig`: each lot's state and picture, `lotSprite`, the same till it changes; each site's while you know it), and the grid is made again at once. Its 2 s timer now runs only while some man is still making for his place: an army settled (every man at rest or given up) keeps its grid (`MarchArmy#groundStep`, the grid's lifetime apart from the step). Dropping the timer outright left men forming round walls and giving up otherwise (tests/corridor.js), so it stays for them. Measured standing by Ashford, 20 s: 17 grids made before, 6 now; march formations 2.05 ms a frame before, 1.22 now. Fingerprints held. Tests: marchbuildings.

**A slit between buildings is no way** (2026-11-04; the owner: "unit seems to stuck if building is built like this"): Ashford's guards walked into the 8 px gap between two houses by its smithy, narrower than a man, and stood there, since no way led out of it. `world/march.js` `MarchGround#shutSlits` shuts any open run along a row or a column of the march's grid that is shut on both sides, a building's footing on one of them, and no wider than `MARCH_MOVE.slit` px (8). The lane a tile wide between Ashford's castle and its houses stays open. Test: `tests/marchslit.js`.

**No freeze on the first frame** (2026-11-03; found with the frame timings): a new game's or a load's first frame on the map took 2.5 s, one lord's first step making every holding's centre picture to read its walls (`world/router.js` holdShut, `lotSprite`). They are made now behind the loading bar, its last step "Raising the walls" (`holdShutWarm`, one holding at a time; cached on each lot): that first frame 0.22 s. Fingerprints held.

**Your band's way, walkable** (2026-11-10, the owner: "Please fix pathfinder and the guide path to use a walkable path otherwise my character would walk into obstacle and sometime stuck"; `world/world.js` `heroWay`, `world/router.js` `wayRouted`, `WAY_FOOT`, `WAY_FOOT_LOT`, `footMask`, `wayOpenSpot`, `Router.open`; tests: herowalk): (1) once the router has your way to a goal you keep to it till that trip is done: a step that found the straight line clear went straight, the next, a hair off it, went back to the router's first point, and you swayed on the spot for ever, never counted stuck; (2) your way, and the way drawn ahead of you (the same steps, `wuiWay`), goes round a holding's buildings (houses, halls, workshops, wells, carts; fields and pastures walked over): such a tile costs `WAY_FOOT_LOT.cost` (30) times as much and no straight line crosses one but where you stand or are going; dearer, never shut, so a goal walled in by buildings is still reached through them; only your band's ways change; (3) a click on water, a peak, a holding's shut keep or walls, or a building walks you to the nearest open spot you can reach (within `WAY_FOOT_LOT.near` tiles), never to a wall to stop short. 60 trips from holdings' gates (a probe): before, 27 walked through buildings, 2 stuck, 2 swaying for ever; after, none.

**A shut crossing shut to every step** (2026-11-03; a code review: "direct player movement accepts a closed bridge tile that the router rejects"): the straight-line check (`world/router.js` `clearLine`) kept off a holding's walls only, so any straight step, the router's own and your hero's, crossed a bridge shut by `Router.close`; it keeps off a shut crossing now as the tile searches do, and your hero's last fallback step (`world/world.js` heroStep's walk) keeps off one too (`crossingOpen`). Nothing in play shuts a crossing yet (Phase 5's seasons). Tests: crossingwalk.

**Frame timings** (2026-11-03; after a code review: measure before optimising, on the phone itself): `ui/frameprof.js` `FrameProf`, tuned by `FRAMEPROF`. Off by default; Settings → Developer tools → Frame timings, or `perf` in the console, shows a readout at the top: a frame's update and draw ms (median and worst over 5 s), the frames a second and the slow ones (over 33 ms), and the five systems costing most (each timed as it runs, through `Systems.timer`, set only while on: off, nothing is timed). Tests: frameprof.

**Map weapons apart** (2026-11-03; the owner: "make a weapon sprite to look separated from the horse ... the same concept as battle"): the map's marchers are drawn as battle draws its fighters, the body bare and the weapon its own sprite in his hand, at rest or on the march (`gfx/weapons.js` `mapHeld`, `HOLD`; his look `gfx/sprites.js` `spriteLook`, kept on him: `world/march.js` `marchLook`), behind his body when it falls there; a flag-bearer carries his flag only. The horse's chest had a pixel jutting at the foot of its neck: gone (`people.js` `HORSE_ART` body, stand and run).

**Standing about** (2026-10-31; the owner: idle animation, "breathing and fidgets"; `gfx/render.js` `idleLife`, tuned by `BODY_LIFE`): a figure standing never lifts as a whole (a breathing bob made them look to float, so it was taken out); a man on foot now and then glances back or shifts his weight, a horse tosses its head, a beast sways. In battle and among the map's marchers (`world/march.js` drawMarcher), from each one's id and the clock, drawn only. Tests: bodylife.

**The wounded under their groups** (2026-10-31; the owner: "wounded still not showing in march formation"): men fallen sick on the march (supply.js) and hurt by an outcome are kept under their own groups (`world/world.js` `woundTroops`, P.woundedGroups) as a battle's wounded are, so the March and Army windows show them there. Tests: marchwounded.

**Unit of the battle**: kills are counted per source group (`B.grpKills`, `applyDmg`); the battle report names your troop group with the most kills (ties: `resolve.damageDealt`). Not shown in tournaments, for heroes and companions, or when none of your groups fought. Tests: `tests/battlejuice.js`.

## Unit kits: orders, stances, skills

doc/UNIT_KITS_DESIGN.md. **Step 1 built (2026-11-01): the refactor, the same game** (the battle fingerprints held). **Step 2 built the same day: the new rules** (below, "Step 2"; what follows here is step 1's, its keys and ladders now behind step 2's switches). One name for each kind of thing, each its one table:
- **Orders** (`data/kits.js` `ORDERS`): Move and Attack by the mouse (Shift a waypoint, a drag a line, Ctrl or D an attack-move), Charge (V: at a run), Hold (H), Withdraw (W). Advance is a Move now (the Pace stance's Walk).
- **Stances** (`STANCES`): a row of the order bar each, for the groups with a soldier who can: Pace (Q; its Charge and Walk buttons), Discipline (F: hold ground), Shooting (M: one cycle of Fire at will, Hold fire, Skirmish, Melee; its buttons pick one, pressed again Fire at will), Shield wall (B), Mounted (J: Dismount while any rider is up, else Mount; the two skills with their cooldowns till step 2). The flags under them are as before (`GRP_FLAGS`; `FLAG_N`, their words, from `STANCES`).
- **Formations** (`FORMS`), as before.
- **Traits** (`data/abilities.js` `TRAITS`; `ABILITIES` is the characters' skills, `HERO_SKILLS`, and the traits, one list as before).
- **Skills** (`battle/unitabilities.js` `UNIT_SKILLS`, `UNIT_ABILITIES` its old name; `SKILLS` is your hero's proficiencies): bursts with a cooldown, on the **skill bar**, three slots on **Z, X, C**, each selected group its own skill in each (`groupSlot`); its old letters (G, I, U, Y, O, N, P) quiet aliases.
- **Kits** (`KITS`: each kind's ladder, the rank each skill and perk comes at, `RANK_SKILLS` its old name; a kind with none has each skill its soldiers qualify for from rank 1).
- **Commands** (`battle/commandpowers.js` `COMMANDS`, `COMMAND_POWERS` its old name): your hero's, Rally the Line (R) and Stand Fast! (S).

**`UnitKit`** (`battle/unitkit.js`): a unit's loadout, worked out once from its record (roster group, AI army's, or a battle group's rank alone) and kept on it, made again when its type, rank, picks or ladders change: its traits, stances, skills in slot order (its ladders' first, each at its rung's rank, then the rest its soldiers qualify for; a locked one waits in its slot), perks (Long reach, Steady aim, Braced flanks, Marksmen), rungs reached (`rungs(rode)`: no foot drill on a horse), a pick's gain (`up`) and proficiency (`prof`). The battle (`rankskills.js` `rankRungs`, `rankSkill`, `rankAllows`, `rankUp`, `rankUnit`), the AI captains (through `groupHasAbility`), the order bar (`groupSlots`) and the unit card (`unitcard.js` `troopGrowth`) read it. Tests: `tests/unitkit.js`.

**The order bar** (`ui/battlehud.js` `bhudBar`, built from the tables at load): the orders (Hold, Attack-move), the stances (Pace: Charge, Walk; Discipline; Shooting; Shield wall; Mounted), the formations, the skill bar (`bhudSkills`: three slots, a slot whose units differ marked +, a locked one dimmed with its rank), Withdraw last on the right. Today's buttons keep their `data-order`, `data-flag` and `data-form`, in their order. On a phone, in the Orders drawer, every button in one grid six a row (`tests/mobilebattlehud.js`).

### Step 2: the new rules (built 2026-11-01)

doc/UNIT_KITS_DESIGN.md §10.2, the owner's answers in §11. Each part has its own switch, on in the game (off: step 1's, as above; `tests/unitkit.js` and the other step-1 suites run with all of them off: `Kit.kits(false)` in `tests/kit.js`):
- **Ranks give stats only** (`RANKS.statsOnly`, §6; `data/ranks.js` `RANKS.per`): each rank above the first +3% damage, health and aim, +2 morale, +2% vigor (`UnitKit` `stats`; `rankskills.js` `rankUnit`, `rankAim`, `rankMor`, on his start in `morale.js` `moraleInit`, and a lab's in `lab.js` `initialize`, unscaled by a band's morale: the owner, 2026-11-01). Drill +1% each. No skill from a rank. The ranks named Recruit, Trained, Seasoned, Veteran, Elite (`rankWord`).
- **Mastery: five levels** (`RANKS.mastery5`, §7; `RANKS.levels`): Adept 300, Expert 700, Master 1,200, Grandmaster 1,800, Legend 2,500 experience past rank 5 (`masteryWord`); the badge's metal underneath (bronze, silver, silver, gold, gold). Each level one pick from the kind's menu (`data/kits.js` `KITS.kinds[k].menu`, the options in `MASTERY`): unlock a skill (`sk`), improve a skill or stance (`up`, `by`: again as its own step), a perk (`perk`, its numbers on the soldier or a flag read where it acts), or Drill. The pick window: the Army window, a troop group's Development (`partypage.js` `masteryHtml`), the kind's menu with each option's effect in words, those it may not take dimmed (`UnitKit#opts`). An AI unit picks by its kind's order (`ai`; `rankskills.js` `aiOf`), on the fly, nothing saved.
- **Kits** (§3-§5): each kind's default skills (spear Lunge and Rally; foot Rally; bow Rapid shot; crossbow Volley; cavalry and horse archers Spur; scouts Scout ahead; mage none); a unit's kinds by class, shot and tags (`unitkit.js` `kitKinds`; a scout its kind's and the scouts'; slings and javelins a Thrower kind of their own).
- **Brace and Pavise, stances** (`KITS.brace`, §3; `battlecontrol.js` `plant`, `posted`, `planted`): Brace planted where they stand (`KITS.braceAt.set` s; Quick brace at once), no timer, no cooldown; braced, front and flanks take no charge bonus nor shock, pushes barely move them (`unitabilities.js` `postStep`), Brace+ unhorses more; a move order ends it. Unbraced spears take a charge's bonus (the always-braced front is gone; the Hedge of spears perk braces the front unordered). Pavise (crossbows): bolts and arrows from the front ×`KITS.pavise.cover` while planted (`kitGuard`, from `morale.js` `flankMul`); a move ends it, the foe they are set on does not. Lunge, the spears' default skill (3 s: +25% reach, +20% damage, the enemy's front pushed back; 40 s).
- **Traits Anti-cavalry and Anti-large** (`KITS.traits2`, §4; `data/abilities.js` `TRAITS`): ×1.6 against riders (every spear), ×1.4 against the big (spears of tier 2 and up: pikes, veterans), in `matchups.js` `matchup`.
- **The cap of 5 and the tech tree's skills** (`KITS.cap`, §5): a `BUFFS` entry's `skill` (`world/buffs.js` `Buffs.skills`) in the unit's next free slot; Fire arrows the first (`BUFFS.fireArrows`, its building a **placeholder**, the smith's, till the owner names one): lit, its shots ×`KITS.fire.cd` slower and ×`KITS.fire.aim` less true (Hot coals: no aim cost), those it hits shaken (`fear`). An AI lord's archers have it from his level `KITS.aiFire`; bands never.
- **The keys** (`KITS.keys2`, §7b; `data/kits.js` `KIT_KEYS`, `kitKey`): Q W E the stance slots (`groupStances`, `commands.js` `stanceSlot`); Z X C V B the skill slots; H Hold, G Charge (a true order, `chargeOrder`), D Attack-move, R Walk/Run, F Discipline, T the formation cycle (`formCycle`), Backspace Withdraw, A the AI; Y Rally the Line, U Stand Fast!. M and S are free; the old letters' aliases gone. The order bar (`battlehud.js` `bhudBar`, `bhudStances`): Hold, Charge, Attack-move; Run, Walk; Discipline; the three stance slots, each its key and what it holds for the selection; the formations; five skill slots; Withdraw last. Step 1's stance rows are kept hidden (their flag buttons still the flags'). On a phone, in the Orders drawer, six a row.
- **AI** (`commander.js`): Brace when a charge comes (`braceOn`, `braceCan`, `braceUse`; its timing, the owner, 2026-11-01: `chargeIn`, `AI_TACTICS` `braceAt` 180 px, `braceMom` .75, `braceT` 1.9 s, `braceIn` 30 px, `braceKeep` 120 px: only for riders or a big beast riding straight at its front, closing at three quarters of their top speed or more (not a feint nor a ride round), soon enough to plant the spears before they strike, none standing in the melee; taken up once the charge has struck or turned away; again only for a new run-up. AI on AI, `tests/visual/kitbalance.js`: Pikemen beat Knights 6 of 6, braced or not; before, 0 of 6 braced, the brace planted too late and kept against riders in the melee), Pavise when shot at, Shield wall under arrows as before; a kit's skills by their moment (`captainSkills`: `UNIT_SKILLS[id].ai`, melee, shoot, charge, threat).
- **Old saves** (§9): a pick's old id read as the new menu's (`KITS.old`: brace Brace+, reach Long reach, aim Steady aim, marks Marksmen, rally Rally+, rapid Rapid shot+, fire Hot coals); a save with no `kits` piece (`core/ranks.js`, order 355) has its groups' rank-given rungs kept as picks up to their levels (`KITS.oldRungs`: spear Long reach at 3, Hedge of spears at 5; bow Fire arrows at 2, Steady aim at 3, Marksmen at 5; `kitsMigrate`).
- **The unit card and the Army window** (`unitcard.js` `kitChips`, `partypage.js` `troopKitHtml`): its traits, its stances in their slots, its skills in theirs (where from: its kind, its mastery, bought), its earned perks (★), its rank's and mastery's names.
Tests: `tests/kits2.js` (a phone's window).

### The skills

The selected groups' skills are shown on the skill bar; a press (or its key) uses it for every selected group that can. A group has a skill when at least half its fighting troops qualify. Durations and cooldowns run on combat time (pausing stops them); a lit button shows the seconds left, a shaded one the seconds until ready, and the tooltip says why one is unavailable.

| Skill | Slot | Who | Effect | Lasts | Cooldown |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Brace flanks** | spearmen 1 (rank 2); G too | spear and pike infantry | stands fast where it is; a charge on its front or flank gets no charge bonus (spearmen always brace on their front while fighting; Brace flanks adds the flanks; the rear is never braced); pushes barely move it; any move, attack, advance or withdraw order ends it | 8 s | 40 s |
| **Fire arrows** | bowmen 1 (rank 2) | bow and crossbow archers | shots deal fire damage (magical: armour doesn't stop it; wards and resistances apply), 10% more | 12 s | 45 s |
| **Spur** | riders 1 | cavalry | full charge momentum at once and 35% more speed, at a run, for 12 stamina per rider; momentum is kept while it lasts, then the group returns to its earlier pace unless given a new order | 3 s | 35 s |
| **Rally** | spearmen 2 (rank 4), foot 1 | foot units (not bowmen) | +15 shared morale (at most 100; boosts above the recovery ceiling fade at `GROUP_RESOLVE.fade`, 1 per combat second) and an end to Breaking; not while routing or with morale off | at once | 60 s |
| **Rapid shot** | bowmen 2 (rank 4) | bowmen | shots 30% faster | 8 s | 45 s |
| **Dismount** | the Mounted stance (J) | troop riders in the saddle (one is enough) | they step down where they stand and fight on foot (no charge; their foot drills, Brace flanks and Braced flanks, theirs again); their horses wait there; they stay on foot till Mount | at once | 4 s |
| **Mount** | the Mounted stance (J) | troop riders on foot whose horse lives (one is enough) | each walks back to his own horse and mounts, enemies near or not | at once | 4 s |
| **Scout ahead** | after its kind's (mounted archers 3) | scout troops only (`data/units.js` `SCOUT_KINDS`, `isScout(kind)`: Ranger, Horseman, Squire, Mounted Archer, Ram Rider, Elf Marksman, Hart Rider, Hart Archer, Orc Hunter, Wolf Rider, Wolf Archer; never a character) | sight in the fog of war ×1.6 (`battlefog.js` `fogRange` × `abilitySight`) and speed ×1.25 (`abilitySpeed`, with Spur's); with the fog off only the speed (its tooltip says so). The owner, 2026-10-20: it replaces the commander's Scout | 12 s | 45 s |

API for captains: `g.abilities()` lists `{id,active,left,cooldown,why}` (`why===''`: usable now) and `g.useAbility(id)` returns false when refused. Per-group state `g.abil` is non-enumerable (out of snapshots and saves). Tests: `tests/unitabilities.js`. Every AI captain uses them too (`AI_TACTICS` in commander.js): Brace when riders or a big beast with momentum come within 80 px of a spear group's front (left braced while one is within 120 px), Rally a wavering or Breaking group, Spur a fresh charge on a mark 60–140 px off with stamina ≥ 45, Fire arrows when their mark wears mail or plate or is weak to fire. Tests: `tests/aitactics.js`. Scout ahead (`captainScout`): a captain's scouts range ahead while some of the foe still fighting is unseen and either it sees none of them or the fight is in its first `UNIT_SKILLS.scout.aiEarly` (20) s; with every foe in sight (no fog, no ground hiding them) never.

## Commander powers

An army's own orders from its commander (`battle/commandpowers.js`, table `COMMANDS`, `COMMAND_POWERS` its old name), apart from the groups' skills and the hero's own skills (`charmind.js`). The **commander** is your hero (`B.hero`) on your side, their lord or chieftain (their first leader on the field) on theirs, else none. A power works only while the commander is alive, on the field and not running. Rally the Line and Stand Fast! reach the groups of the commander's side that have a soldier within the **command radius**: 220 px, +8% a level of Leadership, at most +60% (352 px). Leadership: your hero's `skill('lead')`; a lord's own Leadership; a chieftain's (anyone's without the skill) half his level. Cooldowns run on combat time and shrink 4% a level of Leadership, down to 60%.

| Power | Key | Effect | Cooldown (Leadership 0 → 10) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Rally the Line** | R | every routing unit of your side in range rallies through morale.js's own `rally()` (it stops, holds and re-forms; a unit rallied once and routing again is shattered, as ever) with +10 on top of the rally's 40; every wavering or Breaking unit in range gets +20 and stops breaking. A unit it rallied cannot be rallied by it again for 120 s (`cmdRallyAt`). Refused, no cooldown spent, when no one in range routs or wavers | 60 → 36 s |
| **Stand Fast!** | S | every unit of your side in range (not routing): +10 morale, 40% less morale shock, and it cannot drop to wavering (nor break) for 12 s (`standUntil` on its resolve; `BattleResolve.standing`/`standFloor`). A gold glow under its soldiers and a Stand Fast badge on its card and icon (`grpMorale(...).stand`, `statusOf` 'standfast') | 45 → 27 s |

The commander's **Scout** (O, the enemy seen through the fog for the first 10 s) was removed on the owner's call, 2026-10-20: scouting is the scout units' own Scout ahead (the skills).

**The HUD:** a commander bar down the left of the field (`bhudPowers`, `#bCmd`), round gold-rimmed buttons distinct from the order bar: a sweep that empties as the cooldown runs out and the seconds; lit while Stand Fast! is on; dimmed (still pointable) with the reason in the tooltip (name, effect, cooldown, radius, Leadership, "needs your commander alive and the units within range"). Pointing at Rally or Stand Fast! draws the command radius round your hero and rings under the units it would reach (`drawCommandPowers`, render.js); a use shows it for a moment, with a ring from the commander, a word over him, a callout ("Rally!", "Stand Fast!"; theirs in their colour) and the log. Touch: the buttons are 44-46 px; a tap uses the power. Keys Z, S (in the Help's controls).

**No order:** a power goes through none of `playerOrder` (commands.js): no group's `orderSeq` moves, a character's fall-back goes on, and Auto stays as it is.

**The AI** (`powersThink`, from `commander.js` `command`; every 0.5 s of the fight): the enemy's commander always, yours under Auto. Rally the Line when a unit in range routs, or two waver; else Stand Fast! when a unit in range wavers, or as two of them (all, if fewer) come within 120 px of a foe they see. No random numbers.

**Switches:** `COMMAND_POWERS.on` (`tests/harness.js` turns it off for the older suites, so their fingerprints hold); the lab's `powers` key (true/false) overrides it, and its `commander` key `{left, right}` names a side's commander (null: none; `{group, lead}`: the first soldier of that row commands, with that Leadership). No power in tournaments. Per-side state `B.cmdp[team]` (made on first use).

**Balance** (`tests/visual/powerbalance.js`: AI-on-AI mirror armies with a commander each, Leadership 3, seeds 1-10, left/right wins): neither 5/5 (mean 75 s); both 5/5 (69 s); left only 6/4; right only 4/6 (so the side with powers wins about one battle in ten more); in the fog, both 3/7 (81 s), neither 8/2 (41 s) (measured with the commander's Scout, before its removal). Rally the Line and Stand Fast! are each used about once a battle a side. Tests: `tests/commandpowers.js`.

## Touch controls

For a phone on its side. Touch reads Pointer Events with `pointerType 'touch'` (`TOUCH` in `ui/input.js`: `hold` 450 ms, `slop` 10 CSS px, `ring` 34 CSS px); mouse, pen and keys are unchanged.
- **Battle:** tap a group to select it, tap again to send it there or onto the enemy tapped (a tap acts when the finger lifts). Hold still for about half a second (a gold ring fills; red over an enemy) to move the selection there or attack the enemy held; hold, then drag, to lay out the line as a right-drag does. Drag without holding to box groups. Two fingers: pinch to zoom about them (`camPinch`), move both to pan; a second finger cancels what the first was doing (a held line's ghost simply goes, nothing sent, the selection kept) and the two then pinch, so a pinch never selects or orders.
- **Calling a drag off (mouse and keys):** a left-click during a right-drag, or Esc, drops the line ghost: no order, the selection kept, and letting go of the right button does nothing (`dragCancel`, input.js; a left press with the right held comes as a `pointermove`, so both are read). Esc also calls off a box or a finger's press; a drag takes Esc before the window chain, so it opens no menu. Tests: `tests/dragcancel.js`.
- **Every formation drags** (2026-10-31; the owner: right-drag "seems to support only lines"; then "make the column or other formation work similar to line"): the dragged width sets a line's, loose order's, a pack's and a wedge's (its widest rank: the point, then ranks growing to it). **Column** is a formation of its own (`FORMS.column`): files two abreast (`cw`), a file's gap between columns; a drag sets how many columns stand side by side, the rest of the men behind, so the same line is deeper than a line's (`formations.js` `dragFiles`, `formSlots`; the march formation designer the same). The March formation preview draws each man at the map's own size for its scale, as his spacing is (`ui/march.js` `marchSpriteK`).
- **Map:** tap to travel (as the finger lifts); a tap on another party opens its ring (Meet or Attack, Follow, Look), a double tap approaches it. Hold still for the action ring; its Look shows the card of what is there (its tooltip, "Tap:" for "Click:"); the next tap puts it away without travelling. On a phone (the window's shorter side 540 CSS px or less: `MAPZOOM.SMALL`) the former default map scale is now the zoom-out limit. Auto starts about 50% closer, with additional zoom-in steps (`MAPZOOM.PHONE_START`, `PHONE_MAX`; Auto in Settings). The HUD, minimap and action ring retain their screen size.
- **Notch and safe areas:** `viewport-fit=cover`; HUD frames, buttons, messages, banners and windows sit inside the `--e-*` edges (`--edge` plus `env(safe-area-inset-*)`); the hold card is clamped with `safeInsets()` (input.js), read from `.hudlayer`'s padding.
- **No page zoom:** the HUD is `touch-action:none` (unit cards `pan-x`, selected unit `pan-y`); windows are `pan-x pan-y`, so they scroll but never zoom; pull-to-refresh is off; on iOS `gesturestart`/`gesturechange` are cancelled.
- **Line preview on phones:** on a coarse or small screen (`GHOST` in formations.js) the dragged line is drawn in screen pixels: a 3 px gold line, 5 px slot dots and an arrowhead over a dark edge. Desktop unchanged.
- **Install:** served over http(s), `manifest.webmanifest` (fullscreen, landscape, icons `icon-192.png`/`icon-512.png`) and the `apple-*` tags let you add Ashmark to a home screen; from `file://` the manifest isn't linked.
- Tests: `tests/touch.js`, `tests/mobile.js`. Still to check on a real phone: iOS callout and page-zoom suppression, Android `pointercancel` during a pinch, notch safe areas, rotation mid-gesture, and whether 450 ms and 10 px suit real thumbs.

## Withdrawal balance

W still orders selected groups off the field, without attacking on the way: through your edge, or a flank if the enemy cuts them off (see **Field sizes and ways off the field** below; with exits off, through your edge). Withdrawal now uses the existing fatigue rules: running spends stamina, tired/spent units have their normal speed penalties, and units below `FATIGUE.runMin` can only walk. Slowing effects still apply. The hero pays from the existing hero stamina pool.

On first contact with a live, non-routing melee threat during a withdrawal order, a unit spends `WITHDRAW.disengage` (0.8 combat seconds) disengaging at `WITHDRAW.backstep` (35%) of its current fatigue/slow-adjusted movement speed. This also applies near the exit. A shooter in Melee Mode counts as a melee threat; a ranged-only or routing foe does not. The bounded phase occurs once per withdrawal order, so a close pursuer cannot reset it forever. Repeating W does not reset it; canceling withdrawal and ordering it again requires disengagement again.

Disengaging units remain vulnerable and still do not strike. An uncontested exit remains immediate; no automatic casualties or additional escape roll were added. Existing battlefield losses, campaign escape odds, rearguard losses, morale/renown penalties, allied overlap rules and routing behavior are unchanged. `tests/withdrawal.js` covers these mechanics using isolated battle fixtures; RTS, retreat settlement and flee suites cover their connection to campaign rules.

## Field sizes and ways off the field

**Five sizes** (`FIELD_SIZES` in `battle/setup.js`; the chosen one is `BFIELD`, `B.fsize`): small 960×544, big 1280×720, large 1792×1008, very large 2400×1344, huge 3200×1792 (multiples of 32 across and 16 down, so route, fog and spatial cells fit). A battle's size comes from the soldiers in it, counted from the armies before anyone spawns: under 60 small, under 150 big, under 300 large, under 600 very large, else huge. The world tile then steps it one up on open plains (open ground or road, not a swamp) and one down in forest, a mountain pass or a swamp. A held bridge stays big to large and an ambush small to big; a tournament is small. No `Math.random` is drawn. The first line of the battle names the size. The lab's `size` key (`small`, `big`, `large`, `vlarge` or `'very large'`, `huge`) forces one, sizes on or off; a sized lab may set its armies up to the field's width less 400 px apart.
