**Room behind the lines:** each side deploys in a zone `back` px off its own edge, `depth` deep (`fieldZone(team)`; 32/96/256/512/832 px of open ground behind it, small to huge), and its lines form up over the field's middle band (`band`, `fieldBand`) rather than its whole height. Everything sizes itself from `BW`/`BH`: the camera (`camFit` fits the whole field, under 1 on a big one; the wheel adds 0.5 and 0.75 steps on such fields, zooming in is unchanged; the small map takes the field's shape), the route grids (a query's A* budget is a quarter of the cells on a field larger than `ROUTE.budget`'s), the fog and spatial grids, the ground and the blood layer. Big layers (ground, blood, the shooters' reach) are drawn only where the camera looks (`drawInView`), and soldiers, rocks and trees out of view are not drawn (`battleEnts`).

**Contract with the ground's generator:** `BattleSetup.start` sets `BW`, `BH` and `BFIELD` before `groundSpec`, spawning, `measureField` and `buildTerrain`; generation reads them as ever: `buildTerrain` takes the field's height from `BH` and its deployment zones from `BFIELD` (`terrain.js` `terrainZone`: `[back, back + depth]` off each edge) when the field it builds is the sized one; today's field keeps today's zones (the outer 30%). Out of a battle `BH` is today's 600 again (`fieldDone`), so the world's previews of a field read today's.

**Ways off the field** (`EXITS` in `battle/morale.js`, `B.exits`): every open stretch of all four edges is a way off: where a foot soldier may stand (deep water, rock and the field's closed rows are not), stretches under 48 px dropped. **Own side, flanks if cut off** (the owner's rule): a withdrawing or routing group (or a lone soldier) runs for its own side's edge (yours west, theirs east). It turns to a flank (top or bottom) or the far edge only when no point of home can be reached (water, rock, cliffs) or every one is cut off: an enemy group of at least a quarter of its number nearer that point than it is, or standing within 120 px across the straight way there (`exitCut`, `cutBy`, `across`); one chasing from behind cuts nothing off. Among the points still allowed it takes the one, every 48 px, that costs least: the length of its way there (`routeDist` round cliffs, rock and water; straight where nothing blocks; none it cannot reach), dearer toward the enemy and cheaper away from them (`away`), plus its danger, the enemy its straight line passes within 200 px of (`danger`, `weight`), plus the chase, any enemy group with less than a 400 px lead on that point (`lead`, `chase`), each by its strength against the group's own. It is chosen when the group starts to run, looked at again every 2 s and kept unless another costs under 80% of it. Each man makes for the nearest point of that stretch within 120 px of the chosen one. Your withdraw order follows the same rule at once (the order line points there); so do the enemy's captains. In AI-vs-AI lab battles at every size (fog on and off) about 97% of those who break run for their own edge; the rest were cut off. The field shows a faint strip along every usable stretch, blue where your men head (with **Withdraw**), faintly red where theirs run. Those who get away are counted as before, whichever edge they took (`u.left`, `B.withdrawn`, `B.fledFoes`).

**Switches:** `FIELD_SIZES.on` and `EXITS.on` (both on in the game); `tests/harness.js` turns them off so the older suites keep today's field and the two edges. Tests: `tests/fieldsize.js` (choice, lab key, zones, camera and small map, grids, painting a huge field, exits and blocked edges, a rout and a withdrawal, the escaped counted, an AI battle at each size to the end, timings at 400 and 800 soldiers).

**Cost** (headless, no GPU): simulation 2.7–3.3 ms/frame at 400 soldiers and 7–9 ms at 800 at every size; drawing the whole field 9–10 ms at 400 and 17–23 ms at 800 (zoomed in, less). A huge field paints its ground in about 0.35 s (arena) + 0.15–0.25 s (terrain); each full-field canvas is 22 MB there (arena, ground, blood; the shooters' reach when shown).

## Fights inside (2026-11-06)

**What** (the owner: "small fights in caves", hideouts "inside with more camp detail", up to 8 go in; `battle/interior.js`, doc/INTERIORS_DESIGN.md): a cave's beasts, a ruin's guardians and a hideout's bandits are fought inside, on the `cave` field (1280×720), not on the field outside. `startBattle` hands such an encounter (`Interior.on`: kind `cave`, or `camp` at a `Hideout`) to `InteriorSetup`, a `BattleSetup` on its own ground.

**The ground:** `InteriorLayout` (seeded by the place: the same each visit) lays chambers joined by passages (one loop at times) and a mouth from the west edge; a cave's ragged, a ruin's cellars square-cut (`cellar`), a hideout a cave with its camp (`hideout`; in ruins, `den`). Its rock is the battle terrain's rock (`plan.interior` in `BattleTerrain.raster`), so routes, steering, sight, missiles and the ways off the field obey it; the mouth is the one way out. Every chamber is reached from the mouth on the cells (`joined`, else the next seed). Painted once by `InteriorArt` (stone floor, wall faces, stalagmites, bones; a cellar's flags and crates; the deepest's chest; daylight at the mouth), as the tournament arena paints its own ground.

**Going in:** the cave's Enter and a hideout's Attack open the page (`Interior.show`: a row a companion or group, − and +, at most `INTERIOR.men`; Back, Enter, Esc). Only those chosen fight (`createUnits` keeps them); the rest wait outside and cannot fall, auto-resolved too (`Interior.losses`). No one rides (`Dismount.down`). Scouts seen, or guardians woken, take the best of your band (`Interior.pick`).

**The dark:** the fog of war is always on inside, each man seeing `INTERIOR.sight` of his range (`B.sightK`), the unseen darker (`B.dark`), drawn as plain dark, a wall seen with the floor before it. The foes wait in their chambers until roused (`insideRoused`: a foe they see within `notice` px, or one of theirs hurt; until then their captain gives them no orders), the strongest (a chieftain) in the deepest.

**Two kinds of hideout** (the owner, 2026-11-06): a bandit hideout (tents in a ring of stakes) is fought on the map as a camp, as before; a bandit lair (`Hideout#tunnel`, `lairLook`: `HIDEOUT_LAIR.share` of them by a hash of the tile, a cave's mouth by hills or peaks, else a dug tunnel; `gfx/structures.js lairSprite`; its own name, set as it is made) is fought inside. **A lair's camp:** its bandits in squads of `INTERIOR.squad` through the chambers; a stake palisade across the way in, a gap to pass (`palAt`, solid on the cells); tents, bedrolls and a fire a chamber, each fire's light showing whoever stands in it to both sides (`B.lights`, `battlefog.js fogGrid`); the chieftain's tent and the plunder in the deepest.

**Switch:** `INTERIOR.on` (on in the game; `tests/harness.js` turns it off, so the older suites keep the field). Tests: `tests/interiors.js`.

**Siege walls** (the owner, 2026-11-06: "real siege walls, now"; `battle/siegeworks.js`): the assault on a walled holding (`SiegeWorks.on`: kind `siege`, `wallOf` > 0) goes to `SiegeSetup`. Its wall (`SiegeWorks`, laid on the cells by `BattleTerrain.raster` `plan.siege`): a wall-walk `SIEGE_WALL.lv` levels up (palisade 1, stone and great 2), its outer face a cliff but at the ladders, its inner one but at the stairs; towers and the shut gate rock. The ram (built by `RAM_DAYS`): your foot at the gate take `SIEGE_WALL.ram` a second each (`SiegeWorks#step`, from `battleStep`); broken (`breach`), the gate's cells open, the ways and sight made again, every defender roused. The defenders' shooters on the wall-walk, foot behind the gate, the rest in the yard, holding till roused (`B.holds`, interior.js `insideRoused`); everyone on foot. Painted by `SiegeArt` over the climate's ground. Villages and raids keep the open field. `SIEGE_WALL.on` (off in the harness). Tests: `tests/siegewalls.js`.

**Siege engines and collapsing walls** (the owner, 2026-11-10: "wall collapse from siege weapons"; `battle/siegeworks.js` `SIEGE_ENGINE`, `SiegeWorks#sections`, `placeEngines`, `engineStep`, `mark`, `aimAt`, `loose`, `land`, `collapse`, `SiegeArt#rubble`, `trebuchet`; `world/siege.js` `siegeEngines`): your siege camp builds a trebuchet after 6 days, one more every 3 (3 at most). In the assault they stand 330 px back from the wall and each looses a stone every 8-11 s at the weakest standing section of it (the wall in stretches of 5 rows between the gate and its towers, each tower a section too), or the one you click (`ui/input.js`: a click on the wall with no man under it); a stone hits by .7, takes 85-125 of the section's strength (220, 380, 520 by the walls; a tower 1.6 times) and hurts the men within 22 px. A section at nothing comes down: a rubble slope one level high, climbed from either side and sheer to the walk still standing beside it, its men falling, the field's ways and sight made again and every defender roused; a tower's standing picture goes with it. Its cracks show on the wall as it weakens. Behind stone or great walls the defenders' mangonel on the tower nearest the gate shoots at your trebuchets (200 strong each) and is lost with its tower. On the battlements: your captain sends his foot at the breaches first (each worth two ladders), then the ladders, and leaves the ram once the wall is breached (`commander.js` `siege`, whose orders had thrown on an undefined `d2` and so were never given); a man on the wall-walk shot from below takes half the harm (`SIEGE_WALL.cover`, `SiegeWorks#cover`, `projectiles.js`); a way in made, the gate down or a section fallen, the defenders' foot not yet fighting go to hold it from inside (`holdGap`, `SIEGE_WALL.hold`), their lords and the rest roused. Tests: siegewalls.

**The town behind the wall** (the owner, 2026-11-10: "go with your suggestions"; `battle/siegeworks.js` `SIEGE_TOWN`, `SiegeWorks#townPlan`, `plan`, `SiegeArt#house`): the besieged town is built inside its wall. Its centre building (citadel, keep, hall or fort by the holding) stands on the gate's street .6 of the way in, its own kinds of houses (house, smithy, tavern, chapel, granary, warehouse) around it on a jittered 74 px grid, about half the plots filled, in its builders' style (`PX.buildingModel`, drawn at 2x as standing props); their footprints block the way and a 46 px street is kept open from the gate to the centre. Tests: siegewalls.

**The fight at the wall, and its look** (the owner, 2026-11-10: "improve the walls and ladders and siege details"; `battle/siegeworks.js` `SIEGE_FIGHT`, `SiegeWorks#ladderStep`, `throwDown`, `raiseUp`, `pitchStep`, `placeMantlets`, `cover`; `SIEGE_ART`, `SiegeArt#stone`, `tower`, `clutter`, `spent`, `mantlet`, `ladPic`, `ladders`): a defender on the walk at a ladder's top with none of yours up there and one of yours on it or at its foot throws it down (odds .3 each 1.2 s), your men on it falling; its face sheer till your foot stand at its foot for 5 s and raise it again (your captain's men waiting there do). While the ram works the defenders pour pitch on its crew (a palisade's, stones) every 8-11 s. Your camp sets out 3-5 wooden mantlets 60 px before the wall, clear of the gate and the ladders: a man of yours behind one takes half the harm of a shot from the wall. Drawn: the stone wall's battered foot in courses of big blocks grimed at its base by the climate (moss, snow, sand) with scree before it, the parapet's shadow on the walk, loops in every other merlon, the walk's heaps (stones to drop, arrows, barrels, braziers glowing), stairs stepping down into the yard, the gate's sills; the towers' corbels, crosslets and plinth; a palisade's rope lashings; ladders of thick rails and lashed rungs hooked over the parapet, or lying askew in the field; spent arrows stuck in the ground before the wall; the stones' dust (its colour had drawn nothing). Tests: siegewalls.

**The wall's height, its bricks, the castle and its standard** (the owner, 2026-11-10: "the ladder should be realistic angle; add bricks, wall details; the inside should be like castles (bigger structure) and have a flag to capture"; `battle/siegeworks.js` `SIEGE_ART.wallZ`, `SiegeWorks#zAt`, `liftStep`, `flagStep`, `takeFlag`, `SiegeArt#leanPic`, `face`, `bailey`, `court`, `flagProp`, `raiseFlag`; `SIEGE_FIGHT.flag`; `SIEGE_TOWN.kc`, `towers`, `road`, `earth`; `commander.js` `siege`; `gfx/render.js` `drawUnitAt`; `commands.js` `rtsUnitAt`): the men on the wall-walk are drawn up its height (15, 22, 26 px by the walls: `u.z`, drawn only, `u.lifted` putting their shadows and rings at their feet), a man up a ladder by how far up it he is, down the stairs by how far down; the ladders lean from their feet in the field up to it, their shadows flat on the ground; a click picks a man where he is drawn. The stone wall's outer face is staggered courses of bricks over a base of big blocks, each brick its own tone, now and then one gone; hangings of the lord's colours, torches burning in their sconces, arrow loops and spouts along it; the towers' faces bricks too. Behind it a castle's bailey of trodden earth (by climate) with patches of grass, its keep three times its model with two towers by it, a cobbled road from the gate to the court and on to the keep; in the flagstoned court (braziers at its corners, kept clear of houses) the lord's standard (`outpostSprite`, twice its size) where the lords stand. Yours alone within 44 px of it for 8 s (lost again at half that while theirs alone stand there; an arc of your colour round it shows the taking) take it: every defender breaks and your banner goes up on it. Your captain sends any group inside the wall with no one to fight to the standard, and never back out to a ladder. Tests: siegewalls.

## Developer console and battle laboratory

Click **DEV** (top right), press **F2**, or press the physical backquote key to open developer tools. Escape closes the window; simulation stops while it is open. Up/Down recalls command history. Tools work from the title screen, world map and battle; teleporting requires the unobstructed world map.

Campaign cheats affect live state and can persist in normal saves. Keep a separate save if you want to undo them. These are explicit commands, not arbitrary JavaScript.

| Command | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `money 1000` / `money -100` | Add/subtract gold without allowing a negative balance. |
| `where` | Show world-pixel and tile coordinates. |
| `tp 1200 800` / `tp tile 50 60` | Teleport to walkable map coordinates; cancel previous travel/chase. |
| `goto Ashford` | Teleport near a holding; names containing spaces need no quoting. |
| `get player` / `get player.attr` | Inspect campaign state and discover fields. |
| `set player.attr.agi 12` | Edit an existing scalar field. Values use JSON syntax, including quotes for strings. |
| `get game.day` / `set game.day 30` | Inspect state; game writes are limited to day, clock, speed and paused. |
| `units bow` | List matching unit definition IDs. |
| `roster` | List current battle unit IDs, sides, health and positions. |
| `get unit.1` / `set unit.1.s.spd 80` | Inspect a live fighter or override its stats; use IDs from `roster`. |
| `battle hum_bow 5 hum_foot 5` | Start a paused isolated matchup; `battle` alone opens the selection form. |
| `pause`, `step 60`, `restart`, `exit` | Control a battle test. Step advances 1–600 frames at 1/60 second per frame and pauses afterward. |
| `speed 3` or `speed(3)` | Your band's map speed × 3 (`CHEAT.speed`, `bandPace`); `speed 1` resets. Testing only: never saved. |
| `noNeeds true` / `noNeeds(false)` | No hunger, thirst or stamina drain, nor their tolls on your hero (`CHEAT.noNeeds`, `supplyHour`, `supplyStage`). Testing only: never saved. |
| `enc` / `enc child` or `enc('lostChild')` | List the road's encounter kinds; spawn one (by id or name) on its own ground just past your sight, whatever its conditions. From the world map. |
| `quest` / `quest bounty` | List the quest kinds; offer one at the holding nearest you (a lord's kinds by a lord of its realm) and take it. |
| `chains` (or `quest chains`) / `chain 1` / `chain 1 go` | List the quest chains (chain quests) under way: their step and what each waits for; one's state: its step, what the step began, the event it waits for, its memory, its past; take you to where its step points. |
| `ander` (or `ander()`, `enc wounded ander`) | Lay Ander The Fallen, wounded, by the road near you (his chain forced; refused once he has been met this game). From the world map. |
| `passengers` / `escort` or `escort 3` | List those you escort (quest number, who, where, due day, food); take you to an escort's place (the first, or by quest number): it arrives once the world runs. |
| `help`, `clear` | Show command examples or clear console output. |

Player edits do not rebuild an already-spawned hero's battlefield stats; edit `unit.<id>.s` to change live stats. Writes cannot replace objects, create arbitrary fields, traverse prototypes, change unit identity/group links, or edit linked shared Unit/campaign objects. Stat edits copy the live stats first so they do not alter the shared unit definitions. Extreme but finite developer values can still invalidate gameplay assumptions; these are debugging tools, not validated progression commands.

The **Battle test** tab builds two armies, each with **1–10 independent groups**, including optional characters. Duplicate troop types stay separate. Add/remove/reorder rows, set counts, names, formations and files (0 = auto). Each troop group permits 1–100 fighters, with a performance limit of **200 fighters per army**. Wedge requires at least six cavalry; formations must fit their field/starting zone. The builder rejects unsuitable formations instead of silently changing them.

Your current hero may occupy one left-side group. Companion choices are independent level-1 templates on either side, with one copy of each companion per army; they do not use or injure your campaign recruits. Characters require count 1. Troop-only tests have no extra fighting hero and do not inherit campaign troop talent/perk bonuses. Both sides start from their own unit kind's base morale. Hero tests use your current build/equipment, including its normal effects on the left army.

Choose terrain, climate, seed, player-side AI, initial pause and morale/routing. **Deploy left army before battle** freezes the field and uses the normal right-click/right-drag placement controls, independently of the campaign deployment preference. The right army deploys automatically in its chosen formations. **Begin** ends deployment; if Start paused is checked, the battle remains paused for stepping. Separation applies when deployment is skipped. The existing `battle hum_bow 5 hum_foot 5` command remains a quick one-group matchup without deployment.

Built-in presets include combined arms, duplicate formations, mounted archers vs footmen, and footmen vs berserkers. Save/load up to 20 named user presets in the separate `ashmark.lab.presets.v1` browser-storage key. Save / replace overwrites only the named user preset; built-ins are read-only. Delete affects only the selected user preset. **Use last test** copies the last successful configuration into the draft. Editing the draft or live stats does not change the configuration used by **Restart**.

The persistent test toolbar offers Play/Pause, Step, Restart, Exit, Tools and **Report**. The report compares each configured group on both sides: surviving, remaining-on-field and dead counts, kills, effective damage dealt/taken, morale samples (recent six shown, older samples in JSON), and route/withdrawal/field-exit timestamps. Routed exits and ordered withdrawals have separate counts and timestamps. Effective damage is post-mitigation HP removed, capped to the target's HP remaining; dodges and overkill do not count. Damage without a configured source is explicitly uncredited. The downloadable JSON includes the normalized configuration and seed, bounded event/morale histories with truncation counts, and a plain final unit snapshot. Survivor counts include living units that exited; kills count lethal damage only. Normal selection, move, attack, facing, speed and formation controls remain available. Escape and the HUD menu open the test tools. Starting a test is allowed from the title screen or unobstructed world map, never over a campaign battle or unresolved choice.

The laboratory swaps `P`/`G` contents for private copies while preserving their record identities, and restores the original nested records, screen and random function on exit. World simulation is not advanced. Saving/loading is blocked during a test; completion freezes the field instead of running campaign settlement, so test rewards, casualties, hero training, ammo and gold edits are discarded. Restart restores the campaign then recreates the same configuration. Seed initializes the existing random stream; exact simulation reproducibility still depends on matching frame/input/render activity.

Implementation: `js/ui/developer.js`, `js/battle/lab.js`; the normal setup and simulation remain in use. `tests/developer.js` exercises commands, real form/keyboard/toolbar events, private stat edits, natural battle completion, restart and restoration, and save/load isolation. `tests/armylab.js` covers independent armies, character templates, validation, deployment, maximum-group setups, restart, presets and actual builder controls. `tests/labdiagnostics.js` covers isolated damage attribution, group metrics, route/withdraw events, morale samples, bounded history and JSON serialization. `tests/visual/developer.js` prepares a desktop/narrow preview (`&tab=console` selects the console); `tests/visual/armylab.js` previews a mixed-army preset and its deployed field.

## Running tests

Each suite in `tests/` runs headless in Chrome or Edge by opening `Ashmark.html?test=<suite>`. `tests/harness.js` loads `tests/kit.js` first, the shared helpers (`Kit.suite`, `Kit.begin`, `Kit.world`, `Kit.done`, `Kit.goHome`, `Kit.travel`, `Kit.frames`, `Kit.stub`/`rolls`/`seeded`, `Kit.stubStream(name, v, fn)` (a named stream's draws fixed, as `Kit.stub` fixes Math.random's), `Kit.meet`, `Kit.engage` (a battle begun as Start Battle begins it, a world battle's armies formed up first), `Kit.watchMarch` (marks, or hides, the marching armies while a frame is drawn: drawn smaller on purpose), `Kit.panel`/`text`, `Kit.q`/`qa`, `Kit.key`, `Kit.storyKinds`: the test quest chain and gathering kinds, registered in the test page only); it is not a suite, and the sweep leaves it out. Suites on it so far: `relations`, `reputation`, `leaders`, `quests`, `monsters`, `coreparts`, `story`, `kinds`, `quest chains`, `streams` (S4: a test-only encounter kind and a changed weight leave the world, the shared Math.random and a battle as they were; the streams saved, loaded and started afresh; the estates' sort keys; the house order's line). Windows (PowerShell):

**World battles in the tests** (2026-10-30): the harness runs every suite with world battles on, as the game does (`WORLD_BATTLE.on`): an encounter met on the map is fought in the map's own circle, and the map shows the marching armies. A suite of the battle screen's own rules (its field, its controls and HUD, the fingerprint suites; the screen caves, sieges, tournaments and the lab still fight on) opts out with `window.ARENA=true` as its first line. `tests/battlescreen.js` checks the battle screen itself with world battles on: a cave, a tournament and the lab each open on their own field, and a band met on the map in its circle.

```
.\tests\run-tests.ps1                                  # every suite (about 4 min)
.\tests\run-tests.ps1 -Parallel 4                      # every suite, 4 browsers at once (about 1.5 min)
.\tests\run-tests.ps1 -Suites rosters,parties,garrison # selected suites
.\tests\run-tests.ps1 -Suites siege -Seed (1..8)       # reproducible random scenarios
```

Every run is on the generated world, the only one. `-Gen`, the old switch for it, adds nothing; it is kept so old commands still
work (`-Classic` and `?gen=0`, today's map's, are gone with it: D49).

A suite with a line `window.PHONE=true` (mobilehud, mobilebattlehud) runs in a phone's window, 844 x 390 with touch (the runner's
`Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride`), so the page's CSS and the mobile HUDs see a phone (2026-11-01).

From bash: `bash tests/run-tests.sh [-j N] [-s SEED] [suite ...]`. On Windows it hands over to the PowerShell runner; elsewhere (or with `ASHMARK_DUMP_DOM=1`) it runs a `--dump-dom` browser per suite.

After each suite the runner prints `# N pass, N fail, N.N s`, and at the end `# summary: ...` and `# failing: ...`; it exits non-zero if any line starts with `FAIL`, `EXC` or `ERR` (every line is counted, not just a suite's first). Each worker reuses a locked profile slot under `%TEMP%shmark-tests` (a new Chrome profile costs 10-20 s before its first page) and clears its stored data first; it never attaches to a user browser. `-Browser` picks another Chrome/Edge, `-TimeoutSeconds` the per-suite deadline. Seeded runs reset the random stream just before `RUN()`; a page can also be opened as `?test=siege&seed=1`.

`tests/visual/` holds pages run by name, not in the default sweep: screenshot scenes (`visual/shot`, `visual/<feature>`) and reports that never fail: `visual/perf` (hot-path timings), `visual/smoothperf` (the map's frame as shown), `visual/sightperf` (the map's frame and its fog, alone and with a hundred men: fog.js Sight), `visual/chunksim` (the world's frame at Large and Huge, tiers on and off; the minimap's fog and dots), `visual/worldsim` (five 120-day campaigns), `visual/monsterbalance`, `visual/dragonbalance`, `visual/matchupbalance` (BAL lines), `visual/moraleaudit` and `visual/confidenceaudit` (morale diagnostics), `visual/powerbalance` (commander powers, AI on AI). `mapview` also draws the whole map over the page, so `Ashmark.html?test=mapview` is a quick way to look at it.

Every suite runs on the generated classic world (doc/WORLD_DESIGN.md §15.2); the suites read places, never fixed coordinates (a battle fingerprint's fixture stands on a fixed point and ground: the old map's, the road by Ashford's gate). The outcomes and the world battle that read the world round them keep one set of constants (`battleresolution` `REC`, `aishare` `REC`), and the world fingerprints one table: `worldprint` checks W1–W8 and B2 against `EXPECT` (the generated world's, called G1–G8 while the old map kept W1–W8; its own table went with it, D49), and `loading` compares its new game in steps with the same table. `rts` and `groundhash` read no world (their fields are their own). `tests/worldsanity.js` holds §8.14's Phase 2 rules on 8 Normal and 2 Large seeds, each world made as a new game makes it: every holding and camp reachable from every capital, also with the seasonal crossings shut; each capital's liking and land; capitals and realms balanced; a fair start for each race; ports on water joined to the sea or a port; rivers never uphill, ending in water, no great river freezing, crossings at most 60 tiles apart; each climate 6% of the land (no emptiness, wild land, the lair and the shape metrics, later phases', as INFO). Reports for the generated world: `visual/worldsanity` (the same over 30 and 10 seeds), `visual/worldscale` (per preset: generation by stage and the typed arrays it makes, putting in, a chunk painted, the router, `updateWorld`, the day's jobs, the save plain and gzipped), `visual/loadtimes` (each wait, per size) and `visual/btypebalance` (Hold the bridge's share of the world's fights).

## Art

Everything is drawn by code (`js/gfx/sprites.js`, `js/gfx/worldart.js`); nothing is loaded from `graphics/`. Buildings on the world map come from the PX engine in `../engine` (see its README and `gallery.html`), and so do its woods, hills, mountain ranges and small things (`worldnature.js`, `gallery.html?view=nature`). Every holding is a set of placed buildings, each on its own tiles of the grid (`js/world/lots.js`): its centre (keep, citadel or hall, whose walls are the holding's fortification: Fortify upgrades it), houses for its people, the buildings of its estates, and fields, pastures and pits flat on the tiles they keep.

**Towns of models (the building redesign's stage 3, 2026-10-23).** Every building, site and camp on the world map is a model of `engine/buildmodels.js` at the owner's scale: 16 px objects, 32 px the smallest building, 64 the big ones, the clusters and the centres of villages, towns and castles, 128 a city's citadel. A lot is its model's cell (`lots.js` `lotSize`, `centreN`). The culture chooses the architecture: the realm a holding was founded under (`HOME`, which a capture leaves as it was: `FAC_CULTURE` vale, karn, sylvan, the Horde's ashgor, the Salt League's league; `holdingCulture`); its owner flies the banners (banners and accents in the owner's colour are not in the models yet). Its centre is `PX.centreModel` by tier, with its walls (`wallOf`); its houses are its culture's (`HOUSES`, a village's and a castle's, a town's and a city's), each holding 2-4 households of `HOUSING.household` (10) people (`lotHouseholds`: the model's homes), so the housing counts as before with fewer, bigger houses; its estates' buildings are types or clusters (`ESTATE_LOT`: a farmstead, else a farmhouse; a market's and a brewery's yards of 64). Each lot faces its street (`lotYaw`, saved: the nearest road or track, S 0, W 2, N 4, E 6; an outer estate turns to the track laid to it; the centre straight on). Its land: its climate's make (desert, swamp, frost with its snow), else its holding's landscape (`holdingEnv`: forest, highland or coast, the Vale's variants); the frost's cover brings snow by thirds toward it (`lotSnow`, the seasons' hook). The yards, the models' ragged ground layers, are painted into the land under them (`worldart.js` `townGround`, `TOWN_GROUND`: the land shows through by material, so a yard takes the biome and the climates' covers and fades into it; never a square patch), every shadow joins the one shadow pass at `PX.MODEL_SHADE`, the standing pictures by their front edge among the figures. A lot placed, taken away or changed has its chunks painted again (`WorldArt.relot`). The models near you are made a few ms a frame ahead (`lotsAhead`). The layout at the new scale: the inner estates by the centre first, then the houses round them (side by side with each other and the workshops in its streets, `gap:'row'`), ringed round the centre's middle (`centreMid`); the outer works measured from the gate (`o.gate`), their fields within the workers' reach of the door (`FIELD.reach`); a port's pier from any tile of its building beside the water (`pierSpot`); mines and quarries may stand on a hill's wooded flank; no track starts beside or behind a centre (the art would lead it round its corners); a village clears 110 tiles of woods (`PLACE_CLEAR`, was 80). Sites and camps are their models too (`structures.js` `siteSprite`, `campSprite`: a cave and ruins 32 px, a shrine 16, a bandits' camp and travellers 32, a hideout 64; their yards drawn under them, the fire at `marks.fire`, the flag at `flag`). Tests: `tests/townmodels.js`; pictures: `tests/visual/townshots.js` (a "SHOT name data URL" line each). Realms grow their holdings over time; in your fiefs you place what you build, and it goes up behind scaffolding. Flags are their own animated sprite.

The world has its own water and roads (`worldart.js`, doc/WORLD_DESIGN.md §12.1): water with curved shores, shaded by width, banks with reeds and stones, fords with stepping stones, stone and wooden bridges, ferries, rapids, brooks along the flow (painted by `worldnature.js`), sand rims and shallows on the coasts, and roads painted along their smoothed lines at any angle. `tests/visual/rivers.js` shows every piece for approval (a page of crops: screenshot it at 1400 x 2200).

After the owner's play-test (2026-10-22): every road's end at a holding is led round its centre (`GENART.yard`) and up into its gate, over worn ground (`gateLead`); a crossing is met straight on its axis and a bridge's deck by a ramp of road as wide as it (`straightOn`, `offDeck`); a fork's end is carried onto the way it joins (`joinEnds`); a ford is a band of rippled shallows fading from the river's colour, rounded into gravel on each bank, its stepping stones scattered along it (`fordAt`). On both maps the frost's snow is laid by its cover a tile (`snowCover`, `FROST`: how deep in the frost, how cold below the frost line on the generated world, how high), rising over the frost's edge and thinning out in patches over the grass beside it (`landRGB`), and the swamp's open ground stands with sparse low mangroves (`engine/worldnature.js` `FEN`). `tests/visual/worldart.js` shows each of these in the game's own view for approval (`&part=1`: screenshot it at 1200 x 2340).

After the second play-test (2026-10-22): the generated world's shores are curves (`wpx`: the water tiles read as a smooth field, a quadratic B-spline's or bilinear, and cut at a half, so lakes are rounded, rivers bend and a staircase of tiles reads as a slope; never more than some 6 px into a tile of the other kind, passing stays by tile; the land's nature keeps clear of it by `spec.wet` and `round`); whatever is coloured by climate on and by the water (its tones, shallows, sand, mud, foam and fords) shades across a climate's edge by the climates' share round each tile corner (`ClimMix`): no more blocks of the frost's water in the grass's (the noise by Thornbrook); a river's tones run into a lake's (`RG`), no square seam; a ford's shallows and banks by climate (`GENART.ford`: sand and gravel, the frost's slush and ice, the desert's sand, the swamp's mud). The estates' worked ground lies on its land (`structures.js` `estateGround`, `PEN`, `FURROW`): a pasture is the land's own ground fenced, grazed and trodden in patches in its climate's colours (trampled snow with the grass showing through, dry tufts and trodden sand, mud and puddles), a field's soil, crops and furrows are its climate's (snow in the frost's furrows, water now and then in the swamp's), its edge ragged into the ground round it, a quarry's spoil and rim snowy in the frost. The trees come by climate from a table (`engine/worldnature.js` `PX.WORLD_TREES`: broadleaf and some pines, the odd bare or broken tree; snowy pines, firs and bare birches; bog willows, mangroves and snags; palms, acacias and a dead tree now and then: 5-20% leafless or dead, more at a wood's edge), and the swamp's forest is a bog: fewer, low bog trees hung with moss over peat pools. `tests/visual/worldart.js` (the default, part 2: 1200 x 4660) shows each piece; `&part=3` each climate's trees (950 x 2100).

After the owner's play-test 2 (2026-10-22): the generator brings every road to a holding at its gate, from in front (`worldplace.js` `placeBox`: the centre and the ring beside and behind it shut to the road search), so no road is led round a centre over the water beside it; no building or field stands where a road is painted (`lots.js` `roadPaint`); caravans keep to the roads (`Router.travel`'s `road`); the camps and scenes on the road (the gamblers, the wounded traveller) stand beside it (`ENC_ON.byroad`).

After the third play-test (2026-10-22): the climates' edges blend as the frost's (`worldart.js` `coverOf`, `COVER`, `landRGB`): each climate's cover a tile (its tiles counted 3 tiles each way, its own tile's .55 or more), the grass under it darker and wetter by the swamp, yellowing by the desert, and the swamp's and the desert's ground laid over it in patches that thin out over some 3 tiles, a mossy or dusty rim on each; the 3-px jittered blocks between climates are gone (the water still shades by `ClimMix`). Their small things mix across the edge too (`engine/worldnature.js` `PX.worldNatureMix`, `PX.WN_MIX`: pools, reeds, moss and mangroves on the grass by the swamp, ripples, cacti and scrub by the desert, fewer farther out, the grass's tufts on their edge), and a wood has a fringe (`PX.WN_FRINGE`: its floor's shade reaching a few px out, ragged, and undergrowth and trees thinning out over two tiles round it; acacias, not palms, by the desert's woods). A ford is a real one (`GENART.ford`, `fordAt`, `fordTrack`): its shallows a bar that wanders off the line, the water lightened toward its bed in two dithered steps, pebbles where shallowest, short ripples across the flow, the frost's ice plates at its edges; a gravel (sand, mud, slush) bank where it meets the land, and a worn track down each bank to the water, kept clear of nature; a few rocks scattered over it, never in a row (capped with snow in the frost, mossy in the swamp, foam on their downstream side), reeds at a swamp ford's edges. The roads meet play-test 2's: a fork's end is carried up to 2 tiles onto the way it joins (`GENART.join`), a road turning back off a bridge runs straight past the ramp first, and a road leaving a ford sideways starts in its shallows (`offDeck`), not across the river from the far bank; `lots.js` reads the painted roads by `artRoadMarks`. `tests/visual/worldart.js` `&part=4` (1200 x 1960) shows the fords in each climate, the grass meeting the swamp and the desert, and a wood's edge.

After the owner's play-test 3 (2026-10-22): no estate across a river with no way over. The generator bridges each holding's water near it (`worldplace.js` `placeHoldXing`: where water lies within 8 tiles and land beside it within 10 is not walked to from the gate, or only far round, a bridge of 3 tiles at most, else a ferry on a great or navigable river) and joins every bridge to the roads (a track from each end no road takes, straight on from it, to the nearest road on its bank; a bridge no road reaches taken up); every house, estate and field stands on ground walked to from its holding's gate within 28 tiles, not far round, its own bank first (`lots.js` `lotWalk`, over `placeWalk`; building later and placing by hand too). The owner's KPI, every estate's workers home and back on time: `tests/workerways.js`; the bridges on the roads: `tests/bridgeroads.js`.

After the fourth play-test (2026-10-22): what read as swamp on the grass was a hill's shade (dithered, with its cast shadow), so the grass's hills shade lighter (`PX.WORLD_NATURE.grass.hill` `lo`, `sh`). A hill runs on under a road or a yard (kept-clear ground that is no water neither stops a rise nor its shading, but takes no tufts or rock); its palette and a wood's floor are the climates' mixed by their covers a pixel (`PX.WN_BLEND`: the frost's by the snow's), not by tile. The trees across a climate's edge are an ecotone (`PX.worldTreeClimate`, `PX.WN_ECO`; `worldart.js` `ecoLean`: the generated world's temperature, moisture and height leaning toward each climate as the generator scores them): mixed stands over some 3 tiles each way, the desert's acacias out of it, trees in the snow frosted (`freeze`), out of it snowless (`thaw`). A road's colour blends across a climate's edge by the same covers (`roadPal`, `GENART.rblend`). A brook crosses a road (`PX.WN_XING`; `artGenRoads` hands its bands to the brooks): a splash ford over a track (wet ruts, stones), a stone culvert over a great road; one running along a road stays off it. A bridge's foot: a stone abutment (wood: a sill) as wide as the deck, on land only (`artGenXing` `abut`); the shore's sand or mud by the nearest water that is no bridge's (`wnear`); worn ground (`trample`), bank stones, reeds and trees never on the water as drawn. A track from a bridge's end (`worldplace.js`) keeps its end there (`joinEnds`) and, turning back, runs `GENART.back` tiles out first, clear of the ramp. `tests/visual/worldart.js` `&part=5`.

After the fourth play-test, the generator (2026-10-22): climates are smoothed and tidied into big pieces (`worldgen.js` `genClimTidy`, `GEN_CLIMATE.blur`, `strip`, `minArea`), every brook ends in water (`GEN_RIVER.brook`, checked by `genRiverCheck`), and holdings spread over the land (`worldplace.js` `PLACE.capMin`, `space`, `room`, `pull`).

After the fifth play-test (2026-10-22, agent T; `tests/visual/worldart.js` `&part=6`): a road or a ford's track through a wood is a forest track, not a tile of bright grass: a road's tile with 4 or more of its 8 neighbours wood is a lane (`worldart.js` `P.lane`, `GENART.wood`: its path narrower and wandering, `laneW`, `segBand`), the land's nature makes it a wood's tile (`PX.WN_LANE`): the forest floor up to the path's edge over the kept-clear band, ragged, trees and undergrowth beside the path and some crowns over its edge, their shadows on it, by the ways' band read anywhere (`roadAt`: the roads' and a ford's track's, `fordBand`). A ford's shallows are a ragged bar bending as an S across the river (`GENART.ford` `bend2`, `rag`), not a box. A road over the hills rises with them (`PX.WN_HILL.road`) and a rise's cast shadow is soft (`PX.WN_HILL.soft`): no straight cut along a road or the light. Mountains come in kinds (`PX.WORLD_MOUNTS`, `PX.worldMountKind`, `PX.WN_MOUNT`: desert sandstone buttes with scree fans, pale cracked granite, rare dark basalt with columns, snow-capped alpine peaks, low forested mountains on wet grass, the swamp's mossy knolls), by climate and the generator's elev, temp, moist and rough, a range one kind's shape, its look blended across a climate's edge; peaks vary in slope, ridges and stretch. The generator's oases and ponds (`WM.oases`, `WM.ponds`, `ringOf`) get rings (`PX.WN_RING`): an oasis's date palms, tall grass, shrubs and, by a holding, gardens, its ground greening into the sand; a hollow's, an oxbow's or a spring's reeds, bushes and flowers; a tarn's stones and scree, frozen in the frost; a swamp pool's reeds, moss and pads; their water a soft blob (`GENART.pond`), kept a pixel at a time, not by tile (no square round it), a pond's bank wet earth (a tarn's gravel). Brooks are no longer cut short by the art: over clear ground, into and out of water as drawn, beside a road they run along. The fog of war's edges are a cool, pale haze before the dark (`WFOG.haze`, `ringHaze`; `mist.js` a level's `haze`), not a darkening of the ground.

After the fourth and fifth play-tests, the layout (2026-10-22): a track runs through the woods to each outer estate no way comes near (`lots.js` `estTrack`, `LOT_TRACK`: laid as a holding is laid out, taken up when the world goes in again and laid again from a save's lots; the way graph charted again with them, `router.js` `wayRechart`, and the land reckoned over them), and the workers keep to the roads and tracks where they go (`WAY.folk`). A holding in the woods gets a ragged clearing for its houses and fields (`worldplace.js` `placeClear`, `PLACE_CLEAR`, as stage 8 begins). An outer estate with no room within 12 tiles looks out to 18, its door at most 22 steps from the gate (`LOT_FAR`: Redspire's lumber camp). Big oases draw desert peoples (`PLACE_OASIS`); ponds and oases are never bridged. Stage 7 regrows its realms from their capitals while too few fit or their land is unbalanced (`PLACE.regrow`, `placeEven`), and a capital's liking is the one it was chosen by (`g.work.like`: the ground opened under a dwarf capital on a hill had failed it).

After the fifth play-test, the generator's water (2026-10-22): stage 4 keeps rivers out of the desert (no rain there, the drainage rising into it, terminal lakes: `GEN_RIVER.desert`); stage 5b `genPonds` lays springs at brook heads, ponds (hollows, pools, tarns, oxbows: `GEN_POND`) and desert oases (`GEN_OASIS`), listed in `WM.ponds` and `WM.oases` ({i, r, kind or big, tiles}) for the art.

After the owner's art notes (2026-10-22, agent AR; `tests/visual/worldart.js` `&part=8`, the crops `&part=9`): a group of hill tiles is one broad rise, not a small bump a tile (the pale blobs on open land were those bumps: each hill tile's spot on a 22 x 18 px lattice drew its own 32-px rise, its lit side the grass hill's pale tone); every wood tile is a full stand, at its edge, in a small wood, by a road or a shore too (`PX.WN_WOOD`); a farm's plots grow real crops in rows (`worldart.js` `FARM`, `fieldCrop`, `farmPlot`; `PX.worldCrop`, `PX.worldCropThing`): wheat, barley and rye with stooks, vegetables, potatoes, vines on stakes, orchards, flax, hay, herbs, the desert's irrigated plots with their channel and date palms, the frost's rye, turnips and fallow under snow, by the climate and the holding's people (the Vale's wheat, the Sylvan Court's herbs and orchards, the Karn's terraced rye and potatoes, the Ashgor's herd ground), `FARM.ripe` the seasons' hook. A farm's plot is drawn through `farmPlot` once `structures.js` `estateGround` calls it (a one-line hook; until then the old furrows show).

After the owner's play-test of 2026-10-23 (agent EW; `tests/workplaces.js`, pictures `tests/visual/workplaceshots.js`): workplaces stand where they make sense. One table, `data/economy.js` `ESTATE_LAND`, says what each needs of its land besides its ground (`ESTATES` `ground`), and it decides both whether a holding has one (`Buildings#canHave`: no spot in its land, no such estate; the AI never starts one; the Fief greys its Place with the reason, `Buildings#whyNot`, "No ore in this land") and where it stands (`lots.js` `lotFits` by the estate's key, `landFits`): a **mine** with 3 of its 4 tiles at the rock (a hill, or beside a mountain) and an ore vein of its holding's land within 3 tiles; a **quarry** at the rock the same way; both turn their backs to the rock (`rockYaw`, as the land lay before the estates' tracks: `trackWas`, `kindWas`); a **lumber camp** with 40% of the tiles in and round it wooded (was 2 tiles); a **farm** and its fields on fertile ground (`FERTILE`: not the desert's dry sand; there, moist 20 or more, or water within 3 tiles); a **pasture** on grass or hills and a **port** by its pier's water, as before. No mine digs from nothing (`resources.js` `resOut`: the old iron and copper with no vein is gone; such a holding buys its iron). A **pasture's herd** comes from its land (`data/critters.js` `PASTURE_HERD`, `PASTURE_LAND`; `critters.js` `pastureLand`, `pastureHerd`): grass cattle or sheep, hills sheep or goats, the forest's edge pigs or goats, frost reindeer or sheep, desert goats or camels, swamp pigs or water buffalo; its people's taste (`LIVESTOCK`) only tips the choice. The new **camel**, **water buffalo** and kept **reindeer** (`tamereindeer`, drawn as the wild one) give their pasture their own day (`CRITTERS` `make`, `Estate#makes`): camels milk, cheese, a little meat and hides (furs), buffalo meat, milk and cheese. With every new rule off, all the fingerprints are as before.

After the owner's play-test of 2026-10-23 (agent BA; `tests/ships.js`, `tests/worldui.js`, pictures `tests/visual/undershots.js`):
a boat passes under a bridge. A ship whose waterline lies above a bridge's last row, its box meeting the bridge (`world/ships.js`
`decksOver`), sorts before whoever is on the bridge and, once drawn, has the deck put back over it (`shipUnder`): the kept scene's
own pixels (`memCv`: the deck as the map shows it) through the bridge's picture (`WorldArt.deck(c)`: cut from the chunks' own
drawing, `artGenXing`'s rects, kept per bridge), within its box, on the scene's whole pixels. Its wake and net go under with it; a
trade ship's label stays over it. Stone and wooden bridges; fords and ferries as before; battles have no boats. About 0.05 ms a
ship under a bridge; a ship away from them is drawn as before. Going to attack a party (a chase of a foe: `wuiKind` 'foe'), the
marker over it is crossed swords (`worldui.js` `wuiSwords`: `ICONS.attack` in the foes' red, outlined in the figures' navy,
dropping and bobbing as the arrow does; at least `WUI_K.swords` (11) CSS px high, at a whole multiple of its pixels when zoomed far
out on a phone); its ring and footprints as before; a plain move keeps the gold arrow, a friend his arrow. The party ring's Attack
shows the same icon (`icon()` takes an 11 × 11 grid as well as 9 × 9).

After the play-test of 2026-10-22, the generator and placement: no tiny patch of land (`worldgen.js` `genPatchTidy`, `GEN_PATCH.min` 4: a piece of open ground, wood, hills or peaks under 4 tiles, 4 ways, joins the kind round it most, at stage 5b and again at stage 10; never into peaks but a pocket among peaks alone, never into water; isles and road verges stay). No brook through a holding (`genHoldBrooks`: every brook within a holding's ring and one tile more cut back up to its springs; `worldplace.js` `placeDry`: a centre's box never on water or a brook; `lots.js` `brookAt`, `LOT_BROOK`: no building on or beside a brook, no field on one). Ports only on harbour water (`worldplace.js` `portWater`, `PLACE_PORT`: the sea, an inland sea or a lake of 400 tiles or more, a navigable or great river; a fishing pier on any other lake or a river 2 wide or more; never a pond, an oasis or a stream; `lots.js` `pierOk`, `ships.js` `dockOf` on its pier's own water; the League likes harbour water, `PLACE.regrow` 5). Sites (`data/world.js` `DENSITY.grow.by.sites`, a rule a kind): caves, ruins and shrines grow with the land at a power of .35 (1.5 times the base on the classic seed), hideouts .8 as the bands, the travellers' camps with the road (`worldRoads`: a great road's tile 1, a track's .5, an estate's track none; 21 to 1,680 tiles, one to 80); `sites.js` `SITE_GAP` keeps the travellers 30 tiles apart and 16 from the outlaws' camps and hideouts, and draws them beside a road (`TravellersCamp.pick`). An estate's track keeps its painted line, corners rounded as the art rounds them, off every lot and field (`lotClear`, `softClear`).

To look at the result, take a screenshot of a prepared scene (`scene` is `world` or `battle`; `town`, `mount` and `zoom` are optional):

```
chrome --headless --allow-file-access-from-files --window-size=1400,880 --screenshot=shot.png "file:///.../Ashmark.html?test=visual/shot&scene=world&town=Skullrock&mount=1&zoom=1"
```

## Sound

Everything heard is synthesised by WebAudio as it plays (`js/core/audio.js`, `js/core/music.js`): oscillators, filtered noise and envelopes, no files (fetching is unreliable from `file://`, and a phone downloads nothing). One `AudioContext`, made or woken by the first key, click or touch (the autoplay rule), suspended while the tab is hidden. Each sound and each mood feeds the effects or the music bus, both into master, then a compressor and a soft clip, so a big melee never clips.
- **Effects:** `sfx(name, {vol, pan, pitch, delay})` plays a cue of `SFX` (each cue: a small recipe, `gap` its rate limit, `max` how many at once, `pri` who gives way at the cap); `sfxAt(name, x, y)` plays one heard from a point of the field: panned, quieter away from the middle of the battle camera, unheard three half-views off. At most `AUDIO.voices` (24) sound at once; past it a more important cue takes the oldest lesser one's place, else it is dropped. Cues: UI `click confirm back refused coin levelup notify`; world `encounter town day`; battle `horn charge clash hit block loose arrowhit brace gallop rout rally victory defeat`.
- **Music:** `music(mood)`: `menu`, `world`, `town`, `battle` (Dorian, Mixolydian, Aeolian; lute or harp, drone, recorder or fiddle, frame drum and tabor) and the stingers `victory` and `defeat`. Each mood draws three motifs from its seed; each four-bar phrase is made anew from them (repeated, moved, inverted, a note split, a new cadence and chord walk), so it is recognisable but never loops. A lookahead scheduler puts notes on the audio clock 0.4 s ahead; a new mood crossfades over 1.8 s; a stinger plays out before the next mood comes in.
- **The game:** `audioWatch()` (main.js's loop) sets the mood from the scene (title, road, town window or streets, battle, its stinger once over; the Game Menu keeps what is under it) and plays the encounter alert, a town's gate and bells, the day's chime, level-up and the horn as a fight begins. Every button (one listener in ui.js) clicks, confirms (primary), goes back (a bottom bar), chinks coin (gold changed) or refuses (a red message).
- **The battle:** each hook asks `sfxOn()` first, so with sound off it reckons nothing. Blows and missiles (attacks.js `Fighter.hit`, projectiles.js `Missile.strike`; `blowSound`): on a shield's front `block` (the test vfxHit splinters by), an arrow or bolt `arrowhit`, steel on mail or plate `clash`, magic, beasts and the rest `hit`; a ranged troop's shot `loose` (attacks.js `Fighter.attack`, not magic); an arrow into the ground a soft `arrowhit`. Only one in `SND_EVERY` of each kind is played (a counter, never a dice), then the cues' `gap` and `max` (the blows, shots and strikes at most 16 at once) and the voice cap. morale.js: `rout` as a group routs (`route`), `rally` as it rallies; unitabilities.js: each ability's `snd` (Brace `brace`, Spur `gallop`, Rally `rally`); commands.js `setOrder`: your charge order, `gallop` with riders in the selection, else `charge`; commander.js: `gallop` as AI riders set off on a charge; battlehud.js `calloutAmbush`: `charge` from the ambushers.
- **Settings:** Settings → Sound: on/off and three volumes (`PREFS` `mute`, `volmaster`, `volmusic`, `volsfx`, stored as `ashmark.<key>`). **Shift+M** mutes anywhere (M opens the full map).
- **Off:** `AUDIO.on=false` (`tests/harness.js` sets it) or no WebAudio: every call is a cheap no-op that never throws. Sound never touches `Math.random` (own `mkRng`), so seeded battles are unchanged.
- **Gallery:** `sounds.html` plays every cue (battle cues also at the left and right edge), each mood and stinger, has the volume sliders, a voice-cap box, a peak meter and a battle-stress button (60 random battle cues in 2 s). Tests: `tests/audio.js` (offline rendering: every cue and mood heard, never past full scale; limits; settings; the mood following the scene; a lab battle heard, under the cap, and fought the same with sound on or off).

## The screen
One screen, filling the window at a whole-number scale (F11 or the corner button for full screen). Everything lies on it: your
band's card top left (your hero and level; the band's stamina and health bars, its tags; gold, food and drink in days, troops,
power, morale, pace; what runs short is red: see Supplies), the minimap top right, a short hint top middle, messages bottom left
(they fade; the Journal's Chronicle, L, keeps lasting events), the dock bottom right (Camp Z, Map M, Hero C, Skills K, Bag I, Journal J,
Help H; then zoom, save, full screen, and the Game Menu, Esc or Tab), battle orders bottom middle in a battle. Hold the mouse or a finger on the map for the action ring.
Hover anything on the map for what it is, its strength against you and what a click does. Fog of war (js/world/fog.js) keeps
what you have explored.

**The main menu** (`ui/mainmenu.js` `MainMenu`, its art `gfx/menuart.js`; the owner, 2026-10-22, from the concept picture
doc/assets/ashmark-main-menu-pixel-art-reference.png, which the game never loads). The page opens on it with no world made, forged,
installed or drawn: `worldmap.js` installs the classic world at load only for the tests (`?test=`) and the wiki;
the loop draws the menu instead of the game while it shows (`main.js`). A world is made when a campaign starts (`session.js`
`begin`) or a save loads (`save.js` `loadGame`), and let go again when the Esc
menu's Main menu or Exit game, or the game over's Back to the title, takes you back (`ui.js` `toTitle`, `session.js` `dropWorld`).
Down the left a banner (iron posts and rod, dark cloth ragged at its foot) with the emblem (a pale tree whose roots run down
into three peaks), ASHMARK in pixel capitals and five framed buttons: **New Campaign** (the New game page, then the New
world screen, then forging: the same flow as before), **Continue** (the latest save; dimmed with none), **Load game** (all seven save slots),
**Settings** (the Settings window), **Quit** (the tab closes where the browser lets a page close itself, else a page says to
close it); under them Wiki. Arrows move between the buttons, Enter presses one, Esc does nothing there. At the
bottom right a framed line of lore (`MAINMENU.lore`) changes every 9 s. Behind it all the land at dusk (`MenuScene`): sky,
clouds, stars, ranges, sea, ships, a smoking volcano, a dragon far off, a castle on its hill with houses, fields and a windmill,
a river under a bridge between towers, pine woods, a column marching under banners, your camp by its fire; the engine's own
trees, building models and people figures graded to dusk. Seeded (`MENU_ART.seed`), baked once a size (about 40 ms on a
desktop) and only the moving bits drawn after, 12 frames a second (still with reduced motion). Its pixels are whole device
pixels: the scene about 420 game px high (2× at 1600 × 900; mobile skips the scene). The title's other pages are windows
over the scene, the banner put away. On mobile, the menu fills the screen with its buttons centred; neither the background scene nor the banner cloth is allocated or drawn. Short landscape screens omit the emblem so all five buttons remain visible; portrait keeps it. Resizing back to desktop restores the illustrated scene. Save/load rows use their own `save-slot` class, with metadata escaped and wrapped inside each row, independent of inventory gear slots. The page reaches a working menu in about 0.4 s here (it was about 2.0 s on the generated
world when it forged or installed the world first). Tests: `tests/mainmenu.js` (the real page in a
frame: no world at load, the buttons, Continue dimmed, the keys, New Campaign, 844 × 390 and 1600 × 900, the scene seeded, a
campaign and back), `tests/menu.js`. Visual fixtures: `tests/visual/menulayout.js` (title, load, save, esc-load; long metadata and scrolling).

**The full map** (`ui/map.js`; doc/WORLD_DESIGN.md §3.4, §12.4): **M**, a click or a tap on the minimap, or the dock's Map beside Camp (a phone's way in), opens the whole world

**The full map as an old map** (2026-11-08, the owner: "make it look more like maps with a paper textures and color tones. Make the map allow pinch for zoom in and out or drag; use holding sprite or places sprite instead of dots or circle"; `ui/map.js`, `style.css` §the full map): the land an ink wash on old paper (`fmWash`: each ground's colour muted, `FULLMAP.sat`, laid over `FULLMAP.paper`, `wash`), the coasts inked (`coast`), a realm's border in its colour darkened (`ink`), what you never saw the bare paper (`unknown`); over the picture its grain, two faint stains and a darkened edge, the whole page torn along its sides (`fullMapPaper`: a grain tile and an edge mask, CSS `--grain`, `--edge`), on a dark table. Each holding is its own centre building (`fmIcon`: `structures.js` `lotSprite` cut to what is drawn, burnt when ruined), its realm's colour under it, its name in ink (serif italic; from 2 px a tile, a town's, a city's and a castle's always); each place you have found (a cave, ruins, a shrine, a hideout, a goblin camp: `Discovery.places`, `s.seen`) its sprite (`siteSprite`); sized by kind (`FULLMAP.icon`) × the zoom (`fmIconScale`); a province's danger above its seat once the names show; you a red seal. Any zoom between the first and last of `FULLMAP.zooms` (`fullMapScale`, the marks placed by CSS `--z`, so nothing is rebuilt): two fingers pinch it about their middle and pan with it, the wheel (a trackpad's pinch) zooms about the pointer, a drag (finger or mouse) pans, a tap zooms in a step, − and + step through the zooms; it opens filling the view (`fullMapFit`). Tests: `fullmap` (the scale, the steps, a pinch, a drag, the wheel, the sprites, the places only once found).

**The notice board's papers torn** (2026-11-08, the owner: "paper on the board should look more torn, not modern like paper"; `ui/noticeboard.js` `look`, `style.css` §the notice board): each paper torn its own way (`data-tear` 0-3 by its place and kind: a corner torn away, a bite out of a side, the foot ripped ragged), foxed specks, its edges browned to burnt, its Accept an ink stamp on the paper. Tests: `noticeboard`.

**Mills turning** (2026-11-08, the owner: "for mills or any buildings please draw mills separately from the building to animate it"; `engine/buildmodels.js` `Plan#rotor`, `Plan#millWheel`, `PX.ROTORS`, `PX.rotorFrame`, `PX.withRotors`; `engine/model.js` `m.rotors`): a windmill's sails and a watermill's wheel (every culture's) are no longer in the building's picture but its rotors: the model keeps only a ghost of each (a disc the fit counts, so the building is the same size) and returns each rotor's hub and the screen vectors of its plane, foreshortened as the building is turned. The game draws them over the building turning (`gfx/structures.js` `drawRotors`: `PX.ROTORS.spin` turns a second, 8 frames a quarter turn of sails, 6 an eighth of a wheel, kept), still where you remember the building outside your sight, none on a ruin or a building site; the title screen's windmill turns too (`gfx/menuart.js` `millSails`). `PX.ROTORS.on` off: drawn into the picture as before. Tests: `buildmodels` (every culture's mills carry their rotors, frames by angle, edge-on still seen, off as before). (No estate builds a mill yet: they stand in the clusters and on the title screen.)

**Small fixes** (2026-11-08, the owner's notes): a battle's experience shown in whole numbers (`battle/resolution.js` `ArmyReport#since`, `ui/loot.js`), "1 foe" not "1 foes"; a holding's tip says why its gate will not have you and what it asks (`world/holding.js` `gateTip`: the padlock under it on the map, `gfx/structures.js` `holdingLabel`); a shield on the arm a pixel in, against it (`gfx/dollfigs.js` `DOLL_FIGS.off`).

**The gate's padlock** (2026-11-08, the owner: "make the lock city icon animated and more cool, it should floating around castle"; `gfx/structures.js` `gateLock`, `GATE_LOCK`): over a holding whose gate will not have you, a gold padlock (pixel art, made once) floats over its top (over its flag), drifting in a slow figure of eight, a glow breathing under it, two sparks circling it; each holding its own phase. Drawn only. Tests: `gates`.

**A holding from afar** (2026-11-10, the owner, a phone: "Saw a bug of locked holding, not sure but please check whether we can discover them. And why there still a text? And there is a weird blue square dot"; `gfx/structures.js` `holdingPlaque`, `lordCrown`; `world/discovery.js` `Discovery.reach`, `Discovery.meet`): a holding as you last saw it (out of your sight) showed its name in grey under its centre (left from before the map lost its names, and its true name even if you had not discovered it) and dropped its padlock. A lord inside is a small crown, not a 3-pixel square (`lordCrown`). Then (the owner: "a panel bar above holding to show name (once discovered), lord crown visit icon and lock icon in one place"; `holdingPlaque`, `drawPlaques`, `HOLD_PLAQUE`): one dark plank over each holding, in sight or as you last saw it, drawn over everything with the map's words: its padlock while its gate will not have you, its name once discovered (else its kind, greyed: `Discovery.nameOf`), a crown for each lord inside (drawn twice their size; in your sight only), a red "!" while besieged. Then (the owner: "Can you make a bar more interesting?", and "It's too bland, can you make it like more ribbon and more detail?"; `drawPlaqueRibbon`): a plank of dark wood with its grain, framed in gold, a rivet at each corner and a gold crest on its middle set with its owner's colour, on a ribbon in its owner's colour, gold-edged, lit along its top and stitched, that runs out behind it each side and droops to a fold, turning under into a lower tail cut in a swallow tail, its owner's heater shield before the padlock (a pale down it, gold-rimmed; free folk's plain brown: `plaqueShield`), each lord a gold crown (9 × 8) set with his realm's colour; a remembered bar a little dimmer. The keep (the owner: "the castle building looks a bit odd for the balcony vs tower roof"; `engine/buildmodels.js`): where its towers wear cones, its donjon wears a hip roof over its battlements and its turrets rise over it (the capital's citadel too), and its low windows are left off where its walls' towers stand before them. Discovery: a holding spreads 8 to 18 tiles from its heart, so its `DISCOVER.r` tiles now count from its edge (its farthest building, field or wall: `Discovery.reach`), and coming to its gate makes it known at once, shut to you or not (`Discovery.meet`, from `approachHolding`). Tests: discovery, buildmodels.

**The top right on a phone, and the discovery's title** (2026-11-08, the owner: "the top right on mobile is not well aligned", "the discover should be at center of the screen"): on a real screen the minimap (`gfx/render.js` `worldMiniRect`) keeps clear of the screen's edge and a phone's notch as the HUD does (`--edge` and the safe insets, CSS px, with its frame), and is no narrower than the panel under it (`ui/underway.js` `uwMin`: `UW_MIN`, or 40% of a phone held upright), so their edges meet; the tests' fixed view as before. The discovery's title (`style.css` `.discover`) rises in the middle of the screen (a phone on its side: at 32%), on a soft dark ground, over the messages.

**No line crossing a border** (2026-11-08, the owner: "remove the log when you travel cross to the region or territory"; `world/territory.js` `landWatch`): the realm underfoot is still kept (`G.landFac`) and named on the HUD, but entering a realm's lands, your own or the wilds no longer writes to the log. W7 re-recorded (the save holds the log).

**The minimap, a piece of the full map** (2026-11-08, the owner: "the map show on the worldmap should be a zoomed in version of new map"; `gfx/render.js` `MINIMAP`, `worldMiniView`, `drawMiniMap`): the minimap is the full map's parchment (`ui/map.js` `fullMapPic`, now for a box of tiles too; its grain `fmGrain`) cut round the screen's middle, at least 96 tiles across (or 3 times what the screen shows, never more than the map), with a darkened paper edge, the screen's own view inked on it, each holding and place you know as its full-map sprite (`fmIcon`, a holding on its realm's colour), the parties, quests and rumours as dots, and you as on the full map. Its picture is a box twice the view, made again when the view leaves it, the realms change or (at most once a second) you see more of it; it is drawn on its own canvas at the screen's pixels and laid on whole. Its rectangle, the tap that opens the full map and the panel under it are as before; in a battle on the map a tap on it still moves the camera there (`battle/camera.js` `miniHit`). `MINIMAP.on` false: the whole map as before. W6 re-recorded (proof: with it off every hash holds). Tests: fullmap, mapzoom, worldbattleedges.
in a window (header with the world's name, size, how much you have explored and − + for the zoom; the map beside its legend;
Close). M again or Esc closes it; the world waits meanwhile. The picture is made from the tile data (`fullMapPic`: the minimap's
colours, rivers by width, each realm's tint and a stronger line where two realms meet, the dark over land never seen); the
holdings (by kind, named from town up, villages from 2 px a tile), the crossings on land you have seen and you are marks over it.
It opens at the largest scale that fits, never under 1 px a tile (1 px per 2 tiles on a map wider than 640: Huge), so Normal
shows at 1 px a tile, Large scrolls and Huge is 512 × 384; + − step it, a click zooms in where it lands (1:1 by region at
Huge), a drag pans. On a phone on its side the legend slims to a column and the map scrolls. Tests: `tests/fullmap.js`.

World and battle HUDs use the same brown frames and cream labels: the company card, day/speed controls, dock, order bar and selected unit cards. Both canvas minimaps share `gfx/render.js`'s `MAP_FRAME` and `drawMapFrame`; their map and click rectangles are unchanged. The full map has a brown legend and charcoal unexplored land. Fitness and morale meters match the Army's sage green; warning states, mana and faction colours remain distinct.

The shared interface uses the management windows' brown surfaces, gold frames and cream text (`style.css` tokens). Buttons, cards, headers, input borders and HUD frames follow that palette; battle side colours and status colours keep their meaning. New world frames the scrolling settings and preview separately, with the same controls and navigation. In portrait they scroll as one column, with Advanced settings inside their frame and the preview below.

**The New world screen** (`ui/worldsetup.js`; doc/WORLD_DESIGN.md §4, Phase 2b): after your race and past (a
past's card names `world:<past>`, `session.js` `begin`), the world's settings, then "Forging the world…". Left (it scrolls):
the presets (Classic first and chosen at first, then Normal, Large, Huge, Wild Frontier, Crowded Realms; touching a setting makes it Custom, and a mix that is a
preset's again shows as that preset), the seed (typed, Random; a world code typed or pasted there sets everything), the world code
with Copy (`worldsettings.js` `worldCode`, `parseWorldCode`: `ASH1-<preset>-<every setting and the size, base 36>-<seed>`), the
size (or Custom: width and height with − and +), the holdings (Fewer, Normal, More: `HOLDING_DENSITY` times the area's default,
27 / 40 / 66 at Normal / Large / Huge, More today's 37 / 55 / 90; or a number with − and +) and the Advanced fold-out (the rest,
those later phases read marked later). Classic locks everything that changes the map; monsters, bandits and your start stay open.
Every change is clamped at once, with a note when a limit moved a value. Right: the preview (no city marked as your start): the quick
sketch (`worldgen.js` `genSketch`: the real land field and climate noise at 256 points across, mountains, rivers and holdings
approximate; about 70 ms at any size), redrawn 200 ms after a change, or the real map when it is made (the world in); no buttons there (the owner, 2026-10-22: Random is the seed's own, Enter in the seed redraws); the counts and the time it takes to forge here (measured once, kept in `localStorage`). A generated world but
Classic starts as its seed says (`session.js` `seededStart`: its first sites, herds, bands and beasts with the game's number 0
and Math.random the 'start' stream's), so one code starts one world; what happens in play stays random. The Game Menu's World
button opens the World page (every setting, the world code with Copy, what it holds); a save's line reads "Redwater · Large ·
seed redwater". Tests: `tests/worldsettings.js`; screenshots: `tests/visual/worldsetup.js` (`?test=visual/worldsetup&v=1`).
Easier (2026-11-08; the owner: "Make the world building settings more easier. Improve ux for settings", "map creation preview should
not show or highlight the city you start"): the left column runs easiest first: World type (the presets, and Surprise me: a world of
chance, its size Normal or Large, its holdings and every land, people and danger setting rolled, a fresh seed), Size, Holdings,
Danger (monsters and bandits, always shown), More options folded (`WS_GROUPS`: Land, People), and last Seed and sharing (the seed,
Random, the world code with Copy, a line on what a code is for; its notes shown there). Each setting has a line of what it does
and each choice a tip (`WS_HINT`). Sacred places, founding sites and start, which nothing reads yet, are no longer shown (kept in
the world code at their preset's). The preview marks no holding as your start. The preview's caption no longer collapses on a phone.

People menu removed (2026-10-24): Journal (J) owns known lords and realms; Army (C) owns companion management, and Quests (Q) owns jobs and their givers. The dock has no People button and P is unassigned. World sightings still update saved last-known reports; hall audiences return to the hall when left.

The quest tracker (2026-10-30; the owner: "a quest tracking tab at the left-hand side for quick access like RPG games"; a new quest's popup "below that tracking tab, disappearing automatically in 5 secs, with an animation"): `ui/questtrack.js`. On the world map, under your band card (a phone: at the top left, at most two, the card being a sheet there), the quests you track (`world/quests.js` `QuestTrack.list`: `P.tracks`, the newest first, at most `QUEST_TRACKS`), each its title, what to do now and which way; a click opens it in your Quests; the one marked on the map (`P.track`) in gold. A quest you take (`Quest#accept` tells `quest:new`) shows its note below them (beside them on a phone) for `QTRACK.show` s, its bar running down: Track adds it and marks it, × lets it go, as the time does. Several taken at once: their notes stack, each its own time, at most `QTRACK.stack` (3; a phone: the newest two, each one row). Untracked in your Quests, it leaves the tracker and none is marked; a marked quest over, the next tracked one is marked (`QuestTrack.drop`). Tests: questtrack, questwindow.

The quest's reward (2026-10-31; the owner: "when submitting quests make a popup window at the center to show what you've received, with icons"): `ui/questreward.js` `QuestReward`. Each quest paid adds its card to one window in the middle of the screen, over whatever is open: a job's (`Quest#pay`: its gold, a friendly lord's bonus apart, experience, renown, a holding's reputation, a realm's honor, the giver's regard) or a story's end and a step's (`forms.js` end, `steps.js` pay: what its outcome gave you, items and goods with their own pictures, troops and a companion joining among them), each an icon over its amount (`QUEST_REWARD`: icon and label by kind). A new quest's journal line no longer says to track it with Q. Continue or Esc closes it; on a phone each reward is a compact row. Tests: quests.

Handing in at a holding (2026-11-12; the owner: "make quest submission at holdings more better"): `world/quests.js` `jobsBody`. Your jobs reported here come first, in two parts. **Ready to report**: each a short card (`readyRow`): its title with a Ready stamp, who gave it, what it pays (less if late, said), Report or Hand over; with two or more, Report all and their gold in all (`q-turnall`). **Reported here, not done yet** (`laterRow`): its title and what is left ("Still to do: …", or "You have …" for a hand-over), no button. Either card folds the whole of the quest (who and why, its cause, steps, facts) under Details (`qwMore`). Several reported at once fill one reward window, "In all" first when more than one (`QuestReward.total`: alike rewards added up). Tests: turnin, quests, noticeboard.

Under way (2026-10-30; the owner: staying "more compact, at a corner of the screen", "a panel that groups all the special actions"): `ui/underway.js`. On the world map, under the minimap (its right edge the minimap's, clear of a phone's notch), one small box: a line for each thing your band is doing that lasts, each with its way out (`UNDERWAY`, one row per kind): staying in a holding (its name and price; Leave), in camp (Break), making camp (Stop), following (Stop), by road (Off), your siege (its day) or raid (how far; Open, its window). It replaces the stay banner and the day bar's By road and Following pills. Nothing under way, in a battle or under a window: no box. Tests: underway, stay, follow, roadband, mobile.

Spoils left on the field (2026-10-30; the owner: withdrawn or beaten with beasts left, "you can still get all loots, but you have to come back at that area and claim it"; "the beast may wander around there, looters or bandits may also seek this"): `world/fieldloot.js`. When a fight against beasts ends in a withdrawal or a defeat (`resolution.js` `conclude`; a battle of several sides when beasts hold the field, `sides.js` `settle`), what it would have paid stays where it was fought (`fieldSpoils`: gold for each man slain, the beasts' mats, meat, furs and gear rolls, none in your bag yet), the pack's lair moves there (it wanders around it), and the side quest Spoils left on the field points you back. There with no beast within `FIELD_LOOT.guard` px, the spoils are yours (the bag full, the rest stays). Each day a band within `FIELD_LOOT.seek` px may set out for it (`lure`, `b.lootGo`, `BandBrain`); there, it takes them and the quest is over. In `FIELD_LOOT.days` (5) they are gone. Tests: fieldloot.

Witnessing and distractions (2026-10-24): battle markers and witnessed logs now use unit sight and concealment (`spotted`). `world/distractions.js` keeps short, runtime-only cues from noisy events and ongoing fights within 1.25× current sight: a directional wordless cry (`audio.js` `distantbattle`) and white waves from the source, or rolling in from its offscreen edge (`worldui.js`), like encounter waves. Hearing adds no name, outcome, log, map mark or saved knowledge. Nearby reports coalesce; cues expire, stop when seen/out of earshot, respect mute/reduced motion, and reset on world/load. Existing delayed rider messages are unchanged. Tests: `worldwitness`, `worldlog`, `worldui`, `fights`, `audio`; screenshots: `visual/worldwitness`.

World-map labels (2026-10-24): queued army counts, holding names and nearby building labels use `sprites.js` `mapTxt` / `mapTxtPic`: semibold Arial at final device-pixel resolution, with a thin dark outline and an 11 CSS-pixel minimum. Glyph pictures are cached by text, color, alignment and device size, then copied 1:1 on whole device pixels through the camera transform. Map art retains its pixel grid; legacy battle/street canvas text retains `txt` / `txtPic`. No new font download or simulation/save change. Tests: `maplabels`, `smoothmove`, `worldui`, `lordnames`, `worldprint`; before/after fixture: `visual/maplabels` (`legacy=1` selects the old labels).

Journal retention (2026-10-25): built. Story, quests, people, places, events, rumours and news remain in history. Action and system feedback (Hurry, route toggles, battle orders, equipment changes and save confirmations) appears briefly on screen only. Use `log(m, cls, kind, ref)` for lasting events and `toast(m, cls)` for operational feedback; `action`, `system` and `command` kinds are also excluded from history and knowledge discovery. Recognizable older control messages are filtered on opening, saving and loading the Chronicle; linked stories, rumours and war reports are preserved. The save format is unchanged. Tests: `journalretention`, `chronicle` (49 checks passed).

Normal world fog in combat (2026-10-25): built. Battle uses the same campaign vision range, remembered-land renderer and mist/dimming as travel, centred on the combat hero. World range uses Scouting, equipment and the ground at that actual position. Battle-specific fog cut-outs are removed (since 2026-11-01 each of your men sees: Your sight, below). The yellow dashed circle retains its larger sight/count-based size and limits movement only; it never changes sight or exploration. Unexplored terrain stays hidden. Enemy visibility and combat-effect clipping use that same normal world range. Tests: battleworldfog (7), battlesizesight (15), including a pixel-identical fog comparison between combat and travel.

Your sight, every man's (2026-11-01; the owner: "the vision and fog of war should be based on all army units not only heroes"): built. `world/fog.js` `Sight` (`ARMY_SIGHT`, `yourSight(V)`): the union of every man's sight in your army.
