On the map each marcher of yours (`march.js` `MarchArmy`, carried with your middle until it next steps; you alone without one, or far from it) and your army's middle, `vis()` wide as before (`SIGHT_EYES.middle`: a few men spread round a wide ring never see less than the army did), in a world battle (till it is over) each of your side's living, unrouted men still in it; each sees `vis()` times his kind's share (`data/world.js` `SIGHT_KIND`, `sightShare(kind)`, the one table for the map and battle: on foot 0.786, shooters 0.929, riders 1.071, characters 1; a scout, `data/units.js` `SCOUT_KINDS`, ×1.6; in battle a scout scouting ahead farther still), as the enemy's men already did. Its eyes `{x,y,r}` are only the men whose disks make its edge, the farthest out first: a man whose rim and middle the kept ones cover within `SIGHT_EYES.tol` (3 px) adds nothing (the tolerance doubled while more than `max`, 16, but never past `most`, 6 px: no edge man's sight is lost), so a hundred men keep about 12 eyes in a block and about 25 in lanes, and the edge stays within ~2 px of every man's. Far out of it, `edge` answers from the men's box alone. Gathered again when you, your army or the battle moved on (at most once a frame); built again only when someone moved over half a px. It answers `sees(x,y,k)` (`inSight`; `worldobject.js` `spotted`, in a wood each man's 0.45), `edge(x,y)` (px out of it, the fog's smooth strengths in `drawWorldFog`); its eyes reveal the land (`reveal`) and cut the remembered scene out (`fogMem`: each eye a feathered disk drawn once a radius and stamped, `fogDisk`). `worldEye()` stays the one point (your middle; your hero in a world battle) for the camera, labels and the scout's ground (`parties.js` `sight`). `WorldBattleArea#sight(0)` is its eyes. Switched to it: what is drawn (parties, beasts, herds and fights through `spotted`; caravans, refugees, workers and mythic beasts by `sees` past it; beasts' and caravans' minimap dots; picking, `world.js` `pickAt`), what you learn (the journal's sightings, scouts spotting sites, the world log, the refugees' robbery seen), ambient life (where it is born and its homes round your sight). Left measured from your middle, as game rules: who spots you (`travelDetection`), the road's encounters (`road.js`, `encounters.js`), word of far events (`was:` within 1.5 or 2 sights), sounds you hear (`distractions.js`), waylays found (`waylay.js` `sources`), the march's active range (`march.js`), trails read (`tracks.js`), the battle area's size. Cost (`tests/visual/sightperf`, `tests/sight.js`): a build 0.02-0.05 ms with a hundred men; a marching frame's fog, cut-out and reveal 0.3-0.4 ms more than a lone hero's. Tests: `sight` (21), with `fog`, `battleworldfog` and `battlesizesight` brought to it.

Army wages (2026-10-25): roster cards and the common unit inspector show each group�s daily wages; the Army header and Condition tab show the rounded bill. Condition explains soldiers, mounted surcharge, companions and detached companion-party soldiers. `world/world.js` `BAND_PAY` and `bandPayroll` are shared with the actual daily deduction. Rates are unchanged: 0.5g per soldier including wounded and detached troops, +0.5g per fit mounted soldier in your army, 2g per companion, no hero wage; round the combined bill up once. Food and caravan costs are separate. Tests: armywages. Wages by tier (2026-10-30; the owner: "scale by tier"): each soldier a day by his kind's tier (`BAND_PAY.tier`: peasants 0.25g, tiers 1 to 3 0.5g, 1g, 2g; `soldierPay`), a fit rider 0.5g more, wounded and soldiers in companion-led parties paid as well; the unit card's Upkeep the same.

Group management and Auto Reinforce (2026-10-25): Army�s roster is titled Battle Group. The Army no longer has New recruit group or the old Reinforce target action; towns retain manual recruitment. Manage Group in the inspector owns renaming, ordering, splitting/merging and a saved per-group Auto Reinforce toggle. While travelling within normal interaction reach of a friendly, unblocked hiring holding, enabled troop groups buy matching volunteers through RecruitmentService at normal prices and standing requirements, up to group and army capacity (including wounded). Gold and volunteers limit partial fills. Full groups remain enabled for future losses. Heroes/companions cannot auto reinforce. The frame registry runs auto reinforce at 148, before arrival. Tests: autoreinforce, recruitment, structure.

Leader headcounts (2026-10-25): displayed world army numbers include a fighting lord, chieftain or detached companion once; player fighter totals include the hero and fit companions. Army muster now adds the hero into its fit total explicitly. Enemy encounter previews use the same main leader list as battle setup; an existing chief troop is reused rather than counted twice. Reinforcement previews include their participating leaders. Soldier-only size/capacity, wages, knowledge reports and combat actor creation are unchanged. Tests: leadercounts.

World sprite counts (2026-10-25): Party.draw adds a separate leader figure only for a character added to the troop roster in combat (hero, lord, companion, or a chieftain absent from the troop list). Ordinary parties and patrols use their existing troop figures; a chief already in the troop list is drawn once in that formation. Banners remain at the army anchor. Monsters keep their existing per-beast drawing. The troop roster, movement and combat creation are unchanged. Tests: marchcounts compares actual render calls with real battle actors; marchlooks checks appearance (22 checks passed).
World-to-battle appearance (2026-10-25): WorldBattleArea.place carries each matched marcher's variant and source army colour into combat. Separate leaders keep variant 0, matching their travel figure and horse coat. World actors use actorSprite for body, animation, warmup, ghosts and fallen sprites; unitLook applies the same cloth to held weapons and sidearms. Team markers and health bars retain side colours; separate tactical battles retain their original palettes. Combat sprite warmup runs again after appearance handoff. Tests: marchappearance compares pre-battle pixels with actual battle actors, mounted leaders, hero, weapons and return to travel; marchlooks checks existing appearance (16 checks passed).
Worker world battles (2026-10-25): EstateWorker draws its recruitKind battle sprite at world scale with stable worker variant and holding cloth while WORLD_BATTLE is enabled. Villagers resistance uses WorldBattleSetup on the current map; workers start at their existing positions and their ordinary figures are suppressed during combat. Matching actors retain worker race, equipment, variant and colour; estate guards use the same world battle. Survivors keep combat positions, dead workers cannot reappear after a loss, and population/replacement costs are charged once. Existing victory loot, conduct, guard settlement and manual worker interactions remain in use. No save piece is added. Tests: workerworldbattle (12), conduct (89), guards (7).
Travel pivots and animal bands (2026-10-25): leaderless armies use one existing formation member as their moving pivot. A lone creature stands exactly at its band position, without orbiting an offset or requesting follower paths. Multi-unit formations centre their pivot group around that member; followers retain terrain-aware routes and bounded speed. Recruitment/losses retain a surviving pivot. Heroes, lords, companions and separately counted chiefs keep their existing anchors. A chief already in the troop list becomes the pivot. Monster packs and wild herds show a small cached paw marker above their existing leader, with group count where applicable; wild herds use their first animal as centre. Travel rings use that same centre. After combat, a surviving pivot keeps the band's position, or another existing survivor is promoted; the fixed yellow battle boundary never moves. No fighter or save piece is added. Tests: marchpivots (18), marchpaths (22), marchcounts (14). (Pivots and separate leaders replaced 2026-10-31: below.)

**Every army's middle is no one's** (2026-10-31; the owner: "an invisible point at the center now hero can be placed any where in the army circle", "make sure that all other lords, bandits and other world entity apply the same concept"):
- An army's position (its middle, where its rings, label, clicks and detection are) is an invisible point. No one is pinned there; every figure marches to its place like a soldier (`world/march.js`).
- Its leader has a row of his own: your hero (`hero`), a lord, a companion leading his party or a chief not counted among his troops (`leader`, `marchLeaderOf`). Yours stands at the middle, behind the infantry, unless placed; another army's leader where its march style puts him (below). You place yours in the March formation window, by drag, the arrows or Reset group; the middle shows there as a small cross.
- An army with no leader (a band whose chief is a troop, a beast pack) has its chief's group as its leader in its style (a pack: its biggest group at the middle). A beast pack's marker stands over its middle.
- A caravan's wagon still stands at its middle (`marchCentrePiece`), its guards round it.
- Travel awareness sees your hero where he marches. A march army left behind by a jump counts for nothing until it forms again.
- The army is where its men are (2026-11-01; the owner: "why don't you calculate the center based on unit's position"; `MARCH_MOVE.menCentre`). Its x, y are its men's middle (`MarchArmy#mid`: each man's spot less his place, their median; his places turned first by the turn that best lays them on the men, so a wheel under way neither drags it back nor flings it out; no places, their midst), written back at the end of each step. Sight, meetings, the camera and saves read it.
- What moves an army (a brain, your march, a test) moves its guide (`MarchArmy#gx, gy`), round which its men's places are laid. The `march guides` system (frame 48, before the brains) hands each army's owner its guide back to move; `march formations` (145) takes the move, steps the men and writes their middle back. The guide is never more than `MARCH_MOVE.lead` (4 px plus 0.6 s of the army's step) from the men's middle, any way: men held by a building, a ford or a wall hold it, so the ring cannot run off to a click. Men march at the army's step, at least their pace, at most `road` times it (its step: how far its guide went over the time since it last moved, at most a mid tier's moment). In open ground it covers its pace; through a town about 7% slower than before (its men go round the buildings).
- A jump re-places every man at his place round the new point (as a new man is set): `MarchArmy.jump(o, x, y)`, a move while the army was not marched (out of a holding, a camp, out of range), a move between moments (`march guides` sees it: `Kit.goTo`, `heroAtGate` from a window), or one past its own ring in a moment. Past `MARCH_MOVE.teleport` the army is made afresh, as before. Put where it stands with its men (`relocate`, `settle`, a battle's end) its guide goes there. A man on shut ground (a building's feet, after a battle) is put on the nearest open ground. Men on ground their middle's box does not reach (across a river, its bridge or ford past the box's edge: a battle's end) widen the box by `MARCH_MOVE.wide` (192 px) while they are so, so their way round is in it. Between steps (a battle's end, the game paused) the banner stands at the men's middle as they stand (`MarchArmy#centre`). Off (`menCentre` false): the old leash and leap guard, exactly.
- Footing (2026-11-01; the owner: "my army get stuck when the unit try to form a formation near blocking or water"; `MARCH_MOVE.footing`). With men on both banks of a river, their middle, and the guide by it, fell on the water: the map's way from there found none and every order was dropped. Now an army's guide and point are put on the nearest ground a man can stand on whenever they fall on water or a peak (a ferry's water is open; a building's feet are not the map's, so they are left alone). `tests/marchfooting.js`: Millbrook's bridge, with a control run with footing off.
- About face (2026-11-01; the owner: a column sent the other way was "kind of chaos, it takes several seconds just to turn around"; `MARCH_MOVE.about`). An army sent back the way it came turns where it stands: a column's tail becomes its head (`MarchColumn#turn`); a march formation keeps its layout and flips its facing at once; the guide goes on at full pace. The chosen order is reversed for that leg. A march formation's comes back after 200 px or a halt (men ahead of their places stand aside past its flank till their places come level); a column's by the countermarch loop below. `on:false` gives the old re-form or wheel. Outriders (`marchOutriders`): ×1.08 map pace only while every group rides and has it. Tests: `marchabout`.
- Countermarch loop (2026-11-01; the owner: "move in long oval arc or circle ... make them one line each and move around in circle to rotate between head and tail"; `MARCH_MOVE.about.loop`, `MarchArmy#loopStart`/`loopEnd`; `marchstyles.js` `MarchLoop`). A column turned back keeps its order reversed while it marches. At a halt, once its men stand in their places, it walks a racetrack oval where it stands: two lanes as long as the column, round ends, single file, all the same way round, half a lap (20 men about 4 s, 50 about 10 s, 80 about 16 s), then stands as the straight column in the chosen order, facing the way it went. Its pace 0.8 of the walk, reached over 1 s, held down by its slowest-turning men. No room (water, a building, a cliff within 2 px of the oval) leaves the order reversed till the next halt; a new order mid-loop stops it. `loop.on` false gives the old restore. Every army: yours, lords', caravans'. Tests: `marchabout`.
- **Paths round obstacles** (2026-11-01; the owner: "can you make the pathfinder better for obstacles? right now the units seem to bump into boundaries, houses, etc. which sometimes make them stuck"; `battle/route.js` `PATHS`). Each route grid keeps its clearance (`routeRoom`: px from each cell to the nearest shut cell or two-way cliff, made once per version); a body keeps `routeNeed`'s room (its half-width, or a narrow lane's middle, so a narrow gap never shuts), and searches (`routePath` need), straight lines (`lineRoom`, `lineBody`) and steps keep it. On the map an army whose men are blocked makes flow fields toward its front and rear (`routeFlow`, `MarchGround` `flow`), its men following them down, those the fields do not lead mending or searching their own; steps slide along walls (`routeSlide`), men pushed into a wall's room pushed out, one held by a wall searching, squeezing, then stepping back. In battle each group has a field to its way's end (`TerrainRoutes` `groupFlow`), ways mended (`mend`), men pushed out of a wall's room. Buildings on the map are solid on their ground plans (`marchFootings`, read off each model's faces: roofs, overhangs, flat parts, gatehouses and gated fence runs open), so men walk behind tall ones, faded through them (gfx/depth.js); a holding's centre keeps its picture's foot (the world's router knows no footings yet). Cost: the march 0.380 → 0.344 ms a step for 60 men down a street; a 150-man battle's movement as before, its AI fight about 3% more (its men keeping their room). Every switch off: the old ways. Tests: `paths`.
- **The army routes as one** (2026-11-02; the owner: "should the pathfinder calculated as a whole group first then make the unit move together?"; battle/route.js `PATHS.corridor`). On the march an army's way through its ground is found first (`MarchArmy#corridor`): from its rear, by its guide, along the guide's own way ahead (router.js `Router.ahead`; yours walked straight: your goal), each leg searched with its widest body's room and string-pulled, kept till the ground, the grid or the goal changes and made again through the old one's points so it keeps its side. Its blocked men follow it: their fields seeded along it (`routeFlowWay`, a step off it `PATHS.off` times dearer), down it when a man's place lies further along, else back up it; a man's own way only while it keeps within `PATHS.stray` px of it; a place on a building's footing pulled toward it (`PATHS.pull`). Halted, men fan out to their places as before. In battle a group's field is seeded along its way (`TerrainRoutes#groupFlow`). Ashford's citadel: none of 61 round the far side (30 before). Tests: corridor.
- **Holdings' walls are solid** (the owner: "the holding walls is not correct because the unit can walk through it"; `PATHS.holding`). A centre's footing (`holdFooting`) is its ground plan filled and swept up its picture by `HOLD_FOOT.back` (Ashford's citadel: its lower 75%), its gate open. The world's router shuts the tiles it covers (router.js `holdShut`, SHUT bit 2: every search, `clearLine`, no wall corner cut, no road edge over them), the holding's own tile, its gate, open; read again when a centre is re-laid, ruined, rebuilt or walled. Everyone goes round to the gate; a holding is reached at its gate (`reachRadius`).
- **Columns hold steady** (`TRAVEL_STYLES.steady`). A foe is seen at `alert` and lost only past `alert × clear`; a switched style holds `hold` s, but a foe newly near breaks a column at once; a column that cannot form (more than `stuck` of its men blocked) stays a formation for `retry` s. A short By-road trip of yours goes the plain way, not back to a road behind you (40 px: 2.5 s, before 13.9 s walking away first). **By road is your own column switch** (`yourChoice`; the owner: "for player army it should not auto change formation if enemy is near since player can toggle on and off himself"): your army never forms up by itself at a foe; every other army still does.
- **A place that cannot be reached is given up** (`MARCH_MOVE.formGiveUp`; the owner: "after 5 secs or xxx cooldown timer then it just stop trying and stand there", "this will apply to all formations in marching or in battle"). A man no nearer his place by `formGain` px in `formTry` (5) s while his army or group stands stands where he is, at rest, with the army; it counts as formed. All try again on a new order or once the guide is moved `formMove` px; none is left behind. In battle (actors.js `slotGiveUp`) it replaces `RELOCATE.max`; a foe in reach is still fought. Walking round to it (his way a way round: a cliff to its ramp, `via`), every `FORM_TRY.walk` px he walks counts as nearing it, though farther from it as the crow flies at first (2026-11-07: a line sent to a one-way top had given up below its cliff). A man too far from his group's way to take it follows its field (terrain.js `slotWay`: `flow`), not straight at his place over a cliff; his place itself is marked `.home` (it was `.at`, which every array has as a method, so `via` was never true).
- Another army seen by its nearest man (2026-11-01; the owner: "a huge army roaming around but suddenly disappear even with in just few feet away. and even weird when in the forest"; `worldobject.js` `spotted`, `manSpotted`; `SIGHT_EYES.armies`). An army marched man by man is in sight while any of its men is, each by his own ground (a wood's nearer sight at his own feet), not by its middle alone; only the men in your sight are drawn (`march.js` `marchDraw`), so a host's van shows as it comes on and the rest stays in the fog. Yours and your companions' are drawn whole. Tests: `armyspotted`.
- Your way's footprints (the yellow guide path) begin at your army's head (2026-11-01; the owner: "the guide path (yellow one) can you make it to start at the head too?"; `gfx/worldui.js` `wuiOut`: the foremost man the way it sets out), none over the army behind him. Tracks too ("please also change the tracks to the head as well"; `world/tracks.js` `trackHead`, `TRACKS.head`): an army marched man by man lays its prints where its foremost man walks, not at its middle. Tests: `worldui`, `trackhead`.
- Arrived with your army's head on your goal (2026-11-01; the owner: "only the head should be enough but only when the formation is completed"; `march.js` `marchAim`, `marchArrived`, `MARCH_MOVE.headArrive`). Sent somewhere, your march formation makes for a point short of your goal by its front (its foremost place's reach ahead of its middle as it faces that way), so its head stops on the spot and it forms behind it; arrived once every man is in his place, or after `arriveWait` 3 s of its guide standing (men held off theirs). A column's guide is its head already; a chase, a follow and a camp to pitch keep their goal itself. The arrival puff goes by your nearest man (`worldui.js`). Off: your middle on the goal, as before. Tests: `headarrive`.
- Following keeps on (2026-11-01; the owner: "by following, can you just make it keep follow without stop?"; `world.js` `FOLLOW.keep`). Out of sight you keep to its trail; it inside a holding, you wait at its gate (told once) and take up its trail when it comes out; a battle, a talk or an encounter pauses it. It ends only when the party is gone (dead, broken) or by your own order: Stop following, another order, a camp, a stay. `keep` false: as before. Tests: `follow`.
- Meeting and following another army at its edge (2026-11-02; the owner: "I interact with other army, like meet, follow, it'll focus on the center"; `march.js` `marchMeetAt`, `MARCH_MOVE.meetEdge`). Meeting (`approach`, a chase), your goal is his man nearest you, not his middle (the meeting still when the outlines touch, `worldBattleReach`); following (`followStep`), you keep `FOLLOW.trail` tiles behind his ring's edge, never among his men. Off: his middle, as before. Tests: `meetedge`, `follow`.
- A column keeps pace (2026-11-02; the owner: "the unit will walk a bit, then stop, then walk again"; `march.js` `marchLead`, `MARCH_MOVE.follow`). In a column on the march a man with a comrade walking just ahead in his file, his way, has his speed capped to the comrade's (and a little to close a gap past their bodies): he never comes up on him and halts for want of a sidestep (an accordion: a hero behind 6 men stopped dead twice in 60 px). The way is found as ever; one standing, or a formation, a crowd: as before. Tests: `marchjam`, `roadcolumn` and the rest of the march.
- The map's camera eased after your army (2026-11-02; the owner: on a short walk "the camera will snap"; `gfx/render.js` `camFollow`, `CAM_FOLLOW`). Not locked on your army's point (which wobbles a few px for seconds as its men settle), but within `dead` (6) px of the screen's middle it stands still and past that glides on over `ease` (.25 s); a leap past `snap` px is gone to at once. The tests' fixed view keeps the locked camera unless one asks (`force`). Tests: `camfollow`.
- A freeze fixed (2026-11-02): a corridor's leg of no length ending where none stands (a point inside a holding's walls) stepped in place for ever (`MarchArmy#corridor`), so the game froze; found following a lord. Now it stays, its step-through capped at the grid's width. Tests: `corridor`, `follow`.
- A lord you do not know changes his mind quietly (2026-11-01; the owner: "I randomly get to know lords that I'm not even met yet"; `world/relations.js` `Relations.change`). A deed's share felt by a lord's friends and rivals (`ripple`) logged their names, and a name in the log makes him known (`knowledge.js` `hear`), so lords you never met turned up, whereabouts unknown. Now a change is told only of a lord you know; it still counts. News, rumours, talk and sight still make lords known. Tests: `relations`.
- Its ring is the outline of the ground its men stand on (the owner: "how about making a ring like freeform based on the area?"; `MarchOutline`, `MARCH_RING`). Each man is a soft disc (his body's half and `pad`; a caravan's wagon one too) on a grid of `cell` px about the middle; the outline is where the field is `threshold` (a lone man's disc edge), by marching squares, drawn as smooth quadratic curves. Men within about `merge` px make one shape; along a wall it is concave; men far apart make islands; holes are left out. The dashed line is the same field at the level found `WORLD_BATTLE.interaction` px out from the outline (the median of a few points). Eased by the army's time (`grow` fast, `shrink` slowly); made again only when a man moved `tol` px about the middle or while it eases; buffers reused. Made afresh, no easing from its old shape, when a man moved `jump` px about the middle at once or men moved with no time gone (a battle's end: the owner, 2026-11-01, "after the fight the units scattered around and the army ring look weird"); the fallen are left out (of the outline, the meetings and the banner's middle). Only for armies on screen (`marchCircle` with the army, from `marchDraw`); herds and your camp keep the circles. Cost with 100 men: about 0.03 ms a frame to remake it, 0.03 ms to draw it. Off (`MARCH_RING.on` false): the circles.
- Meetings measure to the outline (the owner's decision, 2026-11-01): `worldBattleReach` gives back the distance between the two middles less the gap between their nearest men (each less his disc, less `interaction`), so `dist <= reach` holds just when the outlines touch; a place (`ringReach`) when your nearest man's disc and `interaction` touch its reach (its footprint toward you); the road's hail and ambush spring from your nearest man (`marchNear`). Boxes first (`MarchArmy#box`), an answer kept for the moment. Not marched man by man (no army, far, in camp), world battles off, or `MARCH_RING.meet` false: the circles, as before. Tests: marchring (Ashford's castle and market, a straggler behind a wall, an L, the road's events, a natural world at 1-8x), marchpivots, placereach, follow.
- A whole army is clickable (2026-11-01; the owner: "can you make a whole army clickable in the world map? right now it's hard for me to press at center"; "when I click at the lords, or other army, band"). Marched man by man (`marchPicked`), a lord, band, patrol, companion's party, caravan or beast pack answers anywhere on its men's figures as drawn (each man's opaque box, `MarchArmy#figure`), anywhere inside its outline (its field at `MARCH_RING.threshold`) and on its banner, for the click (its ring: Approach, Follow, Look) and the hold ring alike; a caravan on its cart, ox and drover too (`MarchArmy#hit`, `world.js pickAt` `army`). Ranked: a banner (9.5), then a figure (10-12, by how near its box's middle), then any building or place under the pointer, then an outline (13 on, the nearer army's man first). Its small ring round its middle (10 px; a beast's 11; a caravan's 11, not its army's ring) stays and ranks as ever, so a small band is never harder to click. Inside your own outline, your man nearer (or the pointer on him), it is ground: a click travels, a hold opens the map's ring. A finger's tap has `MARCH_PICK.tap` (8) CSS px of slack round each figure and the outline. Hover picks the same, its brackets round all its men (`MarchArmy#figBox`). Cheap: a box test per army, the outline's field read in O(1), its men looked at only when the pointer is in its box; one answer object kept per army. World battles off, an army not marched man by man, or `MARCH_RING.on` false: the old circle and leader's figure. Tests: armyclick, clicks, mapfigures, touch, ring.
  - Its ring opens where you clicked it, not at its middle (`ui/input.js` `partyRing(o, wx, wy)`). A character's crossbow (your hero's, a companion's) is drawn and shot as a crossbow, its reload a crossbow's (`gfx/people.js` `lookOf`, `battle/actors.js` `shotKind`); was drawn and shot as a bow.
- The March formation window never zooms closer than an army 36 px across (`MARCH_PREVIEW.minR`), so a lone hero is not drawn huge.
- Campaign travel is 1.6× the base pace (was 2×; the owner: "drop campaign speed about 20%", `WORLD_SCALE.travel`).
- Crossbows on the bows' scale (the owner: "improve range of crossbowmen"): Crossbowman 320, Arbalester and the dwarves' Crossbowman 380, against a Bowman's 400. They still hit hard and slow.
- In a battle on the map each leader starts at his marcher's place (`marchRowOf`: a leader's battle group stands for the row `leader`), with variant 0 as on the map. After it, an army's middle goes where its leader's place puts it (`MarchArmy#middleOf`), else to its survivors' midst (`settle`). Yours goes where your hero's place puts it.
- Tests: marchpivots, marchdrag, marchcounts, marchpaths, marchlooks, marchappearance.

**March styles** (2026-10-31; the owner: AI groups "bunch too close" round their leader; room between groups and styles like a real battle setup, so you read an army's intent from its order; `world/marchstyles.js`, numbers in `MARCH_STYLES`):
- Every army not yours with no saved layout stands in a style. Offensive: riders in front in wedges, the infantry behind the centre round its leader, the shooters at the rear. Defensive: a spear or shield line in front, the shooters behind it round its leader, the riders in columns on the flanks. Screen (bands): a loose screen of foot, the archers out on its sides, the chief behind. Pack (beasts): round its leader or biggest group. Convoy (caravans): the guards round the wagon, facing out.
- Chosen by its kind (band, beasts, caravan), else its troops' race as a battle captain reads it (`temperFor`: orcs offensive, dwarves defensive, elves a screen), else a lord's errand (to war, a siege, a relief, a marshal's host, riding out to defend: offensive; home, patrol, rest: defensive), else defensive. No `Math.random`.
- Groups stand `gap` (8 px) apart, at most `wrap` (3) abreast a rank. Each takes the form its place asks for when its men suit it (`FORMS` who; a wedge 6 riders at least, a pack two), else loose, else a line; its files from the ranks the style asks.
- **Yours: battle lanes** (2026-11-01; the owner: the battle screen opened with mages level with the infantry; "keep battle lanes": yes). Your army with no layout saved stands in the style `lanes` (`MARCH_STYLES.yours`): the infantry in front, the mages a step behind it beside your hero, the archers and crossbows behind them, the riders on the flanks a little back (beside your hero's rank or the shooters', whichever leaves them least far out: `flank` a list, `MarchStyle#flanks`). Several of a kind stand abreast, at most `wrap` (4) and no wider than `span` (2) of its widest groups; then a rank behind. Your hero is at the middle, on the lanes' line (`mid`); your companions beside him. Each group keeps its own formation and frontage (`own`: the March window's), so a battle opens in the shape you gave it. A group you placed in the March window stands where you placed it; the rest, a new one too, in their lanes; Reset all puts the lanes back. The same lanes for any army with a saved layout. A battle opens from them, on the map and on the battle screen (`setup.js` `arrangeMarch`: x forward, y across).
- One ring rule for every army, yours too (the owner: "big army, bigger interaction touchpoint"): `marchOuter` is its room (`marchRoom`, where you place your groups: `marchSpace`, the old ring by its size and widest group, or as far as its default layout goes, whichever is bigger) or its whole layout with its farthest man `margin` (4 px) inside, whichever is bigger. Interaction is that plus `WORLD_BATTLE.interaction`. A man's spot counts his body (`marchPad`, `marchSlots`: a rider's or a big beast's wider). Your lanes stand clear of each other, so a big army is wider than the old ring was (97 men in nine groups: 124 px, the old ring at most 64 with its groups on each other).
- `marchLayout` makes an army's rows and ring again only when its style, size, groups or saved layout change; `marchRows` and `marchOuter` read it. A battle starts from it (on the map, and the battle screen's `arrangeMarch`).
- A saved layout is centred on the groups it has now (the box round their places, `marchLayout`): your hero placed at the front and marching alone is his army's middle, the ring round him, not round the places of groups away (the owner, 2026-11-01: the ring off him, swinging round him as the march turned). The lanes, round your hero already, are as they are.
- A night watch's torch is in its carrier's hand: its leader's marcher, else its first (`MarchArmy#hand`, `marchTorch`), at his hand's height, moving with him; its light is cast there. Your band carries no torch.
- Tests: marchstyles (the lanes on the map, turning with the march), rts (the lanes in battle), marchpivots, marchspace.

**Road travel by army type** (2026-11-01; the owner: "when travel by road it will force the marching to columns in order to snap them near the road, this should apply for all armies, otherwise no road bonus"; doc/LIVE_WORLD_DESIGN.md §6; `world/marchstyles.js`, numbers in `TRAVEL_STYLES`):
- **The road's speed is the men's.** An army's ground is the mean of `speedAt` under 8 of its men (`world.js armyGround`, fixed ones spread through it), used by `partySpeed`, `travelMul` and a caravan's pace. A column on the road has all of the bonus, a ring across it a share, an army half in a wood is half slowed. An army not marched man by man (far off, world battles off) goes by its style: a column on a road the whole bonus there, any other `share` (.35) of it.
- **Who marches in a column** (`TravelStyle.column(o)`, decided every `every` frames or when its trip changes): a caravan always; a lord on a trip of more than `long` (400 px) while no realm at war with his holds land within `near` (160 px); a patrol on its beat, hunting nothing; yours with By road on and a road near; a companion's party on a trip of its own. Bands and beasts never. An army in a host keeps its place in the host's column of armies, in its battle style.
- **The column** (`MarchColumn`): 2 abreast, in its battle style's order front to back (the van, the main body with its leader, the rear; a caravan's guards half before its wagon, half after; yours in the order you set, below), each rank 1.6 bodies behind the last. Its places lie back along its guide's own trail (`MarchTrail`, a point every 3 px), so it bends with the road; past the trail's end, straight back. Its guide is its head; its point (where it is, its banner, its ring) is its middle along the trail, gliding there. A man trailing his place on the march steers along the way just ahead of him (`steer`), so he follows a bend rather than cut it.
- **Forming takes time.** Into a column or out of one, the men walk to their new places; until 80% stand in them the guide goes .6 of its way (into a column) or .15 (out of one: an army that has seen a foe all but halts). Falling in, a man ahead of his place holds his ground beside the way till it comes up; each group's places go to its men front to back as they stand. Out of a column it forms round the column's middle. A column whose head turns back on itself turns about the same way: out of column, then into a new one.
- **Detection.** A column that detects a foe (one its men see, or your army, hostile, that it sees or fears) takes its battle style at once. By every man (2026-11-01; the owner: "Every unit spots now and has its vision range, so the army should depend on all units' capabilities"; `TRAVEL_STYLES.menSight`): each man of an army marched man by man (near you) sees `alert` (150 px) times his kind's share (`SIGHT_KIND`: a rider 161 px, a man on foot 118, a scout ×1.6) to the foe's ring, its eyes merged as yours are (`world/fog.js` `ArmySight`, `armySight`); so a column's head sees furthest down the road, and its flanks no further than its men do. Its eyes are made only when a foe is within reach of asking, and built again only when its men moved over half a px (a build of 30 men, some 0.03 ms). Yours sees by the same eyes and needs the foe in your sight too. An army far from you (no men marched), or `menSight` off: `alert` px round its ring, as before.
- **Lords keep to roads** (2026-11-01; `TRAVEL_STYLES.lordKeep` 1.1, as your By road's `ROAD_GO.keep`): a lord's trip more than `long` px when he sets out on it (`brains.js` `LordBrain#way`, kept the whole trip) is routed with off-road tiles costing 1.1 times as much (`router.js` `wayKeep`), so he keeps to the road and his column gets its pace (some 91% of his steps on a road, 88% before); a shorter trip goes as the land does. 1: as before. Tests: `armysight` (19). One ambushed, the foe unseen (lying in wait), stays a column till contact, and its world battle opens strung out. Your own sight is short without Scouting (110 px): a band on the road ahead is seen in time to form up only with scouts out.
- **Your column's order** (2026-11-01; the owner: "make march formation a menu alongside Roster", "add By road formation to let you decide the orders of units (in column)", "drag and drop adjustable preview ... locked in column form order"): the Army window's March formation tab (`ui/march.js`; the Roster's button gone), two views by a switch at its top. Field: the formation editor as before. By road (column): `ColumnEditor`, a preview of your column as it marches (`MarchColumn` itself: 2 abreast, its ranks and room), front at the right (as the Field view faces), each man on his ground in his block's shade, each block numbered. Only the order changes: drag a block along it (the others slide to make room), or tap one and then its new place (a phone), or pick it in the list and move it Ahead or Behind. Characters (your hero, each companion) are one-man entries. Apply order saves `P.army.column` (the rows' ids, front first; absent, an old save's too: the default); Reset order goes back to the default (by how far forward each stands in your field formation); Discard drops the draft, as opening the tab afresh does. `marchstyles.js` `columnOrder(o, rows)`: the rows in column order, yours by `P.army.column` (a group not in it, a new one, at its default place: after the one before it there), every other army by how far forward each stands. Tests: `tests/columnorder.js`.
- Your By road: a column; Under way's By road row reads "in column" while it is one. Following a column, you trail its middle, not its head (`marchPoint`, `followStep`; a host's trail too).
- `TRAVEL_STYLES.on` false: no columns, every army's ground at its point, as before (the fingerprints' proof).
- Tests: roadcolumn, worldscale (the road's speed a column's), marchring, hosts, follow.
- The Party card's ground is its men's (the owner, 2026-11-01: "Road" shown while most of the army stood in the woods): the ground most of the same 8 men stand on (`world.js` `armyGroundMix`, the men `armyGround` weighs), "· part on the road" when some but fewer than half are on it; its tooltip each ground's count, the ground's pace, and By road's hint.

**Places reached from your ring** (2026-11-01; the owner: "my hero have to be near camp or building to interact ... the spawn will be overlap with my army"; as for the lords, "big army, bigger interaction touchpoint"):
- A place is reached when your army's interaction ring touches its footprint (`world/march.js` `ringReach`, under `worldBattleReach`): your ring (`marchInteractRadius(P)`) plus how far the place reaches from its point toward you (`WorldObject#reachRadius`, measured to the edge of a box by `boxReach`). A click on it, or a chase, halts your army at its edge; your hero never walks into it.
- Each place's footprint: a site or camp, its picture's opaque box (`Site#reachRadius`); a holding, its own building only (its centre's lot: `lotBox(Layout#centre)`; the owner, 2026-11-01: "the holding interaction should be separated from estate buildings and houses"), each house and estate at its own lot (`House`/`Estate#reachRadius`); anything else `REACH` (14 px). A holding at war with you returns null: reached at its gate as before, so a siege's and a raid's leash (80 px from the holding) still hold.
- A fight at a place: men made for it with no army on the map (a camp's or a hideout's garrison, an estate's guards: not a party's, a joined fight's or workers') muster at the place, pushed out to its far side from your army till the two armies' rooms stand `WORLD_BATTLE.clear` (24 px) apart (`WorldBattleArea.clearOf`); standing on it, along your facing. Their chieftain forms with them, not on the place (`madeBy`); the muster forms them from their side's start. Parties' men stand where they are, as before.
- `G.lastSite` is forgotten `REACH` past where your army reaches the site. With `WORLD_BATTLE.on` false, every place is reached at `REACH` as before.
- Tests: placereach, siteopen, clicks, herowalk, encounters, worldbattle.

**The minimap** (2026-10-31; the owner: "isn't clickable"): a click or a tap on it opens the full map, as before. A phone's tap also sent its click to what the map then put under the finger (its zoom's −); that click is now eaten (`input.js` `eatClick`). A mouse over it shows a hand and the tooltip "Click: the full map (M)", not what lies on the map under it. Tests: fullmap.
Reinforcement allegiance (2026-10-25): worker/villager resistance and faction-owned party encounters now identify the defending realm. forceRealm reads the real source army before a copied force record. reinforceSide gives faction allegiance priority over friendship, neutral help rolls and stale helper lists; same-realm troops defend their own side, allied defenders support that realm, and ambiguous allied conflicts stay out. Personal companion parties remain loyal to the player. Field-battle membership is preserved where one realm appears on both sides. Initial helpers are aligned before previews and spawning; WorldBattleArea.teamFor uses the same decision for late arrivals. Unaffiliated outlaw bands only reinforce matching band kinds. Existing peace/war, neutral chance, range and helper-cap rules still apply when no faction decision resolves the side. No new diplomacy penalty is applied just to choose a side. Tests: reinforceallegiance (21 passed); existing reinforce has the same 30 pass/13 fail result with this change enabled and disabled.
Waterskin interaction range (2026-10-25): world-mode shoreline and well refills use the same `marchInteractRadius(P)` as the army�s outer dashed ring. Reach grows/shrinks with army size; action-menu and automatic filling share the range through supplyLand/fillReach. Valid drawn water near map edges remains reachable; off-map water is excluded. Refill timing, empty-skin requirements and movement cancellation are unchanged. Tests: waterskinreach (14 passed).

World routing: escaped units return through a reachable terrain route instead of a straight radial line. Physical world-battle missiles scale with character sprites. Regression coverage: tests/worldretreat.js.

March formation's wounded (2026-10-30; the owner: "should show current wounded units as well"): each group's wounded drawn in its last places, faded (`MARCH_PREVIEW.hurt`), a red cross on each (`marchHurt`); the selected group's line says how many are fit and wounded; a group all wounded is named under the field, out of the formation. Its dashed footprint is still its fit men's. Tests: marchwounded.

March formation guides: dashed unit footprints follow actual slots and must fit inside the army placement circle. Selected ranged groups show base attack coverage; the live world-battle overlay follows native map scaling. Shared firing geometry lives in ui/battlehud.js; editor guides and previews in ui/march.js. Tests: marchrange and formationfacing.

March editor dragging: right-drag a line to set the selected group frontage, placement and facing; touch users choose Draw formation first. Move groups repositions normally. Apply saves the relative heading for travel and starting world-battle formations; Back discards edits. Wedges preserve their shape and all formations stay inside the army circle. Tests: marchdrag.

March circle: allowed radius and view framing stay fixed throughout an editing session. Overlarge frontage fits inside that circle. Formation guides show facing arrows while drawing and after placement.

World battle effects: magic bolts, cosmetic glows, particles/trails, ghosts and ground-stuck arrows match the world sprite scale. Slash detail and area-outline weights are enlarged while combat reach and area radii remain accurate. gfx/vfx.js owns shared drawing-scale helpers; gfx/render.js applies them to projectiles and combat marks. Tests: worldeffects.

Battle fog integration: use normal fogMem/drawWorldFog directly; WorldBattleArea.fog was removed. After merging renderer changes, battleworldfog and worldeffects check the complete world-battle drawing path.

World battle zoom centres the camera on the battle area on each wheel, keyboard or pinch zoom step. Manual panning remains available; the fixed battlefield boundary is unchanged. Tests: battlezoom.

World-battle accuracy and concealment: projectiles use swept, sprite-scaled hitboxes (including cavalry); flat shots aim at scaled body height. BattleSight tree/terrain concealment also runs in world battles within the normal world sight range, initialized after actors are placed. Hidden enemies stay out of rendering, picking and AI target lists until detected or revealed by attacking. Tests: worldhitvisibility and concealment.

World-battle bystanders: nearby mobile nonparticipants retreat to reachable ground outside the battle area, including armies, workers, wildlife, monsters, caravans, refugees, ships and moving road encounters. Reinforcements keep joining. Routes respect land/water/flight; original orders remain for afterward. Local reaction state is owned by BattleBystanders in battle/worldbattle.js. Tests: battlebystanders.

Local bandit defence: nearby eligible lords and guards reliably reinforce bandit fights on their faction’s current territory, without a neutral-help roll or odds check. Hostility and faction allegiance remain authoritative. Rules live in battle/engage.js; tests/localbanditdefence.js covers initial and arriving helpers.

Outdoor world encounters: caravans show their real marching guards around the cart and carry those actors into seamless combat with matching counts, race/type, faction cloth and casualties. Estates, outdoor camps and ruins also fight on their map location. Cave interiors and siege/raid assaults retain special battlefields, as do tournaments and the lab. Tests: outdoorworld and caravanparty.

Recovery timing: wounded troop and companion cards/details show estimated time remaining and ready day/hour. Army Condition includes troop-type recovery estimates. Camp, lodging and Medicine affect forecasts through current healing rates; troop daily rolls mean actual dates can vary. Recovery rules are unchanged. Tests: recoveryeta and recoverypace.

Travel concealment: a closed-eye badge over your hero indicates forest cover outside battle. It disappears when you leave cover; detection rules are unchanged. Tests: travelconcealicon.

Travel stealth eyes: closed / partial / fully open eyes show unaware / suspicious / detected. Each travel observer builds its own suspicion within the existing cover-adjusted sight range. AI pursuit, avoidance and automatic encounters wait for detection; player-initiated interaction remains available. Visible observers have individual eyes; your army has an overall gauge. Hidden entities are not revealed by markers. Runtime state and TRAVEL_AWARE tuning live in world/worldobject.js. Tests: travelsuspicion and traveldetection.

Travel stealth UI is icon-only. Your army rings match the overall eye colour: teal undetected, amber near detection, gold seen by neutral/friendly observers, red seen by hostile observers. Circle sizes and the yellow battle boundary stay unchanged.

Travel detection eyes use larger fixed screen sizes: 18px for observers and 36px above your army.

An observer still suspicious of you but gone from its list (a beast slain in an ambush, a band beaten, a caravan taken: they leave without a mark) is forgotten at once, its eye with it: `TravelAwareness.observers` asks only those `WORLD_LOD.grid` still has filed (`ChunkGrid#has`), refiled as soon as a list shrinks (the owner, 2026-11-01).

Wildlife hunting (2026-10-27): Map > Hunting area, hold a nearby spot, then Begin hunt. The entire fit army and hero enter a real world battle at their current marching positions, without hunter selection or army preparation. Each visible animal within the fixed circle becomes its own species battle unit. Auto Hunt starts enabled for this encounter, without changing the normal battle AI preference; use ordinary orders or the AI toggle to take command. Melee and ranged units fight together with normal weapons, range, cooldowns, abilities and ammunition. Wildlife uses individual species escape stamina, follows reachable exits, and never regroups after escaping the boundary. Hunters stay inside. End hunt (mobile: More) opens normal loot: meat and species trophy chances only for kills, no war gold, renown or prisoners. Survivors return to the map at their actual positions with fatigue preserved. The map preview is world/hunting.js; battle/hunting.js owns battle setup, wildlife AI and settlement. Tests: wildhunt, huntfield, huntcompat, huntlive (normal physics, terrain, fog and AI).

Hunt tracking (2026-10-27): running animals leave prints in the hunt (battle/hunting.js HuntTracks, numbers in data/exertion.js HUNT_TRACK). With no prey in sight, Auto Hunt follows the freshest trail any hunter can read (70 px, 8 more a level of Scouting), a few prints ahead of the nearest, else goes to where an animal was last seen (the herd's place on the map counts as its first sighting), so game hidden in woods or gone out of sight is found instead of the hunters standing still. The prints your hunters read are drawn on the hunt field. Prints are small real shapes (gfx/tracks.js TRACK_GLYPH: a boot sole and heel, a hoof's curve, a paw's pad and toes), turned to the nearest of four ways, lit on their up-left edge (TRACKS.glow), in lanes TRACKS.lane px apart. Tests: hunttracks.

Travel pace feedback: walking/running changes are silent. gfx/worldui.js draws a cached animated walking or running figure on the path; Hurry widens footprint spacing and accelerates the trail. Reduced motion keeps the guide still. Exhaustion refusal remains a warning. Tests: worldui, journalretention, clickhurry.

Pace icon refinement: the path guide uses a footprint for walking and the same footprint with two upward chevrons for Hurry. The running chevrons pulse subtly; reduced motion holds them still.

Travel suspicion (2026-10-25): detection takes 2 seconds on open terrain or 4 in forest at 1x. Hurry builds 1.5x faster; within 35% of sight range, 3x faster. Direct contact (at most 8 world pixels) reveals immediately. Suspicion fades over 3 seconds out of range, follows world speed and freezes while paused/in menus/battle. Lords, bands, guards, monsters, caravans, refugees, frightened workers and wildlife use the same recognition gate; firing a hunting shot alerts its herd. Range and forest modifiers remain unchanged; no travel line-of-sight/facing or NPC-to-NPC awareness changes. Suspicion is runtime-only, reset on world load/new game or taking shelter. Tests: travelsuspicion, traveldetection, wildhunt, structure.

Joining an ongoing world fight (2026-10-25): WorldBattleArea records the original Fight and every participating army, counts their fighters including leaders, and encloses all existing formation circles/spreads before fixing the boundary. Original party travel sprites/rings and the campaign fight tally are hidden during tactical combat. Existing combatants enter immediately; new helpers retain the pending reinforcement path. Joins against monsters carry the same fight identity. fightPlace relocates cached MarchArmy figures with their anchor and clears stale routes; combat placement snaps blocked starting figures to nearby open ground. Tests: joinworldfight (16), fights (18). The broader worldbattle run passed 33 checks but still reported five formation-offset/editor/save assertion failures; those assertions were left unchanged.

Travel pace badge refinement (2026-10-25): removed the separate pace figure from the path. gfx/render.js drawTravelPace reuses badgePx('walk'/'run') from battle unit cards over your hero, at 14 CSS pixels, in a row after concealment when present. Only a travel destination/chase/follow shows it; idle, sheltered, camped and combat states hide it. Path footprints retain their Hurry spacing/speed, and pace switches remain silent. Tests: worldui and travelconcealicon.

The badges keep to your army (2026-11-08; the owner: "the moving icon seems to not smooth attach to the army"): the cover and pace badges, and a band's marker, are put to the screen's own pixel (`snapV`) as the figures under them are, not to whole game pixels, so they glide with the army instead of stepping a game pixel (several screen pixels on a phone) at a time. Tests: travelconcealicon.

An army's label over its men (2026-11-08; the owner: "recheck whether all sprites is in the center"; `gfx/render.js` `armyHome`, `ARMY_TAG`): its count, the eye watching you and its banner stand over its men themselves, a middle man's place across (a column's middle; one part of an army split by a river, never the empty ground between) and a head over the topmost man near him; before, marching, they stood over its point at the front of its column. Its ring was already round its men (measured: within 3 px). Tests: figures, marchring.

World escape fatigue (2026-10-26): wildlife, lords, bands, guards, companions, caravans, refugees, monsters and frightened workers spend their own escape stamina only while fleeing. Species/party capacities differ; speed gradually falls to 55% and stamina recovers after a safe break. Changing targets never refreshes it. Automatic chase speed bonuses are removed. Player stamina, Hurry and the existing footmen/mounted/army-size travel formula remain; fatigue multiplies NPC normal speed. Their stamina is not shown on the map (the owner, 2026-10-28: the badges were removed). Remaining stamina is saved. Tests: escapestamina, fleeroutes, wildlifethreats.

Travel awareness observes each marching group at its physical location, with natural forest cover and recent sighting memory. All groups follow their army leader. The Hold concealed command has been removed; obsolete saved hold anchors have no effect. Normal and Ambush attack choices remain available through enemy interactions.

Travel detection now builds suspicion per group, measured to the group's nearest man (2026-11-01; the owner: "nearest man"; `TRAVEL_GROUPS.nearest`, your hero at his own marcher), with its footprint, forest cover and movement; before, a group's middle less at most 12 px of its spread, so a long file's tail beside a lord went unseen. Group size adds at most 25% sight distance. Moving groups build suspicion 1.35x faster. An observer estimates power only from groups it has detected, remembering them for 8 seconds, and pursues visible bait instead of an unseen hero. Other observers have independent knowledge. Existing terrain concealment, attack reveal and charge ambush shock apply in combat; there is no extra damage multiplier. World forest tiles also protect concealment at coarse battle-grid edges. Tuning: TRAVEL_GROUPS in world/travelgroups.js; recognition in world/worldobject.js. Tests: travelgroups, travelsuspicion, traveldetection, worldhitvisibility.

March editor: army-sized placement view by default; Show range opens the selected ranged group’s reach overview. Undo keeps the last 20 edits, Reset group restores only the selected group, and red drag outlines flag requests outside the placement boundary before they are fitted inside. Terrain obstacles remain resolved during travel.

Deployment: the repository root has an `index.html`. Run `python3 scripts/build-web.py` from the root to create `dist/site/` and `dist/ashmark-web.zip` with all runtime dependencies. See `DEPLOY.md` for hosting and phone testing.

Mobile world HUD: one bottom dock keeps Pause, a collapsible speed picker, Map, Army and Menu together. The compact day/party strip opens the full condition card; opening speed or tapping away closes it. Menu opens a bottom sheet with all windows, including Skills and Help. Existing desktop and battle controls keep their layout. Bottom safe areas and viewport resize are respected.

Fleeing on the world map: wildlife, hunted animals, frightened parties, caravans and fleeing road encounters use Router.flee. Open escapes stay direct; obstacles use a bounded reachable route held across frames. Routes reconsider a shifted threat or changed crossing, never cut blocked corners, and stay runtime-only. Normal travel, movement speed and saves are unchanged.

Mobile Map opens zoom out, zoom in and the full map. The mobile battle HUD keeps balance, horizontally scrolling unit cards and a single bottom row (Begin/Pause, Speed, Orders, More, Menu). Orders and commander powers open in a labelled drawer; More contains view controls, auto skills, health bars and selected-unit details. Drawers dismiss on outside taps or Escape. Existing controls are restored for desktop, with combat and deployment rules unchanged.

Awareness eyes use a symmetric eyelid with the pupil contained inside it. Their cached artwork is rasterized at display density and placed on physical pixels, keeping player and observer eyes undistorted across map zooms. Detection states and colours are unchanged.

Mobile awareness eyes now use opaque pixel-art eyelids and pupils, with integer-sized pixel blocks at each display density. They avoid antialiased curves, self-canvas mirroring and fractional scaling; awareness states and colours are unchanged.

The player awareness eye uses the same 18 CSS-pixel size as observer eyes.

Wildlife also flees nearby lords, bandits, patrols, companion parties, caravans, refugees, outdoor workers and monsters, including during hunts. `WildlifeThreats` (`world/critters.js`) indexes nearby threats and keeps escape headings stable; cover reduces detection range. Sheltered people and livestock do not trigger this behavior. Player stealth still uses travel awareness.

Normal world battles preserve actual marching positions, then visibly form initial armies near their meeting positions, keeping saved march offsets and troop formations. They step back only for formation clearance (20 world pixels between fronts where space permits), capped at 16 world pixels per army; larger battle circles do not increase this retreat. Combat, cooldowns and morale stay frozen during this movement. Once all reachable positions are occupied, the battle pauses; Start Battle begins combat. Ambushes, surprise attacks and joining an ongoing fight skip preparation. Late reinforcements arrive normally without restarting preparation. The sandbox keeps its own start controls; impassable ground adjusts formation slots. Battle hold progress draws in CSS pixels over the completed frame, independent of map zoom and camera scale. Commander abilities act around the hero; unit abilities act on eligible selected troop groups, with separate labels in Orders.

Forming up has a time limit (2026-11-01; the owner: "we only give forming up around 10 seconds. If exceeded, just start off like that"): `BATTLE_MUSTER.max`, 10 s of its own time (paused time not counted). Past it the battle is ready as it stands, no one moved; a man not yet in his place walks there once Start Battle is pressed, as any man of a holding group does (`actors.js`, round the river by the field's routes). A group's anchor in a house, the water or ground its men cannot reach moves to the nearest cell they can stand in and reach (`BattleMuster#stand`), so its way in battle ends at its place; each man's own unreachable place moves the same way when he is planned (`musterCell`). The muster waits under a window really open (`G.modal` with the overlay shown), never on `G.modal` alone. The owner's freeze ("I upgraded [my hero] ... I press attack and the game froze"): your hero's active skill in a slot had its text written over the units' skill bar (`combat.js` `HeroHands#updateBar` writes every `[data-slot]` on the page), the next HUD tick threw in `battlehud.js` `skillState` and the game's loop stopped, the battle frozen "Forming up". `skillState` now skips missing parts and `bhudSkills` rebuilds a bar written over (`tests/musterstuck.js`).

Group banners follow actual soldier positions during preparation and readiness, independently of the frozen combat clock. Interacting with an enemy that has not detected or recently seen your army offers Normal attack and Ambush attack. Normal attack uses preparation; Ambush attack starts immediately at current positions. The choice is rechecked when selected and also applies to monsters and caravans.

Mobile zoom keeps the former default view as its farthest view. The starting world and sprites are about 50% larger (rounded to whole device pixels), with further zoom available. Saved wider views clamp to the new limit. Desktop zoom, menus, buttons and minimap sizing are unchanged.

Wildlife awareness eyes sit beside group markers with room for their count labels at every map zoom. Start Battle keeps its centered position while pressed on touch and mouse devices.

Wildlife, wolf mounts and the tiger redrawn (2026-11-08; the owner: "make mount like wolf or warg a bit more shorter", "recheck the tiger sprites", "change wildlife sprites as well to have more detail"). The map's animals (`world/critters.js` `CRITTER_MAP`) are each a few pixels larger, with their marks: a cow's patches and horns, a sheep's fleece and dark face, a pig's snout, a goat's horns and beard, a rabbit's and a hare's long ears (the hare's tipped black) and white scut, a hen's comb and tail, a fowl's neck, a deer's ears, neck and white rump, a stag's and a reindeer's branched antlers, an ibex's swept horns, a gazelle's flank stripe, a buffalo's horns swept back from its head. A wolf's and a warg's mount body (`engine/beasts.js` `MOUNT_ART.wolf`) is four columns shorter, shifted to stay under its rider. The tiger (`KINDS.tiger`) is two columns shorter on longer legs, its stripes three slanted bars, a pale belly only under its chest, and a raised paw in its own colour (never a black block from the stripes' ring). Fingerprints held (W1–W8, the battle suites, `rts` 1, 2, 44). `huntfield`'s two checks (wildlife fleeing at its exhausted speed, a melee Auto Hunt closing) fail as they did before (at 557ebde too).

The dwarves' ram shorter in the leg and shaggier (2026-11-08; the owner: "Ram leg should be shorter more fur"; `engine/beasts.js` `MOUNT_ART.ram`): its legs two px shorter and a px thicker, its fleece in curls (a light pair over a shaded pair), tufts along its back and a ragged fringe under its belly; the whole of it a px lower, its rider with it. Fingerprints held (W1–W8, the battle suites, `rts` 1, 2, 44). `horselegs`' two old-horse pixel checks (`HORSE_ART.on` false, `.tall` false) were already failing before this (stale since the mounts' detail pass); the ram's new pixels only change the hash they print.

Multi-army world battles (2026-10-27): roaming army encounters (bands, lords, patrols, caravans, monsters and joined map fights) allow up to eight armies including the original participants. New reinforcements wait if their full force would take active combat above 300 fighters; original armies are not truncated. Each army keeps its allies, neutrals and enemies; a new army hostile to both sides creates a free-for-all. Targeting, projectiles, abilities, morale and AI use those relationships, while orders and casualties retain source-army ownership. Arrival is physical and never restarts preparation or enlarges the fixed circle. Army colours, approach messages and the HUD identify participants. Player withdrawal settles only their fight; remaining hostile armies continue as a saved campaign fight. Rewards use actual player-army kills, with leader captures earned by defeating that leader. Hunting, tournaments and fixed-site assaults retain their own battle flows. Tests: freeforall, freeforallplay, structure, huntlive; actor/attack snapshot comparisons match the previous codex commit (including pre-existing test failures); no fingerprints rerecorded.

Your allies fight on (2026-10-28, built): in a battle of several armies (battle/sides.js `BattleSides#check`), your army down is not the end while an army beside you still fights ("Your army is down. Your allies fight on."). They win: Victory, "your allies held the field": no captivity, no gold lost, rewards by your army's kills as before; each army beside you thinks better of you (a lord `RELATION.fightBeside`, his realm `REINF.honor.beside`, a watch's holding `REINF.rep`). They fall too: Defeat, through the escape roll and captivity as any lost battle (the old "lose a quarter of your gold, carried to a holding" is gone from this path). Withdrawing is still a withdrawal. Tests: allyhold.
And the others (2026-10-30): an army not at war with you that fights your foes keeps the battle going too (`BattleSides#fightOn`; a foe that flees or falls still counts, so it leaves them yours). Win: "Your army fell, but the others held the field", no captivity. While your army is down, a bar says so with **Skip to the end** (`skipBattle`, `BATTLE_SKIP`): the rest is fought out at once, as it would go, at most 180 s of it, else the stronger side by what is left holds the field. A cornered band's parley says what decides whether it yields (`YIELD_WHY`: near half your strength they seldom give up, far weaker nearly all do; renown and Persuasion help), and a refusal says why (`yieldNo`). Tests: allyhold, people.
A band that yields hands over its spoils (2026-11-01; the owner: "disarm bandit should also show renown loots from them like battle ends"): each ending (Prisoners, New recruits, Disarmed) opens a won fight's window (`parley.js` `yieldEnd`, `ui/loot.js` `lootShow`): its head and story, the renown and gold it brought, the prisoners, and the pile to take or leave; Continue back to the map (Esc as the bar; never leaving gear behind for you). Its spoils are a win's (`yieldSpoils`: `ON_WIN` party's drop, a chieftain's purse by `spoilsWon`), and those disarmed and sent off leave their weapons: the drop once more for every `YIELD_SPOILS.arms` (4) of them. No experience: no fight. Each keeps its own reward (prisoners and their stolen gold; the men, at morale and honor; mercy and renown +1). Its chieftain is your captive if you take them prisoner, else he gets away (`chiefEscapes`). A camp or hideout that yields (`lairFate`) leaves its page for the same window (sites.js `Lair` site-fate), its spoils a beaten camp's (`ON_WIN` camp's drop, its chieftain's purse and plunder), the place empty and its sheltering bands sent off as before (`lairGone`). A lord who yields does too: his ransom, renown +8 and the honor it costs as ever, his spoils a beaten lord's (`ON_WIN` lord's drop, his purse), the soldiers sent home unarmed leaving their weapons. Tests: yieldspoils, people, sites.
Two foes on you (2026-10-30): when your army is down and the foes still fighting are at war with each other (a watch and bandits), the battle goes on between them (`BattleSides#rivals`; the bar: "Your foes fight on against each other", with Skip to the end), and whoever holds the field takes you (`victor`: the strongest still fighting, their health by their tier; `captorEnc`): its realm's men, its lord or its band, who grow by the win as in a battle of two (`chiefWins`, `lordWins`, a watch's crime cleared). A battle is Withdrawn only when you withdrew (`retreating`: men leaving under a withdraw order, `B.withdrawn`); a man routing off the field is no withdrawal. Tests: allyhold, freeforall.
Your army down, in any battle (2026-10-30): once none of your own men (your hero and your groups) is fighting while the battle goes on (`sides.js` `ownDown`: allies on your side, others against your foes, or your foes against each other), a bar over the field offers **Skip to the end** (`skipBattle`: the fight run out at once in the field's own steps, at most `BATTLE_SKIP.max` s, then the stronger has the field) and **Auto-resolve** (`resolveBattle`: settled now, won with the chance of your side's strength left against theirs, `armyPower`). Tests: allyhold.
Spoils (2026-10-30): won, by your army or by your allies after it fell, the spoils of every foe beaten (gold, renown, prisoners) are shared by each winning army's part in the fight, the harm it did the foe (`BattleSides#shares`; yours counts from before you fell); a lord or watch beside you in a battle of two takes its cut the same way (`engage.js` `reinfCut`, else by its men). Lost or withdrawn: nothing but the experience your army earned (your hero's for its kills, `BATTLE_SPOILS.lostXp` each, and your troops'). Tests: battlespoils.

No prisoners when your army is down (2026-11-04; the owner: "if your army is all downed I think you should not take a prisoner"): won with none of your own left standing (your hero and every man of yours fallen; `resolution.js` `armyDown`), you take no prisoners, no chieftain captive (he gets away, `chiefEscapes`) and no lord (he rides home, `lordWon` take false), unless a quest asks for one taken (a quest's `takesAlive(e)`: none does yet). The gold, renown and experience are shared by your part as ever. The report says so. Each army's performance score is on the report, under its name: the harm it did its foes (`BattleSides` damage), and, won, its share of the spoils (`ArmyReport.row` score, share; `ui/loot.js`). Test: `tests/battlespoils.js`.
Arrivals (2026-10-30): an army joining a world battle shows where it will come in: a marker on the ring toward it (`worldbattle.js` `drawArrivals`, `ARRIVAL`), in its colour by where it stands with you (yours, ally, foe, neutral), its banner over it and its name, an arrow pointing in; it pulses while the army closes in and its men walk in, and fades once all are in. Tests: sidestall.
Who rides in unasked (2026-10-30; the owner: beasts joining a battle in a holding, bandits piling into big clashes): bands and beasts not of the kind you fight (`worldbattle.js` `JOIN_RULES`; a pack or band riding to its own kin joins as ever) come only from `reach` px past the ring (120; lords, watches and companions keep `WORLD_BATTLE.join`, 220) and weigh the fight every `every` s (`WorldBattleArea#tempted`): a band joins when the field is small (no side over `band.small` men fighting) or worn (after `band.after` s with `band.worn` of its men down), by `band.chance`; a beast only if it is a hungry kind (aggro), never within `beast.near` px of a holding, by `beast.chance`. Tests: joinrules.

Only one that knows of the fight rides in (2026-11-01; the owner: a troll came in that he could not see near the fight): its edge within the joiner's own sight (`WORLD_SCALE.sight`, by kind: lords and watches 90 px, bands 95, beasts 70) plus the fight's noise (`JOIN_RULES.hear`: 20 px, 0.5 a fighter, at most 60), never past the old reach (`WorldBattleArea#knows`). Your companions' parties and the lords riding in your host are with you, and join as before; kin still always come, but only those who see or hear it. Tests: `joinrules`.
Room on the march (2026-10-30; the owner: "units cannot overlap"): each marcher has his body, as in battle (`world/march.js` `marchBody`: foot 11, mounted 16, big 18, at `WORLD_BATTLE.sprite/scale` on the map); a row of bigger bodies than its formation's spacing marches wider (`marchRoomK`); a step that would bring two closer than their bodies is turned aside, goals keep a body's room (`MarchGround#snap`), and after each step `MARCH_MOVE.part` passes push apart any two still too close (the pivot and a leader drawn apart never pushed). Tests: marchspace.

A smooth march (2026-10-31; the owner: units forming up "not walking but shaking"): a man's place keeps its own spot when there is a body's room there (`MarchGround#snap`; before, each place took a whole 4 px cell, smaller than a body, so places hopped from cell to cell as the army moved and their men stepped back and forth); his walk shows for `MARCH_MOVE.stride` s after his last step, so it does not flicker; a settled man stays put till his place is `wake` px off while the army stands; and one hemmed in by his own side while it stands squeezes through slowly (`squeeze`), the parting pass making room, so none is left stuck far from his place. Turning ("not smooth at all when turning"): a place in a body's way (the leader's, beside him) is pushed straight out of it rather than hopped to a cell's middle; the wheel is slower (`MARCH_MOVE.turn` .9 rad/s, was 1.5); and a walking man swings his way at most `steer` (.45 rad) a step, so he curves round. None goes faster than the march (marchwheeling). Marching bodies (2026-10-31; the owner: "like unit really have physics"; doc/LIVE_WORLD_DESIGN.md §2): each marcher has a velocity and a heading; his heading turns at his battle kind's rate (`physKind`, `PHYSICS.kinds`: a footman 7 rad/s, a knight 2.2) and his speed comes up to the army's pace over his kind's time (`marchKind`: a footman .24 s, a knight .78 s, × `MARCH_MOVE.body`), braking as he nears his place; he goes only the way he faces, so no jump aside and no sudden stop. Round a friend he keeps to the side he took; one hemmed in a while (blocked, or only stepping round) squeezes through, the men he passes giving way (`squeeze`). Sharp swings in a turn: 19% of steps at first, then 9%, now under 1%. A marcher's walk steps by the time he has walked, `MARCH_MOVE.cadence` (9) frames a second as in an arena battle, not by the ground he covers: the map's time runs fast, and by distance his legs flicked about 21 frames a second ("like running or shaking"). Tests: marchsmooth.

No army freezes in a narrow place (2026-11-01; the owner: an army whose men jam in a crossing must never freeze): the army's middle never runs ahead of its men (`MARCH_MOVE.leash`), so men jammed in a ford or on a bridge, pressing on one another, held it for good (your band following a lord stood still; battle lanes put every group of an army of foot ahead of your hero, all pressing into the crossing at once). Now a man stuck `MARCH_MOVE.jam` (1.5) s while the army marches squeezes through as one does when it halts (`squeeze`), single file through the narrow place, the parting pass making room. Tests: marchjam (a one-tile ford, a bridge and a pass in a cliff, foot only and with riders: through, never standing more than 3.5 s; without the rule, 37 s and for good), hosts.

Filling places (2026-11-01; the owner: "when unit groups moving in march formation or battle, just make units try to fill the position instead of fixed to one specific unit"): a group's men are interchangeable; its characters (your hero, a companion, a lord, a chief) keep their own place, and no man changes group. Each new set of places goes to the men by the least walking in all (`core/assign.js` `SLOTFILL`, `assignSlots`): a line turned about takes its back rank for its front, nobody walking through the ranks. Battle (`battle/formations.js` `layForm`, `fillForm`): a move, a new facing, width or formation; a man fallen, the formation laid again for the rest (`battlecontrol.js` step, `FORM_FILL`); every `FORM_SWAP.every` s, its free men trade the places they hold when that saves `FORM_SWAP.gain` px of walking (`BattleControl#refill`: men pushed or back from a rout). A man at grips (a foe within his reach and `FILL_GRIPS` px) keeps his place through a re-form (`slotKept`) till his fight is over, then the formation is laid again round all. The march (`world/march.js` `MarchArmy#pick`, `#refill`, `#fillStep`): men to places when its rows are made again or a man falls; at once when its column forms or breaks; when its places have turned `SLOTFILL.march.turn` rad, and every `SLOTFILL.march.every` s while it marches, when that saves `SLOTFILL.march.gain` px; any new share makes its outline afresh (`MarchOutline` leap: its middle moves with the share). `MarchArmy#forGroup` gives a group's men in their places' order, so a world battle sets each where his place's man stood. Cost: 15 men 0.02 ms, 30 men 0.06 ms, 100 men about 0.8-1.5 ms (greedy). `SLOTFILL.on` false: as before (each man by his index; `fileIn` forming a column; front slots first in battle, pairwise swaps). Tests: `tests/slotfill.js`.

The woods cover a world battle's fighters (2026-10-31; the owner: "when battle, the forest seems not to cover units"): fought on the world map, each soldier is drawn as the map's own figures are, with the trees in front of him put back over him, faded over your own men (`gfx/render.js` drawBattle through `depth.js` `worldOverdraw`, under the map scene's transform, each drawn under the field's). About 6% more a frame with 120 men in a wood. Arena battles were already sorted with their trees. Tests: battlewoods.
No battle stands still (2026-10-30; the owner's army stood idle as the watch routed and bandits joined): an army coming in is known where it stepped in (`heralded`: every other side's last sight of it), a captain whose foes are only leaving the field goes looking instead of charging at them and standing (`commander.js`), toward a world battle's ring middle when it knows nothing, and the stall watch (`stallWatch`) sends every group the AI has at its nearest foe still fighting once no blow has landed for `FOE_CMD.stall` s. Tests: sidestall.
Camp attacks (2026-10-30; the owner: "when you try to attack a bandit camp you won't get into the battle screen"): a camp's men, with no army of their own (sheltering bands, a camp after a load), stand for the camp in the world battle (`WorldBattleSetup#initialize`: `e.ref`), so its setup no longer throws and the battle opens. Tests: campattack, campbands.

Battle army relationships (2026-10-27): ui/battlehud.js BattleArmyHud adds an Armies drawer to world battles. Tap an army banner to read its allies, enemies and neutral parties without changing selection. A compact reinforcement list shows screen-relative direction, player relationship, approach and fighter-limit waiting states; tapping a notice opens that army. Arrival callouts use friendly, hostile or neutral colours. The drawer follows the mobile bottom dock, scrolls within the viewport and closes with Escape or an outside tap. Tests: armyintel (portrait and landscape mobile), mobilebattlehud.

Multi-army target coordination (2026-10-27): commander.js ArmyTactics wraps the existing arm tactics only when independent battle sides are active. AI groups favour nearby threats, retain useful formation targets, switch promptly when a target is defeated or unreachable, and assemble scattered formations before another distant attack. A nearby threat cancels assembly. Fleeing opponents are pursued only in immediate contact, for three seconds or 90 battle pixels, then ignored briefly; ordinary two-sided combat, hunting, manual targets, army speed and stamina retain their existing rules. Actors, characters and spatial targeting share the automatic pursuit contract. Tests: armytactics, freeforallplay.

Curved cavalry cycles (2026-10-27): commander.js CavalryRide coordinates mounted melee wings when physics is enabled. They flank through approach waypoints, charge briefly, peel toward the clearer side and loop behind the target for another run-up. Recovery waits for formation and stamina; a nearby threat allows defence. Bridge staging, surprise rushes, mounted archers and tight-ground fallback keep the existing tactics. physics.js PHYSICS.horse gives moving mounts a speed-dependent turning radius, low-speed steering, retained forward movement through turns and a committed turn side when the destination is behind them.
